# Project agent memory

This file is the project's committed home for project-intrinsic agent knowledge: build, test, release, architecture, and sharp-edge notes that should travel with the code.

- Add durable project-specific notes here as they are discovered through real work.

## Runtime

The native Swift cockpit (`native/`) is the only app in this repo. The old Python/FastAPI backend, its WKWebView shell (`desktop.py`), and its py2app packaging (`build_app.sh`, `setup.py`, `pyproject.toml`) were removed once the native app became feature-complete - see `native/README.md` for build/run instructions.

## Native app (`native/`, Swift + SwiftTerm)

- The sole cockpit surface. Build with `swift build` / run with `swift run` from `native/` (Command Line Tools only, **no Xcode / `xcodebuild`**). First build is ~90s. Full run + captain-validation steps live in `native/README.md`; the authoritative design + verified API shapes are in `data/cockpit-native-design-scout/report.md` (sections 3, 4.3, 5, 6, 7, 9).
- **SwiftTerm is vendored, not a remote SPM dependency** (`native/Vendor/SwiftTerm/`, pinned to upstream 1.15.0 / commit `dd2fb8ac...`; `Package.resolved` no longer exists, since there are no remote dependencies left). This is a deliberate patch, not a stylistic choice: SGR-2 (dim/faint) text rendering (`NSColor.dimmedColor(towards:)` in `Mac/MacExtensions.swift`, mirrored in `iOS/iOSExtensions.swift`) blended a flat 50% toward the background regardless of whether that background was dark or light - fine on dark themes (~4.9:1 contrast) but collapsing to ~2.9:1, nearly invisible, on light ones - and none of `dimmedColor`, `getAttributes`, `buildAttributedString`, or `mapColor` are `open` (nor is `TerminalView.draw(_:)`), so there is no override point from outside the module. The patch (new file `Dimming.swift`) targets a fixed 4.5:1 WCAG contrast ratio against the background via bisection, capped at the original 50% so dark themes render unchanged. See `native/Vendor/SwiftTerm/README.md` for the full writeup and how to re-apply the patch if SwiftTerm is ever upgraded.
- **Second SwiftTerm patch: truecolor de-emphasised text (cockpit-native-fixes5).** The `dimmedColor` patch above only fires for the SGR-2 "faint" attribute - some tools de-emphasise text via a literal 24-bit truecolor foreground instead (`ESC[38;2;r;g;bm`), which carries no theme awareness at all since the child process chose concrete RGB bytes with no idea what background they'd land on. Confirmed live via `tmux capture-pane -e` on a real firstmate session: Claude Code renders its own status lines ("Searched for N files...") as `ESC[38;2;153;153;153m`, not SGR-2 dim - ~6.7:1 contrast on a dark theme (fine) but ~2.55:1 on light (illegible), and `getAttributes`'s `.dim` branch never touches it. `getAttributes` (`Apple/AppleTerminalView.swift`) now also checks `attribute.fg` for the `.trueColor` case and calls the new `NSColor.legibleColor(against:)`/`UIColor.legibleColor(against:)` (`Mac/MacExtensions.swift` / `iOS/iOSExtensions.swift`, backed by `Dimming.contrastFixBlendFraction`), which remaps *any* truecolor foreground that doesn't already meet the contrast floor - self-gating (no-op once contrast is sufficient) and hue-preserving (blends toward whichever of black/white the color can reach the most contrast against, not simply "toward its own background" like the dim patch - that direction has to be chosen by evaluating both endpoints, since a foreground already close to one extreme has almost no headroom left in that direction). See `native/Vendor/SwiftTerm/README.md`'s "Second patch" section.
- **Third SwiftTerm patch: wrap redraw boundary (`fm/grandline-terminal-wrap-duplicate-char`).** A captain-reported bug where a long line's wrapped continuation row could show a stray duplicate of the original line's leading (colored) character. Extensive testing ruled out `Buffer`/`Terminal`'s own wrap and reflow logic (`insertAsciiRun`, `insertCharacter`, `reflowNarrower`) and a fresh full-render bitmap as sources of *content*-level duplication - all correct in every scenario tried. The actual fix targets a real asymmetry in `TerminalView.updateDisplay`'s invalidation-rect math: it already extended a partial redraw's dirty rect down by one cell when the range didn't reach the last screen row (to catch descender/tall-glyph overhang), but had no symmetric upward extension for a range that didn't start at the first screen row - exactly a wrap's continuation row's shape. Extracted into a pure, testable `TerminalView.invalidationRegion(...)` and fixed symmetrically; see `native/Vendor/SwiftTerm/README.md`'s "Third patch" section for the full investigation, including the explicit caveat that this could not be proven live in a sandboxed, display-less environment (`cacheDisplay` bypasses incremental redraw, which is exactly the class of bug this fix targets) - covered by `TerminalWrapRedrawSelfTest.swift`'s geometry-only self-test, not a visual repro.
- **The console** (`main.swift` -> `ConsoleController`) hosts a **dynamic tab collection**, not a fixed two-tab enum. Tabs are `[TabModel]` (`TabModel.swift`), each owning a `CockpitTerminalView` + a `TabLaunch` (re)start recipe, rendered as `TabChipView` chips in a growing tab bar. Supports **new** (`⌘T`/`+`), **duplicate** (`⌘D`, re-runs the same argv), **rename** (double-click / `⌘⇧R` / right-click, name is per-tab and never touches the process), **close** (`⌘W`; closing the last tab opens a fresh shell so the window is never empty), and `⌘1`…`⌘9` select. Tab ops are on the **Tab** menu; zoom+theme on **View**. `ConsoleController.openFirstmateHost()` opens the built-in Shell + Mirror pair; `loadView` calls it at startup so the app still lands there by default, and it is also the pinned Hosts-sidebar entry's connect action (see below). This is Phase 0 of the connection-manager work (design: `data/cockpit-ssh-manager-research/report.md`, Section A4/A5 + D Phase 0). Later phases add the dashboard + embedded backend (P3) and packaging/signing (P4); the console stays backend-free.
- **Navigation shell.** `AppShellController` is the window's root: a fixed, always-visible `IconRailController` (84pt, labeled+sectioned - see the dedicated bullet below) on the left, and to its right a `TopBarController` (destination title, the Search pill with its ⌘K badge, and the theme-picker button) above a body area. `RailDestination`'s case order (`IconRailController.swift`) drives the rail's top-to-bottom icon order directly - since Swift derives `CaseIterable.allCases` from declaration order, reordering the destinations is a one-line change there (currently `overview, console, hosts, review, settings`, below the sailboat "Home" mark and above the avatar - captain-corrected in cockpit-native-theme-audit-review, overriding fixes4 Fix 2's console/hosts-first order). The body holds all five destinations (`.overview`, `.hosts`, `.console`, `.review`, `.settings`) as permanent children with only `isHidden` toggled - never rebuilt - so navigating away never tears down `ConsoleController`'s running tabs. **`.hosts` and `.console` are decoupled destinations** (cockpit-native-ui-fixes2): `HostsController` and `ConsoleController` are each a standalone full-size body view, not panes of a shared `NSSplitViewController` - Console shows just the Firstmate Shell/Mirror tab strip, and Hosts is reachable on its own rail icon exactly like Settings. Connecting the pinned Firstmate entry (`HostsController.onConnectPinned`, wired in `main.swift`) opens its Shell/Mirror pair in the shared `ConsoleController` and calls `AppShellController.show(.console)`. Connecting a *saved* host goes through a sixth kind of destination outside the `RailDestination` enum entirely - see "Dedicated host pages" below. `.overview` shows `FleetController` (see below); `.review` shows `ReviewController` (cockpit-native-theme-audit-review): the same PR data/actions as Overview's "Ready to merge" (`OpenPRsSource.fetch()` + `FleetDataSource.mergedPRs`), grouped by forge instead of merged into one list; `.settings` shows `SettingsController` embedded directly (not a separate window).
- **`fm/grandline-sidebar-labeled-nav` redesigned the rail from icon-only to a Slack-inspired labeled/sectioned nav**, per a captain-approved Lavish design plan. `IconRailController.width` is 84pt (up from 60pt). The per-host icon block sits between two `NSBox(.separator)` hairline dividers with a small muted "HOSTS" label above it (`hostsSectionLabel`/`dividerAboveHosts`/`dividerBelowHosts`) so it reads as a visually distinct section - kept visible even with zero saved hosts, so an empty state still shows "here's where hosts go" rather than the utility group shifting up.
- **`fm/grandline-sidebar-badges` added "needs you" count badges to the rail.** `IconRailController.attachBadge(to:dest:)` overlays a small pill on every rail button's top-trailing corner - attached generically in `railButton(for:)` regardless of which visual shape that builder produces for a given row (originally two different shapes, unified into one by `fm/grandline-sidebar-nav-polish` below; the badge overlay needed no changes either time since it always positions off the button's own bounds); `setBadgeCount(_:for:)` is the only mutator and hides the badge outright whenever `count <= 0`, per `PRODUCT.md`'s "quiet until it matters." The badge is a fixed white-on-`.systemRed` pill, deliberately not a `HelmTint`-derived color - it's meant to read as an alert the same way regardless of which of the 12 Helm themes is active, matching how macOS itself never re-tints a Dock badge or Mail's unread count. Two destinations wire real counts today, both from data already computed elsewhere, never invented for this badge: `.review` gets `ReviewController`'s own `mergedPRs.count` (the same open-PR list the page renders, via a new `onOpenPRCountChanged` callback fired from `render`), and `.overview` gets `FleetController`'s own `needs_decision`/`blocked` task count (the same set its banner already text-summarizes as "N tasks need your call", via `onNeedsDecisionCountChanged`) - not `.hosts` or `.console`, since neither destination actually shows that signal. `AppShellController` wires both callbacks straight to `rail.setBadgeCount` and calls each controller's new `refreshIfNeeded()` once at launch (a public wrapper around each controller's existing private `refresh()`) so the badges have a real count before the captain ever visits either destination - no new poll loop was added; live updates otherwise ride the same triggers those two pages already had (page visit's `viewWillAppear`, the manual refresh button, a merge action's post-merge `refresh()`). Verified live (not `swift build` alone, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` + a temporary `IconRailController.debugBadgeState(for:)` (both reverted before commit) dumped real badge geometry/visibility after a real launch - `.review` showed a real nonzero count (this machine's actual open PRs) while `.overview` stayed hidden (no task genuinely needed a decision at the time) - then drove a synthetic nonzero→zero cycle confirming show/hide, and swept `helm-dark`↔`helm-light` confirming the badge's red fill is unaffected by the theme swap.
- **`fm/grandline-sidebar-nav-polish` (follow-up, live captain screenshots) made every row in the rail labeled and fixed a centering bug.** `railButton(for:)` is now the single builder for every `RailDestination` (daily-use and utility alike) - the old branch that gave utility destinations (Tools, Vault, Docs, Updates, Bootstrap, Settings) a compact icon-only style is gone, and `hostRailButton(for:)` picked up the same icon-over-label treatment so pinned hosts show a name too. `RailDestination.isDailyUse` still exists, but only to decide `navStack` membership (top block vs. bottom-anchored utility group) - it no longer selects a different visual style. **Root cause of the off-center active-highlight bug**: `NSButton.attributedTitle` lays out text using the attributed string's own paragraph alignment, not the button's `alignment` property - `restyle(_:)`'s `attributedTitle` construction had no `.paragraphStyle` attribute at all, so it defaulted to natural/left, which then also dragged `imagePosition = .imageAbove`'s icon left (the cell centers the image over the title's actual left-aligned glyph run, not the full button width). Fixed with a shared `attributedRowTitle(_:color:)` helper carrying a centered `NSParagraphStyle` - any future code building an `attributedTitle` on an `imageAbove` button needs the same explicit center alignment, or this class of bug recurs. `attachBadge`'s positioning (previous bullet) needed no change here since it anchors off `button`'s own bounds, not a hardcoded size - it still lands correctly now that every row shares this one labeled shape. Also added a hairline divider (`dividerAboveNav`) between the logo mark and the first daily-use row, matching the existing `dividerAboveHosts`/`dividerBelowHosts` style, per captain request in the same pass. Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) reading real `NSButtonCell.titleRect(forBounds:)`/`imageRect(forBounds:)` against real button bounds - confirming title/image/bounds center-X all match for every row type (daily-use, utility, host) - plus real host-label text, badge attachment on a labeled utility row, and divider frames, swept across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`.
- **`fm/grandline-rail-followup-fixes` (follow-up, live captain screenshots) found `fm/grandline-sidebar-nav-polish`'s own "fixed and verified" centering claim was incomplete, fixed the badge/Shift/separator issues from the same screenshot round.** **Centering, re-derived from scratch**: the paragraph-style fix above is real and correct for horizontal centering (re-confirmed live), but a real rendered bitmap of an *actually active* row (not just constraint math) showed a second, vertical bug that fix never touched - `NSButtonCell`'s stock `.imageAbove` layout does not vertically center the image+title block in the cell's actual resolved bounds, anchoring it near a fixed low offset instead, so 100% of any extra height the button resolves to beyond its own internal "ideal" size (confirmed live: every row resolves several points taller than its explicit `heightAnchor` constant, an unrelated `NSButtonCell` quirk) lands as dead space above the icon - invisible on an inactive row with no background to reveal it, obvious on the active row's tinted highlight. Fixed with `CenteredImageAboveButtonCell`, a small `NSButtonCell` subclass (bottom of `IconRailController.swift`) overriding `imageRect`/`titleRect` to explicitly center the image-above-title block itself, both axes, in whatever bounds the button actually has - `railButton(for:)`/`hostRailButton(for:)` both assign it in place of the default cell. No amount of `.paragraphStyle`/`alignment` tuning could have fixed this, since both only affect the glyph run within a rect the stock cell had already decided on. **Badge overflow**: `setBadgeCount` now shrinks the badge (8pt font, tighter 3pt label insets, pulled 2pt further in from the button's corner instead of 4pt) specifically once the count renders as 2+ characters (10-99, "99+") - a single-digit count keeps the original sizing untouched, per a captain screenshot showing "12" overflowing past its icon. **Shift → Tasks**: every user-facing "Shift" string now reads "Tasks" - `RailDestination.title`, the topbar destination title (`AppShellController.show`), the main menu bar's "Shift" menu and its "Search Shift…" item, and `ShiftMenuBarController`'s status-item tooltip + its popover header. Internal type/file names (`ShiftController`, `ShiftStore`, etc.) are deliberately untouched - this was a label-only rename. The menu bar status item's icon also changed from `arrow.triangle.2.circlepath` (a refresh glyph, unrelated to what the item does) to `sailboat` - the same symbol as the rail's own logo mark - since a standalone menu bar item has no nearby app branding of its own. The rail's `.shift` row deliberately keeps `checkmark.circle` rather than also switching to `sailboat`: unlike the standalone menu bar item, the rail already shows the real sailboat logo mark a few rows above this one, and a second sailboat there would read as a duplicate icon, not a clearer one. **Daily-use separators**: a hairline `NSBox(.separator)` now sits between each daily-use row (Overview | Console | Hosts | Tasks | Review) inside `navStack`, width-pinned to `navStack.widthAnchor` with a small inset - scoped to that group only, per the captain's ask; the per-host block and the utility group are unaffected. **Verified live** (not just `swift build`, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below): a temporary probe rendered the real `IconRailController` to an off-screen bitmap via `NSView.bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay(in:to:)` (no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission needed - this draws the view's own layer, it doesn't capture the screen) on an actually-active row, confirming equal top/bottom gaps around the icon+label block before vs. after the cell fix, the double-digit badge sitting fully within its icon's corner instead of overflowing, "Tasks" rendering in place of "Shift", and a divider between every daily-use row - swept across `helm-dark` and `helm-light`, reverted before commit.
- **`fm/grandline-rail-utility-separators` (follow-up) extended the daily-use group's hairline separators to the utility group (Tools | Vault | Docs | Updates | Bootstrap | Settings) for visual consistency, per captain feedback after seeing the daily-use dividers built.** The utility rows are not built as an `NSStackView` (unlike `navStack`) - they're individually positioned `NSButton`s anchored in a bottom-up chain (`settingsButton.bottomAnchor` off `avatar.topAnchor`, each button above it chained the same way) so the per-host block above can grow/shrink freely. A stack-based divider (`navStack`'s arranged-subview approach) doesn't apply here, so each of the 5 separators is a plain sibling `NSBox(.separator)` spliced into that same bottom-up anchor chain (`dividerXY.bottomAnchor` off the button below it, the next button's `bottomAnchor` off the divider's `topAnchor`), sized/centered to match the daily-use dividers' width formula (`Self.width - 28`, centered on `root`). Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) rendering the real `IconRailController` off-screen and dumping every `NSBox(.separator)` subview's frame - confirmed all 5 utility dividers render with non-zero, evenly-spaced frames in both `helm-dark` and `helm-light`, with no change to the daily-use group's or per-host block's own dividers.
- **Per-host pinned rail icons (fixes4 Fix 3).** Below the fixed destinations, `IconRailController.setHosts(_:)` renders one extra icon per saved host (its own icon glyph + accent, tooltip = label; click connects via `onConnectHost` exactly like the Hosts list's Connect). Kept live by `HostStore.observe(_:)` - a list of closures (not a single overwritable `onChange`, since both `HostsController.reload()` and the rail's `setHosts` need to hear every add/rename/delete) - `main.swift` registers the rail's observer alongside the Hosts panel's own.
- **Dedicated host pages (cockpit-native-host-pages).** A saved host's rail icon (or the Hosts list's own Connect - both call `AppDelegate.connectToHost`, so there is one connect path for a saved host, not two) opens its **own** page: an independent `ConsoleController` holding only that host's ssh tab(s), never the Firstmate console's Mirror/Shell tabs. `AppShellController.connectHost(_:args:)` builds one lazily per host id via an injected `makeHostConsole` factory (so `AppShellController` stays ignorant of `SSHKeyStore`/`SnippetStore`, matching how it already knows nothing about host persistence), embeds it into the shared body container exactly like a fixed `RailDestination`, and keeps it in a `[UUID: ConsoleController]` dictionary for as long as that host stays saved. The dedup fix for the "second click opens a second tab" bug: `ConsoleController.connectSSHIfNeeded` only calls `openSSH` when `tabs.isEmpty`, so a re-click of an already-connected host's icon just re-shows and re-focuses (`focusCurrentTab()`) the existing page - a deliberate second session still needs the tab chip's own Duplicate (`⌘D`). `ConsoleController(opensFirstmateOnLaunch:)` is `false` for these (the shared Firstmate `console` keeps it `true`, its Shell/Mirror pair unchanged). Deleting a host (`HostStore.observe` diffing ids in `main.swift`) tears its page down via `AppShellController.removeHostConsole` (`ConsoleController.shutdown()` + `removeFromSuperview`/`removeFromParent()`), and `applicationWillTerminate` shuts every host console down too (`AppShellController.shutdownAllHostConsoles()`), not just the shared one. None of this is a `RailDestination` case - `IconRailController.activeHostID`/`AppShellController.connectHost` track "a host page is showing" as a separate piece of state from the fixed-destination enum, mirrored on both sides so rail highlighting and body-view switching agree. Tab-menu actions (⌘T/⌘D/⌘W/zoom/theme, wired in `main.swift` with no explicit `.target`) still resolve correctly per page because each host's `ConsoleController` is a real `addChild` in the responder chain - whichever page's terminal is first responder is what the action reaches, exactly like the Firstmate console already worked.
- **Fleet dashboard (`FleetController.swift` + `FleetData.swift`, cockpit-native-ui-fixes2).** The `.overview` rail destination is a real port of `backend/fleet.py`'s `snapshot()` and `backend/openprs.py`'s `open_prs()` into Swift - greeting header, calm/loud answer banner, stat tiles (working/ready-to-merge/queued/done/projects/watcher), and an "In flight" list of working crew. **No embedded backend**: `FirstmateHome.swift` resolves `$FM_HOME` the same way `backend/config.py` does, then `FleetData.swift` reads `data/projects.md` / `data/backlog.md` / `state/*.meta` directly and shells out to `bin/fm-crew-state.sh` per task (the same authoritative, non-tailed state read the Python backend uses); `OpenPRsSource` walks `$FM_HOME/projects/*` and re-implements `openprs.py`'s remote-parsing + `gh pr list` + Bitbucket-REST logic natively. `FleetController.refresh()` runs this whole pipeline off the main thread and re-renders on completion - there is no live websocket push (unlike the web app), only a refresh on `viewWillAppear` plus a manual refresh button. **`fm/grandline-overview-drop-duplicate-pr-list` removed Overview's own itemized "Ready to merge" list** (one row per PR with its own Review/Merge actions) - it was a captain-flagged triplication of the exact same `OpenPRsSource.fetch()` + `FleetDataSource.mergedPRs` data `.review` (`ReviewController.swift`, below) already presents in full, grouped by forge, plus the stat tile that already summarized it. The "ready to merge" stat tile is the one signal left on Overview (per the captain's own call, Overview stays a quick pulse-check, not a second copy of Review's list) and is now clickable - it calls `onNavigateToReview`, wired by `AppShellController` to `show(.review)`, so a captain can still jump straight from the count to the actionable list. `FleetDataSource.mergePR`/`bin/fm-pr-merge.sh` (the Merge action) is unaffected - `.review` still calls it directly.
- **Hosts / SSH connection manager (Phase 1, `data/cockpit-ssh-manager-research/report.md` A2/A3, C1, D Phase 1).** `HostsController` (Termius-proportioned, 220-260pt, list, per-host SF-Symbol icon + Helm accent, quick-connect, add/edit/delete) is its own rail destination (see above); Add/Edit Host is a dedicated full-page window (`AppDelegate.presentHostEditor`), not a sheet on this narrow panel - `HostsController.onAddOrEdit` forwards the request up through `AppShellController` to the app delegate, which owns `HostEditorController` and the host store. Row zero is always a pinned, non-deletable "Firstmate" entry (anchor icon) whose connect action is `ConsoleController.openFirstmateHost()` - it has no backing `Host` record, so `HostsController`'s private `HostListRow` enum carries a `.pinned` case alongside `.header`/`.host`. A saved `Host` (`Host.swift`) is `Codable` and persisted by `HostStore` to `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/hosts.json` (override `FM_HOSTS_FILE`) - the native app's first on-disk persistence. **Secrets stay off disk:** `Host.password` is excluded from `CodingKeys` (session-only, memory); the only persisted credential is `keyID`, a reference into the Phase 2 key store below - never a path or key material. **Connect** for a saved host reaches its own dedicated page (see "Dedicated host pages" below) whose one ssh tab runs `/usr/bin/ssh` with the host's argv (the C1 drop-in) plus, when `keyID` is set, a `-i <path>` resolved fresh on every start (see below); the tab takes the host label + accent, and the tab chip's own Duplicate (`⌘D`) re-runs the same launch (re-resolving the key) for a deliberate second session. An ad-hoc quick-connect (`HostCatalog.parseQuickConnect`, no saved `Host` behind it) still opens as a plain tab in the shared Firstmate `ConsoleController` via `ConsoleController.openSSH(...)` directly, since there's no host id to pin a dedicated page to. The Hosts menu's Quick Connect routes through `AppShellController.revealHostsQuickConnect()`, which selects the Hosts destination before focusing it. The topbar Search control is unrelated to Hosts now - see "Find" below.
- **SSH keychain (Phase 2, `data/cockpit-ssh-manager-research/report.md` A1, C3, D Phase 2; Keychain model corrected in cockpit-native-fixes3).** `SSHKey.swift` is non-secret metadata only (label, type, derived public key, fingerprint, certificate), persisted by `SSHKeyStore` to `keys.json` (override `FM_KEYS_FILE`) exactly like `HostStore`. Private key bytes and passphrase live **only** in the macOS Keychain (`KeychainKeyStore.swift`: `SecItemAdd`/`SecItemCopyMatching`/`SecItemDelete`), stored with plain `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` (never iCloud-synced) - **not** a `SecAccessControl` ACL. That's a deliberate correction, not the original design: on a CLT-only, ad-hoc-signed build (`TeamIdentifier=not set`), `SecItemAdd` with *any* `kSecAttrAccessControl` fails `errSecMissingEntitlement`, confirmed with a standalone probe outside the app - see `KeychainKeyStore.swift`'s file header for the full writeup, including why a `keychain-access-groups` entitlement isn't a fix either (ad-hoc signing it gets the process killed outright, no provisioning profile). The Touch ID / passcode gate is therefore enforced in-app (`KeychainKeyStore.authenticate(context:)` calling `LAContext.evaluatePolicy` before the read), not by the OS ACL - revisit once the app has a real Team ID (Phase 4 packaging). Generation and PEM/OpenSSH import both shell out to `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen` (`SSHKeyGenerator.swift`) rather than reimplementing the OpenSSH key format or its passphrase KDF - `.ppk` files are detected and rejected with a conversion hint, not parsed. `SSHKeyStore.addNew(_:privateKeyData:passphrase:)` is the one path that writes a brand-new key (Keychain first, then metadata) - both the Keys screen and the host editor's inline "+ New Key…" popup entry (`HostEditorController.presentNewKeySheet`, still a sheet - now on the host editor's own window rather than the sidebar's) go through it, so a new key can be created without leaving the host form. At connect time `SSHKeyMaterializer.materialize` does the one Keychain read (the Touch ID prompt) and writes a private `0600` temp file for `ssh -i`; `ConsoleController.connectSSH`/`cleanupSSHKeyTempFile` own its lifecycle (fresh file per tab start/reconnect, deleted on close/exit/quit). The "SSH Keys" browser is the Hosts destination's **SSH Keys tab** (`HostsController.swift` + `KeyEditorController.swift`; Keys menu, `⌘⇧K`/`⌘⇧N`) - it was a separate 380x520 window until Phase 5 of the full-app UI audit folded it in, and its row Delete action (`SSHKeyStore.delete(id:)`) removes both the metadata and the Keychain items - see `KeychainKeyStore.delete(id:)`. A `swift build`/`swift run` binary has no stable code-signing identity, so Keychain access can prompt again (or rarely fail to re-read) across rebuilds; `native/build_native_app.sh` now codesigns the assembled `.app` with a fixed local identity to keep that identity stable across rebuilds (see the `build_native_app.sh` bullet below and its "Local signing setup" section in `native/README.md`) - not a full Phase 4 Developer ID/notarization setup, just a per-machine local dev cert.
- **Power features (Phase 3, `data/cockpit-ssh-manager-research/report.md` B1/B2/B4/B5, D Phase 3).** All additive `ssh` argv or extra `Host` fields, never new transport code. Jump hosts: `Host.jumpVia` (a saved host's label or a raw `user@bastion`) resolved and chained by `HostCatalog.proxyJumpChain` into `-J a,b,c`. Agent forwarding: `Host.agentForward` -> `-A` (the system agent's `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` is already inherited via `childEnvironmentDict`). Known-hosts prompts: no code needed - `connectSSH` never overrides `StrictHostKeyChecking` and nothing filters the ssh tab's PTY output, so they render like any other terminal output; verified by inspection, not a UI. Groups/tags: `Host.group`/`Host.tags` (present since Phase 1) now drive sidebar section headers and a tag-chip filter row in `HostsController`. Port forwarding: `Host.portForwards: [PortForwardRule]`, edited via `PortForwardingController` as a sheet on top of `HostEditorController` (a window since the nav-redesign task, not a sheet itself - see above), turned into `-L`/`-R`/`-D` flags. Snippets: `Snippet`/`SnippetStore` (mirrors `HostStore`), managed from the Hosts destination's **Snippets tab** (`HostsController.swift`; Snippets menu, `⌘⌥N`/`⌘⌥P`) - its own 360x480 window until Phase 5 of the full-app UI audit folded it in; Run sends text+Enter to the active tab via `TerminalView.send(txt:)`; a host's `startupSnippetID` fires the same way ~1.5s after ssh starts (best-effort, no "shell ready" signal exists to hook). Session logging was also part of this phase and has since been **removed outright** - see the next bullet.
- **`fm/grandline-remove-session-logging` deleted the session-logging ("record") feature outright** - the captain's call; it is gone, not hidden or defaulted off, so nothing below should be re-added without a fresh decision. What went: the Console toolbar's `record.circle` button and its `⌘⇧L` / Tab-menu "Toggle Session Logging" item, `ConsoleController`'s `toggleLoggingForActiveTab`/`startLogging`/`updateLogButton` and the `defaultLoggingEnabled` auto-start, `CockpitTerminalView`'s `startLogging(to:)`/`stopLogging()`/`isLogging` **and its whole `dataReceived(slice:)` override** (session logging was that override's only reason to exist - anything wanting a host-bytes choke point again has to re-add it, see `LocalProcessTerminalView.dataReceived` in the vendored SwiftTerm), `AppSettings.sessionLoggingDefault` plus its `fm.sessionLoggingDefault` `UserDefaults` key and the `FM_LOG_SESSIONS_DEFAULT` env var, Settings > Terminal's "Log sessions by default" row, and `BackupSettings.sessionLoggingDefault`. Two things worth keeping:
  - **Dropping an optional field from the `.glbackup` bundle needs no `formatVersion` bump, in the same way adding one already didn't** - `JSONDecoder` ignores a key the struct no longer declares, so a bundle a captain exported from an earlier build still imports with its now-unknown `sessionLoggingDefault` simply discarded. That direction is now pinned by a permanent `BackupSelfTest` case (`FM_RUN_BACKUP_TESTS=1`) that injects the removed key into a real encoded bundle's JSON and asserts the decode succeeds *and* that the injected bytes genuinely carried the key, so it cannot pass vacuously.
  - **The Console toolbar has no leftover hole**, measured rather than eyeballed: the record button was the *last* item in `HelmPageToolbar`'s trailing group, so the remaining controls just shift right and the group still ends flush at the same trailing inset (real off-screen renders across all 12 themes, plus a before/after geometry sweep - trailing-most control at maxX 1390.5 both before and after, every inter-button gap 4.0pt). Two probe readings that look like defects and are **not**: one gap measures 3.5pt rather than 4.0 (the labelled "Compose" button resolves to a non-integral 90.5pt width - sub-pixel rounding, identical on the pre-change build), and `SRELeadStatusPill` is 24pt tall next to the 28pt icon squares (by design, also identical pre-change). Settings' Terminal card genuinely collapsed: the gap under its last row went 104pt -> 22pt, i.e. the card no longer reserves the removed row's height.
- Every tab is a `CockpitTerminalView` (the P1 paste-hardening `LocalProcessTerminalView` subclass - do not touch its paste override; it is what makes screenshot-paste-into-Claude work). Terminals fork their child **in-process** via `startProcess`, so there is no websocket byte-pump and no per-keystroke round trip.
- **Scrolling:** SwiftTerm 1.15's `scrollWheel` already scrolls normal-screen buffers smoothly and content-wise (accumulates precise trackpad deltas -> whole lines 1:1, no page-jumps; `scrollSensitivity` default 1.0), so shell tabs feel like WezTerm with no extra code. Each terminal gets a **10,000-line scrollback** via `term.terminal?.changeScrollback(...)` (SwiftTerm default is 500). The Mirror tab is tmux on the alt-screen and pages inherently - do not try to force smooth-scroll there.
- **The Mirror tab now follows firstmate's own resolved runtime backend instead of assuming tmux unconditionally (`fm/cockpit-mirror-herdr-aware`, resolving the standing `cockpit-herdr-scout-decision-herdr-awareness` backlog item).** `FirstmateBackend.swift` is the single resolution point. **`fm/cockpit-mirror-backend-resolve-fix` corrected a live bug in it**: `resolve()` still sources the real `bin/fm-backend.sh` and calls its own `fm_backend_name` for an *explicit* `FM_BACKEND` env / `config/backend` override (never reimplementing that precedence), but no longer falls through to `fm_backend_name`'s ambient-marker (`HERDR_ENV=1`) auto-detection when neither is set - that auto-detection reads the *calling process's own environment*, which structurally never carries herdr's marker for this standalone GUI app (unlike a crewmate spawned from within firstmate's own already-running session), so it always silently landed on the `tmux` default even on a real herdr fleet. Absent an explicit override, `resolve()` now checks for live evidence instead - a real, running herdr session matching `herdrSessionName()` via `herdr session list --json` - and only then returns `.herdr`; see the file's own header comment for the full reasoning and why this must not be "simplified" back to a single `fm_backend_name` shell-out. `ConsoleController.connectMirror` calls `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` on every connect/reconnect and picks a `MirrorSession` case (`TabModel.swift`) accordingly:
  - **tmux** (unchanged): `TmuxMirror.swift` ports `backend/terminal.py`'s grouped-session lifecycle to Swift `Process` - same `new-session -d -s <group> -t <session>` / `select-window` / `window-size latest` / `status off` / teardown, same `cockpit_<sess>_<pid>_<hex>` naming. A crash (not a clean quit) can leave a stale `cockpit_*` group, same tradeoff as the Python version. **Known follow-up (still open, tmux-only):** `setUp` only calls `select-window` when the target names one explicitly (`firstmate:<N>`); with just a session name it inherits whichever window was active in the real session at that instant. Pin `firstmate:<N>` until there's a real fix - see the doc comment on `TmuxMirror.setUp`.
  - **herdr (`HerdrMirror.swift`) is a real `herdr session attach <session>` client, not a read-only mirror - and its tab is named "Herdr", not "Mirror" (`fm/cockpit-mirror-herdr-real-attach`, superseding the original `cockpit-mirror-herdr-aware` polling approximation described below in earlier history).** The original v1 shipped a `bash -c` loop polling `herdr pane read --format ansi` on a ~1.2s interval specifically because of an unresolved question: does a second concurrent `herdr session attach` disturb an already-attached client, the way a *non*-grouped tmux attach would? `fm/cockpit-mirror-herdr-real-attach` answered this live before changing anything - not by assumption - **and confirmed it's safe**: verified against a throwaway, disposable herdr session (`fm_test_attach_verify`, created and destroyed solely for this test, never the machine's real, live-in-use `default` session) that two independent `herdr session attach`/`herdr --session` clients coexist against one session with no disconnects, errors, or visible disruption to either - `herdr pane list` reported one shared pane serving both the whole time, the same shape as tmux's grouped-session model. With that confirmed, the fix is deliberately NOT a bespoke mirroring/rendering mechanism: `HerdrMirror.attachExecutable`/`attachArgs` are just `herdr session attach <session>` run through the exact same `LocalProcessTerminalView.startProcess` PTY path every other tab already uses (`ConsoleController.connectMirror` barely changed), so herdr's own client renders its own real UI (sidebar, tab bar, full interactivity) with zero custom rendering code here. The old pane-discovery heuristic (`firstMatePane`, `<session>#<pane-id>` pin syntax) is gone with the polling code that needed it - a real attach shows the WHOLE session, not one pane's text, so there's nothing to pick. `ConsoleController.connectMirror`'s herdr branch launches this tab with `currentDirectory: FirstmateHome.root.path` (not `shellCwd()`, which is the *shell* tab's own captain-configurable preference) - a deliberate, explicit call for this tab specifically. One incidental finding worth knowing before testing anything herdr-attach-related again: herdr refuses to launch when it sees its own `HERDR_ENV=1` marker in the environment ("nested herdr is disabled by default") - irrelevant for two independent GUI-launched clients, but `childEnvironmentDict()` (`TerminalEnvironment.swift`) now strips `HERDR_ENV`/`HERDR_SOCKET_PATH`/`HERDR_PANE_ID`/`HERDR_TAB_ID`/`HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID` from every terminal child's environment regardless, mirroring why `TMUX` is already stripped there.
  - `mirrorTarget()` (`TerminalEnvironment.swift`) still checks `FM_MIRROR_TARGET`, then Settings > General's "Mirror target" (`AppSettings.mirrorTarget` - field name unchanged, it's shared plumbing behind both backends' tab, not a UI label), before falling back to a backend-appropriate default: `firstmate` for tmux (unchanged), or `FirstmateBackend.herdrSessionName()` for herdr (a plain session name only now - no pane-pin syntax, see above). As of `fm/grandline-mirror-resolve-race-fix` (below), `mirrorTarget()` is a thin wrapper over `FirstmateBackend.resolveMirrorTarget().target` - do not pair a call to it with a separate `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` call for the backend kind.
  - Settings' session picker (`TmuxMirror.listSessions()`, see below) stays tmux-only - it was not extended to herdr, since the brief scoped that specifically to target resolution, not this tab's rendering.
  - `TabLaunch.defaultName` for `.mirror` reads the `kind` frozen into that launch spec (see below) rather than calling `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` fresh - "Mirror" for tmux, "Herdr" for herdr.
- **`fm/grandline-mirror-resolve-race-fix` closed a real, captain-reported boot-race bug in the resolution above**: `openFirstmateHost()` used to build the Mirror tab's launch spec from `mirrorTarget()` (one `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` call, at tab-creation time, deciding the *target* string) while `connectMirror` separately called `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` again (a second, independent call, at actual-connect time, deciding tmux vs. herdr). Right after a machine restart, herdr's own background server can flip from down to up in the gap between those two calls - reproduced live (`MirrorResolveRaceSelfTest.swift`, `FM_RUN_MIRROR_RESOLVE_RACE_TESTS=1`) by forcing exactly that down-then-up flip via a fake `herdr` executable shadowed onto `PATH` between two real `FirstmateBackend.resolve()` calls: the first call saw no live evidence and fell back to the tmux-era literal target `"firstmate"`, the second saw herdr now live and picked the herdr path, and running `HerdrMirror.setUp` with that stale target reproduced the captain's exact error, `[herdr] Cannot mirror 'firstmate': no running herdr session named 'firstmate'`, even with a genuinely live herdr session (named `default`). Fixed with `FirstmateBackend.resolveMirrorTarget() -> (kind:, target:)`, which makes exactly one live-evidence check and returns both pieces together - every caller that needs both (`TabLaunch.defaultName`, `ConsoleController.openFirstmateHost`/`connectMirror`) gets them from this one call, never from a `resolve()`/target-derivation pair. `TabLaunch.mirror` now carries `kind` alongside `target` (`case mirror(kind: FirstmateBackendKind, target: String)`), frozen at tab-creation time; `connectMirror` no longer calls `resolve()` itself, and `reconnectActive`/auto-reconnect (`processTerminated` → `startTab`) both replay the same frozen pair rather than re-resolving, so a reconnect can't introduce a fresh disagreement either. `MirrorResolveRaceSelfTest.swift`'s self-test also confirms no regression against an explicit `FM_BACKEND=tmux` override and against this machine's own real, live herdr session (read-only `session list --json` only - never started/stopped/restarted, per this project's herdr-lifecycle safety rule).
- **Thirteen confirmed AppKit gotchas worth knowing before touching tab chips, a scroll view, the host editor form, a dense row layout, or any full-size destination/window root.** (10) A horizontal `NSStackView` left at its default `.gravityAreas` distribution (never set explicitly) does not honor arranged-subviews' hugging/compression-resistance priorities to absorb slack width at all - those priorities only matter under `.fill`/`.fillProportionally`/`.fillEqually`. Under `.gravityAreas`, all views added via the plain `views:` initializer or `addArrangedSubview` land in the *center* gravity area and get laid out at their natural size with no defined "who grows" rule, so leftover width is resolved by Auto Layout's own tie-breaking - which can drift between runs/rows depending on transient sibling content (a spinner swapped for a button, a longer status string) even with no code change. This is what caused the Updates page's per-row disclosure chevron (`UpdatesController.buildRow`'s `topRow`) to sit flush against the trailing edge for some rows and stop short with a stray gap for others, inconsistently, after expanding a row's log. There's a second, compounding trap: even under `.fill`, an `NSStackView` container's *own* horizontal hugging priority (not its children's) defaults lower than any priority you set on its arranged-subview children - so a `trailingStack` of `.required`-hugging buttons/pills can still itself get chosen to absorb the row's slack instead of the intended flexible text label, unless you also set `.required` hugging/compression-resistance on the container view itself. Fix needs both: set `topRow.distribution = .fill` AND give every non-flexible arranged subview (including any nested `NSStackView` container, not just its children) `.required` hugging/compression resistance, leaving only the one view meant to flex (the title/detail text stack) at `.defaultLow`. Confirmed live via a temporary `FM_DEBUG_CHEVRON`-style env-gated probe (`performClick(nil)` on the disclosure button + `ThemeManager.shared.setTheme` cycling, dumping `NSView.frame` for the chevron/trailing-stack/text-stack after each) - reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below. Same root cause hit a third time (cockpit-bootstrap-row-width-parity): `BootstrapController.buildStepRow`'s `row` (a horizontal `[leftColumn, bodyStack]` stack wrapping each stepper step's content box) also left `.gravityAreas` distribution unset, so `bodyStack` - and therefore the `stepContentBox` nested inside it - stayed shrunk to its content's natural width even though the step's own outer card correctly filled the page (the card's `background` *was* already width-tied to the page via an external `widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: stack.widthAnchor)`, so the empty gap looked like a "card too narrow" bug but was actually this same nested-row distribution gap one level in). Confirmed live via an `FM_DEBUG_BOOTSTRAP_WIDTH`-style probe dumping `stepContentBox` frame widths before/after the fix (169-467pt narrow -> 1044pt full-width, tracking window resize correctly afterward) - fixed by adding `row.distribution = .fill` plus `.required` hugging/compression resistance on `leftColumn` and `.defaultLow` on `bodyStack`, the same shape as the Updates-page fix above. (1) `TabChipView.beginRename()` (fixes4) used to call `window.makeFirstResponder(label)` *and then* `label.selectText(nil)` - `selectText(nil)` alone already makes an editable field first responder and starts editing, so the redundant prior `makeFirstResponder` call makes AppKit think a session is already active and needs ending before `selectText` starts its own. That fires a spurious `controlTextDidEndEditing` with the *pre-edit* text right there, permanently flipping the "is renaming" flag false before the user types a single character - the rename UI looks like it's working, but the real commit on Return later hits a now-stale guard and is silently dropped. Root-caused via `Thread.callStackSymbols` inside the delegate callback, not by reading the code. Never call both; `selectText(nil)` is sufficient on its own. (2) `NSGridView` column widths: setting an explicit pixel width on one column (e.g. the field column) while leaving the other unconstrained does NOT make the constrained column absorb extra space on window resize - the *unconstrained* column absorbs 100% of any extra width instead, since nothing else defines where it should stop. That's what caused the "Add Host" form's labels to drift into a growing empty gap as the window widened. Fix: give the column that should *stay* narrow (labels) the explicit width, leave the column that should *fill* (fields) unconstrained, and pin the `NSGridView`'s own width to its container (`widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: stack.widthAnchor)`) so the fill column has a definite total to fill. (3) A standalone `NSWindow` with `contentViewController` assigned (as opposed to embedded in the shared app-shell body) keeps re-deriving its own frame from its content's Auto Layout fitting size for as long as that content contains a **required equality** chain with no independent lower bound (cockpit-native-host-pages) - not just once at open. Confirmed live (`FM_DEBUG_HOSTEDITOR`-style probe: `setFrame`/`display:true` right after, then read `win.frame` again after a runloop tick): capping-and-centering the host editor's form column with a required `stack.widthAnchor == content.widthAnchor - 48` (paired with a `<=520` cap) made AppKit snap the *whole window* back to 568pt (the one width where that equality has zero slack) within one layout pass, even right after an explicit user resize to 1000pt - the window was effectively stuck. Swapping that one `==` for `>=`/`<=` inequalities (keep the `<=520` cap, position with `leadingAnchor >=`/`trailingAnchor <=`/`centerXAnchor ==` instead of a width tie) removed the trap entirely - verified holding any width the user drags to. This is *why* `HostEditorController`'s max-width-centered column uses inequalities, and it generalizes: any future standalone window with Auto-Layout-capped content needs the same inequality-not-equality shape, or it will silently refuse to stay resized. (4) An `NSScrollView`'s document view belongs width-pinned to `scroll.contentView.widthAnchor` (the clip view), never `scroll.widthAnchor` (the outer view) - caught in code review, not by inspection. With "Show scroll bars: Always" (System Settings, the default with a mouse attached), a non-overlay vertical scroller reserves a real ~15pt track that narrows the clip view without narrowing `scroll`'s own frame; pinning to `scroll.widthAnchor` lets the document view's trailing edge render underneath that track. `HostEditorController`, `FleetController`, and `ReviewController` (the last two fixed in cockpit-native-fixes5) all use `scroll.contentView.widthAnchor`; `SettingsController`, `AutomationController` and `BootstrapController` all had the wrong version until `fm/grandline-design-audit-phase0` fixed all three (audit §5.6) - every scroll-backed page in this app now pins to the clip view, so a new one is the only way this can come back. (5) In a horizontal `NSStackView` row (icon + title/subtitle text + a trailing status pill and/or buttons), leaving every subview's compression resistance at its AppKit default (750, all equal) means a long title squeezes *every* subview roughly proportionally under narrow width, not just the title - and since the trailing pill/buttons are themselves small containers with edge-pinned labels, squeezing them below their fitting width can visually read as the badge "wrapping" even though nothing is a wrapping label (cockpit-native-settings-compact, `FleetController`'s PR/task rows). Fix: give the icon and every trailing control `.required` compression resistance (and hugging) so they never shrink, and give the title/subtitle text stack `.defaultLow` compression resistance so it's the one thing that truncates first. Generalizes to any row mixing fixed-size chrome with variable-length text. (6) `NSViewController.dismiss(_:)` is a documented no-op unless the view controller was presented via `presentAsSheet`/`presentAsModalWindow`/`presentAsPopover` (or has a `presentingViewController`) - for a plain top-level window whose `contentViewController` was just assigned directly (`HostEditorController`'s Save/Cancel/Delete, presented by `AppDelegate.presentHostEditor`), `dismiss(self)` silently does nothing. Fixed (cockpit-native-host-form-fixes) by closing the window directly (`view.window?.close()`) instead; verified live via `NSButton.performClick(nil)` on the located button plus a `win.isVisible` before/after check (no Accessibility permission needed - `performClick` runs the exact target/action path a real click does). Any future standalone-window view controller needs the same direct-close pattern, not `dismiss(self)`. (7) A second regular `NSWindow` opened while another app window is full screen docks into that same full-screen Space as a full-width tile by default (macOS's standard behavior for a second standard window) - this is what turned `HostEditorController`'s centered form back into a full-width layout, but only in full-screen mode. Fixed by setting `win.collectionBehavior = [.fullScreenAuxiliary, .moveToActiveSpace]` and `win.level = .floating` on the host editor's cached window in `presentHostEditor`, so it floats over the full-screen Space instead of tiling into it. Verified live via a temporary env-var-gated probe (`window.toggleFullScreen(nil)` then dump `win.frame` vs `screen.frame` - width stayed 640pt against a 1512pt screen, not stretched to match). Any future utility window opened over a possibly-full-screen main window needs the same `collectionBehavior`/`level` pair. (8) `NSVisualEffectView` with `.sidebar` material and `.behindWindow` blending mode - used for a real split-view sidebar, where it blends against the desktop through the window's own edge - renders an incorrect tint when used as a *full-size* destination or standalone window root, since `.behindWindow` blending composites against whatever is behind the *window* (desktop/other apps), not other content inside the same window (cockpit-native-theme-audit-review; same root cause independently hit and fixed for the Hosts sidebar in PR #18's Fix 6, then for the icon rail and the SSH Keys / Snippets windows in this task - the latter two are now tabs of the Hosts destination, see Phase 5 below). Forcing the view's own `.appearance` does not fix it - only removing the vibrancy material does. Any full-size destination or standalone window's root should be a plain `NSView` with `wantsLayer = true` and a `HelmTheme`-derived `layer.backgroundColor`, exactly like `HostsController`/`FleetController`/`ConsoleController` already do; reserve real `NSVisualEffectView` sidebar material for an actual split-view pane with narrower, non-full-window geometry. (9) A plain `NSView()` used as an `NSScrollView`'s document view is **not flipped** by default, so y=0 is its *bottom*, not its top - while `FleetController`/`ReviewController` pin their `NSStackView` content to the document's *top* anchor (so the header is the visually topmost, highest-y arranged subview), a document shorter than the scroll's viewport (true before their background `gh`/Bitbucket fetch populates the section stacks) rests against the *bottom* of the clip view by default, leaving a blank gap the size of the shortfall sitting above the header - this is the "empty black area above the header for several seconds" bug (cockpit-native-loading-state). Confirmed live with a temporary geometry probe: with a plain `NSView`, the header sat at y=438 in a 668pt-tall viewport with 254pt of content (668-254=414, matching the gap exactly); swapping in `SettingsController`'s existing `FlippedView` (`override var isFlipped: Bool { true }`) pinned the header to y=24 and kept it there from frame 0 through 3s of simulated load. `SettingsController` already used this `FlippedView` + an explicit `scrollToTop()` in `viewWillAppear` (its own earlier Fix 4) - `FleetController`/`ReviewController` were simply built without carrying that pattern forward. Any new `NSScrollView`-backed destination needs the same `FlippedView` document view + `scrollToTop()` pair, or it will silently reproduce this bug the moment its content starts smaller than the viewport (e.g. during an async data load, or a genuinely short list). Follow-up (cockpit-native-fixes5): a captain report of the gap recurring specifically on the *first-ever* Overview visit after a cold launch could not be reproduced via extensive live instrumentation (geometry, `needsLayout`/`needsDisplay` flags, and appearance-transition timing all measured correct at every checked point, both on the first `isHidden`-toggle-triggered `viewWillAppear` and after the async `render()` grows the document height while already visible) - see that task's PR description for the actual probe transcripts. `viewWillAppear` and the end of `render()` in both controllers now force `view.layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()` immediately before `scrollToTop()` regardless, closing the two theoretical gaps the investigation could identify (a first-ever layout pass racing the automatic appearance notification; the scroll position never being re-pinned after the loading-skeleton's short content is replaced by full-height data) even though neither could be proven to be the captain's exact cause. (11) A **second, distinct** flavor of gotcha (3)'s "window stuck at a fixed size" trap - this one doesn't need an explicit absolute cap at all. Any plain `NSView()` added as a subview and given manual constraints must have `translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false` set on it *before* those constraints are activated - if it's left at its default `true`, AppKit *also* synthesizes required constraints pinning the view to whatever frame it happened to have at that moment (for a freshly-`NSView()`-initialized view, `.zero` - i.e. required width == 0 and height == 0), and those silently fight any explicit "fill the parent" constraint the moment the parent tries to grow. Confirmed live (`fm/grandline-window-size-lock-fix`, right after `fm/grandline-docs-no-window-fix` (#156) fixed a real crash-on-launch bug in the same file): `DocsController`'s Runbooks tab's `runbookEditorContainer` (a bare `NSView()`, hidden by default since it's the "edit" state, not the "list" state) never got this line - #156's fix simply exposed it for the first time, since before that fix the app crashed at launch before ever laying out the Docs page at all. The captain's whole app window (not just the Docs page) refused to grow past a small fixed size and snapped back on every resize/maximize attempt, **even while a totally different destination (Console) was showing** - because every `RailDestination` is mounted as a permanent, `isHidden`-toggled child up front (see "Navigation shell" above), and a hidden plain `NSView`'s constraints still fully participate in the window's Auto Layout fitting-size computation, contradicting the intuitive assumption that "hidden" means "excluded from layout" (true for a hidden *arranged subview of an NSStackView*, false for an ordinary hidden `NSView`). Root-caused by bisection (temporarily unmounting one `RailDestination` at a time, then one container/constraint at a time within `DocsController`, down to reproducing the exact lock with nothing but an empty, hidden `NSView()` plus 4 fill constraints and no content at all) rather than by reading the constraint list alone - `NSLayoutConstraint`'s own "Unable to simultaneously satisfy constraints" console warning never fired for this, since AppKit resolves the conflict by silently adjusting the *window's* frame instead of logging a break. Fix: add the missing `translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false` line. Generalizes: any `NSView()`/`NSTextField()`/etc. stored as a `private let` and initialized inline (no `.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false` alongside the property-building code that gives it manual constraints) is a live instance of this trap waiting to happen, whether or not it's ever shown - grep any new full-size destination or hidden-by-default subview for this before assuming its constraints are "just fill, should be safe." (12) **The correction to gotcha (10)'s second trap, measured rather than reasoned (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase3`):** `setContentHuggingPriority`/`setContentCompressionResistancePriority` are **no-ops on an `NSStackView`** - both constrain a view against its *intrinsic content size*, and a stack has none (`NSView.noIntrinsicMetric` on both axes; its size comes from constraints to its arranged subviews). So gotcha (10)'s advice to "also set `.required` hugging on the container view itself" does not actually do anything, and a nested stack stays the parent's preferred stretch target. Measured live inside `ToolRowLayout.build`: with `topRow.distribution = .fill` and `.required` *content* hugging set on `trailingStack`, that trailing stack still absorbed 919pt of a 1056pt row while the `textStack` it was supposed to yield to sat at its natural 69pt - which is the actual mechanism behind audit §5.4's ragged status column. The stack-level APIs are `NSStackView.setHuggingPriority(_:for:)` and `setClippingResistancePriority(_:for:)`; `ToolRowLayout.columnHugging` is the one place this app applies them, and `HelmAccentRow` uses the same pair for its own nested title row (where the wrong pair let a long title push the whole row wider than its card instead of truncating - caught in a real off-screen render, not by reading the code). Rule of thumb: **content**-priority APIs for a leaf view (label, button, image), **stack**-priority APIs for an `NSStackView`, and an explicit width constraint for anything that must be a fixed column. **The no-op is not specific to `NSStackView` - it is true of *any* view with no intrinsic content size, a bare `NSView()` spacer very much included**, and that generalisation cost real time in `fm/grandline-visual-polish-round2`: the first attempt at right-anchoring `ToolRowLayout`'s status column set `.defaultHigh` *content* hugging on the row's spacer to hold it collapsed, and measured the spacer absorbing 1024pt of a 1352pt row anyway while the text column sat at its 200pt floor - the exact pre-fix geometry. A spacer that must stay collapsed needs a real low-priority `width == 0` constraint, not a hugging priority. (13) **A window only holds its own size at `NSLayoutPriorityWindowSizeStayPut` (500), so any content constraint above 500 can resize the whole window** - a third distinct flavour of gotchas (3) and (11), and the one that needs no explicit window-level cap at all. `ToolRowLayout`'s fixed-name-column constraint (§5.4's fix) shipped at `.defaultHigh + 1` (751) paired with a required `textStack.width <= nameColumnMaxWidth`; between them they capped the **entire app window** at `520 / 0.42` plus the row/card/page insets = 1410pt wide, on every page carrying those rows. Measured live on a 1512x982 screen (`fm/grandline-design-fidelity-fixes`): the window refused to grow past 1410 however it was asked, `isZoomed` reported `true` at that size, and genuine macOS full screen rendered 1410x949 **centred with a black bar down each side** - which the captain reported as "the window doesn't cover the laptop screen." None of `maxSize`, `contentMaxSize`, `resizeIncrements`, `aspectRatio` or a window delegate was involved, and `NSLayoutConstraint`'s own "unable to simultaneously satisfy" warning never fired - AppKit just quietly resized the window instead. Root-caused by swapping the content view for a plain `NSView` (cap vanished), then bisecting destinations, then the constraint itself. Dropping the priority to 499 fixed it with no layout change at all. Generalises: **a required `<=` plus a >500 proportional/equality constraint on the same view is a window-size cap**, and the intended-per-row priority band for anything that must beat the stack defaults but never touch the window is 251-499. `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS`'s `checkRowDoesNotResizeWindow` guards it. (14) **A correctly-declared required `==` width tie can still leave a view stuck at a stale, wider frame after the window shrinks - a real, live-captured bug (`fm/grandline-live-gap-rootcause-scout`), not a theoretical one.** A scout task attached read-only (`lldb -p <pid>`) to the captain's own real, running instance and captured `AppShellController.bodyContainer` (the view immediately right of the icon rail, holding the top bar + every destination) frozen at `{84, 0}, {1428, 949}` while the window's real, current frame was only `{1033, 949}` - `1428` being `1512 - 84`, i.e. the *screen's* width minus the rail, not the window's. `bodyContainer.trailingAnchor == root.trailingAnchor` (`root` being this window's own `contentView`, which the OS keeps in sync with the window's content rect unconditionally - confirmed live, `contentView.frame` matched the real window exactly) was already declared correctly, at the default required priority - the declaration alone was not the gap. `AppShellBodyWidthSelfTest.swift`'s own regression run (a real `AppShellController` mounted in a real `NSWindow`, resized through `setFrame(_:display:true)`) proved something subtler and more general: **a required equality constraint does not self-verify on every resize** - after a sequence of resizes (in particular a *shrink*), the constrained view's `.frame` can simply not be re-derived from the live constraint graph unless something forces a fresh `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()` pass following that specific resize; removing the fix reproduced the exact stale-width failure in that same test, on a completely fresh view hierarchy with no other page ever visited, no theoretical required-vs-required conflict needed. `main.swift`'s own launch sequence is a second, compounding reason this specific view was vulnerable: `window.setFrame(Self.defaultWindowFrame(), display: false)` (screen-sized, matching the `1512` this bug's own numbers echo) followed by `setFrameAutosaveName(...)` silently restoring the captain's own smaller saved frame on top of it - both with `display: false`, deferring the very layout flush that would otherwise catch this. **Fix**: `AppShellController` now names `bodyContainer`'s leading/trailing constraints as stored properties and calls a `reassertBodyContainerWidthTie()` method - once right after activating them (closing the pre-visible launch-time race above) and again on every `NSWindow.didResizeNotification` (registered globally, `object: nil`, matching `ToolsController.containerWidthMayHaveChanged`'s own convention two paragraphs below) - which reactivates either constraint if AppKit ever left it `isActive == false` and forces `view.layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()` regardless. `AppShellController` was, before this fix, the one major structural container in this app with *no* resize-driven defensive re-derivation at all, unlike `ToolsController`'s grid or `SettingsController`'s theme grid (both of which learned this exact lesson - "don't fully trust Auto Layout's continuous updates to never get stuck" - independently, per their own bullets). `AppShellBodyWidthSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_APP_SHELL_BODY_WIDTH_TESTS=1`) is the permanent regression coverage, via `AppShellController.bodyContainerFrameForTests`/`.debugBreakBodyWidthTieForTests()` test-only hooks - **confirmed live to actually catch the regression, not just to pass**: temporarily removing the fix's initial call and its resize observer reproduced a stale, too-wide `bodyContainer` after a real resize sequence in 2 of the file's 3 cases, restoring the fix made all 3 pass again. `data/grandline-live-gap-rootcause-scout/report.md` has the scout task's full live-lldb evidence and reasoning; this fix could not itself force-reproduce the captain's exact live conflict (no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below, plus this bug's own root cause turning out to be resize-sequence-dependent rather than a single deterministic trigger) - the self-test instead proves the *mechanism* (a required tie needs a live re-assert, not just a one-time declaration) rather than the exact captain-witnessed sequence of events.
- **`HelmDesignSystem.swift` is the app's design system: `HelmMetrics` / `HelmType` tokens plus `HelmCard`, the one card container (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase1`, Phase 1 of the full-app UI audit - read §3.2, §6.2 and §6.3 component 1 of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching any of it).** Before this there were **five** card recipes visible side by side (radius 10 / 12 / 13 / 14, two fill opacities), `card(icon:title:content:)` copied byte-for-byte into four controllers, its theming loop copied verbatim into six, three page gutters (18 / 20 / 28pt), and 18 distinct `systemFont` sizes with three of them all doing "card title". None of that encoded a product decision. What to know now:
  - **`HelmCard` is `ShiftPanelView` renamed and widened - that class no longer exists.** Its chrome is recipe C's (opaque `chromeBackgroundHex`, `chromeLineHex @ 0.6` border, radius `HelmMetrics.rCard`, a real 1pt header divider at `@ 0.5`), the model the audit chose, plus `SettingsController.card`'s icon-tile + title + subtitle header. **A `HelmCard` themes itself** - its own icon tile, title and subtitle included - so a page's `ThemeManager.shared.observe` closure calls `card.applyTheme(theme)` and nothing else. Do not add another `cardBackgrounds`-style registry, and do not hand-roll a rounded background view for a page section.
  - **Two ways to give it a header.** `setHeader(symbol:tint:title:subtitle:actions:)` is the structured one (`actions` land at the card's trailing edge - that is where Bootstrap's "Run full setup" progress track and button live); `setHeader(symbol:tint:titleLabel:subtitleLabel:actions:)` is the same thing reusing **caller-owned** labels, for text the page rewrites live ("GitHub (3)", "In flight (2)"); `setHeader(_ view:insets:)` is the arbitrary-view escape hatch for a header carrying its own count badge / filter row / buttons (Shift, Vault, Dictation use it). `setBody(_:insets:)` defaults to **flush**, which is what a scroll view or a list stack whose rows carry their own inset wants; real content passes `HelmCard.contentInsets`.
  - **`HelmCard.applyCardSurface(to:theme:cornerRadius:)` is the single definition of the card fill/border.** A surface that genuinely shares that chrome without being a card yet - `HelmStatTile` (Phase 4), which is where Updates' own stat tiles ended up - calls it rather than re-deriving the colours, so a page still has exactly one fill opacity. **The border carries real load, it is not decoration:** `chromeBackgroundHex == backgroundHex` in `gruvbox-light` / `tokyo-night-dark` / `tokyo-night-light`, so in those three the border is the only thing separating a card from the page (unchanged from before - the old `surface @ 0.6` fill composited to the same colour in exactly those themes).
  - **`HelmMetrics`** holds the spacing scale (`s1`-`s6`), radii by role (`rChip` / `rControl` / `rCard` / `rPanel`), the one `pageGutter` (24pt - every card-bearing destination now measures exactly this), and the three icon-tile sizes. **`HelmType`** holds the type scale by role - `pageTitle(_:)` / `sectionTitle()` / `rowTitle()` / `body()` / `caption()` / `kicker()` / `metric(_:)`. Reach for a token, not a literal. `HelmType.Voice` exists so the registered captain decision `grandline-full-ui-audit-decision-page-title-voice` (promote `ShiftFont.serif` app-wide, or retire it) has somewhere to land - it is **not** settled, and Shift's own serif headers were deliberately left alone.
  - **Out of scope when Phase 1 shipped, each its own later phase:** `HelmAccentRow` (component 2, shipped in Phase 3), `HelmStatTile` / `HelmEmptyState` / `HelmSegmentedTabs` (components 4-6, shipped in Phase 4), and recipe E - Bootstrap/Automation's `line @ 0.08` step-content boxes, which belong with the stepper component. Verified live for all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes` with a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) that walked each destination's real view tree after a real layout pass: 39 cards across 11 destinations, one radius / one fill / one border / one 1pt border width / one 24pt gutter per theme, plus real off-screen PNG renders of all 14 destinations confirming every page's content still renders inside its new card.
- **`HelmButton` is the app's one button, and the stock `NSButton` bezel is now a self-test failure (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase2`, Phase 2 of the full-app UI audit - read §3.2's "Buttons" section and §6.3 component 3 of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching it).** Before this the app had **124 stock `bezelStyle` buttons across 29 files** and **18 `keyEquivalent = "\r"` default buttons across 15** (both counts re-verified against `main`, not taken from the report), plus 13 `NSPopUpButton`s - all of which paint macOS's own grey bezel and system blue (`#0a84ff`) regardless of which of the 12 Helm accents is active. That is what made a fully-themed app still read as generic macOS. Now: **1** `bezelStyle` site remains and it is not a button (`TabChipView`'s inline-rename `NSTextField`), 128 call sites are `HelmButton`, and 13 are `HelmPopUpButton`. What to know:
  - **Four variants, picked per site from what the button already did**, not assigned mechanically: `.primary` (opaque `accentHex` fill + `selectionTextHex` label - at most one per sheet footer / card header), `.secondary` (bordered, theme-derived - the default that replaced the stock bezel), `.quiet` (borderless, muted, hover-only background - toolbar and link weight), `.destructive` (a contrast-corrected wash of the theme's own red). The recipe is `UpdatesController`'s Refresh pill - the one correctly-themed action that already existed - promoted into a real control.
  - **How it stops being a bezel:** `isBordered = false` means AppKit draws no bezel *at all* (verified by rendering a real default button both ways - the stock one composites its bezel material, the unbordered one captures fully transparent), so `keyEquivalent = "\r"` keeps the Return shortcut while the blue look has nothing left to paint. Confirmed against the strongest form of the old behaviour: with `window.defaultButtonCell` explicitly set, the button still pixel-samples as the theme accent. Chrome comes from the view's own layer; the label comes from `attributedTitle`, because **`contentTintColor` does not colour a string title, only an image** - three pages had each hand-rolled that workaround before it was shared (`UpdatesController`'s "Install in Bootstrap", `CommandLibraryViews`' Copy and Favorite; all three are now just a variant plus an optional `tint`).
  - **It themes itself** (like `HelmCard`): its own `ThemeManager.observe`, unregistered in `deinit`. Deliberately not a per-page button registry - that is exactly the shape of `ThemeManager.swift`'s checklist item 4, this codebase's most-repeated bug class. **Consequence: a page must not set `font` / `attributedTitle` / `contentTintColor` / `isBordered` on a `HelmButton`** - `restyle()` owns all four and will overwrite them on the next theme change.
  - **Migrating a site is a type change only.** `title`, `target`, `action`, `keyEquivalent`, `isEnabled`, `isHidden`, `identifier`, `tag` and `setButtonType(.pushOnPushOff)`'s `state` all keep working, and `controlSize` is overridden to map onto `Size` so an existing `controlSize = .small` line needs no edit. One real gotcha it exposes: **a control that signalled selection through `NSButton.state` was relying on the stock bezel's on-state, which no longer exists** - Settings' font-size presets and Hosts' tag chips now carry that state as `variant = selected ? .primary : .secondary` instead.
  - **`NSPopUpButton` needed no reimplementation** (§6.5 put that out of scope): it *is* an `NSButton` subclass, so `HelmPopUpButton` de-bezels it the same way and keeps the menu, `selectItem…`, `titleOfSelectedItem` and `indexOfSelectedItem` intact - verified live before it was written. Its label and chevron do follow `contentTintColor` (unlike `HelmButton`'s string title), since a popup draws its title from the selected menu item.
  - **`NSSwitch` is the one control that genuinely cannot be themed** - measured, not assumed: its header declares only `state`, and it answers `false` to `setTintColor:`/`setContentTintColor:`/`setAccentColor:`/`setFillColor:`/`setBezelColor:`. Its runtime property list does expose a `trackColor`, but that is private API and deliberately unused here. So the 6 switches still render in system chrome; whether to build a bespoke toggle is registered as a captain decision (`grandline-full-ui-audit-decision-nsswitch-theming`), not silently built.
  - **Deliberately untouched, with reasons:** Console's and Tools' tab-bar toolbar glyphs (already borderless, never part of the bezel count, and their per-state tinting - Block View's accent while showing - would need per-site rework; the report files that restyle under §6.4's "notable rework", and `HelmButton`'s `tint` is the seam it will use), and `LockScreenController`'s unlock pill (already a hand-rolled bespoke filled button, and §6.4 assigns the lock screen only `HelmType`/`HelmField`).
  - **Later phases:** `HelmStatTile` / `HelmEmptyState` / `HelmSegmentedTabs` (components 4-6, shipped in Phase 4) and `HelmField` (Phase 6, still open) - which is why a migrated page's `NSTextField`s, `NSSearchField`s and `NSProgressIndicator`s still read as system chrome next to its now-themed buttons.
- **`HelmAccentRow` is the app's one accent-carrying row, and the checklist row's status column is now a real column (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase3`, Phase 3 of the full-app UI audit - read §3.2's "Accent-bar card with an uppercase kicker" table, §4.14, §5.4 and §6.3 component 2 of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching it).** Before this, one idea had **four independent implementations** plus a fifth surface that had half of it: all agreed on a 3pt bar at radius 1.5 and a radius-10 card, and disagreed on everything else - three separate kicker constructions at three kern values (0.9 / 0.7 / 0.6), two badge treatments, and one outlier (`SRELeadChatView.accentCard`) with no border at all whose kicker was coloured from the raw tint hue. Row-rendering code across the five surfaces went from 530 lines to 171 (thin data->`Content` adapters), against a 378-line shared component of which 217 are code. What to know:
  - **`HelmAccentRow` is `NotificationRowView` promoted, not a redesign.** That class is gone; the notification panel is now a caller (`NotificationPanelViewController.makeRow`) of the component it donated, which is exactly what the audit asked for (§6.4: "becomes a *caller* of the component it donated"). Its geometry, `HoverHighlightView` card, `tint @ 0.4` border, 26pt circular `IconTileView` badge and `ToolRowLayout.pill` chip are unchanged.
  - **The rule that matters when extending it: the kicker is `HelmTheme.mutedInk`, never the tint hue.** A `HelmTint` is safe as a fill or a bar and is *not* automatically safe as text (`HelmContrast`'s doc comment, audit §5.7). The tint reaches the bar, the badge and the chip and never the kicker. `HelmContrastSelfTest.checkAccentRowRecipe` asserts that plus one bar weight / badge size / radius / kicker font+kern across all 12 themes, and `checkNoHandRolledKickers` fails the build on any `.kern:` outside `HelmType` - **both confirmed to catch a real regression, not just to pass** (colouring the kicker with `tintColor` and planting one hand-rolled `.kern: 0.7` each reproduced the exact failure).
  - **Structure is fixed at `init`, content at `configure`** - so a reused `NSTableView` cell just gets re-pointed. `chipPlacement` is `.trailing` for a wide list row and `.belowBody` for the 340pt notification panel; `leadingControl` swaps the badge for a caller-owned control (Shift's `ShiftTaskCheckBadge`, so click-to-toggle is unchanged); `contentView` swaps the title/meta labels for a caller-owned body (SRE Lead's rendered-markdown stack) while keeping the kicker, bar, badge and card. `hover: false` for a row that is not interactive - a hover highlight on something that does nothing is a lie (SRE Lead's transcript cards and Overview's read-only rows pass it).
  - **§5.4's status-column jitter is fixed in `ToolRowLayout`, which affects all five of its callers** (Updates, Bootstrap's software checklist + drift card, Automation, GitHub Sync, Vault). The row is now a real three-column table: a fixed name column, the status column (`statusViews` - the pill by default, plus the spinner/label that replaces it while a check runs), flexible space, then the actions (`trailingViews`) and the chevron pinned trailing. Measured live: 64pt of pill-x spread across five real rows (50pt across the real page's thirteen) -> **0pt**, with the chevron still pinned. **This bullet used to claim "a fixed name column is the only formulation that works, because measuring back from the trailing edge cannot pin the pill when one row's actions are 'Check' and another's are 'Check' + 'Install in Bootstrap ->'". That is only true of measuring back from the actions themselves, and the fixed leading column caused two live defects of its own** - see `fm/grandline-visual-polish-round2` below, which replaced it with a reserved trailing slot. `build`'s `trailingViews` now means *actions only*; a caller that just wants a pill passes neither argument.
