  - **Why `HelmAccentRow` and `ToolRowLayout` were deliberately *not* merged**, despite both now being "a row with a tint": one is an alert/task card (bar, badge, kicker, body, chip) and the other a dense checklist row (fixed columns, action buttons, a disclosure chevron and an expandable log). They share the primitives that carry the visual language - `ToolRowLayout.pill`, `IconTileView`, the accent-bar idiom, `HelmType` - and nothing else, because their layouts genuinely differ. Merging them would have produced one type with two disjoint halves.
  - **Two real defects the migration surfaced, both fixed here:** `FleetController`'s own `pillLabelView` was a *second* copy of the §5.7 "hue as its own label" bug (Overview's rows now go through the contrast-corrected `ToolRowLayout.pill`), and the two Shift lists' 74pt `rowHeight` clipped the meta line's descenders once the rows moved onto the shared type scale - now 78pt, set from a measured 75pt fitting height plus the row view's 2pt insets rather than a guess.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`, this repo's screenshot substitute) of all five surfaces across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-light`, driving the **real** `NotificationPanelViewController`, the **real** `ShiftController` page (seeded through a real `ShiftStore` against a scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`), the real `SRELeadChatView` with a real Finding + Recommendation exchange, and `FleetController`'s own row builder - plus the geometry probe above against the real `UpdatesController`. A lesson from that pass worth keeping: **a hand-hosted `NSTableView` in a probe sizes its rows to their own content, not the column**, so ragged card widths in a probe render are the probe's artefact - mount the real page before believing a width bug.
- **`HelmStatTile`, `HelmEmptyState` and `HelmSegmentedTabs` are the app's one stat tile, one empty state and one sub-navigation control (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase4`, Phase 4 of the full-app UI audit - read §3.2's "Stat tile", "Empty state" and "Sub-navigation" tables, §5.5, §6.3 components 4/5/6 and §6.4's adoption map of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching any of them).** Three independent components shipped together because each is small; they are separable and were built as three separate concerns. Call-site code across 15 files went from 1219 lines to 830 (**-389**), against 610 lines of shared component of which **363 are code**, plus 172 lines of new self-test. What to know:
  - **`HelmStatTile`** replaced **four** copies, not the three the audit counted: Overview (mono 15 semibold, 10/8 padding, 50pt), Shift (`ShiftFont.mono(19)`, 12/10, 56pt), Updates (mono 19 **bold**, 15/13, `surface @ 0.60`, 67pt), and `ShiftController.reviewStatTile` - a byte-identical duplicate of Shift's own `statTile` whose doc comment said it existed *only* because the two rows shared one theming array, so "whichever rebuilds last wins the next `applyTheme()` pass for both". A tile that themes itself deletes that reason. Shift's proportions won (§6.3's named model); the fill is `HelmCard.applyCardSurface`, which Phase 1 made public for exactly this, so a page can no longer have a tile surface and a card surface that disagree. Click support is Overview's, now a closure rather than a `Selector`.
  - **`HelmEmptyState` supersedes `ShiftEmptyStateView` - that class no longer exists**, the same rename-not-wrap call Phase 1 made for `ShiftPanelView`->`HelmCard`. Its `.compact` size *is* `ShiftEmptyStateView` (22pt glyph, body copy, the `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` fix that stopped "No saved secrets yet…" rendering as "No / sa"); `.standard` is `DocsController`'s Playbook state (40pt glyph, real title, room for an action), which §3.2 called the most complete one. `boxed: true` adds `HelmCard`'s fill/border for a state sitting straight on the page background (Overview, Review). **The action slot is a caller-owned `accessory: NSView?`, deliberately not a `(title, closure)` pair** - the app's one empty state with an action already owns a `HelmButton` *and* a spinner it enables/disables around its own async sync, so a component-owned button would have taken behaviour away rather than shared it. Four bare-label sites (Docs' runbook grid + postmortems, the rail's hosts flyout, Block View's table, the notification panel) gained the full treatment; each judged individually, and none was left a bare label, because every one of them is the whole content area of its container rather than a caption beside something else.
  - **`HelmSegmentedTabs`** is `ShiftController.buildTabRow` promoted. The audit's real finding here was that Shift's capsule and Docs' bare pills were **already byte-identical code** for the pill itself (12pt medium label, radius 7, 10/6 insets, accent wash) and differed only in the wrapper, so one recipe rendered as two different-looking controls a rail click apart; Updates' filter was a third near-copy at radius 6 in a radius-8 container. Docs' pills gained the capsule (§6.4's own recommended direction) and Updates' filter uses the new `.compact` size, for a control that sits in a toolbar beside a search field rather than under a page title. It deals in **caller-owned string ids**, not indices, so each page keeps its own enum (`ShiftTopLevelView`, `DocsTab`, `ToolFilterMode` all gained `String` raw values) and its own switch logic - the component switches nothing itself. `select(_:)` moves the pill without firing `onSelect`, which is what keeps it right when a view is reached from a menu item or the ⌘K palette rather than a click.
  - **`TabChipView` was deliberately left alone.** Console's and Tools' strips are closable, user-created, dynamic chips - "manage a set of tabs", not "switch between a fixed few views". Per the audit's own framing those are different concepts, and folding them together would have produced one type with two disjoint halves (the same reason §6.3 keeps `HelmAccentRow` and `ToolRowLayout` apart).
  - **A real §5.7 defect fixed in all three, and it is the reason `HelmContrast.legibleTintedText` now exists.** Two of the stat-tile copies painted a tinted metric with `HelmTheme.nsColor(tint.hex(in: theme))` directly, and *both* segmented-tab copies painted the active pill's label `= accent` - a hue used as text with no correction. Measured: the tinted metric reached **2.12:1** (gruvbox-dark red) and the active tab label **2.27:1** (solarized-light). `legibleTintedText` (promoted out of `HelmButton`'s own private copy, `HelmUIComponents.swift`) blends the hue toward the theme's own ink by the smallest step that clears 4.5, and has an `overAnyOf:` form for a component that cannot know which fill its label lands on. **The tabs case needed one more thing worth remembering: composite with `HelmContrast.mix`, not `NSColor.blended(withFraction:of:)`.** `blended` converts both operands into a *calibrated* RGB space before mixing, so its result drifts from the straight-sRGB composite alpha blending actually performs and `HelmContrast.ratio` then measures - small but real, and it left the active label at 4.39 in gruvbox-light while the correction believed it had cleared the floor.
  - **`FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS` gained three checks** (one recipe per component per theme, plus both hue-as-text floors and §5.5's collapsed-empty-state shape), and **all three were confirmed to catch a real regression, not just to pass**: reintroducing the raw-hue metric, the raw-accent tab label, the `surface @ 0.6` tile fill and the removed `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` fix each reproduced the exact original failure by theme and by measured ratio.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`) of the **real** mounted `FleetController`, `ReviewController`, `UpdatesController`, `DocsController`, `VaultController`, `ShiftController`, `IconRailController`, `BlockContainerView` and the real `NotificationCenterController`'s real popover, across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-light` - plus real handler drives: the one clickable Overview tile fired `onNavigateToReview` (and a plain tile fired nothing), each page's tabs were switched through their **real `NSClickGestureRecognizer` target/action** rather than by calling `onSelect`, and Docs' "Sync Now" was `performClick`ed and confirmed to disable itself and show its spinner. Overview's, Review's and Vault's empty states only exist when their data is genuinely empty (this machine has real PRs, tasks and Vault secrets), so their own real factories were called directly and rendered - each measuring a real non-zero height and body width, which is §5.5's `bodyH=0` failure checked rather than eyeballed. **Two probe artefacts worth knowing before writing the next one:** a bare `wantsLayer` probe root paints *nothing*, so a standalone component grid renders on white and every muted/translucent colour looks wrong; and a standalone component has no page observer, so it needs the same `applyTheme` call a real page makes from its `observe` closure or it keeps the previous theme's colours through a switch. Both looked exactly like real theming bugs.
- **`HostsController` is the Hosts destination, and it owns SSH Keys and Snippets as segmented tabs - the two floating windows they used to live in no longer exist (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase5`, Phase 5 of the full-app UI audit - read §4.4, §4.5, §5.2, §6.3 components 1/2/3/5/6 and §6.4's Hosts and "SSH Keys / Snippets" rows of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching any of it).** The one phase of the rollout with a real *structural* change, correctly sequenced last because it depends on components 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 all existing; the captain approved the navigation change (registered decision `grandline-full-ui-audit-decision-hosts-keys-snippets-merge`). `HostsSidebarController.swift`, `KeysSidebarController.swift` and `SnippetsController.swift` are **gone**, replaced by `HostsController.swift` + `HostsListSection.swift`. Nothing about connecting changed: the rail entry, the pinned Firstmate row's connect, per-host dedicated pages, quick-connect, the host editor window, and both editor sheets all behave exactly as before. What to know:
  - **What was actually wrong.** Hosts was still laid out as the 220-260pt Termius sidebar it had been before `fm/cockpit-native-ui-fixes2` promoted it to a full-size destination: at 1136pt it rendered Connect / Edit / Delete as three ~370pt `.fillEqually` buttons pinned to the bottom edge, with one 78pt row above ~800pt of dead space. Keys and Snippets were "near-identical twins" (same `footerButton` helper, same table setup, same `selectedX`/`clickedX`/`updateButtons` plumbing) in two floating windows. None of the three row views had an `applyTheme` at all.
  - **The page.** A `HelmSegmentedTabs` row over a **capped, centred content column** - an `NSLayoutGuide` with `leading >=` / `trailing <=` / `centerX ==` / `width <= 940` plus a **`.defaultHigh`** `width == 940`, never a required `==` tie (AGENTS.md's host-editor gotcha (3)). One `HelmCard` per tab, whose header carries the count, the explanatory subtitle (Keys' and Snippets' old page captions) and that tab's **add** action. The in-page "Hosts" title is gone - it duplicated the top bar's, which is what §4.4 flagged.
  - **`HostsListSection` is the one list behind all three tabs**, fed a `[Item]` array: an `NSTableView` (never an `NSStackView` of permanent rows - `DiffResultView.swift`'s header has the measured reason) whose `.record` rows are `HelmAccentRow` cards, with group headers and a `HelmEmptyState` rendered as rows of the same table. The empty row grows to fill whatever the other rows leave, so "nothing here yet" is centred in the card body rather than pinned to its top 180pt.
  - **The footer strip is gone; actions live in the row.** Each row's primary action (Connect / Edit / Run) plus a `⋯` overflow menu sit in `HelmAccentRow`'s new `trailingAccessory` slot, and the same array builds both that menu and the row's right-click `menu`, so the two cannot drift apart. **The pinned Firstmate row keeps an invisible `overflowSpacer` in that stack** rather than hiding the `⋯` button, because an `NSStackView` drops a hidden arranged subview out of layout entirely and the action column would go ragged - measured: 112pt wide starting at one x for every row.
  - **Three additions to `HelmAccentRow`**, each mirroring a slot it already had: `trailingAccessory` (caller-owned trailing controls, like `leadingControl`), `Content.tintHex` (a literal hue for a record carrying a **user-chosen** colour - a saved `Host`'s own `accentHex`; everything else still passes a `HelmTint`), and `isRowSelected` (see the selection bullet above). Plus `Content.metaIsCode` / `HelmType.code()` for a meta line that is literally a command or a fingerprint. Its click recognizer now sets `delaysPrimaryMouseButtonEvents = false`, without which the recognizer swallows the mouse-down and an enclosing `NSTableView` never sees the click that should select the row.
  - **`SSHKeyType.accentHex` became `SSHKeyType.tint`.** Those were four fixed hexes lifted from `HostCatalog.accents`, so they were picked against `helm-dark` and knew nothing about the other eleven palettes; once Phase 5 put that hue on a real accent bar the Ed25519 cyan measurably washed out against Gruvbox Light's cream page, seen in a real render. A `HelmTint` resolves per theme and keeps the per-algorithm differentiation.
  - **Menu items are unchanged in title and shortcut** - "Manage Keys…" (⌘⇧K), "New Key…" (⌘⇧N), "Manage Snippets…" (⌘⌥P), "New Snippet…" (⌘⌥N) - but now target `AppShellController.selectKeys` / `newKeyFromMenu` / `selectSnippets` / `newSnippetFromMenu`, which select the Hosts destination and the right tab instead of opening a window.
  - **Two real defects the migration surfaced, both found in a render rather than by reading the code**, and both the same AGENTS.md gotcha (12): a 90pt "Connect" button rendering ~900pt wide, because `setContentHuggingPriority` is a **no-op on an `NSStackView`** (the stack-level `setHuggingPriority` is the one that bites) *and* because the `HelmButton`s inside it needed `.required` content hugging of their own; and the `alphaValue = 0` first attempt at reserving the overflow slot, which `cacheDisplay` renders **visibly** (this repo's screenshot substitute ignores `alphaValue`), hence the real spacer view.
  - **Line-count delta** (code lines, excluding comments/blanks): the three list surfaces 945 -> 815 (**-130**, and 3 files -> 2), `main.swift` 502 -> 454 (**-48**, the two window methods gone), `HelmUIComponents.swift` 482 -> 462 (**-20**, `HelmTableRowView` deleted), against `HelmDesignSystem.swift` +44, `AppShellController.swift` +21 and `KeyEditorController.swift` +8 - **net -125**.
  - **`KeyEditorController`'s last two stock controls are gone**: both `NSSegmentedControl`s (Generate/Import, Ed25519/RSA) are `HelmSegmentedTabs` now, and its status line's `.systemRed`/`.systemGreen` go through `HelmContrast.legibleTintedText` off the active theme. Its fields, text-view borders and form scaffold are deliberately untouched - that is Phase 6 (`HelmField` + one shared form scaffold), still open, along with Phase 7's polish pass.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`) of the **real** mounted destination at its real 1136pt body geometry - all three tabs, populated and empty, across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-light` - plus 47 live assertions driving the **real** handlers: add/edit/delete end to end for a snippet (real editor sheet, real Save button), a key (real `ssh-keygen` generate, a real Keychain write, then deleted again) and a host (real editor window, real store write, real confirm alert answered while `runModal()` blocked), Copy Public Key onto the real pasteboard, Duplicate, the tag-chip filter, ad-hoc quick connect, the pinned row's connect-only menu, and a real Connect click opening that host's dedicated page. All probe instrumentation was reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention.
- **`HelmForm.swift` is the app's one form language - `HelmField` (the sunken-field recipe plus the controls built on it), `HelmFieldCard` / `HelmToggleRow`, and `HelmFormSheet`, the scaffold all six editor sheets are now built from (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase6`, Phase 6 of the full-app UI audit - read §4.7, §3.2's "Sunken form field" entry, §6.3's `HelmField` supporting extraction and §6.4's "All 6 editor sheets" row of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching any of it).** Before this, six sheets that are all "create or edit one record" - Task, Follow-up, Project, Snippet, Command, Host - measured 180 / 261 / 302 / 357 / 520 / 640 wide, with four separate `NSGridView` label columns, six footer constructions, and Priority rendering as a clickable field card in the task editor and a stock `NSPopUpButton` in the very next sheet (the audit's own illustration of the split). Call-site code across the six went **1614 -> 1038 lines** (-576); everything this phase touched except the shared component and the self-test went **3989 -> 3361** (-628), against 803 code lines of shared component. What to know:
  - **The scaffold is the controller's root view**, not something dropped inside one (`view = form`). That is what lets it own the sheet background, the forced appearance and the single `ThemeManager` observation the whole sheet needs - three things all six editors hand-rolled and three of six got wrong (a plain `NSView` with no explicit fill paints nothing, AGENTS.md gotcha #8). A controller with chrome the scaffold knows nothing about hooks **`onApplyTheme`** rather than registering a second observer.
  - **Every editor ends `loadView` with `form.refreshTheme()`.** `ThemeManager.observe` fires synchronously at registration, which for a scaffold is before the caller has added a single row - so the first firing always finds empty registries. This is the general form of the fix for audit §5.1 (the task editor's kickers rendering in the system `.labelColor`, i.e. *brighter* than the body text they label), and the fourth confirmed instance of that trap here.
  - **`HelmField.fill(_:)` is the one sunken fill, and the three copies are gone** - `ConsoleComposerPopover.fieldFillColor`, `ShiftController.detailFieldFillColor` and `ShiftTaskEditorController.shiftEditorFieldFillColor`, each of which carried a doc comment pointing at the others. Phase 2 had landed the definition as `HelmButton.controlFill`; it lives on `HelmField` now.
  - **A field's text is not the page's ink.** The fill is `chromeBackgroundHex` nudged 8% toward ink, which is measurably closer to the ink than the surface the palette's contrast was pinned against - enough to drop `solarized-dark` from 4.55:1 to **4.20:1**, caught by the new self-test rather than by eye. `HelmField.ink(_:)` / `.mutedInk(_:)` run Phase 0's correction one level in; `HelmContrast.legible` moved off `HelmButton` for this (its third caller).
  - **`NSTextField.backgroundColor` paints over the layer fill.** With `drawsBackground = true` the *cell* paints its own `backgroundColor` - default the system `.textBackgroundColor` - on top of whatever the layer holds, so setting only `layer.backgroundColor` (what all three hand-rolled copies did) leaves a near-black/near-white system box over the theme fill. Found in a real render: the Host editor's fields came out visibly darker than the field card beside them while both layers reported the identical colour. `HelmField` sets both.
  - **Card for a form field, popup for a table cell.** Every form-level enum-like choice across the six sheets is a `HelmFieldCard` (label inside, chevron, pops an `NSMenu`) - `configureChoices(_:selectedIndex:extra:onSelect:)` covers the whole popup boilerplate, and `extra:` is how the Host editor's "+ New Key…" sentinel survives. `HelmPopUpButton` stays for a dense inline choice (the Command editor's per-parameter kind picker), where a 50pt card in a six-control row would be absurd. `HelmField.controlHeight` (32) is chosen so a *labelled* field lands within a point of `HelmFieldCard.height` (50), which is what lets one row mix the two shapes.
  - **`sizeToFitContent()`** is `ShiftTaskEditorController.resizeToFitContent` generalised: `presentAsSheet` reads the root view's frame verbatim, so a hardcoded height leaves slack that `.gravityAreas` injects into an arbitrary, sibling-dependent row. Four of the six shipped a literal height (320 / 400 / 420 / 620) and none matched its content. Non-scrolling sheets only; the two long forms (Command, Host) use `scrolls: true`, which pins the heading and the footer and scrolls the field column between them.
  - **The Host editor is still a window, and its capping is still inequalities.** `HelmFormSheet.cappedColumn` uses `>=` / `<=` / `centerX` plus a `.defaultHigh` preferred width, never a required `==` tie - AGENTS.md's host-editor gotcha (3). `closeEditor()` still closes the window directly (`dismiss(self)` is a documented no-op there). The other five are `presentAsSheet` and still `dismiss(self)`.
  - **Two adjacent `.bezelBorder` scroll views were fixed because the new source guard flags them**, not proactively: `PortForwardingController`'s rules list (a nested sheet on the now-fully-themed Host editor) and `BackupUI`'s import preview. Settings and the Lock screen remain out of scope, as the brief scoped them.
  - **`FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS` gained two checks** - one measured (all six field shapes resolve to one radius / border / fill per theme, the fill is distinguishable from *both* page surfaces, and field ink + muted both clear 4.5:1) and one source guard (no re-derived `fieldFillColor`/`styleSunkenField`/`0.08`-blend copy, no `borderType = .bezelBorder`). **Both were confirmed to catch a real regression, not just to pass**: reverting the field ink to the raw `chromeInkHex` reproduced solarized-dark at 4.20:1, a wrong field-card radius failed in all 12 themes, and a planted private `fieldFillColor` was reported by file:line.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders of all six sheets, populated, across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-dark`, plus 21 live assertions driving the **real** handlers - save writing the right record for each of the six, validation refusing an empty required field, Delete firing, Cancel not saving, Esc/Return key equivalents present, the task editor's ⌘Return firing through a real `NSWindow.performKeyEquivalent`, and its natural-language date detection filling the due controls. Probe instrumentation was reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention. Three real defects the renders caught (the `backgroundColor` overpaint above, a too-tight lead-to-section gap, and a placeholder wrapping into two clipped half-lines in a narrow column) were fixed before shipping.
  - **Phase 7 closed the rollout** (see the next bullet). Settings and the Lock screen adopt `HelmField` in §6.4's map but were deliberately left alone here.
- **`HelmPageToolbar` and `HelmResponsiveGrid` are the app's one page-toolbar strip and one wrapping card grid, the serif is now the app-wide page-title voice, and the rollout is finished (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase7`, Phase 7 - the polish phase - of the full-app UI audit; read §3.2's "Typography - no scale" and "Page toolbars" sections, §4.2, §4.6, §4.8, §4.10, §4.12, §6.2 and §6.4's Console/Review/Settings/Tools rows of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` before touching any of it).** Five independent fixes, no new product behaviour. What to know:
  - **The page-title voice decision is settled, and it is enforced rather than documented.** The registered captain decision `grandline-full-ui-audit-decision-page-title-voice` went the way §6.2 recommended: **promote** `ShiftFont.serif` to the app-wide page-title voice at exactly one size (22), rather than retire it. So `HelmType.pageTitle(.serif)` is now the **only** caller of `ShiftFont.serif` anywhere in the app - `HelmContrastSelfTest.checkPageTitleVoice` greps `Sources/` and fails the build on a direct call, which is what stops a fifth size appearing (the audit measured four: 15 / 17 / 19 / 22 / 23). A hero title is for a page whose title carries **information** - a greeting, or the name of the record being viewed - never for restating the destination's own name, which the top bar already shows. Consequences worth knowing: Shift's section headers and `ShiftConflictController`'s headings moved to the sans `HelmType.sectionTitle()` (serif at 14/15 was one of those four sizes), and **the six editor sheets stay sans on purpose** - `HelmFormSheet` sets its heading at `sectionTitle()`, and `HelmTextField(.lead)` is an *input* the captain types into, not display type.
  - **Review's in-page hero title is gone** - it read literally "Review" under a top bar already reading "Review" (audit §4.6). Its lead line is the live PR count instead, seeded at construction rather than left blank (`render` only fills it in when the background fetch returns, and with no title above it an empty string left the header as a lone refresh glyph). Docs' equivalents - a "DevOps Playbook" toolbar label plus "Runbooks"/"Postmortems" headings each restating the active tab pill directly above them - went with the toolbar rework below. **Hosts needed no change: Phase 5 already removed its duplicate title**, so the brief's Hosts item was already done; its per-tab `HelmCard` header (e.g. "Hosts (2)") is a card header carrying a count, a different level of the hierarchy, and was deliberately left alone.
  - **`HelmPageToolbar` is the horizontal chrome strip a destination puts under the app top bar** - one height (44), one `chromeBackgroundHex` fill, one `chromeLineHex` hairline, one leading/trailing inset, a leading slot (a tab strip / sub-nav) and a trailing slot (that page's actions, with the clearance inequality that keeps a long tab strip truncating rather than running under them). Console (42pt), Tools (42pt, its own comment said it was a hand copy of Console's) and Docs (44pt) each built their own; Docs additionally stacked a **second** 40pt bar inside its Playbook tab, which is now gone - those controls moved into the one toolbar's trailing slot, shown per active tab (`DocsController.updateToolbarActions`, which also hides the Runbooks actions while the runbook editor owns the page).
  - **`HelmPageToolbar.iconButton` is the one toolbar-glyph recipe: a bordered icon square, i.e. `HelmButton(.secondary)`.** This is the fix for the audit's "two icon-button languages, 40pt apart" - the top bar renders bordered 34x34 squares while Console's toolbar, one bar below, rendered up to eleven chrome-less glyphs. Console's two **state-coloured** glyphs (record-red while logging, accent while Block View is showing) now set `HelmButton.tint` / `symbolName`, never `contentTintColor` / `image` - `HelmButton.restyle()`/`rebuildImage()` own those two properties and overwrite them on the next theme change. This is the seam Phase 2 said it was leaving for exactly this.
  - **A real, generalisable AppKit finding, measured not reasoned: Auto Layout constrains an `NSButton`'s *alignment rect*, not its frame, and `NSButton.alignmentRectInsets` is not zero** - `(top: 3, left: 0, bottom: 2.5, right: 0)` at `.regular`, `(2.5, 0, 2.5, 0)` at `.small`. A `HelmButton` paints its chrome in its own layer, which fills the **frame**, so a plain `heightAnchor == 28` renders a visibly **33.5pt** tall box: not square, and 5.5pt taller than the 28pt `TabChipView` beside it. Measured in a real window (28 -> 33.5, 34 -> 39.5, a fixed +5.5). `iconButton` subtracts the button's own insets - read from the button, never hardcoded - so the frame lands on exactly the shared side length. **This is also the mechanism behind this file's long-standing rail note that "every row resolves several points taller than its explicit heightAnchor constant, an unrelated `NSButtonCell` quirk"** - it is not a quirk, it is the alignment rect, and any future code that needs an `NSButton`'s *visible* box to be an exact size has to compensate the same way.
  - **`HelmResponsiveGrid` is `ToolsController.rebuildGrid`'s math, lifted out of it** - column count from the container's real width, and a partial last row padded with invisible spacers so `.fillEqually` divides by the same count and a lone leftover card never stretches. The audit called Tools' grid "the best card grid in the app", which is precisely why it should not have been a private copy: `DocsController.layoutDocGrid` had already hand-ported it (its own comment said so) and **Settings' theme grid had not** - a fixed `columnsPerRow = 4` of fixed 108pt cards, which is what left the audit's ragged 4/2/4/2 last row and never responded to window width. All three call one definition now. Settings also gained the `NSWindow.didResizeNotification` hook Tools already had (gated on `!view.isHidden` for the same measured reason - see the Tools-page resize regression note above) and its card's own minimum grew 108 -> 150, measured: at 108 the longest theme names do not fit beside the active checkmark, and a real render showed one card resolving to 130pt while its siblings sat at 107, its label's compression resistance beating `.fillEqually` (fixed with `.defaultLow` on that label). **Known remaining behaviour, not a defect:** at a very wide window a 6-theme group is one row with trailing empty slots, exactly as Tools' own grid behaves with a partial row - filling the row instead would stretch a theme card to ~250pt, so it was left matching the reference pattern.
  - **Tools' tab strip is hidden entirely with zero tabs open** (audit §4.8: a 76pt strip of dead chrome whose only content was a "+" that opens the picker already filling the page). `HelmPageToolbar.setCollapsed(_:)` owns both halves - `isHidden` **and** the height to 0, because an ordinary hidden `NSView`'s constraints still participate fully in layout (gotcha (11)) - and the component owns that height constraint so a page cannot end up with two required ones fighting. ⌘T still opens a tab from the menu, and the strip reappears with it.
  - **The rail's two flyout rows now say so.** "Hosts" and "Setup" are the only rows that pop a flyout instead of navigating, and nothing signalled it; each carries a small trailing `chevron.right` (`IconRailController.attachFlyoutIndicator`), tinted with that row's own resolved colour so it brightens to the accent along with the icon and label when the row is active. It sits vertically centred on the icon and pinned to the row's trailing edge, deliberately **not** overlapping the icon - see `attachBadge`'s own comment for the three live-rendered attempts that proved no corner-overlap amount clears every glyph's ink.
  - **`FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS` gained three checks** - the toolbar recipe (one height / fill / hairline per theme, plus the glyph really being a bordered `.secondary` square of the shared side), the grid math (columns monotonic in width, rows filling the container exactly, every row padded to a full column count), and the page-title voice (both voices at 22, distinct faces, and the source guard above). **All four failure modes were confirmed to reproduce**, not just to pass: a `.quiet` glyph, the alignment-inset compensation reverted (reported the exact 33.5), the spacer padding removed, and a planted `ShiftFont.serif(19)` plus a 20pt `pageTitle` - each failed by name and line, then restored to PASS.
  - **Verified** with 75 real off-screen PNG renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`) of the **real** mounted `ConsoleController`, `ToolsController`, `DocsController` (all three tabs), `FleetController`, `ReviewController`, `ShiftController`, `SettingsController` and `IconRailController` across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-light`, with zero AutoLayout break warnings - plus real measurements rather than eyeballing: the glyph's frame in a real window, the three pages' toolbar geometry per theme, the theme grid re-flowing 3 -> 5 -> 6 -> 7 -> 9 columns across a real `NSWindow.setFrame` sweep with every card equal width, Tools' strip at 0 -> 1 -> 2 -> 0 tabs, and each rail row's chevron frame and tint. Probe instrumentation was reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention. **The full 7-phase rollout of `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` is now complete**; anything further is a new, separately-scoped task. (A follow-up **visual fidelity pass** against the audit's own prototype shipped as `fm/grandline-design-fidelity-fixes` - see the next bullet.)
- **`fm/grandline-design-fidelity-fixes` is the visual fidelity pass over the finished 7-phase rollout - the captain compared the live app against the audit's own interactive prototype (`data/grandline-full-ui-audit/prototype.html`, plus 15 paired screenshots in `data/grandline-design-fidelity-fixes/captain-screenshots/`) and found it did not match.** Nine fixes; the two that matter most were real bugs, not restyling. Read the prototype before changing any of this - it is more authoritative than any single screenshot because its real DOM/CSS can be inspected.
  - **The app window could not fill the screen, and the cause was a constraint - not the default frame.** Two independent faults, and either one alone still left black bars. (1) `AppDelegate` opened at a hardcoded 1220x720 and `center()`ed it; it now opens at `NSScreen.visibleFrame` (`AppDelegate.defaultWindowFrame`) with `setFrameAutosaveName` so the captain's own later resize wins, and **`contentViewController` is assigned *before* `setFrame`** - the other way round, AppKit re-derives the frame from the content's fitting size and silently undoes it (measured: 1512x950 -> 960x652, i.e. `contentMinSize` + the title bar). (2) **`ToolRowLayout`'s fixed-name-column constraint sat at `.defaultHigh + 1` (751), above `NSLayoutPriorityWindowSizeStayPut` (500) - so it could resize the whole window.** A window only holds its own size at priority 500; paired with the required `<= nameColumnMaxWidth`, that constraint capped the entire app at `520 / 0.42` plus the row/card/page insets = **1410pt wide**, on every page carrying these rows (Updates, Bootstrap, Automation, GitHub Sync, Vault). Measured on a real 1512x982 screen: the window refused to grow past 1410, `isZoomed` reported `true` there, and **genuine macOS full screen rendered 1410x949 centred with a black bar down each side** - which is exactly `01-live-window-not-fullscreen.png`. Now 499. **The general rule: no content constraint may exceed priority 500 unless you genuinely intend it to drive the window's size.** `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS` gained `checkRowDoesNotResizeWindow`, which asserts both the priority and that a real window carrying one of these rows actually holds a width past the old cap - confirmed to catch the regression (reverting to 751 reproduced the exact 751 constraint and a window shrinking to 1246pt).
  - **`SetupContainerController.swift` is new, and is the only real behaviour change in the pass** (the captain asked for it explicitly). `.updates`/`.bootstrap`/`.automation`/`.githubSync` are still four independent `RailDestination` cases and four independent controllers - the container only re-parents them under one `HelmSegmentedTabs` row so the captain can move between them without going back out to the rail's Setup flyout. `AppShellController` embeds `setup.view` in place of the four, and `show(.bootstrap)` etc. now route through `SetupTab(destination:)` + `select(tab:)`. The top bar says **"Setup"** for all four - the tab row below names the sub-page, matching the prototype and how Hosts already works.
  - **`ToolRowLayout`'s detail line used to render straight across the status column.** `textStack.setClippingResistancePriority(.defaultLow, ...)` is exactly the priority of a stack's own "I am at least as wide as my widest arranged subview" constraint, so with the required `<= nameColumnMaxWidth` winning, the stack was allowed to be narrower than its own content - and an `NSStackView` does not clip. The labels' `.byTruncatingTail` never fired because nothing had ever made their *frames* narrower than their text. Measured: Updates' `firstmate` row reached x=751 while its "Update Available" pill started at x=598, a real 153pt overlap (`02-live-updates-overlap-bug.png`). Fixed with a required `label.width <= textStack.width` on both labels, plus the full string on each label's tooltip (set in `applyTheme`, the one place every caller already calls after changing a row's text).
  - **Hosts is left-aligned, not centred.** Phase 5's capped column used `leading >= / trailing <= / centerX ==`, which floats the page in the middle of a wide window with a mirrored dead gutter either side. The `centerX` tie is gone (`leading ==` the page gutter, cap raised 940 -> 1120), matching the prototype and every sibling destination. (That 1120pt cap is gone too as of `fm/grandline-visual-polish-round2` - it still left a large dead gutter on a laptop-width window, and Hosts is now gutter-to-gutter like every other card-bearing page.) Each host row's kicker is its own role now - `HostsController.roleKicker(for:)` returns the host's first tag, else its group, else "SSH" - instead of "SSH" four times down the list; the accent bar was already the captain's own per-host `accentHex`, so the two together are the prototype's PREPROD / PROD / CI rows.
  - **Overview** matches the prototype's differentiation rather than reading as one uniform slab: the answer banner is a `HelmAccentRow` (accent bar, round badge, "ALL CLEAR"/"NEEDS YOU" kicker) instead of a hand-rolled tinted box with a text glyph; the stat tiles that carry a signal are tinted (`ready to merge` accent, `done today` good, watcher by its own health - the rest stay ink, because a colour with nothing to say is noise); "In flight" is a plain section heading (round glyph + title + count chip) rather than a `HelmCard`, since its rows are already cards; and Refresh is a labelled `HelmButton(.quiet)` at the page's trailing edge.
  - **Console's toolbar labels its named features** - Find, Compose, Claude usage, Blocks - via the new `HelmPageToolbar.labeledButton` (same bordered `.secondary` chrome and same alignment-inset height compensation as `iconButton`). Zoom in/out, theme and log stay icon squares: they have no prototype counterpart and labelling five more controls would leave a long tab strip nowhere to go. `updateComposeControls`/`updateUtilizationControls` no longer set `contentTintColor` - a `HelmButton` owns that (Phase 2), and it was only ever a no-op while those controls were image-only.
  - **Docs cards say something.** `DocsRunbookMetadata` (`DocsRunbookData.swift`) derives "Kubernetes · 3 steps" / "Root cause: …" from the document's **own markdown** - no new field and nothing written to disk: steps are the command lines inside fenced blocks (the same lines `native/Scripts/sre_kubectl_mcp.py`'s `_extract_command_lines` counts, so the card and SRE Lead can never disagree), the category is the dominant leading executable across them, and a postmortem's is the first sentence of its own `## Root Cause`. "Updated N ago" is the fallback and stays on the card's tooltip either way. Covered in `DocsRunbookDataSelfTest` (confirmed to catch a regression).
  - **A real pre-existing clipping bug on those same cards, found in a render:** "Debugging High CPU Usage" displayed as a bare "Debugging High", no ellipsis. Two causes, both worth knowing. The delete glyph was a default-size `HelmButton`, which carries a regular button's horizontal padding even with an empty title - measured **87pt** on a 265pt card, squeezing the title column to 104pt; it now has an explicit `docCardDeleteButtonSide` box like a toolbar icon. And `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` was an **over-estimate** of the real column, which is the dangerous direction: AppKit computes a one-line `intrinsicContentSize` at the estimate, lays the label out one line tall, and the text then wraps to two lines *inside* that one-line frame and draws the second outside its own bounds. The formula now subtracts the delete column and the gap in front of it.
  - **DevOps Commands** keeps its Categories overview (name + count, selected highlighted) permanently visible above the selected category's command list, instead of the drill-down that replaced the whole column with a "‹ Kubernetes" back row - so Favorites and Recently used never disappear either, and the back row is gone because there is nowhere to go back from (clicking the open category closes it). The action row has a hierarchy: primary "Send to Terminal", secondary Copy and "Add to Runbook", then a spacer, then quiet Edit / Duplicate / Favorite / Explain. Its two-pane `columns` stack also needed `distribution = .fill` (gotcha (10) again) - at `.gravityAreas` the detail card stopped at ~60% of the page.
  - **Deliberately NOT built: the prototype's "Compare with what ships today" toggle** on Hosts and Updates. That is the prototype's own audit-annotation device (its `ba()` builder), the same category as the "Change:"/"Proposal:" captions the brief says to ignore - it renders the *old* design for comparison and has no place in the shipped app.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders of the real mounted `HostsController` (all three tabs), `FleetController`, `SetupContainerController` (all four tabs), `DocsController`, `CommandLibraryPageView` and `ConsoleController` across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`, plus **27 live assertions driving the real handlers** (Hosts tab switching, the pinned and a saved host's Connect, Add Host; all four Setup tabs both via `select(tab:)` and through the tab control's own gesture target/action, with each page's own actions still present; the command library's category/command row clicks, Copy onto the real pasteboard, Send to Terminal, and the favourite toggle; Overview's stat-tile tints and its ready-to-merge tile firing `onNavigateToReview`) and a real launch of the real app measuring the window in normal and genuine full-screen mode. Probe instrumentation was reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention.
- **`fm/grandline-visual-polish-round2` is the second round of live-use visual fixes on top of the finished audit rollout (captain screenshots in `data/grandline-visual-polish-round2/captain-screenshots/`).** Five defects, one of which was two pages reporting the same root cause. Read `ToolRowLayout.nameColumnMinWidth` and `statusColumnTrailingReserve`'s own doc comments before touching a checklist row.
  - **`ToolRowLayout`'s status column is now measured from the row's *trailing* edge, and that one change is items 4 and 5 both.** §5.4 had made it a fixed 42%-of-the-row name column clamped to [200, 520], which pinned the pill (its actual goal, and it worked) but pinned it *at 42% from the leading edge*. Two live consequences, measured on the real pages before the fix: GitHub Sync's rows all carry short details ("In sync with kunchenguid/<repo>"), so every pill sat with **729.5pt** of dead row between it and the right-anchored chevron ("let us have the status to the right side"), and Updates' real `firstmate` row carries a detail longer than the column, so it truncated **6.0pt** short of the pill with ~670pt of unused row to its right ("the context and the upgrade available is conflicting... it's blocking the context"). Now: `statusColumn.trailing <= chevron.leading - statusColumnTrailingReserve` (160, sized from the real button widths - "Check" 59, "Check" + "Update" 131, "Sync now" 77, GitHub Sync's in-sync rows 0 because a hidden arranged subview leaves an `NSStackView`'s layout entirely - plus the row's own 24pt of inter-column spacing). Measured after: **174.0pt** trailing inset on all 13 real Updates rows and all 8 real GitHub Sync rows, i.e. **0.0pt** spread, with the `firstmate` detail line now 327pt clear of the pill. The 42% multiplier and the `nameColumnMaxWidth` cap are gone; `nameColumnMinWidth` survives as a 499-priority floor only.
  - **What that costs, on purpose:** a row whose action column is genuinely wider than the reserve (Updates' rare `.notInstalled` row, "Check" + "Install in Bootstrap ->" at 208pt, and Vault's two-button rows) shifts left together to its own constant inset. Reserving for *those* would put every ordinary row's pill 250pt from the row's end, which is re-creating the dead gap the reserve exists to close. The row also gained a real 16pt `statusColumnLeadingGap` spacer, because with the column right-anchored a long detail on a narrow window collapses the text-to-pill gap back to the stack's own 8pt spacing (measured at 6pt of visible gap on a 1026pt row).
  - **Two mechanisms this needed that are worth knowing.** (a) A bare `NSView()` spacer has no intrinsic content size, so `setContentHuggingPriority` on it is a **no-op** - see gotcha (12), which this generalises. Holding a spacer collapsed needs a real `width == 0` constraint at 499 (above `textStack`'s 250 stack-level hugging, below `NSLayoutPriorityWindowSizeStayPut`). The first attempt used content hugging and rendered byte-identically to the bug. (b) Removing the required `textStack.width <= 520` cap handed the row's minimum width to the *labels*: an `NSTextField` defaults to 750 horizontal compression resistance, above stay-put, so Updates' real `firstmate` string became a floor - a window asked for 1064pt came back 1103, with the row at 1055 inside a 1016pt container. Both labels are now `.defaultLow`, which is what lets `.byTruncatingTail` fire. `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS`' `checkStatusColumnAligned` was rewritten to assert what the new shape actually guarantees (constant pill **trailing** inset - the pills are right-aligned now and their own widths differ legitimately, so `minX` would report that as a failure - plus "is it actually on the right side" and a hard 12pt text-to-pill floor), and `checkRowDoesNotResizeWindow` gained the long-detail floor case. **All five failure modes were confirmed to reproduce, not just to pass:** the pre-fix leading-edge column (131pt trailing spread), a reserve sized for the widest action set in the app (pill 914pt from a 1400pt row's end), the leading gap zeroed, and the label compression resistance left at the default (window grew to exactly 1055pt).
  - **The SRE Lead pane's "large black empty area" was the transcript painting itself in the terminal's token, not a broken separator.** The brief suspected the accent separator did not run the pane's full height, and said not to trust the two prior fixes that claim to have fixed that. Re-derived independently, and **the separator is genuinely fine**: real pixel sampling down a real off-screen render of the real mounted pane found `accentHex` at every y, and the geometry probe read 3.0x616.0 against a 616pt pane - 100.0% - both before *and* after this task's changes. The actual defect: `fm/grandline-sre-lead-polish` moved `sreLeadPane`/`sreLeadEmptyStateView` onto `chromeBackgroundHex` and missed `SRELeadChatView`, which is what fills the pane once a tab has a chat, so a started-but-unasked pane rendered in `backgroundHex` - the *terminal's* token - and read as a continuation of the terminal beside it. Measured before: `chatFill #05090E` vs `paneFill #111820` in helm-dark, and mismatched in every theme where those two tokens differ (they are identical in `gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`tokyo-night-light`, so no fill change can be what proves this - compare the resolved colours per theme). The pane's input field also had no fill of its own, so its *cell* painted the system `.textBackgroundColor` over the layer - AGENTS.md's documented `NSTextField` overpaint trap - and now goes through `HelmField.makeSunkenTextField`/`applySunken`, with a new 1pt divider above the input row since the transcript and the row share one surface.
  - **The SRE Lead toolbar control is a `HelmButton` now, and `SRELeadStatusPill` no longer exists** (the file is `SRELeadPhase.swift`, holding just the phase enum plus its label/glyph/tint). It was a bespoke dot-plus-label `NSView` that Phase 7 never migrated, even though `HelmPageToolbar.labeledButton`'s own doc comment names "SRE Lead" as one of the four controls the prototype shows that way - so the toolbar shipped three labelled icon buttons and, first in the same row, a grey dot at 90x24 next to Find's 63x28. Now 89.5x28, same recipe, same baseline. State moved from the dot to `HelmButton.tint` **plus the glyph**: `.notStarted` is deliberately untinted (that is the state the captain's proposed screenshot shows, and tinting it even `.neutral` would make the control's own name quieter than its siblings'), and `.ready` is `shield.fill` rather than relying on hue, because `HelmContrast.legibleTintedText` blends a tint toward the theme's ink until it clears 4.5:1 and gruvbox-light's corrected green sat close to plain ink in a real render. The whole shield family was confirmed to resolve before use - `NSImage(systemSymbolName:)` returns nil silently, and this app has shipped an invisible icon that way before.
  - **Hosts has no content-width cap at all now.** 1120pt still left 368pt empty on a 1512pt window and 756pt on 1900pt; it is `leading == +pageGutter` / `trailing == -pageGutter` like `FleetController`, `ReviewController`, `UpdatesController`, `GitHubSyncController` and `VaultController`, all of which were already uncapped. The old comment's worry ("a very wide window still doesn't stretch a host row's two short strings") was already handled a level down - a host row is a `HelmAccentRow` whose Connect / `...` controls sit in its own right-anchored `trailingAccessory`, so extra width lands *between* the strings and the actions. Measured after: 24pt right gap at 1200 / 1512 / 1900.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders and real geometry, before *and* after, from a genuine HEAD baseline (the working tree was stashed, a before-probe built against `main`, then restored) rather than from reasoning about the diff: the real mounted `UpdatesController`, `GitHubSyncController`, `VaultController`, `BootstrapController`, `AutomationController`, `HostsController` and a real multi-tab `ConsoleController` with a real per-tab SRE Lead session driven through the existing fake-`claude` seam (`SRELead.claudePathOverrideForTests`). Row geometry swept 1064 / 1400 / 1512pt and six palettes. Probe instrumentation was reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention. No regression across `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_PER_TAB_TESTS`, `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_BRIDGE_TESTS`, `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_MARKDOWN_TESTS`, `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_POSTMORTEM_TESTS`, `FM_RUN_NOTIFICATION_CENTER_SRE_LEAD_TESTS`, `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS` or the other 13 suites run.
- **Colour contrast is now enforced by a self-test, not by convention (`fm/grandline-design-audit-phase0`, Phase 0 of the full-app UI audit at `data/grandline-full-ui-audit/report.md` - read §5 there before touching any of this).** `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` sweeps all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes` x every tint hue and fails the build's own check if a pill, an icon tile or `HelmTheme.mutedInk` drops below its floor - run it after adding a theme, a `HelmTint` case, or any new tinted component. Phase 2 added two more checks to the same file (every `HelmButton` variant's label against its own fill, and a source guard that fails on any reintroduced stock `bezelStyle`); **both halves were confirmed to catch a real regression, not just to pass** - reverting `.destructive` to the raw hue as its own label reproduced §5.7's exact failure shape (2.71:1 in solarized-dark), and reintroducing one `bezelStyle` line was reported by file:line. Six things it locks down:
  - **`HelmContrast` (`HelmUIComponents.swift`) holds the one rule worth remembering: a `HelmTint` hue is safe as a *fill* or a *bar*, and is NOT automatically safe as *text*.** `ToolRowLayout.pill` used to set the label and the 15% wash to the same hue, which measured below 4.5:1 in **44 of 72** real theme/hue pairs (worst 1.93:1, Rosé Pine Dawn amber) - and it is the app's one shared pill, so that covered nearly every status indicator. `HelmContrast.tintedSurface` keeps the hue for the wash and bisects the label toward the theme's own `chromeInkHex` only as far as the floor needs, stepping the wash fainter (0.15 -> 0.04) when the label alone cannot get there - the same technique as the vendored `Dimming.contrastFixBlendFraction`, with a coarse bracketing scan first because contrast against a fixed fill is V-shaped, not monotonic, in the blend fraction. Its guarantee holds against *both* surfaces a chip can land on (`chromeBackgroundHex` and `backgroundHex`), since the shared pill cannot know which. **Any new component that puts a tint hue on a wash of itself must route through it** - `ToolRowLayout.pill`, `IconTileView.applyTheme` (3:1 icon floor, not 4.5) and `SRELeadChatView.sectionLabel` all do; `NotificationRowView` sidesteps it by colouring its kicker `mutedInk`, never the tint.
  - **`HelmTheme.mutedInk` is no longer a flat 0.7.** That constant was only ever measured against the original 8 palettes and genuinely fails five of the ten sourced-family themes (solarized-dark 3.14, catppuccin-latte 3.47, tokyo-night-light 3.75, rose-pine-dawn 4.04, gruvbox-light 4.37). It now starts at `baseMutedAlpha` (0.7, unchanged for the seven themes that clear it) and bisects *up* per theme only where needed - raising it globally would have washed out helm-dark, which measures 8.36. Cached per theme id; `HelmTheme.mutedAlpha(for:)` exposes the resolved value.
  - **No system semantic greys for text.** `.tertiaryLabelColor` fails 4.5:1 in **every** theme (as low as 1.83:1) and `.secondaryLabelColor` fails in every light one; forcing `root.appearance` only picks the right *side* of light/dark, it cannot make a fixed system grey theme-aware. All 33 such sites across 10 files are gone. `MutedInkLabels` (`HelmUIComponents.swift`) is the registry for a window whose theme observer has no other per-label repaint path (it tints on `add`, so a label is correct before the first theme change); pages that already had a registry (`SettingsController.subtitleViews` via its new `mutedLabel(_:)`, `BootstrapController.dynamicLabels`) use theirs. The self-test greps `Sources/` and fails if either token comes back.
  - **Selected-row rendering for the Hosts / Keys / Snippets lists is theme-derived, not the system accent.** `table.style = .sourceList` + `selectionHighlightStyle = .regular` hands selection to AppKit's private `NSTableRowSidebarSelectionView`, which draws from the **system accent**, not `HelmTheme` - so a selected row was macOS blue on every palette, and `.appearance` cannot fix it because the wrong thing being resolved is the accent, not light-vs-dark. Phase 0 fixed that with `selectionHighlightStyle = .none` plus a `HelmTableRowView` that painted a wash of the theme's own accent *behind* the row. **Phase 5 moved it onto the row itself and deleted that class**: those lists are `HelmAccentRow` cards now, and a card is opaque, so a wash painted behind it is invisible - the same accent-wash + accent-stroke recipe lives on `HelmAccentRow.isRowSelected` (`selectionWash` / `selectionBorderAlpha`). Those three lists were `HelmTableRowView`'s only callers, so it is gone rather than left as dead code; `HelmUIComponents.swift` keeps a note where it was. The tables are also `.fullWidth` rather than `.sourceList` now, so the private selection material is never installed at all. **Consequence to remember, unchanged: those lists' cell views carry `HelmTheme`-derived colours that do not re-derive themselves, so each list's theme observer calls `table.reloadData()`** - a new theme-coloured cell view needs that or it goes stale. Verified live across helm-dark/gruvbox-light/catppuccin-mocha, by measuring the selected card's resolved fill and border against the theme accent *and* against `controlAccentColor`/`selectedContentBackgroundColor` (they must not match).
- **Two smaller live-found defects fixed in the same pass, both worth knowing.** (1) `ShiftEmptyStateView` (`ShiftTypography.swift` - now `HelmEmptyState`, see the Phase 4 bullet) collapses a long single-line string to a few characters per line and then truncates - its label sits in a centre-aligned stack whose leading/trailing constraints are *inequalities*, so nothing obliges Auto Layout to give it a width. Every pre-existing caller happened to pass short or explicitly `\n`-broken copy, which hid it; it now hands the label the real available width in `layout()`. (2) `NSImage(systemSymbolName: "anchor", ...)` returns **nil** on macOS - it is not an SF Symbol - so the Hosts list's pinned Firstmate row rendered with no icon at all since it shipped (visible in the audit's own `helm-dark-hosts.png`). It uses `sailboat` now, the glyph this app already uses for itself. Confirm a symbol actually resolves before shipping it; a nil image fails silently.
- **Historical note on the bullet above:** `mutedInk` shipped as a flat 0.7 in fixes4 Fix 8 after several destinations had each picked their own 0.4-0.65 alpha ad hoc and gone near-invisible in the light palettes. That flat constant is what `fm/grandline-design-audit-phase0` replaced; the "route new muted text through `mutedInk` rather than picking an alpha yourself" instruction it established still stands, and is now enforced by `FM_RUN_CONTRAST_TESTS`. The related rule about forcing a view's `.appearance` to match the theme mode inside its `ThemeManager.shared.observe` callback also still stands for any *remaining* system-semantic colour (`.windowBackgroundColor`, `.labelColor`, control chrome) - it is just no longer load-bearing for muted text, which is now theme-derived outright.
- **Toast.swift is the one save-confirmation pattern (fixes4 Fix 5).** `Toast.show(in:message:)` drops a small auto-dismissing pill at the top of any view. Host saves show it on `AppShellController.view` (not the host editor's own view, since that window closes immediately on Save); key saves show it directly on `HostsController.view` (the Keys tab stays on screen, and its sheet-presented editor closes on top of it).
- **`HelmTheme.swift` holds 12 palettes as concrete sRGB hex**: `helm-dark`/`helm-light`, hand-pinned originals, plus 10 more from cockpit-theme-overhaul (see the dedicated bullet below). **`ThemeManager.swift` is the single source of truth for which theme is active**, persisted by `HelmTheme.id` (migrates a pre-nav-redesign "fm.themeMode" dark/light pref once) - `ConsoleController` and every other theme-aware window/view (`HostsController`, `IconRailController`, `TopBarController`, `SettingsController`, `ReviewController`, `FleetController`, and every sheet editor) call `ThemeManager.shared.observe { ... }` rather than tracking their own copy - see the checklist at the top of `ThemeManager.swift` and `NSWindow.followHelmTheme()` (for a window's own title-bar chrome, which has no view to hook) for the full convention this task established after finding and fixing the same class of bug three separate times. `observe` returns a discardable `ThemeObservation` token (cockpit-native-host-pages) - every one of those call sites is an app-lifetime singleton and can ignore it, but `ConsoleController` keeps its token and passes it to `ThemeManager.shared.unobserve` inside `shutdown()`, since Fix 1 made it the first view controller in this app that can be deallocated mid-session (a deleted host's dedicated page) - skipping that would leak a dead closure into `ThemeManager.observers` on every delete. A window/view using `NSVisualEffectView` (or plain semantic system colors) needs its `appearance` forced to `.darkAqua`/`.aqua` in that observer for its colors to resolve against the Helm mode instead of macOS's own light/dark setting - `SettingsController` needed this added when it moved from its own floating window into the shared, always-themed body area (nav-redesign task). `ThemeMenu.swift` builds the shared DARK/LIGHT-grouped, swatch + checkmark `NSMenu` used by both the topbar's theme button and Settings' Appearance row.
- **`fm/cockpit-theme-overhaul` replaced the 6 formula-generated palettes (Midnight/Graphite/Nocturne/Paper/Frost/Linen) with 10 real, independently-sourced themes across 5 families, each contributing a light+dark pair: Solarized, Catppuccin, Gruvbox, Tokyo Night, Rosé Pine** (`solarized-dark`/`-light`, `catppuccin-mocha`/`-latte`, `gruvbox-dark`/`-light`, `tokyo-night-dark`/`-light`, `rose-pine-main`/`-dawn`). Every value is either lifted verbatim from that family's own canonical repo or a deliberate, documented deviation (inline code comments on each theme cover the specific hex and why) - see `data/cockpit-theme-research/report.md` for the full source URLs, per-family contrast tables, and the reasoning behind every deviation, rather than re-deriving it from the code comments alone. `HelmTheme.pairId` names each theme's light/dark counterpart within its own family; `ThemeManager.toggle()` (the quick dark/light flip, ⌘⌥T) looks that up and flips within the family instead of always landing on `helm-dark`/`helm-light` (those two still pair with each other, unchanged). The Settings Appearance grid (`SettingsController.rebuildAppearanceGrid`) wraps theme cards into fixed-width chunks (`Array.chunked(into:)`) rather than one ever-widening row per mode, so it doesn't need updating again the next time a theme family is added. **`fm/cockpit-fix-docs-audit` fixed the Appearance card's subtitle, which a QA audit (`data/cockpit-audit-docs-updates/report.md`) caught still hardcoded at "8 Helm themes" from before this task added the 10 family themes (12 total)** - it now reads `\(HelmTheme.allThemes.count) Helm themes, light and dark` so it can't drift out of sync again the next time a theme is added.
- **`AppSettings.swift` + `SettingsController.swift` (⌘, / App menu > Settings…, or the rail's gear icon) are the native Settings panel** - three cards, matching the web app's Connection/Appearance/Terminal visual weight (icon + title headers, generous spacing, no Sign-in card - native has no login): **Connection** (mirror-target field + a live `TmuxMirror.listSessions()` session-picker of selectable cards, plus the working-directory chooser); **Appearance** (the 8 `HelmTheme.allThemes` as a DARK/LIGHT-grouped wrapping grid of preview cards - swatch bars + name + checkmark - built by `SettingsController.rebuildAppearanceGrid`, the same source of truth `ThemeMenu` uses for the topbar picker); **Terminal** (font-size presets 12/13/14/16 via `ConsoleController.stepFontSize`, plus three `NSSwitch` toggles: "Reconnect automatically" - `AppSettings.autoReconnect`, read by `ConsoleController.processTerminated` to actually re-run a dropped tab's launch after a short delay instead of only showing the ⌘R hint; "Bell & notifications" - `AppSettings.notifyOnNeedsDecision`, driving `FleetNotifier`'s background poll of `FleetDataSource.parseTasks()` to post a real macOS notification the first time a task newly needs the captain; originally also a session-logging-by-default toggle, removed with the feature itself - see the `fm/grandline-remove-session-logging` bullet). `FM_SHELL_CWD`/`FM_MIRROR_TARGET` env vars still win when set, `AppSettings` (backed by `UserDefaults`) is the fallback.
- **`SudoTouchIDData.swift` + Settings' fourth card, "Security" (cockpit-settings-sudo-touchid)** added a live "Touch ID for sudo" row. Its status reads `/etc/pam.d/sudo` and `/etc/pam.d/sudo_local` directly (Automic Vault's `av harden sudo` hardener has no `--json` listing entry the way the gh/aws hardeners do, since it's a root-only, file-content-driven check) - a direct Swift port of `automic-vault/src/isotopes/hardeners/sudo.rs`'s `pam_tid_enabled`/`line_enables_pam_tid`. A real caveat worth knowing: the appended `auth sufficient pam_tid.so` line only takes effect if `/etc/pam.d/sudo` already `include`s `sudo_local` - not true on every Mac - so the row has a distinct "Unavailable" state for that case rather than assuming it's always configured. The Enable button runs `sudo av harden sudo` (not the plain unprivileged form, which only previews and prints "next: sudo av harden sudo") through the same one-shot Console command-tab mechanism (`ConsoleController.openCommandTab` via `AppShellController.runInConsole`, wired from `settings.onRunCommand`/`onRunCommandTracked` exactly like `bootstrap.onRunCommand`) since a real `sudo` password prompt needs a real terminal. The row itself is rebuilt (not just re-themed) on every status change, mirroring Bootstrap's `ghAuthRow` - since `SettingsController`'s other cards are built once and never torn down, this is the first dynamic row in that file, and its own `rebuildSecuritySection` prunes `hoverRows` of the just-removed row's now-orphaned `HoverHighlightView` entry before appending the new one, so that shared re-theming list doesn't grow unbounded across repeated status checks. **`fm/cockpit-settings-sudo-nixdarwin` added a `.notEnabledNixDarwin` status** for nix-darwin-managed Macs: `/etc/pam.d/sudo_local` there is a symlink chain into the immutable Nix store (confirmed live: `/etc/pam.d/sudo_local` -> `/etc/static/pam.d/sudo_local` -> `/nix/store/.../etc-sudo_local`), and `av harden sudo` refuses to write through a symlink (a correct guard against symlink attacks on root-owned PAM files), failing with a bare ELOOP instead - even bypassing that would be pointless, since nix-darwin regenerates the file from the flake on every rebuild anyway. `SudoTouchIDSource.isManagedByNixDarwin()` detects this via `(path as NSString).resolvingSymlinksInPath` (the full-chain resolve, not just the first `readlink` hop - same idiom `DotfilesData.swift` already uses for its own symlink checks) landing under `/nix/store/`. The row's fix on that class of machine is guidance-only text pointing at nix-darwin's own `security.pam.services.sudo_local.touchIdAuth = true;` option, mirroring the gh row's `isotopeMissingPlainGhInstalled` guidance-only case - the app never edits the captain's dotfiles itself. The status *check* (reading file content for `pam_tid.so`) is unaffected by any of this, since reading through a symlink is transparently fine; only the write path `av harden sudo` uses is what breaks.
- **Find (Fix 4, cockpit-native-ui-fixes2).** The topbar Search pill / its ⌘K calls `AppShellController.activateConsoleFind()`, which brings Console forward and calls `ConsoleController.showFind()` - the exact same in-terminal find the console toolbar's own magnifying-glass icon triggers (SwiftTerm's native find bar on the active tab). There is one search behavior in the app, not two; it has nothing to do with Hosts.
- **Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot.** This machine has a real console session, but the agent's shell process (`claude` under WezTerm) has neither Screen Recording nor Accessibility permission granted, and there is no way to grant either non-interactively (`screencapture`/`osascript "System Events"` both fail, TCC.db is SIP-protected even to root, no passwordless sudo). The effective substitute that actually caught real bugs (fixes4): add temporary, env-var-gated debug code straight into the controller under test - print live `NSView.frame` geometry after a real layout pass, or a `Thread.callStackSymbols` dump inside a suspect delegate callback - `swift build && FM_DEBUG_X=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit`, read the printed numbers/stack, then revert the instrumentation before committing. This is real evidence from AppKit's actual layout/event engine, not a screenshot, but it is not guesswork either - say so explicitly in any PR that relies on it instead of claiming a visual check that didn't happen. **One hard-won rule for any probe that pixel-samples a render** (`fm/grandline-design-system-phase2`): compare the sampled pixel against the expected colour **in `rep.colorSpace`**, never via `colorAt(...).usingColorSpace(.sRGB)`. `bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay` returns a rep in the *display's* profile ("Color LCD" on this machine) when the view is inside a real `NSWindow`, and Generic RGB when it is not - and the sRGB conversion is only correct in the second case. In a window it reported `0.286/0.484/0.575` for a pixel genuinely holding the theme accent's `0.157/0.412/0.514`, which reads exactly like a real colour bug; converting the *expected* colour into `rep.colorSpace` first matched to 0.002. A probe that samples a view rendered standalone (no window) will pass with the sRGB conversion and then mislead the moment someone renders the same view inside a window, so use the rep's own space unconditionally.
- **The app bundle lives at `dist/Manjesh Grand Line.app` (gitignored, renamed from `Firstmate.app` in the cockpit-rebrand-grand-line task).** Built by `native/build_native_app.sh` (plain `swift build -c release` + manual bundle assembly, no py2app, bundle ID `com.firstmate.cockpit.native` - deliberately unchanged by the rename, see below). There used to be a second, older `dist/Firstmate.app` produced by a since-removed Python/WKWebView app via the root `build_app.sh` - that codebase and script are gone, so this is the only app and the only build path now. **Codesigning (cockpit-stable-signing):** the script signs the assembled `.app` with a local self-signed identity named exactly "Firstmate Cockpit Local Dev" (`codesign --force --sign "Firstmate Cockpit Local Dev" --identifier "$BUNDLE_ID"`) when that identity exists in the login keychain (`security find-identity -v -p codesigning`), and otherwise warns and continues unsigned rather than failing - this keeps the app's code identity, and therefore Keychain ACL trust for saved SSH keys (see the "SSH keychain" bullet above), stable across rebuilds instead of getting a new ad-hoc identity every time. See `native/README.md`'s "Local signing setup" section for the one-time per-machine commands to (re)create this cert. **The bundle ID (`com.firstmate.cockpit.native`), the signing identity name (`Firstmate Cockpit Local Dev`), and the Swift module/target name (`FirstmateCockpit`) are all deliberately unchanged by the app's display-name rebrand to "Manjesh Grand Line"** - only user-visible text (window titles, tooltips, button labels, the bundle's `CFBundleName`/`CFBundleDisplayName`) changed. Changing the bundle ID or signing identity would invalidate the captain's already-saved Keychain-backed SSH keys.
- **"Firstmate Latest Updates" (`UpdatesController.swift` + `UpdatesData.swift`, cockpit-native-updates-page, restyled in cockpit-updates-redesign).** A `.updates` rail destination (the captain's own hand-drawn artwork as of cockpit-native-updates-polish - see the `CaptainIcon.swift` bullet below; pinned directly above Settings the same way Settings is pinned above the avatar - see `IconRailController.loadView`'s bottom-anchored chain) listing every tool in the captain's ecosystem (npm-global `*-axi` packages, Homebrew `gh`/`tmux`/the `claude-code` cask, herdr, no-mistakes, and firstmate's own upstream sync), each checked automatically on page load and updatable with one explicit click - Settings-styled cards/rows, per-row spinner + expandable command-output log, a `Toast` + `UNUserNotificationCenter` notification on success. cockpit-updates-redesign (captain-supplied HTML mockup, translated to native AppKit + Helm colors rather than embedded) added: a 4-tile summary strip above the tool list (Tools Installed / Up to Date / Updates Available / Last Checked, the last a self-ticking relative-time label via a `Timer` started in `viewWillAppear`/stopped in `viewWillDisappear`, reusing the same rounded-card chrome as `card(icon:title:content:)`/`SettingsController.card` via the shared `cardBackgrounds` list rather than inventing a second style); a live `NSSearchField` above the cards that hides non-matching rows by name substring and collapses a whole category card once none of its rows match (`UpdatesController.CategorySection`/`applyFilter` - same `NSSearchFieldDelegate.controlTextDidChange` pattern as `HostsController`'s host filter); a per-category-tinted rounded icon tile behind each row's glyph (`tintHex(for:)`, drawn from the active theme's own ANSI slots/accent, never a literal hex); and each row's status pill restyled into a clearer badge (fuller pill padding, Title Case text). The page-level "Check all" action **reverses** cockpit-native-updates-polish's earlier bare-icon-button decision: the captain's mockup showed it as the page's clear primary action, so it is now a prominent, labeled, accent-filled pill ("Refresh", built as a plain `NSView` + `NSClickGestureRecognizer` - the same clickable-styled-card pattern as `SettingsController.themeCard`/`sessionCard` - rather than fighting `NSButton`'s tight default padding on a borderless button) using `theme.selectionTextHex` for its icon/label color, since that token is already contrast-verified against an opaque `accentHex` fill (SwiftTerm's own selected-text-on-accent pairing). It still swaps for the same determinate `NSProgressIndicator` + "Checking… (N/M)" label while checks run, then the same `Toast` on completion - only the idle-state chrome changed, not the behavior. Two non-obvious things any future change to this page should know: (1) `UNUserNotificationCenter.current()` throws an uncaught `NSException` ("bundleProxyForCurrentProcess is nil") when the running process has no real `Info.plist`/bundle identifier - true for the README's own documented `swift run`/bare `.build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` dev workflow - so `UpdatesController.notify` guards on `Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier != nil` first; the packaged app (a real bundle) is unaffected either way. (2) `no-mistakes --version`'s own output ("no-mistakes version v1.37.0 …") contains a decoy "v" inside the word "version" before the real "v1.37.0" token - parse whitespace-separated tokens shaped `v<digit>...`, not the first bare "v" in the string (caught live: the naive version crashed every check silently). no-mistakes has no `--check` flag; its outdated-version banner only prints on stderr from a real subcommand's `--help` (itself a safe no-op), which is what's parsed instead. herdr similarly has no "check without updating" flag - latest-available is read from Homebrew (herdr happens to be homebrew-core tapped here) while the actual Update action still runs herdr's own `herdr update`, never `brew upgrade herdr`. Firstmate's own row shells out to `bin/fm-sync-upstream.sh` (a firstmate-repo file, out of scope for this cockpit repo to commit - see the PR that introduced this page for its exact content) and gracefully reports `.notInstalled` if that script isn't present yet, rather than failing.
- **`CaptainIcon.swift` (cockpit-native-updates-polish) embeds a raster rail icon as a base64 PNG literal, not an `Assets.xcassets` catalog.** This project builds with `swift build` only (Swift Package Manager, CLT, no Xcode project) - `xcrun --find actool` (the tool that compiles asset catalogs) is absent from a CLT-only toolchain, confirmed on this machine, so `.xcassets` is not viable here. An SPM `resources:` bundle was also considered and rejected: `MetalTerminalRenderer.swift`'s own `candidateBundles()` comment documents that this *executable* target's generated `Bundle.module` accessor `fatalError`s (aborts the process, not throws) if it can't resolve the resource-bundle path, and this app's manual bundle assembly (`build_native_app.sh`, no py2app/Xcode step) isn't guaranteed to lay resources out where that accessor looks. A base64-embedded PNG has no bundle path to resolve at all - works identically under `swift run` and any assembled `.app`. Any future non-SF-Symbol rail/UI icon should follow the same pattern: extract/process the asset with `native/scripts/extract-captain-icon.py` as a worked example (luminance/local-contrast thresholding via Pillow), verify the output's alpha channel programmatically (nonzero-but-not-fully-opaque pixel count) before embedding, then wrap it in an `enum` exposing a `static let templateImage: NSImage?` with `isTemplate = true` so it tints via `contentTintColor` exactly like an SF Symbol.
- **The `.updates` rail destination's user-visible title is "Updates"** (cockpit-bootstrap-scaffold renamed it from "Firstmate Latest Updates" - the only `RailDestination` title that repeated the app's own name and ran three words). The bullet above still refers to it by its old task-history name; the page and rail icon itself say "Updates".
- **`.bootstrap` (cockpit-bootstrap-scaffold, phase 1 of a larger, captain-reviewed plan) is a rail destination pinned between Updates and Settings**, following the identical "pinned above Settings" convention `.updates` already established (`IconRailController.loadView`'s bottom-anchored chain; see the `RailDestination` enum's doc comment). `BootstrapController.swift` is scaffold-only as of this phase - a single "Firstmate home" card that lets the captain override where `FirstmateHome.resolve()` looks (`AppSettings.fmHome`, checked after `FM_HOME`/`FIRSTMATE_HOME` and before the hardcoded fallback candidates) via an editable path field, a `NSOpenPanel` "Browse…", and a "Save & Verify" that validates the candidate with `FirstmateHome.homeOk(at:)` before persisting - `FirstmateHome.root` is a `static let` resolved once at launch, so a save only takes effect after the explicit "Restart Manjesh Grand Line" action it offers, never live. Later phases (not yet built) add a software-install checklist and an Updates-page behavior split - see the task that introduced this scaffold for the full plan.
- **Phase 2 (cockpit-bootstrap-dotfiles) added the dotfiles/machine-config card and the global-agent-instructions verification section to `.bootstrap`.** The captain's real machine config is a Nix flake at `~/manjesh/dotfiles` (nix-darwin + home-manager + nix-homebrew), symlinked to the well-known `~/.dotfiles` by that repo's own `bootstrap.sh`/`rebuild.sh`. `DotfilesData.swift` is the model side - live `git`/file checks only, no hardcoded status - mirroring `UpdatesData.swift`'s `run`/`resolveExecutable` `Process` plumbing for git remote/branch/`status --short`, and parsing/rewriting the live `user = "..."` line out of `flake.nix` rather than a literal name in source. Every symlink check (managed items + the three `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` targets) fully resolves the chain with `(path as NSString).resolvingSymlinksInPath` (a property, not a method - `readlink -f` equivalent), not just the first hop: confirmed live that `home.nix`'s `mkOutOfStoreSymlink` targets go through an intermediate `/nix/store/.../home-manager-files/...` hop before landing on the real repo file. `~/.zshrc` is deliberately expected to show "not linked" even when everything is fine - its `home.nix` entry is `programs.zsh` (home-manager *generates* the file), not a `home.file` source symlink, so it resolves straight into the Nix store with no repo path in the chain at all.
