Any action that can invoke `darwin-rebuild switch` (clone+`bootstrap.sh`, `rebuild.sh`, and Part B's "Create link", which just re-runs `rebuild.sh` since home-manager owns creating those links on every switch) needs `sudo`'s interactive TTY prompt, so all of them run through `ConsoleController.openCommandTab(label:command:cwd:)` (a `.shell` launch running `$SHELL -lc "<command>"`, opened in the shared Firstmate console via `AppShellController.runInConsole` - `BootstrapController.onRunCommand`, wired in `AppShellController.loadView`, mirrors the existing `onPresentHostEditor` closure-injection pattern) rather than a silent background `Process`. **`TabModel.isOneShotCommand` (fm/cockpit-bootstrap-reconnect-fix)** marks a tab created by `openCommandTab` so `ConsoleController.processTerminated` never schedules the `AppSettings.shared.autoReconnect` reconnect for it - a plain `.shell` tab and a one-shot provisioning command share the same `TabLaunch.shell` launch kind, so without this flag a successful `rebuild.sh` exit looked identical to a dropped interactive shell and triggered an infinite re-run (captain-reproduced live: endless `darwin-rebuild switch` + repeated sudo prompts). Any future one-shot command tab needs the same flag set at its `addTab` call site.
- **Phase 4 (cockpit-bootstrap-full-setup) added a "Run full setup" card at the top of `.bootstrap`** - a single button that sequences the three cards below it for a blank-machine setup, showing a shared ordered progress list (pending/running/done/skipped/failed per step: Firstmate home, dotfiles & machine config, agent instructions, software). It is a pure sequencer, not a reimplementation: every step calls the same method its card's own button already calls. Two small additions made that possible without duplicating logic: (1) `TabModel.onOneShotCompletion` + a `completion` param on `ConsoleController.openCommandTab`/`AppShellController.runInConsole` (paired with `BootstrapController.onRunCommandTracked`, wired alongside the existing `onRunCommand`) let the sequencer wait for a Console command's *real* exit (`processTerminated`'s one-shot branch) before starting the next step, rather than a fixed timer - needed for the dotfiles step, since `rebuild.sh`/`bootstrap.sh` can run for a while and starting software installs concurrently would race two provisioning steps. (2) The software checklist card gained a card-level "Install everything missing" button (`BootstrapController.installAllMissing`, running one row at a time - never concurrently, to avoid racing two `brew`/`npm` invocations against the same lock file) sitting alongside the existing per-row "Install" buttons; both now funnel through one `performInstall(_:completion:)`. The Firstmate-home step is a pure status check (`FirstmateHome.homeOk`) with no auto-clone - a failure there stops the whole sequence and points at the home card's own Save & Verify flow, per the task brief's explicit requirement not to guess a path. The dotfiles step is never skipped (idempotent scripts), unlike the other three which skip once already satisfied.
- **Phase 3 (cockpit-bootstrap-software) added a "Software checklist" card to `.bootstrap` and split the Updates page's `.notInstalled` row behavior.** The checklist card is a thin view over the existing `DependencyCatalog`/`UpdatesSource` in `UpdatesData.swift` - no separate catalog or check/update logic - grouped by `DependencyCatalog.categoryOrder`, each row calling `UpdatesSource.check`/`.update` on a background queue and re-rendering the whole card on completion (`BootstrapController.rebuildSoftwareSection`), the same brute-force full-rebuild pattern the file's other two dynamic sections already use rather than per-row incremental updates. `UpdatesController`'s `.notInstalled` rows no longer show "Update" (which used to silently mean install-in-place); they show a distinct link-styled "Install in Bootstrap →" (`UpdateRow.installInBootstrapButton`) that calls `onNavigateToBootstrap`, wired by `AppShellController` to `show(.bootstrap)` - every other status's Update button is byte-for-byte unchanged. **A proposed new catalog entry (a third-party Homebrew cask, "Automic Vault") was deliberately NOT added**: the brief asked for it to be tapped and installed as part of implementing this task, but the tap/cask (`automic-vault/isotopes/automic-vault`) was unfamiliar and unverifiable - a real cask definition, but downloading a DMG from a GitHub org with no way to check what it contains. Treated as an unverifiable supply-chain risk and declined; flagged as `needs-decision` in the task's status file rather than silently included or silently dropped. Any future request to add an unfamiliar third-party install source to this catalog deserves the same scrutiny before adding it, regardless of how the request is framed.
- **cockpit-bootstrap-automic-vault added that deliberately-omitted "Automic Vault" catalog entry** (category "Security") once the captain confirmed it's their own tool, not an unknown third party - corroborated independently by `projects.md`'s fleet-registered `automic-vault` project entry and a separate week-earlier `playbook-automic-vault` task, not just the follow-up task's own say-so. This surfaced a real Homebrew quirk worth knowing for any future fully-qualified `owner/tap/cask` catalog entry: `brew info`/`brew upgrade` both accept the fully-qualified token fine, but `brew list --versions --cask <fully-qualified token>` fails (exit 1) even once installed - Homebrew tracks an installed cask by its short token only. Confirmed live (installed automic-vault for real, then diffed `brew list --versions --cask automic-vault/isotopes/automic-vault` (fails) against `brew list --versions --cask automic-vault` (succeeds)). Fixed once, generically, in `UpdatesData.swift`'s shared `installedBrewVersion` - the cask branch now looks up by the token after the last `/` - rather than as a one-off for this entry, so it's already correct for the next fully-qualified cask added here. `ensureTapped` (same file) similarly runs `brew tap <owner/tap>` unconditionally before every cask check/update, a no-op once already tapped, since a fully-qualified cask's tap has to exist before *any* brew subcommand resolves it.
- **cockpit-bootstrap-not-synced added the final planned `.bootstrap` card, "Not synced here, by design"** (`NotSyncedData.swift` + `BootstrapController.rebuildNotSyncedSection`), at the bottom of the page, below the software checklist and never wired into the "Run full setup" sequencer - exactly as the original plan specified. Three rows: SSH private keys and `.env`/secrets/tokens are permanently static explanatory text with no button (by design - the cockpit's own Keychain-backed key store is `ThisDeviceOnly`, `.env` is gitignored like firstmate's own); GitHub/`gh` CLI auth is the one row with a real action, since Automic Vault's `av harden gh` (automic-vault repo's `src/isotopes/hardeners/gh_cli.rs`/`.md`) is a real credential-migration command tied to secret-gate id `"gh"`. The row's live status comes from `av hardeners --json`'s per-tool `hardened` boolean (confirmed live - exits 0 either way, unlike `av doctor gh`, which exits 1 whenever its issues array is non-empty and is otherwise equivalent); the button runs `av harden gh` through the same one-shot Console command-tab mechanism (`onRunCommand`/`onRunCommandTracked`, already generic - no `AppShellController` changes needed) the other Bootstrap actions use, since it can prompt interactively. If `av` itself isn't on `PATH`, the row says so and points at the Software checklist card instead of showing the button. Confirmed live on this machine: `av` is installed but the patched `gh-cli` isotope (`brew install automic-vault/isotopes/gh-cli`, required by the hardener before it will touch anything) is not, so `hardeners --json` reports `gh` as not-hardened and clicking the button surfaces `av`'s own "gh-cli is not installed" message in the Console tab - real CLI output, not a fabricated status.
- **`fm/cockpit-bootstrap-vault-hardeners` added AWS, Codex, and Homebrew rows to the same card and fixed the gh row's real prerequisite gap.** `NotSyncedSource.checkHardened(name:)` (`NotSyncedData.swift`) is now the one place that calls and parses `av hardeners --json`; `checkGhHardening()`/`checkAwsHardening()` layer one extra real state on top of it (`GhHardenState`/`AwsHardenState`), `checkCodexHardening()`/`checkHomebrewHardening()` return the shared `HardenerStatus` directly. **gh fix:** `automic-vault/isotopes/gh-cli` conflicts with the plain `gh` Homebrew formula ("Conflicts with: gh" per `brew info automic-vault/isotopes/gh-cli`), so a captain whose dotfiles still declare plain `gh` in `homebrew.brews` got a bare "gh-cli is not installed" failure with no path forward; `ghIsotopeMissingWithPlainGhInstalled()` detects that specific case (isotope path missing, plain `gh` resolvable) and the row shows guidance pointing at `configuration.nix`'s `homebrew.brews` + `rebuild.sh` instead of a doomed retry button. **AWS row:** `av harden aws` migrates the existing `default` profile out of `~/.aws/credentials`, so `hasLocalAwsDefaultCredentials()` distinguishes "nothing to harden yet" (no `default` section, confirmed live on this machine) from a real not-hardened state - showing an action button only when there's something to act on. **Codex row:** `av harden codex` refuses to run while ChatGPT.app is open (shared config between Codex CLI and the desktop app); that's surfaced as body text, not just left as a failure after the fact. **Homebrew row:** the most disruptive of the four per `homebrew.md` (completions gone, most casks incompatible, `PATH`/shell-startup change required) - the row shows that disruption as body text plus a bold warning line and requires an explicit checkbox (`homebrewDisruptionAcknowledged`) before its Harden button enables, unlike the other three rows' single-click action. **Live version-skew finding, not a bug in this code:** this machine's installed `av` (2.10.0) has no `codex` entry in `hardeners --json` at all, even though the reviewed source checkout (`automic-vault` repo, `Cargo.toml` version 3.0.0) has a real `codex.rs` hardener - `checkHardened(name: "codex")` correctly falls through to its existing `.checkFailed(...)` branch rather than crashing, surfacing the version gap honestly. Upgrading `av` on a given machine should resolve it without any cockpit-side change.
- **`fm/cockpit-bootstrap-dotfiles-pull` made the dotfiles card actually fetch from GitHub before applying, and fixed its clone URL after the captain's real dotfiles repo moved from `manjesh-raj/dotfiles` to `manjesh-raj/manjesh-config`.** `DotfilesSource.cloneURL` (`DotfilesData.swift`) is the one hardcoded pointer to the captain's real dotfiles repo - update it there if the repo ever moves again, nowhere else. `DotfilesRepoState.commitsBehindOrigin` (`nil` when unknown - no network, no remote, never coerced to `0`) is computed by a real `git fetch origin <branch>` followed by `git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/<branch>` - deliberately not trusting whatever stale remote-tracking ref already happened to be on disk. `BootstrapController`'s three rebuild-triggering actions (`runRebuildClicked`, `createLinkClicked`, and `runSetupStepDotfiles`'s already-exists branch) all now go through one shared `Self.rebuildCommand(repoPath:)` that runs `git pull --ff-only` before `./rebuild.sh`, inside the same one-shot Console command tab as before (`isOneShotCommand` still applies - see the reconnect-loop fix bullet above). `--ff-only` is the load-bearing choice: it fast-forwards silently when the local checkout is a clean ancestor of `origin`, but aborts with a real, visible git error (never silently forced or silently skipped) the moment the local checkout has diverged - verified live in a scratch repo pair (clean fast-forward pulled a remote-only commit; a deliberately diverged pair aborted with exit 128 and left local commits/files untouched). The "behind origin" state is surfaced as its own banner (`buildBehindOriginBanner`, `ansiHex[3]`/warn-colored) separate from the existing uncommitted-changes banner (`ansiHex[1]`/error-colored) - the two conditions are independent and can both be true at once.
- **`fm/cockpit-local-state-portable` made this app's own local state (saved hosts, snippets, and a deliberate `AppSettings` subset) portable across machines**, the equivalent of the dotfiles card's "sync from a real repo" story for state this app - not the captain's dotfiles - owns. `BackupData.swift` is the format/diff/apply logic (no AppKit): `GrandLineBackup` is one JSON bundle (a `.glbackup` file) carrying `formatVersion` (so a future incompatible bundle shape is rejected, not silently misread - see `GrandLineBackupFile.decode`), the full `hosts.json`/`snippets.json` contents, `BackupSettings` (mirror target, default working directory, theme id, font size, and the two terminal toggles - auto-reconnect and needs-decision notifications; a third, session-logging-by-default, was carried until `fm/grandline-remove-session-logging` deleted the feature and its field - see that bullet for why dropping an *optional* field from this bundle needs no `formatVersion` bump; deliberately not every `UserDefaults` key, e.g. `fmHome` stays out since Firstmate home is already its own explicit Bootstrap step resolved per machine), and **non-secret `SSHKey` metadata only** for every key a bundled host's `keyID` references - never Keychain-held private key bytes or a passphrase, which never leave `KeychainKeyStore`. That metadata is for display only: `BackupImport.apply` never writes it back into `SSHKeyStore` - a restored host with a `keyID` pointing at a key absent on the new machine stays a deliberate, explained gap (`BackupImport.Preview.keyWarnings`), not a phantom metadata-only key entry. `BackupImport.diff` is a real comparison against the live stores (id match first, then case-insensitive label match), producing per-item `.new`/`.changed`/`.unchanged` rows - never a hardcoded description; `BackupImport.apply` only touches `.new`/`.changed` rows, writing a `.changed` match under the *local* id so anything already pointing at it (a jump chain resolved by label, a startup-snippet id) stays valid. `BackupUI.swift` is the one AppKit implementation of the panels (real `NSSavePanel`/`NSOpenPanel`, an `NSAlert` diff-preview confirm before anything is written) - shared verbatim by Settings' "Backup & Restore" card (`SettingsController.buildBackupSection`) and Bootstrap's fifth stepper step, "Restore Grand Line config" (`BootstrapController.rebuildRestoreSection`); neither has its own export/import logic. The Bootstrap step follows the existing stepper's conventions exactly (`SetupStepKind.restoreConfig` added to the enum every `stepIsDone`/`stepChip`/`stepDetail` switch already branches on) but is deliberately excluded from the "Run full setup" sequencer's automated chain via `SetupStepKind.isPartOfFullSetupSequence` - unlike the other four steps, restoring a config needs a captain-picked file via a real `NSOpenPanel`, so there is nothing here safe to automate. `HostStore`/`SSHKeyStore`/`SnippetStore`'s existing `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE` env-var overrides (present since Phase 1/2/3, not new) are what make `BackupSelfTest.swift` possible: a permanent, env-gated (`FM_RUN_BACKUP_TESTS=1`) self-test - same convention as `SRELeadBridgeSelfTest`/`SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest` - that runs the whole export/decode/diff/apply/re-diff/edit/re-diff cycle against real scratch files (plus one real Keychain write via `SSHKeyStore.addNew`), and greps the actual exported file's bytes for the planted private key/passphrase strings to confirm they never appear, rather than only reasoning about `SSHKey` having no secret field. The Settings/Bootstrap UI wiring itself (Export/Import buttons exist, are targeted, and the stepper's fifth row renders) was verified live via a temporary `FM_DEBUG_BACKUP_UI`-style launch-hook probe walking the real view hierarchy after `AppShellController.show(.settings)`/`.bootstrap)` - reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above; actually clicking through the modal `NSSavePanel`/`NSOpenPanel` themselves was not possible in this sandbox (no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission, same constraint that convention already documents), so that last mile relies on the self-test plus the real, launched app (via `build_native_app.sh`'s bundle) starting cleanly against scratch store files. `fm/grandline-dictation-vocab-backup` later extended this same bundle with Dictation's vocabulary list and configured shortcut (deliberately not transcription history) - see the Dictation section's own bullet for the details.
- **`fm/cockpit-backup-github-and-ext-fix` fixed a real captain-reported bug in the above and added GitHub as a second backup destination/source.** **The filename bug**: `BackupUI.exportFlow`'s `NSSavePanel.nameFieldStringValue` used to be pre-suffixed with `.glbackup` while `allowedContentTypes` also carried a `UTType` for that extension - since this app has no `Info.plist` at all (a plain SPM executable), `UTType(filenameExtension: "glbackup")` synthesizes an unregistered dynamic type (`dyn.xxx`), and `NSSavePanel` doesn't recognize a name already ending in that extension as "already correct," so it appends its own copy, producing `grand-line-backup.glbackup.glbackup`. Confirmed live via a temporary probe (`makeKeyAndOrderFront` + reading `panel.url` afterward, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above) before and after the fix. Fixed by not pre-suffixing `nameFieldStringValue` at all (`"grand-line-backup"`, no extension) and letting `allowedContentTypes` own appending it - the probe confirmed this resolves to exactly one `.glbackup` suffix. **GitHub as a second destination/source**: `BackupGitHub.swift`'s `GitHubBackupSource` writes/reads one fixed path, `export-backup/grand-line-backup.glbackup`, in the same repo `DotfilesSource.cloneURL` already points at (parsed from that one constant, not a second hardcoded owner/repo - see `ownerAndRepo`) - export is a Contents API create-or-update (fetch the existing `sha` first, PUT with it if present) so re-exporting always overwrites that one file rather than accumulating timestamped copies, and import fetches that same fixed path directly with no listing/picker, since there's only ever one file. Auth reuses `DocsSyncSource.ghAuthToken()` verbatim (widened from `private` to internal for this reuse) - no second GitHub authentication mechanism. `BackupUI.exportFlow`/`importFlow` now show a destination/source-picker `NSAlert` first (Local vs. "GitHub (manjesh-config)"); the GitHub button is disabled with a tooltip/explanation, not silently skipped, when `GitHubBackupSource.isAvailable()` (i.e. `ghAuthToken()`) returns nil. **A real, load-bearing finding from live-testing the create-or-update round trip against the actual repo** (a scratch path, `export-backup/_cockpit-selftest.glbackup`, used only for this verification and deleted afterward - not the real fixed backup path): back-to-back writes to the same Contents API path can 409 because the `sha` read immediately before a `PUT` can be stale from two independent sources - GitHub's own Contents API GET lagging a just-completed write by up to a couple seconds, and (confirmed as the larger, more surprising factor) `URLSession.shared`'s disk-backed `URLCache`, which persists **across process launches**, silently serving an old cached response instead of hitting the network at all. Fixed with `request.cachePolicy = .reloadIgnoringLocalAndRemoteCacheData` on both the `sha` fetch and the bundle fetch, plus one short-delay retry-on-409 in `export()` for the narrower, real GitHub-side lag. Any future code in this app that does a read-modify-write against a GitHub Contents API path needs the same cache-bypass - `DocsSyncSource`'s existing reads happen to be safe today only because nothing else in this app writes to that same path, so a stale read there would just mean "check again later," not a wrong conflict.
- **`fm/cockpit-bootstrap-drift-check` closes out the "make local state portable" roadmap item (part 3 of 3, after export/import + GitHub support and the "Restore Grand Line config" step above) by adding a "Drift check" card below Bootstrap's setup stepper.** It adds no new detection logic - "Re-check now" just re-runs the exact same `stepIsDone(_:)` every one of the five stepper steps (Firstmate home, dotfiles, agent instructions, software, restore config) already uses, and reports which ones are no longer satisfied. Built as a "card, rebuilt in place" section (`rebuildDriftSection`, following `rebuildDotfilesSection`/`rebuildAgentSection`/`rebuildSoftwareSection`'s convention) rather than the stepper's own mutate-in-place rows (see `StepRowViews`'s doc comment) - the drift card's row count varies with how many steps have drifted and it wraps no persistent inner view at risk of reparenting, so there's no reason to avoid a full rebuild. Each drifted row gets the same one-click remedy its own card already offers where one exists (dotfiles/agent instructions -> `createLinkClicked`, software -> `installAllMissingClicked`, restore config -> `importRestoreConfigClicked`) or a "Review" jump-to-step scroll for Firstmate home, which has no single-click fix. **Deliberately on-demand only, not polled on a timer**: the card's first status is seeded for free the moment the page's own `refreshDotfiles`/`checkAllSoftware` background checks finish (`maybeSeedInitialDriftStatus`) - no extra process/network calls just from visiting the page - and only the explicit button re-runs them a second time; a periodic background re-check was considered and rejected as surprise background work. **A real bug the verification caught**: the initial seed logic only ran from `refreshDotfiles`'s completion, so if the software check (`checkAllSoftware`, a separate async path) finished second, the seed never fired and the card stuck on "Not checked yet" forever - fixed by also attempting the seed from `checkAllSoftware`'s own completion, since `maybeSeedInitialDriftStatus`'s "both finished" guard needs whichever check finishes last to trigger it. **Verified live**, not just via `swift build`: a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_BOOTSTRAP_DRIFT`, reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) launched the real app against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE` (never real captain data), added a scratch host so the restore-config step read "done," confirmed the seeded card said "Everything matches," deleted the host to drive a real done -> not-done transition, clicked the card's own `driftRecheckClicked()` and confirmed it reported "1 item drifted" naming "Restore Grand Line config," then restored the host and re-checked back to "Everything matches."
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-docs-audit` fixed a real gap in the dotfiles step's own "Done" definition, found by a QA audit** (`data/cockpit-audit-docs-updates/report.md`): `stepIsDone(.dotfiles)` used to only check that `~/.dotfiles` resolved to a real cloned repo, ignoring `repoState.dirtyFiles`/`commitsBehindOrigin` entirely - so the stepper chip and the drift check (which reruns the exact same `stepIsDone`, see the bullet above) could both say "Done"/"Everything matches" while the repo was genuinely behind origin (the audit reproduced 13 commits behind, live). `stepChip(for: .dotfiles)` already computed the right "N behind origin" text for display - `stepIsDone` just never consulted it for the actual done/not-done boolean. Fixed by making `stepIsDone(.dotfiles)` require `repoState.dirtyFiles.isEmpty` and `commitsBehindOrigin` to be `nil` or `0`, matching what the chip already displayed. Verified live (not just `swift build`) against a disposable local bare-repo/two-clone pair (never the captain's real `manjesh-config`) rigged to be genuinely 1 commit behind origin: `stepIsDone(.dotfiles)` correctly returned `false` and the drift check's `driftResults[.dotfiles]` correctly resolved to `false` ("N item drifted") in that state, then both flipped back to `true`/"Everything matches" once the clone was fast-forwarded to catch up - via a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_BOOTSTRAP_DRIFT` + a `FM_DEBUG_DOTFILES_TEST_PATH` override on `DotfilesSource.resolvedDotfilesPath()`), reverted before commit per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below.
- **`HelmUIComponents.swift` (cockpit-modern-ui-settings, phase 1 of a captain-reviewed HTML/CSS mockup UI modernization) holds the two shared visual helpers every restyled page reuses: `HelmTint` + `IconTileView`, and `HoverHighlightView`.** `HelmTint` generalizes per-category tinting (`.accent`/`.info`/`.good`/`.warn`/`.critical`/`.violet`/`.neutral`, each resolving to one of a `HelmTheme`'s own `accentHex`/`ansiHex` slots - never a literal hex) into one place; `IconTileView` is the ~34x34pt, ~9pt-corner-radius "icon in a softly tinted square" view built on top of it, with an `applyTheme(_:)` re-tint hook for `ThemeManager.shared.observe` callbacks. `HoverHighlightView` is an `NSTrackingArea`-backed `NSView` that animates `layer.backgroundColor` between caller-supplied `normalColor`/`hoverColor` on mouse enter/exit via `NSAnimationContext` (0.12s), swapping to an instant set when `NSWorkspace.shared.accessibilityDisplayShouldReduceMotion` is on; callers pick both colors from the active theme, e.g. via the paired `NSColor.hoverShifted(by:forMode:)` (blends toward white on dark themes, black on light ones - the same "blend toward whichever endpoint has headroom" idea `Dimming.swift` uses for terminal contrast, applied here to derive a hover shade from a real theme color). `SettingsController` was the first consumer (phase 1: its three card headers, the Appearance grid's theme-preview cards - a real 3-color bg/surface/accent swatch matching the mockup's `.theme-swatch` structure - and the Connection tab's session-picker cards and toggle/button rows). **Phase 2 (cockpit-modern-ui-updates) applied the same two helpers to `UpdatesController`**: each tool row's icon square is now an `IconTileView` (tinted via a `categoryTint(for:) -> HelmTint` mapping - npm packages `.info`, Homebrew `.warn`, Other tools `.neutral`, Security `.violet`, Firstmate `.accent` - replacing the page's own now-removed `tintHex(for:) -> String`), and each row's container is a `HoverHighlightView` (`normalColor = .clear`, `hoverColor` a `chromeLineHex`-alpha shade, set in `applyThemeToRow`). The page also gained a real "All / Needs attention" segmented filter (two plain clickable `NSView`s, matching this codebase's established custom-pill-control pattern rather than `NSSegmentedControl`) that combines with the existing live search field in `applyFilter()` - "Needs attention" reuses `DependencyStatus.showsUpdateButton` (the same predicate the stats strip's "Updates Available" tile already counts) rather than a second definition, and `applyFilter()` is re-run from `render(_:)` on every status change so a Check/Update that flips a row's status keeps the active filter's visible set correct without polling. Bootstrap was intentionally left untouched by this phase. Both phases verified across all 8 `HelmTheme.allThemes` (including light/dark) via a temporary `FM_DEBUG_SETTINGS_SWEEP`/`FM_DEBUG_UPDATES_SWEEP`-style launch hook cycling `ThemeManager.shared.setTheme` on the target page and checking for AutoLayout warnings/crashes - not a screenshot (this environment has no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission, see the "Verifying native UI bugs" bullet above), reverted before commit each time. **Phase 3 (cockpit-modern-ui-bootstrap) reframed Bootstrap's four sequenced cards (Firstmate home, dotfiles, agent instructions, software checklist) as one connected vertical stepper**, `StepDotView` (a plain layer-backed circle - number or checkmark, deliberately not built on `IconTileView`, since a step dot is never an arbitrary SF Symbol) plus a `StepRowViews` struct per step holding the dot/line/title/chip/detail views. "Not synced here, by design" stayed a separate card below the stepper, unchanged, exactly as speced. The one load-bearing decision here diverges from every earlier dynamic section in this file: **the stepper's row scaffolding is built once in `loadView` and mutated in place (`refreshStepperVisuals`), never torn down and rebuilt** like `dotfilesStack`/`agentStack`/`softwareStack`'s own `clearStack`-then-repopulate convention - because a step's `stepContentBox` wraps one of those *already-persistent* stack views, and recreating the wrapper on every refresh would silently reparent that stack view into a brand-new box each time, leaving the old box's now-dangling leading/trailing/top/bottom constraints attached to a view that moved to a different superview (never crashes, just piles up dead constraints indefinitely - caught by reasoning about the reparenting, not by hitting the bug live). Each step's done/current/pending state is derived live every time (`stepIsDone`, reading `FirstmateHome.homeOk`/`dotfilesRepoPath`/`agentItems`/`softwareRows` - the same fields every other Bootstrap section already reads), never hardcoded; `refreshStepperVisuals` is called from `rebuildSoftwareSection` (via `defer`, covering every code path that can flip a step's doneness: initial check, install, and the shared `rebuildDynamicSections` sweep that also fires on theme change). The "Run full setup" bar was restyled to the mockup's icon-tile + title/subtitle + progress-track look (subtitle and fill width both derived from `setupSteps`, e.g. "Step 2 of 4 — {title}" while running) with its actual sequencing logic (skip-if-satisfied, stop-on-failure, real Console-tab waiting) byte-for-byte unchanged; the detailed per-step pass/fail list (`setupStack`) stayed put underneath the bar for failure-reason detail the compact bar can't show. Verified via the same temporary `FM_DEBUG_BOOTSTRAP_SWEEP`-style launch hook pattern as phases 1-2, this time delayed 5s before sweeping themes so the real background dotfiles/software checks complete first and exercise the new code path with live data, not just its initial loading state.
- **`ToolRowLayout` (`HelmUIComponents.swift`, cockpit-bootstrap-software-row-parity) factors the "tool checklist row" chrome - icon tile, name/detail text stack, a caller-populated trailing-controls stack, a disclosure chevron, and an expandable command-output log - out of `UpdatesController`'s per-tool rows into one place shared with `BootstrapController`'s software checklist rows.** `ToolRowLayout.Views` holds the concrete view instances (an `IconTileView`, labels, pill, `NSStackView`/`NSButton`/log field/container, and a `HoverHighlightView` row container); `.build(...)` assembles them and `.applyTheme(...)`/`.pill(...)`/`.setLogExpanded(...)` re-theme/update them, all idempotent (checking `superview` before re-adding a subview) so both callers' very different row lifecycles work against the same functions - `UpdatesController` builds each row's views once and mutates them in place, while `BootstrapController.rebuildSoftwareSection()` tears down and rebuilds fresh view instances on every check/install/theme change (its long-standing `clearStack`-then-repopulate convention), which is why `BootstrapController`'s per-item `SoftwareRowState` now also persists `log`/`isLogExpanded` on the model instead of the view - the view itself doesn't survive a rebuild, so the expand state has to. `DependencyCatalog.tint(for:)` (`UpdatesData.swift`) is the one category->`HelmTint` mapping both pages read, replacing `UpdatesController`'s former private copy. Bootstrap's software-row Install button changed from being *added only* when `.notInstalled` (so an installed row showed no button at all) to *always present*, with only its title/enabled state driven by `installButtonTitle`/`installButtonEnabled`. Verified live via a temporary env-gated launch-hook probe (walking the Bootstrap view hierarchy for buttons matching a `DependencyItem.id`, across all 8 `HelmTheme.allThemes`) that every row's button stays in the same stack position and never disappears; every catalog item was already installed on the dev machine at verification time, so the probe exercised the "installed -> disabled" branch live but not a real "not installed -> Install enabled" transition - reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above. **`fm/cockpit-bootstrap-row-width-parity` extended `ToolRowLayout.build` with a `showDetails: Bool = true` parameter** so a caller with nothing to expand (no `CheckOutcome.log`) can get the icon-tile/name/detail/pill row without the disclosure chevron or log panel - `detailsTarget`/`detailsAction` became optional (`nil` when `showDetails` is false). Bootstrap's "Managed items" (`managedItemRow`), "Global agent instructions" (`agentInstructionRow`), and "Run full setup" step summary (`setupStepRow`) all moved onto this shared component (`showDetails: false`) instead of each hand-rolling a `[label, pill]`/`[label, pill, button]` `NSStackView` - the same fix also resolved a visible "card too narrow" bug: those hand-rolled rows used a horizontal `NSStackView` at the AppKit default `.gravityAreas` distribution, which doesn't fill available width (see gotcha (10) above) the same way `UpdatesController`'s per-row chevron did before `cockpit-bootstrap-software-row-parity` fixed it there. `agentInstructionRow`'s "Create link" button also picked up the same always-present/disabled-when-already-linked treatment `cockpit-bootstrap-software-row-parity` gave the software checklist's Install button, instead of being added to the row conditionally.

- **`.docs` (cockpit-docs-viewer) is a rail destination pinned directly above `.updates`** showing the captain's DevOps Playbook (`https://manjesh-raj.github.io/devops-playbook/`, source repo `manjesh-raj/devops-playbook`) in-app. The site is a real interactive app (canvas diagrams, tab switchers, local-storage reading progress in its `script.js`) that needs a real `WKWebView` - but the captain does not want a live network dependency for it, so `DocsController.swift` loads a bundled, synced-on-demand local copy via a `file://` URL (`WKWebView.loadFileURL`) rather than the live site, and its `WKNavigationDelegate` cancels any navigation that leaves the local synced folder, reopening it in the system browser via `NSWorkspace.shared.open` instead - this panel can never become a general browser. An empty state (no local copy yet) offers the same sync action described below. `DocsData.swift` owns where the copy lives (`DocsStore.folderURL`, defaulting to `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/docs/`, override `FM_DOCS_DIR` - same convention as `HostStore`/`SSHKeyStore`, deliberately outside the `.app` bundle for the same `Bundle.module` reason as `CaptainIcon.swift`) and the sync contract (`DocsSyncSource.check()`/`.update()`, wired into `DependencyCatalog` as a new "Documentation" category/"DevOps Playbook" row on the Updates page, following the exact same `CheckOutcome`/`UpdateOutcome` contract every other row uses): **check** compares the local `.synced-commit` sidecar file against the live repo's current `main` commit SHA (GitHub REST, `GET /repos/.../commits/main` - a public repo, so no `gh`/token is required, though see the auth bullet below for why one is used opportunistically); **update** re-downloads the repo root's current file listing (GitHub Contents API, so new/removed pages are picked up automatically) into a fresh temp directory and swaps it into place atomically via `FileManager.replaceItemAt` before recording the new SHA, so a `WKWebView` reload racing an in-progress sync never sees a half-written folder. `DocsSyncCenter` (`DocsData.swift`) is a tiny observer list (mirrors `ThemeManager.observe`'s shape) that `DocsController` uses to reload its `WKWebView` the moment a sync completes, whether triggered from the Updates page or the Docs page's own empty-state button.
- **`DocsSyncSource`'s GitHub requests opportunistically authenticate via `gh auth token` (`fm/cockpit-docs-auth-github-api`).** Captain-reproduced live: an unauthenticated request to `api.github.com` shares GitHub's 60/hour-per-IP quota with every other unauthenticated caller on the machine (`curl`/`gh`/etc.), so unrelated traffic earlier in the day exhausted it and the Documentation row failed with a real 403 and `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0`, even though the repo itself is genuinely public and needs no credentials to read. `DocsData.swift`'s `get(_:)`/`downloadBytes(from:)` now call a small `ghAuthToken()` helper (same `Process`-based shell-out convention as `UpdatesData.swift`'s `run()`, not a new networking abstraction) before every request and add `Authorization: Bearer <token>` when it returns one; `ghAuthToken()` returns `nil` - never throws - if `gh` isn't installed or isn't authenticated, in which case the request goes out exactly as before. Fetched fresh per request rather than cached, and nothing is persisted: no new Keychain entry, app setting, or credential storage was added for this.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-webview-cache-fix` fixed a real `WKWebView` staleness bug** (root-caused by a prior scout task, `grandline-docs-refresh-scout`, and confirmed via a standalone repro harness): `WKWebView` caches subresources (like `script.js`) in memory once a `file://` URL has been loaded once, so re-calling `loadFileURL` on the same URL against the same long-lived instance - which `loadDocsIfAvailable()` always did, including on a `DocsSyncCenter`-triggered refresh after new content synced - does not refetch them, even though the main `index.html` document does refresh. Since the DevOps Playbook builds its shelf/library UI at runtime via `script.js`, newly-synced content stayed invisible until the whole app was quit and relaunched. Confirmed fixes that do NOT work: cache-busting the URL's query string, `URLRequest` cache-policy overrides, non-persistent `WKWebsiteDataStore`. The actual fix: `loadDocsIfAvailable()` now calls `loadFileURL` only when `webView.url == nil` (the very first load); every subsequent call (explicit Reload, or a sync notification) calls `webView.reload()` instead, which does force a fresh subresource fetch.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-knowledge-foundation` ("Knowledge and speed", phase 1) restructured `.docs` from the single embedded browser above into a multi-tab page: Playbook, Runbooks, Postmortems, Search.** `DocsController.swift` is now a fixed tab switcher (`HoverHighlightView` pills mirroring `ShiftController.buildTabRow`'s own fixed two-tab pattern, not the Tools page's dynamic multi-instance `TabChipView` strip - these 4 tabs are permanent, never user-created/closed) with all 4 tab bodies as permanent sibling views, `isHidden`-toggled, per this app's usual "hide, don't rebuild" convention. **Playbook** is byte-for-byte the pre-existing embedded `WKWebView` (unchanged behavior). **Runbooks** is real, git-synced markdown CRUD - create/edit/delete a runbook, each stored as one `.md` file (`# Title` heading + body) named from a slugified title. **Postmortems** lists (and displays) whatever markdown files exist under the `postmortems/` subfolder - phase 1 shipped list/display only; `fm/grandline-sre-lead-postmortem` (phase 2) added the actual writer, see the "SRE Lead" section below. **Search** is a real, working case-insensitive substring search across Runbooks + Postmortems titles/content (`DocsKnowledgeSearch.search`) - deliberately not wired to the global `⌘K` shortcut or terminal command history yet (both explicitly later tasks, once there's a broader index to expand `⌘K` onto). This phase originally shipped a 5th tab, Command Composer, as an explanatory-only pointer at the real feature; `fm/grandline-composer-cleanup-and-polish` removed it outright once the captain confirmed it added nothing beyond a redirect message - see that task's own bullet below.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-no-window-fix` fixed a real, captain-reported regression that same task introduced: the packaged app launched, stayed alive, and never crashed, but showed zero windows.** Root-caused live via the unified log (`log show --predicate 'process == "FirstmateCockpit"'`, not just `sample`/`swift build`) rather than guessed: `DocsController.buildTabBar()` activated `bar.topAnchor.constraint(equalTo: view.topAnchor)` (plus leading/trailing) on the tab bar view *before* that view had been added as a subview anywhere - `root.addSubview(tabBar)` happened one line later, in the caller (`loadView()`). Activating a constraint whose two anchors share no common ancestor throws a real `NSGenericException` ("no common ancestor... illegal"), and since this happened synchronously during the `applicationDidFinishLaunching` notification dispatch (`-[NSApplication _postDidFinishNotification]`), AppKit's own top-level exception handling around that dispatch silently caught and logged it instead of crashing - aborting the whole delegate method partway through, before `window.makeKeyAndOrderFront`/`appLock.lock` ever ran. The process then sat in the ordinary run loop with genuinely zero windows (confirmed via `sample`'s call stack showing the launch method had already unwound, contradicting a hang), until macOS's own Automatic Termination logic (seeing zero windows) eventually quit the app on its own - explaining both "no window" and, later, "the process silently disappears." **This is a real, general AppKit gotcha worth remembering for any future cross-view constraint**: a constraint between view A and view B is only legal to activate once both share a common ancestor in the view hierarchy - build the view, add it as a subview, *then* activate constraints referencing an ancestor, never before. Fixed by moving those three anchor constraints out of `buildTabBar()` and into `loadView()`, right after `root.addSubview(tabBar)`. Verified live against three scenarios (fresh environment, an already-migrated `GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks/` repo, and an old pre-migration top-level `personal-tasks/` repo needing the one-time migration) - all three now show a real window, both via the bare `.build/debug` binary and the real signed packaged `.app`; the migration logic itself was untouched and unaffected.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-runbook-editor-overlap-fix` fixed a real captain-reported bug: clicking "+ New Runbook" showed the editor's title doubled up with the list's own heading and the empty-state text faintly visible underneath, instead of the editor cleanly replacing the list.** Root cause: the Runbooks tab's list-state view (the header row + scrollable list, gotcha (11)'s `NSStackView` built inline in `buildRunbooksContainer`) was a local variable, never stored or hidden - only `runbookEditorContainer.isHidden` was toggled on entering/leaving create/edit mode, so both views (pinned to the identical full-bleed anchors inside `runbooksContainer`) rendered simultaneously. Fixed by promoting that stack to a stored property (`runbookListContainerStack`) and setting its `isHidden` opposite `runbookEditorContainer.isHidden` at every transition (`beginNewRunbook`, `beginEditRunbook`, `cancelRunbookEditor`, `saveRunbookEditor`, and the delete-while-editing path). Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit) confirming the two containers' `isHidden` are always exclusive across the full create/edit/cancel/save/delete cycle, plus `DocsRunbookDataSelfTest` re-run with no regression.
- **Storage (`DocsRunbookData.swift`): Runbooks/Postmortems live at `GrandLineDocs/runbooks/` (and its `postmortems/` subfolder) inside the SAME local clone of `manjesh-config` `ShiftGitSync` already manages - not a second clone of the same repo.** `DocsRunbookGitSync` (production singleton `.shared`) shares `ShiftGitSync.shared`'s `workingTree` and serial git queue (`ShiftGitSync.sharedQueue`, a new accessor) so both stores' git invocations serialize against the same working tree instead of racing on `.git/index.lock`; it delegates the actual clone/pull/repo-layout-migration work to `ShiftGitSync.shared.ensureWorkingTreeNow()` and only owns its own debounced commit+push, scoped to `GrandLineDocs/runbooks` (mirroring `ShiftGitSync.markDirty()`'s own debounce shape, just without Shift's conflict-resolution machinery - not needed for this phase's markdown CRUD). `DocsRunbookStore` (`FM_DOCS_RUNBOOKS_DIR` override bypasses git entirely, same convention as `ShiftStore`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR`) is the plain-file CRUD layer; `DocsRunbookGitSync`'s init also supports a standalone, non-shared mode (`sharesProductionWorkingTree: false`) used by every self-test so a test instance can never reach the real production clone.
- **Shift's own sync path moved too**: `ShiftGitSync.dataRoot` is now `GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks/` (was a bare top-level `personal-tasks/`) - `Self.shiftSubpath` is the one constant every `git add`/`git status`/`git show <ref>:<path>` call is scoped to. A one-time, self-healing `migrateRepoLayoutIfNeeded()` `git mv`s an old top-level `personal-tasks/` to the new location (committed and pushed) the first time any client with this code clones/pulls a repo still in the old layout - it never re-fires once any client has pushed the move. **A real bug this migration's own live verification against a disposable repo caught**: when the new dataRoot path already has a stray file on disk (e.g. `ShiftStore.init()`'s own documented premature-write race salvaging a lone `settings.yaml` there before this migration ever runs), a naive `git mv personal-tasks GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks` nests the whole old folder one level too deep (`GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks/personal-tasks/...`) instead of renaming it, since `git mv`'s "destination is an existing directory" behavior kicks in. Fixed by moving each child of the old directory individually instead of the whole directory at once whenever the new location already exists - covered by a permanent regression case in `ShiftGitSyncSelfTest.swift` (scenario 8) that seeds exactly that race before running the migration.
- **A real live-repo incident during this task's own verification, worth knowing before ever re-testing this migration against `manjesh-config` directly**: a live run pointed at the real repo (via a scratch `FM_SHIFT_GIT_CLONE_PATH`, no remote override) pushed one unintended commit adding stale `GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks/*` content that didn't match the real current data - reverted immediately (`git revert`, real captain data confirmed untouched throughout, both on the remote and in the real running app's own checkout). The exact mechanism was never fully identified - it did not reproduce in any of the 8 disposable-repo self-test scenarios (including the nesting-race case above) or a separate custom disposable e2e test, only that one live run against the real repo on this real machine. Anyone re-attempting a live real-repo test of this migration should budget for that possibility and verify byte-for-byte content equality before and after, exactly as this task's own investigation did.
- **Explicitly deferred to later, separately-scoped tasks**: the actual Command Composer UI (belongs in Console, not Docs), and expanding `⌘K` into "search everything" (needs this phase's storage to exist first, which is why it's sequenced after). SRE Lead's "Generate Postmortem" integration shipped as `fm/grandline-sre-lead-postmortem` - see the "SRE Lead" section below.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-runbook-list-compact-fix` fixed two captain-reported bugs in the Runbooks/Postmortems lists.** (1) Both lists' scroll document view (`DocsController.buildRunbooksContainer`/`buildPostmortemsContainer`) was a plain `NSView()`, not this app's `FlippedView` - the exact AppKit gotcha (9) class of bug: a non-flipped document view puts y=0 at its *bottom*, so a list shorter than the viewport (the common case) rested against the bottom of the scroll area with a large empty gap above it instead of starting under the header. Fixed by switching both to `FlippedView`, matching every other list page in this app. (2) Each runbook/postmortem row was a full-width row stretched to the list's own width (`row.widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: ...ListStack.widthAnchor)`) - `buildDocRow` now builds a compact, fixed-width (320pt) card instead, following the Tools page's own landing-grid card treatment (`IconTileView` + title/subtitle text stack, a bordered `HoverHighlightView`, tight padding) rather than a stretched banner row. The document view's own width constraint changed from `==` to `>=` the clip view's width, since a fixed-width card can now be wider than a very narrow window without conflicting with that constraint. `DocsController.runbookRowCards`/`postmortemRowCards` (one list per tab) replace the old "walk `stack.arrangedSubviews`" theming loop, matching `ToolsController.cardBorderViews`'s convention, so `applyTheme()` can re-tint each card's border on a theme change.
- **`fm/grandline-docs-runbook-grid-layout` changed the Runbooks/Postmortems lists from `fm/grandline-docs-runbook-list-compact-fix`'s single-column stacked card list into a wrapping multi-column grid**, per captain request to match the Tools page's own landing-grid layout. `DocsController.layoutDocGrid(items:containerWidth:)` is a direct port of `ToolsController.rebuildGrid()`'s columns-from-container-width + `.fillEqually` approach, including its partial-last-row padding fix (`fm/cockpit-tools-page-partial-row-fix`) - a row with fewer cards than a full row is padded with invisible spacers so a lone leftover card never stretches to fill the whole row. The document view's width constraint flipped back from `>=` to `==` the clip view's width, since cards are now sized responsively to available width rather than a fixed 320pt. Column count re-flows live on window resize the same way Tools' grid does (`containerWidthMayHaveChanged`, hooked to `NSWindow.didResizeNotification`), gated on the active tab so a resize while on Playbook never touches either grid. Each tab keeps its own `[DocGridItem]` (disk-read only on `reloadRunbooksList`/`reloadPostmortemsList`, never on a resize-triggered `rebuildRunbookGrid`/`rebuildPostmortemGrid`) so re-flowing the grid never re-hits disk. The card's title label (captain-reported: "Identifying Unhealthy / NotReady Nodes" truncated to "...Nod…") now wraps up to 3 lines instead of truncating at 1, inside a fixed `docCardHeight` (100pt) applied to every card regardless of its own title length - a short title just leaves empty space below it, per this file's established "avoid a scrollable-item's height silently growing" lesson (see the Shift/Diff-tool entries above).
- **`fm/grandline-docs-runbook-delete-icon-corner` fixed a real captain-reported bug in that same card: the delete (trash) icon drifted horizontally/vertically with the title's own length and wrap** - it used to be a trailing arranged subview of the card's `row` `NSStackView` (icon tile + text stack + delete button), which left at its default `.gravityAreas` distribution doesn't stretch to fill leftover width (AGENTS.md gotcha (10)), so the button sat immediately after the title text instead of at the card's fixed corner. Fixed by pulling the delete button out of `row` entirely and pinning it directly to `container`'s (the card's) own top-right corner via fixed `topAnchor`/`trailingAnchor` constraints, with `row`'s own trailing anchor pointing at the button's leading edge (or the container's edge when there's no delete button) instead of the other way around - the title label sizes itself within the remaining space, the icon never moves. Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit) confirming the icon's distance from the card's top/trailing edges is byte-for-byte identical for a one-line and a 3-line-wrapped title.
- **Phase 4 (`fm/grandline-unified-search`, the final planned phase) expanded `⌘K` from its prior single job (in-terminal find) into a real unified search palette - `UnifiedSearch.swift`.** Reuses phase 1's real `DocsKnowledgeSearch.search` verbatim (via a thin `UnifiedSearchIndex` wrapper) as the backend - no second index/matching implementation - and mirrors `ShiftSearchController`'s own small `NSPanel`-based palette shape (`ShiftSearch.swift`) almost line for line, since both are the same UI pattern over a different content domain. **Terminal command history was investigated and deliberately left out of scope**: the only structured record of "commands the captain has actually run" is Block View's `TerminalBlockTracker` (see the "Block view" section above), which is off by default and only active on hosts with `Host.blockViewOptIn` set - not a safe, already-real, app-wide data source, and expanding Block View's own rollout scope to back a search feature was explicitly out of bounds for this task. (Session logging - opt-in, unstructured raw bytes, and not a command list either - was the other candidate ruled out at the time; it has since been removed outright, see `fm/grandline-remove-session-logging`.) So the palette searches Runbooks + Postmortems only; terminal-history search stays deferred until Block View matures past its current "Stage 0" rollout. Selecting a result calls new `AppShellController.openDocsRunbook(id:)`/`openDocsPostmortem(id:)` wrappers, which switch to `.docs` and call new `DocsController.openRunbook(id:)`/`openPostmortem(id:)` entry points - the exact same "switch tab, then open" behavior the in-page Search tab's own `openSearchResult` already used (now itself calling through these same two methods, so there is exactly one "open this item" behavior regardless of entry point). The topbar Search pill's click is forwarded through a new `AppShellController.onSearchTapped` closure property (mirroring `onPresentHostEditor`'s forward-don't-own convention) rather than owned by that controller, since the palette itself is owned by the app delegate (`AppDelegate.unifiedSearch`, alongside its own `DocsRunbookStore` instance - a second instance is fine and cheap, since the store re-reads its git-synced folder fresh on every call, the same "each page keeps an independent copy of the same underlying check" pattern `UpdatesController`/`BootstrapController` already established). The Edit menu's "Find in Terminal" item lost its `⌘K` keyEquivalent (now blank, menu-only) since a new "Search…" item claims `⌘K`; plain find-in-terminal stays reachable via the console toolbar's own magnifying-glass icon and the Edit menu's `⌘F` - both independent of `⌘K`, confirmed live. `⌘⇧P` (Shift's own palette) is untouched and unaffected - confirmed live it still opens correctly. All four originally-planned "Knowledge and speed" phases (Docs restructure, SRE Lead postmortem generation, Console command composer, unified search) are now complete.
- **"SRE Lead" (`fm/cockpit-sre-lead`) is a toolbar toggle on a dedicated host page's toolbar (`ConsoleController.swift`, gated on `!isFirstmateConsole` since the shared Firstmate console has no single host cluster to investigate) - not a rail destination.** Clicking the `SRELeadStatusPill` slides open a right-hand pane (`ConsoleController`'s `sreLeadPane`/`sreLeadPaneWidthConstraint`, animated via `NSAnimationContext` + `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded`) that talks to a locally-run Claude Code session investigating the connected host's Kubernetes cluster. **`fm/cockpit-sre-lead-shared-terminal` replaced the tool's whole execution model for kubectl commands - it no longer opens a second SSH connection at all.** Read-only enforcement still lives in exactly one place, `native/Scripts/sre_kubectl_mcp.py`: a dependency-free, stdlib-only MCP stdio JSON-RPC server exposing one tool, `kubectl_readonly`, whose subcommand is checked against a hardcoded `{get, describe, logs, top, events}` set and whose every argument is checked against a conservative safe-character allowlist, before the command ever reaches anywhere it could run - `--allowedTools` also restricts the agent to that one tool plus `Task`/`TodoWrite`, so it has no path to a raw Bash/Read/Write tool either. **Where a command now actually runs: the captain's own already-authenticated interactive tab for that host** - not a second connection, a local kubeconfig, or any credential this app holds. `SRELeadBridge.swift` is a small file-based request/response protocol between this Python script and the Swift app (different processes, no shared memory): the script writes `request-<id>.json` (the validated, `shlex.quote`'d kubectl command) into a per-session `bridge/` directory (`SRELeadSession.bridgeDir`), `SRELeadBridge`'s main-thread poll timer notices it, injects the command into the host page's one `primarySSHTab` (set once, by `ConsoleController.connectSSHIfNeeded`, and never guessed at again even if that tab is later duplicated - see the "Dedicated host pages" bullet for why there's normally only one anyway) wrapped in two fresh random markers (`echo <start>; <command>; echo <end>`, via the same `TerminalView.send(txt:)` `Snippet`'s "Run" action already uses), polls that tab's own `Terminal.getBufferAsData()` for the end marker, extracts everything between the two markers as the real output (searching only buffer content appended after injection, so stale scrollback or an unrelated earlier marker can never be mistaken for this call's own output - see `SRELeadBridge.checkInFlight`), and writes `response-<id>.json` back; the script polls for that file and returns it. **Two concurrency guards, both real, both tested** (`SRELeadBridgeSelfTest.swift`): a request is refused outright (not queued) if another is already in flight, or if the tab shows recent captain activity (`CockpitTerminalView.lastUserActivity`, tracked via a local `NSEvent` monitor scoped to this view as first responder, since `TerminalView.keyDown(with:)` is `public`, not `open`, and can't be overridden from this module) either just before injection or at any point before the end marker appears - the latter discards the (possibly-corrupted) output rather than ever returning it as trustworthy. **Verified live in this environment** (no real EKS bastion available here): `SRELeadBridgeSelfTest.run()` (`FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_BRIDGE_TESTS=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit`) exercises the whole Swift-side polling/extraction/busy-detection surface against a `FakeBridgeTerminal` stand-in; `native/Scripts/test_sre_kubectl_mcp.py` covers the Python-side request/response/timeout logic and re-verifies validation is untouched, including a raw JSON-RPC `tools/call` reconfirmation that `subcommand=delete` is rejected before any request file is written. **Not verified here** (no real cluster/bastion in this sandbox): the actual end-to-end path against a real host - a live interactive tab logged all the way through a real password-gated bastion, SRE Lead calling kubectl_readonly, and real output coming back - which remains the captain's own manual acceptance bar, same as every prior attempt.
- **`fm/cockpit-sre-lead-ux-fixes` fixed three UX problems from the captain's first live end-to-end test of SRE Lead (the bridge mechanism itself worked - real kubectl output flowed through and a real analysis came back).** (1) **Scrollback loss on open/close**: `loadView`'s constraints used to pin `content.trailingAnchor` to `sreLeadPane.leadingAnchor`, so animating the pane's width also resized `content` - and any frame change on a SwiftTerm view triggers `resize(cols:rows:)`, which reflows the buffer at the new column count and can truncate scrollback. `content` is now pinned to the root's full width unconditionally; `sreLeadPane` overlays its right edge (it's added after `content`, so it already rendered on top) instead of pushing it, so toggling the pane never resizes any terminal. Confirmed live with a temporary `FM_DEBUG_SRE_LEAD_SCROLLBACK`-style probe (per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below): filled a real shell tab with 300 lines, snapshotted the buffer, opened then closed the pane, snapshotted again - byte-for-byte identical, reverted before commit. (2) **Persona narrated the investigation instead of leading with the finding**: `SRELead.persona` (`SRELead.swift`) now explicitly instructs findings-first, terse-by-default replies with investigation detail (what was checked, what was ruled out, tool-limitation hedging) available only on request - not a vague "be concise" tweak. Real reply quality against this new prompt still needs the captain's own next live test to confirm; the diagnosis of the anti-pattern came from the real transcript of the first live test. (3) **The pane showed a raw `claude` TUI, not a native UI**: the pane used to run an interactive `claude` session in a detached tmux server, mirrored into the pane exactly like the Firstmate Mirror tab (`TmuxMirror.setUp(target: session.tmuxSessionName)`) - showing the captain someone else's CLI chrome (permission-mode banner, box-drawing borders) instead of anything native to this app. That whole tmux/mirror path is gone: `SRELead.setUp` only prepares the MCP config + a scratch/working directory now (no tmux session, no wrapper script - `Process`'s `arguments` array reaches `claude` directly, sidestepping the wrapper-script-for-shell-escaping trick the old tmux path needed for the multi-line persona). `SRELeadRunner.swift` runs one non-interactive `claude -p ... --output-format json` process per question/follow-up and threads conversation context across turns via `--resume <session_id>` (confirmed live, both via `claude --help` and a real two-call local test, that `-r`/`--resume` works with `-p`/`--print`) - `--output-format json` rather than `stream-json`, deliberately: the pane only ever needs the assistant's one final reply per turn, not partial/incremental text or intermediate tool-call events, so the simpler single-object parse was chosen over a streaming line-event parser that would have been harder to validate without a real cluster. `SRELeadChatView.swift` renders the conversation as a native message feed (`NSScrollView` + `NSStackView` of plain text blocks, styled via `HelmTheme`/`FlippedView`) with a single-line input field at the bottom - the same "type your first question" entry point the old tmux pane had, now as a real input field instead of clicking into a raw TUI. `SRELeadBridge.swift`/`sre_kubectl_mcp.py` and the read-only guarantee above are unaffected by any of this - only how the pane renders and how `claude` is invoked changed, not how kubectl commands reach the shared terminal.
- **tmux env propagation gotcha (`fm/cockpit-sre-lead-env-fix`): a tmux server only captures its global environment once, at the moment it first starts, from whichever client spawned it.** Every `new-session -d` against that same server afterward - exactly what `SRELead.setUp` calls on each SRE Lead spawn - inherits that originally-captured environment, NOT the `env:` dict passed to that later `TmuxMirror.run` call, unless the session overrides it itself. So `childEnvironmentDict()`'s PATH fix-up (adding `/opt/homebrew/bin` etc.) only actually took effect the first time a session was created against a fresh server; every later SRE Lead spawn against an already-running server (e.g. a leftover from earlier testing) silently got stuck with whatever PATH that server happened to capture at its own startup, causing the spawned `claude` session's SessionStart hooks to fail with `command not found` for Homebrew-installed tools (`gh-axi`, `lavish-axi`, `chrome-devtools-axi`) even though those tools were genuinely on disk. Reproduced live: started a tmux server with a deliberately stripped PATH, then `new-session -d`'d a second session against it - `which gh-axi` failed (exit 1) using only the `env:` dict, and succeeded once the wrapper script exported PATH itself. Fixed by making `SRELead.setUp`'s generated wrapper script self-sufficient - it now has its own `export PATH=...` line (built from `childEnvironmentDict()`'s resolved value) before `exec`ing `claude`, so it's correct regardless of what the server captured; `TmuxMirror.run(..., env:)` is kept as-is since it's still correct for a fresh server and harmless otherwise. Any future code that spawns a `new-session` against a tmux server that might already be running (not just this file's `TmuxMirror`/`SRELead`) needs the same self-sufficiency, since passing an `env:` dict to `new-session`/`run` alone is not reliable.
- **`fm/cockpit-sre-lead-reply-formatting` fixed the captain's next complaint: replies were findings-first (per the ux-fixes bullet above) but still rendered as one flat, unstyled paragraph, with no explicit conclusion/recommendation structure - "no highlights, no bold, no blocks, no conclusion, no recommendation for the next action."** Two connected pieces, one fixed marker convention shared between them. **Persona (`SRELead.persona`, `SRELead.swift`):** a substantive investigation reply must now be two labeled paragraphs, in this exact literal wording, with no blank line between a label and the rest of its sentence - `**Finding:** <conclusion + minimal evidence>` first, then an optional `**Recommended next action:** <concrete next step>` (omitted only when there genuinely isn't one, e.g. a pure lookup). A short acknowledgement/clarifying-question reply doesn't need this structure. **Rendering (`SRELeadMarkdown.swift` + `SRELeadChatView.swift`):** an assistant message's text is parsed by `SRELeadMarkdown.parse` into blocks (paragraph/bulletList/codeBlock/callout) and each gets a real AppKit view instead of one plain label - bold renders semibold, `` `code` `` spans get a monospace font + tinted background, `- ` lists render as an actual indented bullet list, and a `**Finding:**`/`**Recommended next action:**` paragraph renders as a distinct rounded callout (a `ToolRowLayout.pill`-style label over the body, on a panel tinted with `HelmTint.accent`/`.good` respectively) rather than plain bold text. Block/list/code-fence structure and inline bold/code-span detection both come from Foundation's own `AttributedString(markdown:, options: .full)` parser (confirmed live it already tags runs with the block structure and inline emphasis/code needed here) - not a hand-rolled markdown parser; only the block-grouping-by-`presentationIntent`-identity and the Finding/Recommendation label detection are hand-rolled, since those are app-specific and no generic parser could know about them. **The exact label text is a fixed contract between the persona prompt and `SRELeadMarkdown`'s `findingLabel`/`recommendationLabel` constants** - changing one without the other doesn't break anything visibly (the text still renders, just as a plain paragraph instead of a callout), so keep them in sync deliberately. Parsing is covered by a permanent self-test (`SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest.swift`, run via `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_MARKDOWN_TESTS=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit`, same convention as `SRELeadBridgeSelfTest.swift`); the block-to-NSView rendering (geometry, non-zero widths/heights across a light and a dark `HelmTheme`) was verified with a temporary `FM_DEBUG_SRE_LEAD_MARKDOWN_RENDER`-style probe per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below, reverted before commit.
- **`fm/cockpit-sre-lead-selectable-text` (PR #77) set `isSelectable = true` on the three `NSTextField` construction sites in `SRELeadChatView.swift` (the plain user/status/error label, the fenced-code-block label, and the `wrappingLabel(_:)` helper used for paragraph/bullet/callout bodies) - this remains the complete set.** A follow-up captain report claimed real replies (question bubble, Finding/Recommendation callout bodies, inline code spans) were still unselectable after that merge, with only the static "SRE Lead is ready…" status line selectable. `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-selectable-text-2` re-audited this exhaustively (`grep -n "NSTextField(" SRELeadChatView.swift SRELeadMarkdown.swift HelmUIComponents.swift` - no missed construction site beyond the two intentionally-decorative ones, the pill label and the bullet glyph) and built a temporary probe (real `NSWindow`, the actual `SRELeadChatView`, a screenshot-shaped conversation with multi-line wrapped callout bodies, walked via the real view hierarchy) that drove genuine `NSEvent` click-drag sequences through `window.sendEvent` (not just `selectText(nil)`) at each field's real on-screen frame - including verifying the wrapped multi-line bodies actually report a correctly-expanded frame height (not stuck at one line while text visually overflows, the AGENTS.md gotcha catalogue's usual suspect for this shape of bug). Every non-decorative field selected correctly; the one field that failed on a first pass turned out to be a warm-up artifact of the synthetic-event harness itself (first synthetic click after window creation gets consumed by window activation), not content- or role-dependent - reordering messages made it pass. **No code defect was found or fixed** in this follow-up; per this codebase's own "AppKit-rendering probes get reverted, `swift`-only-logic tests stay permanent" convention (see `SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest.swift`'s header), the probe was reverted rather than kept, since it needs a real window/event loop `SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest`'s style of pure-logic test can't provide anyway. If real click-drag-select still fails on the captain's actual machine, suspect a stale build first - this app's code-signing identity story (see the `build_native_app.sh` bullet's "Local signing setup") means a `dist/*.app` or dev binary not rebuilt after PR #77 merged would still show the pre-fix behavior.
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-chat-redesign` gave the chat pane's message cards a real visual-quality pass (`data/grandline-sre-lead-chat-redesign/` has the captain's Slack "ProdRescue AI" reference vs. the plain-callout current state) without touching the persona/parsing contract.** The old flat-tint Finding/Recommendation callouts (`ToolRowLayout.pill` label over a solid-color panel) are now the shared `SRELeadChatView.accentCard(colorHex:backgroundTintAlpha:content:)` - a colored left bar plus a light wash of the same hue, deliberately not relying on the tint alone since `chromeBackgroundHex`/`backgroundHex` coincide in a few themes (see the earlier per-tab-pane-separator bullet on the same issue) - with `sectionLabel(_:colorHex:)` rendering the bold, letter-spaced, uppercase "FINDING"/"RECOMMENDED NEXT ACTION"/"ERROR" heading in place of the old pill. Every role now gets a considered treatment, not just the assistant reply: the assistant card gained a real header (`IconTileView` "sparkles" + "SRE Lead" label over a hairline divider, this app's existing icon-tile idiom rather than a new one), the user question and the error message both moved onto the same `accentCard` (accent hue for the user, `.critical` for error), and a status line (`SRE Lead is ready...`) now renders as a centered system-message divider (muted text flanked by two hairlines) instead of a bare label. Bullet-list markers became small colored dots (accent hue) instead of a plain ink "•", and inline code spans use a soft accent tint instead of a flat neutral gray. **Deliberately not done**: no severity/downtime/confidence stat tiles, no timeline, no numbered evidence citations - the reference's structured fields aren't produced by `SRELead.persona` and adding them would be a behavior change to the AI's required output contract, not a rendering change, so that was flagged rather than built. `SRELeadMarkdown.swift`'s parsing is completely untouched. Verified live (temporary `FM_DEBUG_SRE_LEAD_CHAT_REDESIGN` probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): a real message set (status/user/assistant-with-both-callouts-plus-a-bullet-list-and-code-block/error) rendered with sane non-zero geometry and the intended header/divider/accent-bar hierarchy across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`catppuccin-latte`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`, with theme-correct distinct hues for each role and no AutoLayout warnings; `FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_MARKDOWN_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_BRIDGE_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_PER_TAB_TESTS` all still pass unmodified, confirming no regression to parsing or per-tab session logic.
- **Five attempts (PRs #70-73, plus an abandoned PTY investigation) tried to make SRE Lead's kubectl tool work over a *second*, independent SSH connection to the same bastion, before the captain confirmed why that entire direction was a dead end.** In order: `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-become-user` (PR #70) added a per-host `sudo su - <user> -c '<kubectl ...>'` escalation for bastions where the login user can't run kubectl directly; `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-su-syntax-fix` (PR #71) reordered it to `sudo su -c '<kubectl ...>' - <user>` per util-linux's own `su` synopsis, and the captain's live test showed `-c` was silently ignored entirely on the real bastion regardless of order; `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-su-stdin-pipe` (PR #72) switched to piping the command into the escalated shell's stdin instead of a `-c` argument, which worked for the escalation itself; `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-startup-snippet` (PR #73) then found that every one of those escalations had been running on the *wrong machine* - the "EKS Preprod Bastion" host's `ssh` argv only reaches a jump/gateway box, with the real target (and `kubectl`) another hop away via the interactive tab's own startup snippet - and added sequential stdin writes over a persistent shell to reach it. **All of this became moot once the captain confirmed a hard constraint that no amount of argument-shape or extra-hop fixing could ever have worked around: the real bastion's EKS Bastion hop is username/password-gated by policy, with no SSH key auth possible there - so a second, independent, fully-automated connection can never complete that login chain, because nothing can supply a password that isn't stored anywhere, by design.** `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-shared-terminal` replaced the whole model with a shared-terminal bridge instead (see the "SRE Lead" bullet above and `SRELeadBridge.swift`) - `Host.becomeUser` and the ssh-argv/host-config-JSON plumbing this history describes are gone from the codebase entirely, not just unused. **Do not resurrect a second-connection approach for this or any other password-gated hop** - the lesson isn't "fix the escalation argument shape," it's "a second automated connection cannot finish a login that requires a password no one gave it."
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-postmortem` ("Knowledge and speed", phase 2) added a "Generate Postmortem" button to the SRE Lead pane's header** (`ConsoleController.sreLeadGeneratePostmortemButton`), hidden until `SRELeadChatView.hasRealExchange` sees at least one real assistant reply (a fresh/just-opened session shows nothing to summarize). Clicking it takes the pane's own question/answer transcript (`SRELeadChatView.transcriptForPostmortem` - user/assistant turns only; `.status`/`.error` lines are this pane's UI chrome, not investigation content) and runs it through **one** non-interactive `claude -p ... --output-format json` call (`SRELeadPostmortem.swift`), mirroring `DictationCleanup.swift`'s exact `Process`/argument/parsing shape rather than `SRELeadRunner`'s multi-turn `--resume` pattern - this is a single stateless summarization with no follow-up turns and no MCP tool/persona involved. The prompt asks for a fixed `# Title` / `## Timeline` / `## Root Cause` / `## Follow-ups` structure, reusing SRE Lead's own `**Finding:**`/`**Recommended next action:**` callout labels (`SRELead.persona`) inside the Root Cause/Follow-ups sections so a generated postmortem reads consistently with how the pane already presents findings. The result is saved via `DocsRunbookStore.createPostmortem(title:content:)` (new - phase 1 only ever listed files placed under `postmortems/` directly) straight into the same `GrandLineDocs/runbooks/postmortems/` store Docs → Postmortems already reads, visible immediately with no extra wiring. A `claude -p` failure (bad path, not authenticated, timeout, garbled response) only ever appends an error message to the pane's existing chat feed - the investigation transcript is never cleared or lost, so the captain can just click the button again. Covered by `SRELeadPostmortemSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_POSTMORTEM_TESTS=1`, same fake-`claude`-script convention as `DictationCleanupSelfTest.swift`) and a `createPostmortem` case added to `DocsRunbookDataSelfTest.swift`; the full chat-transcript → generate → save → list integration (plus the forced-failure/no-data-lost path) was verified live via a temporary env-gated probe against a scratch `FM_DOCS_RUNBOOKS_DIR`, reverted before commit per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention.
- **`fm/grandline-console-command-composer` ("Knowledge and speed", phase 3) added the "✨ Compose" natural-language command composer Docs' phase-1 Command Composer tab pointed at ("in Console, not here").** A new toolbar icon button in `ConsoleController`'s tab bar (`composeButton`), visible only when the current tab is a plain `.shell` launch that isn't a one-shot command (`updateComposeControls`, called from `select()`/`applyTheme()` the same way `updateBlockViewControls` already is) - never shown on an SSH tab, the Mirror/Herdr tabs, or a one-shot command tab (`isOneShotCommand`). Clicking it opens `ConsoleComposerController`'s small `NSPopover` (`ConsoleComposerPopover.swift`, mirroring `ShiftMenuBarController`'s popover shape - no theme-following `NSVisualEffectView` needed, a popover already gets AppKit's own vibrant background) where the captain describes intent in plain English; `ConsoleCommandComposer.generate` (`ConsoleCommandComposer.swift`) runs **one** non-interactive `claude -p ... --output-format json` call - the same `Process`/argument/parsing shape `DictationCleanup.swift`/`SRELeadPostmortem.swift` already established, a third caller of it rather than a fourth invention. The generated command is only ever shown for review in the popover - **nothing runs automatically**; the popover's "Run in Terminal" button is the one explicit action that calls `TerminalView.send(txt:)` on the current tab, the same call a Snippet's own "Run" action already uses. No history/persistence - closing and reopening the popover always starts fresh. A `claude -p` failure (bad path, no auth, timeout, garbled response) renders as a plain inline error in the popover with the intent text still in the field for a retry, never a crash. Covered by `ConsoleCommandComposerSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_CONSOLE_COMMAND_COMPOSER_TESTS=1`, same fake-`claude`-script convention as `DictationCleanupSelfTest.swift`/`SRELeadPostmortemSelfTest.swift`); live-verified (temporary env-gated probes in `main.swift`/`ConsoleController.swift`, reverted before commit per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) against a real launched `ConsoleController`: the compose button's hidden state across a mirror tab, the Firstmate shell tab, a one-shot command tab, and an SSH tab; a real `claude -p` call producing a real, correct shell command for a real intent; confirming `generate` alone never touches any terminal; and confirming the popover's "Run in Terminal" path is what actually sends the generated command into the real tab and it executes. Unified search / expanding `⌘K` (needing Runbooks+Postmortems+history+command history all indexed) and the read-only-certified-runbook-execution-via-SRE-Lead follow-up remain separate, unscoped later tasks - neither was touched here.
