- **`fm/grandline-composer-cleanup-and-polish` did two captain-approved follow-ups once the composer above had shipped for a while.** (1) Removed Docs' now-redundant "Command Composer" tab entirely (code deleted, not hidden - see the bullet above) since it never had content of its own beyond a "the real feature is in Console" redirect; Docs is back to 4 tabs (Playbook/Runbooks/Postmortems/Search), and no replacement pointer was added anywhere else - the real feature's own "✨ Compose" toolbar button is self-explanatory. (2) Gave `ConsoleComposerPopover.swift`'s content view (previously a bare label/field/button) real visual polish, matching this app's established conventions rather than inventing new ones: a tinted `IconTileView` (`.violet`) next to the title, the same "AI feature" treatment `DictationController.buildCleanupSection`'s "Clean up my sentences" card already established; the generated command now renders in a real monospace code-block (a bordered, corner-radius `NSScrollView`/`NSTextView` themed via `HelmTheme`, mirroring `ToolInstance.codeEditor`'s Tools-page styling) instead of a plain `NSTextField`; Copy/Run buttons keep this app's `bezelStyle = .rounded, controlSize = .small` convention (already matched Vault's "Run injected…"/"Copy Name" row buttons, so no change needed there); and a `⌘⏎` shortcut hint was added next to the intent field. `ConsoleCommandComposer.swift`'s `claude -p` invocation/parsing logic was untouched - UI polish only. Live-verified end to end via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): a real Generate click produced a real `claude -p` command with correct non-zero geometry for the icon tile/code block, Copy placed the exact command on the pasteboard, Run fired `onRunInTerminal` only after the explicit click (never before), and an empty-intent click left the UI unchanged with no crash - no regression to the composer's existing safety behavior (nothing auto-runs, `.shell`-tab-only gating in `ConsoleController`, inline error handling) or to any other Docs/Console functionality.
- **`fm/grandline-composer-theme-and-width` fixed two real captain-reported bugs in the popover the two bullets above built and polished.** (1) The popover rendered in plain, unthemed dark gray instead of the app's actually-active Helm theme - the prior bullet's "no theme-following `NSVisualEffectView` needed, a popover already gets AppKit's own vibrant background" reasoning was the bug: the content view had no explicit background fill at all and no `ThemeManager.shared.observe` registration, so it fell back to system vibrancy following the OS's own light/dark setting rather than this app's in-app theme - the same class of bug `grandline-unified-search-fixes` fixed concurrently for the `⌘K` palette. Fixed with the standard pattern (`ThemeManager.swift`'s own checklist): a live theme observer owned by `ConsoleComposerController` (unobserved from `ConsoleController.shutdown()`, mirroring that controller's own token), a `wantsLayer`-backed root view with an explicit `HelmTheme`-derived `backgroundColor`, and `popover.appearance` forced to the theme's light/dark mode. (2) The popover was fixed at 380pt wide regardless of command length, since `root`'s width was never tied to a constraint - only its initial `loadView` frame size. `ConsoleComposerViewController.rootWidthConstraint` now grows to fit the generated command's longest line (measured against the code block's own font), floored at 380pt and capped at 640pt, with the resulting fitting size handed to `popover.contentSize` explicitly. Live-verified across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light` (distinct real per-theme colors + matching popover appearance) and a short/medium/long/extreme-length command sweep (proportional growth between the floor and cap, not a binary min/max) via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` + temporary debug methods on `ConsoleComposerController`/`ConsoleComposerViewController`, reverted before commit per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention; no regression to `ConsoleCommandComposerSelfTest.swift` or the composer's safety gating/Copy/Run/tab-kind restriction.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-side-by-side-composer-height` gave the intent field real multi-line height** (captain: "increase the vertical view... so the user can see the text more and have a better visibility") - the single-line `NSTextField` that scrolled its own content sideways as you typed is gone; `ConsoleComposerViewController.intentTextView`/`intentScroll` (a bordered, corner-radius `NSScrollView`/`NSTextView` pair, fixed at 72pt tall, mirroring the code block's own `codeScroll`/`codeTextView` styling rather than a new look) replaced it. `NSTextView` has no built-in placeholder API, so `intentPlaceholderLabel` is a plain muted label overlaid at the text container's inset, toggled via `NSTextViewDelegate.textDidChange(_:)`. `⌘⏎` is unaffected - it was always `generateButton`'s own `keyEquivalent`, which `NSWindow.performKeyEquivalent:` reaches regardless of first responder, not something the old field's action drove. A plain Return now inserts a newline instead of submitting (the expected behavior once the field is genuinely multi-line). Everything else (tinted icon tile, code-block result view, Copy/Run, the width-grows-with-result-length behavior above) is untouched. Verified live via a temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe (reverted before commit): `intentScroll` measured 72pt tall with no horizontal scroller, a long wrapped intent's used-text width fit inside the view's bounds (no horizontal overflow), and a synthetic `⌘⏎` `NSEvent` sent to the window while the text view was first responder still flipped `generateButton.isEnabled` to `false` (Generate fired).
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-runbook-execution` (the deferred "runbook-execution-via-SRE-Lead" follow-up above) added conversational runbook execution: asking SRE Lead to "run the API latency spike runbook" now looks that runbook up by title in the same `GrandLineDocs/runbooks/` store phase 1 built and runs its kubectl steps for real.** Not a new capability - `sre_kubectl_mcp.py` gained a second MCP tool, `run_runbook` (alongside the existing `kubectl_readonly`, both now in `SRELead.allowedTools`), that extracts the kubectl command lines from a runbook's fenced code blocks and validates *every single one* through the exact same `_validate_args` allowlist function `kubectl_readonly` itself calls - not a second or looser check - before running any of them via `_execute_via_bridge` (the same shared-terminal mechanism `_run_kubectl` already used, factored out of it so both tools share one execution path). If even one line fails validation (not `kubectl` at all, unparseable, or a subcommand/argument outside `{get, describe, logs, top, events}`), `_run_runbook` refuses the whole runbook by name - naming the exact failing step and why - and runs nothing, not even the earlier, individually-valid steps. `SRELead.setUp` passes `SRE_LEAD_RUNBOOKS_DIR` (a fresh `DocsRunbookStore().root`, same "each session keeps an independent copy" convention as `UpdatesController`/`BootstrapController`) alongside the existing `SRE_LEAD_BRIDGE_DIR`; the persona (`SRELead.persona`) tells SRE Lead about the second tool and to relay a refusal plainly rather than trying to run the remaining steps itself as a workaround. A runbook is looked up by its title (the same `# Heading`-or-slug title `DocsRunbookStore`/the Docs page use), not filename - an exact title match wins if unique, else an unambiguous substring match, else it's refused as ambiguous/not-found rather than guessed. No manual "Run" button was added to the Runbooks tab UI - the conversational trigger was the actual ask. Covered by `test_sre_kubectl_mcp.py`'s `RunbookExecutionTests` (lookup by title vs. filename, ambiguous-name refusal, a non-kubectl line, a disallowed verb, a fully-compliant multi-step run, and stopping at the first bridge-execution failure - all asserting the bridge directory sees zero request files whenever a runbook is refused). **Verified live end to end** (no real cluster/bastion in this sandbox, so a Python stand-in for `SRELeadBridge.swift`'s terminal-injection played the role of the shared terminal, answering real request files with realistic fake `kubectl` output - same documented constraint as every prior SRE Lead task): a real `claude -p` call with the real MCP config, persona, and `--allowedTools` conversationally asked to "Run the API latency spike runbook" called `run_runbook`, ran both of that runbook's real steps through the bridge protocol, and replied with a correctly-labeled Finding referencing the actual (fake) evidence; a second real call asking to run a runbook containing one `kubectl rollout restart` step was refused by name, named that exact step and why, left the bridge directory with zero request files (nothing executed, including the runbook's earlier valid step), and pointed at running it manually via a Console tab instead.
- **`fm/grandline-unified-search-fixes` fixed two real, live-reproduced bugs in the `⌘K` palette above and removed Docs' now-redundant in-page Search tab.** **Theme bug**: `UnifiedSearchController.buildUI` never set `content.wantsLayer = true` on the panel's content view, so the very first `applyTheme` call (fired immediately by `ThemeManager.shared.observe` in `init`, before `present()` has ever built a result row) hit a `nil` `contentView.layer` and silently no-opped - confirmed live via a temporary probe dumping the real `layer.backgroundColor` at launch (`bg=nil`) versus after any later theme change (correct color) - so the palette rendered as plain system gray until some unrelated theme switch happened to re-run `applyTheme` after a `HoverHighlightView` row had forced layer-backing on the ancestor chain. Fixed with an explicit `content.wantsLayer = true`, matching this file's gotcha #8. **Dismiss bug**: Escape already worked correctly (confirmed live via a real synthetic keyDown through `NSApp.sendEvent`, not just calling the delegate method directly) - the actual gap was that clicking outside the palette did nothing, since neither this class nor `ShiftSearchController` (which has the identical latent gap, out of scope here) ever installed any outside-click dismissal; a bare `NSPanel` has no `NSPopover`-style built-in for this. Fixed by adding a local + global `NSEvent` mouse-down monitor pair (mirroring `ShiftGlobalHotkey`'s established local+global monitor shape, just for clicks instead of a hotkey) that calls `dismiss()` whenever a click lands in a different window or a different app entirely, installed in `present()` and torn down in `dismiss()`. **Docs Search tab removed**: `DocsController`'s `DocsTab` enum lost its `.search` case and the `buildSearchContainer`/`runSearch`/`openSearchResult`/`NSSearchFieldDelegate` code was deleted outright (not hidden) - Docs is back to 3 tabs (Playbook/Runbooks/Postmortems), the same call the captain made for the Command Composer tab in `fm/grandline-composer-cleanup-and-polish`. The underlying `DocsKnowledgeSearch`/`UnifiedSearchIndex` search logic is untouched, since `⌘K` still depends on it. All three verified live end to end (temporary env-gated probes, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): theme colors matched `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`'s real `chromeBackgroundHex` at every step including the very first render; a real query against real seeded runbook/postmortem data returned the right result and Return correctly dismissed the palette and fired the right `onSelectRunbook`/`onSelectPostmortem` callback; a synthetic click outside the palette dismissed it; Docs showed exactly 3 tabs; and `⌘⇧P` (Shift's own separate palette, untouched) still opened and dismissed correctly with no regression.

## Tools

- **`.tools` (`fm/cockpit-tools-page-core`, phase 1 of 3 of a captain-reviewed HTML mockup) is a rail destination pinned directly above `.docs`**, following the identical "pinned above the next fixed destination" convention `.docs`/`.updates`/`.bootstrap` already established. The landing grid (a wrapping grid of clickable tool cards - `IconTileView` + `HoverHighlightView`, the same pattern `SettingsController.rebuildAppearanceGrid` uses for its theme picker) is the tool picker - see the multi-session bullet below for how it's used post-phase-4.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-page-multi-session` gave the page a Console-style tab strip so several independent instances of a tool can be open at once** (e.g. three separate Diff tabs comparing different things) - the captain's explicit ask, "like duplicating a browser tab." This merged before phase 3 (below) landed, so all nine tools - not just the original six - get this for free. `ToolsController.swift` now only owns the tab strip (`tabs: [ToolInstance]`, a `TabChipView` per tab - the exact same chip class `ConsoleController`/`TabModel` use, not a second implementation), the landing-grid picker, and which tab's view is currently shown; each tab's actual tool logic (inputs, outputs, status, copy-button text - everything every tool puts directly on itself as instance fields) lives in its own `ToolInstance.swift` instance, one per open tab, so two tabs of the same kind hold two genuinely separate objects with no shared mutable state. **New** (⌘T / the tab bar's "+") shows the landing-grid picker; clicking a card there always opens a *new* tab of that kind via `ToolsController.openNewTab(kind:)`, never re-selecting an existing one - the tab-name convention is `ToolKind.shortName` for the first open instance of a kind ("Diff") and `"\(shortName) N"` for the Nth concurrent one ("Diff 2", "Diff 3", counting only currently-open tabs of that kind, so closing "Diff 2" and reopening a Diff tab reuses that name rather than climbing higher). **Duplicate** (⌘D) is `ToolInstance.snapshotContent()`/`restoreContent(_:)` - a `ToolContentSnapshot` enum carrying just that tab's *input* field(s) (never its output, which a fresh tab of the same kind recomputes itself), copied into a brand-new `ToolInstance` of the same kind. **Close** (⌘W) removes one tab; closing the last one returns to the landing-grid picker - Tools' own version of Console's "never leave the window empty" rule, since the picker (not a blank tool) is this page's natural empty state. The Tab menu's ⌘T/⌘D/⌘W/⌘⇧R/⌘1-9 items dispatch through the first-responder chain by **selector name**, not declaring class - `ToolsController`'s tab methods deliberately reuse `ConsoleController`'s exact selector names (`newShellTab`, `duplicateCurrentTab`, `closeCurrentTab`, `renameCurrentTab`, `selectTabByShortcut(_:)`) so those menu items keep working when the Tools page - not Console - has focus, without needing a second menu. **Verified live, not just via `swift build`**: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the convention below) opened two concurrent Diff tabs, set distinct marker text in each, confirmed switching between them preserved each tab's own text (and toggled the correct tab's view `isHidden`) rather than sharing state, duplicated the second tab and confirmed the new tab carried over its exact current input, mutated the duplicate and confirmed the original tab's content was unaffected, then closed tabs down to zero and confirmed the picker returned - plus a mixed-kind check (a YAML tab and a JSON tab open simultaneously, independent). All checks passed with no crash.
- **Nine genuinely functional tools, no stubs**: YAML validate/beautify, JSON validate/beautify (built on `JSONSerialization`, no library needed), Base64 encode/decode, a JWT decoder (header/payload only - no signature verification, called out permanently in the panel's own text, not just after decoding), a Unix timestamp converter (epoch <-> human, both directions, via `ISO8601DateFormatter`/`DateFormatter`), a Mergely-style diff (phase 2, `fm/cockpit-tools-page-diff` - see below), and - phase 3, `fm/cockpit-tools-page-specialist` - a certificate inspector, a cron next-run explainer, and a Kubernetes resource-unit converter, closing out the originally-scoped three-phase Tools page. All nine tools' UI/logic live in `ToolInstance.swift`, one `buildXPanel()`/handler set per kind - phase 3's three tools were built directly against that shape (multi-session merged first), never against the older single-instance `ToolsController`.
- **Phase 3's certificate inspector (`CertInspector.swift`) uses `Security.framework`'s real certificate-parsing APIs - `SecCertificateCreateWithData` + `SecCertificateCopyValues` - never a hand-rolled ASN.1/DER parser**, per the task brief's explicit instruction (the same framework `KeychainKeyStore.swift` already uses elsewhere, just a different API surface). Two non-obvious things confirmed live via a standalone probe before writing the real code (Apple's own header docs don't spell either out): validity dates (`kSecOIDX509V1ValidityNotBefore`/`...NotAfter`) come back as a plain `NSNumber` holding a `CFAbsoluteTime` (seconds since the 2001-01-01 reference date), not an `NSDate` - cast to `NSNumber` and construct via `Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate:)`, not `as? Date`; and each RDN component's "label" in the subject/issuer property list is the raw dotted attribute OID (`2.5.4.3`, `2.5.4.6`, ...), not a human name like "Common Name" - `CertInspector.shortName(for:)` maps the common ones to the short RDN names (`CN`, `O`, `C`, ...) `openssl -subject`/`-issuer` prints, so the two outputs read the same. **Verified against two real certs, not mocked**: `CertInspectorSelfTest.swift` (permanent, `FM_RUN_CERT_INSPECTOR_TESTS=1`) shells out to a real `/usr/bin/openssl req -x509` to generate a self-signed cert with a SAN, parses it with `CertInspector`, and cross-checks subject/issuer/serial/SAN/expiry against `openssl x509 -noout`'s own independent parse of the same file - this machine's `/usr/bin/openssl` is the LibreSSL macOS ships (confirmed live: `-ext subjectAltName` isn't supported, "unknown option -ext"), so the SAN check reads the extension out of a full `-text` dump instead, which both LibreSSL and a real OpenSSL support.
- **Phase 3's cron next-run explainer (`CronExplainer.swift`) hand-rolls the 5-field parser and next-run search in Swift**, per the task brief (a well-understood, small problem that doesn't need a dependency). Standard fields plus `@yearly`/`@annually`/`@monthly`/`@weekly`/`@daily`/`@midnight`/`@hourly` shortcuts and `@reboot` (reported as "runs at startup, not on a schedule," not an error). Implements the classic day-of-month/day-of-week OR rule (`CronExplainer`'s private `domDowMatches`) and searches forward day-by-day (cheap - only descends into the small, bounded hour/minute sets on a day that already matches month + dom/dow) rather than minute-by-minute, so rare combos (e.g. a specific day-of-month in a specific month) stay fast instead of needing millions of iterations. `CronExplainerSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_CRON_EXPLAINER_TESTS=1`) checks both a simple fixed-time headline/next-runs ("5 4 * * *" -> "At 04:05.") and a range/step schedule ("*/15 2 * * 1-5") against hand-verified expected next-run dates and weekdays, not just the headline text in isolation.
- **Phase 3's resource-unit converter (`ResourceUnits.swift`) parses Kubernetes `resource.Quantity` syntax** (binary `Ki`/`Mi`/`Gi`/`Ti`/`Pi`/`Ei` at powers of 1024, decimal `k`/`M`/`G`/`T`/`P`/`E` at powers of 1000 - `K` uppercase is also accepted as a common, harmless typo even though Kubernetes itself only allows lowercase `k`) into an exact byte count, then converts to every common unit at once. `ResourceUnitsSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_RESOURCE_UNITS_TESTS=1`) checks the exact binary/decimal boundary the task's acceptance criteria calls for: `1Gi` is exactly 1,073,741,824 bytes (1024^3) = exactly 1,048,576 Ki = exactly 1.073741824 decimal G - not 1.0 G, which is what an off-by-1024-vs-1000 bug would produce.
- **All three phase-3 tools were verified live in the real running app, not just via `swift build`**: a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_TOOLS_PHASE3`, reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) drove every real handler and real Copy button against real inputs - a real openssl-generated cert (subject/SAN/expiry, then a real clipboard read-back), the same fixed-time and range/step cron expressions the self-test checks plus `@reboot` and the Random button, a CPU millicores<->cores round trip, and the same exact-1Gi boundary case - and swept all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes` with no crash or AutoLayout break. The app was also launched standalone (`.build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` with scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`) and confirmed to start and stay running without crashing. **Not verified**: a real click-through of the actual rendered UI (this sandbox has no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission, per the convention below) - that last-mile visual check is the captain's own.
- **Diff (phase 2) is a direct Swift re-derivation of the reviewed HTML mockup's algorithm, not a port of its JS, and no third-party diff library** - `DiffEngine.swift` (pure logic, no AppKit import) does classic dynamic-programming LCS over lines, groups consecutive delete/insert runs into paired `changed` rows (remainder as pure add/remove against a blank counterpart), then a second LCS pass over whitespace-preserving word tokens for a changed row's two lines to know which words to highlight. `DiffResultView.swift` renders it as a plain `NSStackView` of two-column rows (not two synced `NSTextView`s - this app already had a working "rows in a flipped-document stack view" pattern for every other scrollable list, and it makes word-level highlighting a matter of `NSAttributedString` background-color runs on a label) - lines render single-line/non-wrapping so both columns of a row always stay the same height and therefore aligned. "Show only differences" collapses consecutive unchanged-line runs into a clickable "N unchanged lines" separator (`CollapsedRunView`), keyed by that run's start index so it stays stable across an expand click; `ToolsController`'s Before/After editors prefill a real two-version Kubernetes Deployment manifest (one changed field, one removed line, one added block) matching the pair `DiffEngineSelfTest.swift`'s `manifestPairProducesExactlyOneRemovedOneChangedTwoAdded` case exercises. **A real bug caught during verification**: `DiffResultView.rebuild()` originally activated each new row's `widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: stack.widthAnchor)` *before* calling `stack.addArrangedSubview(row)` - AutoLayout throws "no common ancestor" for a constraint between two views not yet in the same view hierarchy; every other per-tool panel in this file avoids this because they build their `NSStackView(views: [...])` with the full view array up front, but a dynamically-rebuilt stack (rows added one at a time, needed here since row count varies with the diff and the collapse toggle) has to add the arranged subview first and constrain it second. `DiffEngineSelfTest.swift` (same `FM_RUN_..._TESTS=1` convention as `SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest.swift`, run via `FM_RUN_DIFF_ENGINE_TESTS=1`) covers the line/word LCS and grouping logic permanently; the rendering half (row geometry, collapse -> 4 separators -> click -> expand to real rows, and confirming the image-tag change line renders as two distinct highlight/non-highlight `NSAttributedString` runs rather than one uniform run) was verified with a temporary env-gated launch probe per the convention below, reverted before commit.
- **YAML needs a real parser, so `Vendor/YamlSwift` vendors `behrang/YamlSwift` (MIT, pinned to upstream `master` commit `063286d`)** as actual source files under `Vendor/`, not a remote SPM dependency - this app deliberately has zero remote dependencies (see the SwiftTerm vendoring bullet above; there is no `Package.resolved`). `Package.swift` adds it as a second vendored target (`Yaml`, alongside `SwiftTerm`) that `FirstmateCockpit` depends on. See `Vendor/YamlSwift/README.md` for the full rationale. **A real bug caught during verification**: the first version of `YamlBeautify`'s dictionary-value-is-a-list branch rendered the list at the *same* indent as its own key instead of one level deeper, which round-tripped fine for flat manifests but produced YAML the vendored parser itself rejected ("expected dedent after dash indent") for anything with a list-of-maps nested inside a list item (e.g. a Deployment's `containers[].env`) - fixed by indenting one level deeper, the same as the dict-value branch already did.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-yaml-order-perf-fix` fixed a real captain-reported data-fidelity bug: Beautify alphabetized every mapping's keys instead of preserving the source document's order**, at every nesting level including the top-level document keys - root-caused to upstream `Yaml.dictionary`'s associated value being a plain Swift `[Yaml: Yaml]`, which has no defined iteration order (this used to be treated as a deliberate "deterministic output" trade-off in this file and in `Vendor/YamlSwift/README.md`; a real ~35-deployment manifest with a meaningful 10-key order per deployment showed it's a genuine bug, not a style choice). The parser already builds each mapping key-by-key in source order during recursive descent - the bug was purely that the *accumulator type* it folded those keys into had no memory of that order once folded into a plain dictionary. Fixed with a **local patch on the vendored library**: `Vendor/YamlSwift/Sources/Yaml/YAMLOrderedMap.swift` (new, not upstream) defines `YamlOrderedMap`, an insertion-order-preserving map that replaces `[Yaml: Yaml]` as `Yaml.dictionary`'s associated value everywhere - the enum case, its subscript/`ExpressibleByDictionaryLiteral`/`dictionary` property, and every block/flow-map parsing accumulator in `YAMLParser.swift`. `YamlBeautify.swift` now walks `dict.pairs` (insertion order) instead of sorting `dict.keys`. See `Vendor/YamlSwift/README.md`'s "Local patch" section for the full writeup and what re-syncing YamlSwift from upstream needs to preserve. Permanent regression coverage: `YamlBeautifySelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_YAML_BEAUTIFY_TESTS=1`, same convention as the other pure-logic self-tests below) reproduces the captain's exact shape (a top-level `version` key before a `deployments` sequence, each mapping with 10 keys in a specific non-alphabetical order) and asserts byte-for-byte order preservation at every level, plus a map-of-maps case (not just sequence-of-maps) - verified live end to end too, not just via the self-test: a temporary probe opened a real Tools YAML tab, pasted the exact reported shape, clicked the real Beautify button, and confirmed the real output's key order matched the input exactly.
- **The same task also fixed a real, measured perf regression: `ToolsController.containerWidthMayHaveChanged` (added by `fm/cockpit-tools-page-ui-polish`, see below) forced a full Auto Layout resolve of the *entire window's* view hierarchy on every single window resize notification, unconditionally** - it's registered globally (`NSWindow.didResizeNotification`, `object: nil`) and used to call `view.window?.contentView?.layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()` before reading `gridContainer.frame.width`, regardless of whether the Tools page was even the visible destination. Every open Tools tab's full panel lives as a sibling of the landing-grid's `gridContainer` inside `pageStack` (`addTab` calls `pageStack.addArrangedSubview(instance.view)`), hidden but still fully participating in Auto Layout per this app's "hide, never rebuild" convention for permanently-mounted destinations - so this forced full-subtree resolve got measurably more expensive the more Tools tabs a captain had ever opened and left open, and that cost was paid on every window resize app-wide, not just while looking at Tools. **Measured live** (a temporary env-gated probe driving real `NSWindow.setFrame(_:display:true)` calls to simulate a live drag-resize, reverted before commit per "Verifying native UI bugs" below): with Console visible and 0 Tools tabs ever opened, live-resize cost ~3.6ms/frame; with Console visible and 20 Tools tabs open (hidden), the *same* resize cost ~20.2ms/frame - roughly a 5.6x regression paid on a page the captain wasn't even looking at, confirming the multi-session `ToolInstance` architecture (which lets tabs accumulate across a session) as the amplifying factor and the ui-polish-added resize handler as the actual trigger. Neither `FontSizeManager`'s observer fan-out (~7ms with 20 tabs open, trivial) nor `ToolInstance` construction cost itself (~3-7ms per tab, flat, not growing with tab count) turned out to be the cause - both were suspected per the task brief but ruled out by measurement. Fixed by gating the whole handler on `!view.isHidden && pickerShowing` (the grid's column count only matters while the picker is actually on screen) - live-resize cost with 20 tabs open dropped to ~10ms/frame (the remaining gap versus the 0-tab baseline is AppKit's own inherent cost of a window resize touching a larger permanently-mounted hierarchy, pre-dating this bug and out of scope for this fix). `showPicker()` now calls `containerWidthMayHaveChanged()` directly so a resize that happened while a tab was open is still picked up the next time the picker reappears - correctness (the grid still re-flows to a different column count on resize while visible: verified live, 5 rows at a 900pt window vs 2 rows at 1900pt, both before and after this fix) was not traded away for the speedup.
- **Verified live, not just via `swift build`**: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below) launched the real app, navigated to `.tools`, confirmed the grid rendered 5 real non-zero-frame cards and the YAML panel a real non-zero frame on selection, then drove every tool's actual button-click handlers end to end - YAML validate/beautify against a real 3-document Deployment+Service+Namespace manifest (beautified output re-parsed successfully), JSON validate/beautify (valid and invalid input), a Base64 encode/decode round trip, a JWT decode with real `sub`/`iat`/`exp` claim extraction, and an epoch<->human timestamp round trip - and swept `helm-dark`/`helm-light` with no crash or AutoLayout constraint-break warnings.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-page-copy-buttons` added a Copy button next to every tool's output(s)**, reusing `Toast.swift`'s existing save-confirmation pill (shown on `ToolsController.view`) rather than inventing a second confirmation pattern. `ToolsController.copyButton(action:)`/`refreshCopyButton(_:text:)`/`copyToClipboard(_:)` are the three shared helpers - a plain `NSButton` titled "Copy" (or "Copy header"/"Copy payload" for JWT's two independent outputs), disabled by default and re-enabled/disabled after every action that touches its output by checking whether that output is non-empty, and on click writing to `NSPasteboard.general` (`clearContents()` + `setString(_:forType: .string)`). YAML/JSON/Base64 each have one output and one Copy button; the timestamp converter has two independent outputs (epoch→human, human→epoch) and two buttons; JWT decode has two independent JSON outputs (header, payload) held in their own `jwtHeaderCopyText`/`jwtPayloadCopyText` properties - not scraped from the combined header/payload/claims display text - so each has its own accurate Copy button. Verified live the same way as phase 1: a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_TOOLS_COPY`, reverted before commit) called every tool's real click handlers directly, read `NSPasteboard.general.string(forType: .string)` after each Copy click, and asserted it matched the exact output shown, plus asserted every button's real `isEnabled` state before/after triggering both success and error paths - all 23 checks passed. **Standing convention**: every tool added from phase 2 onward (the diff tool, then the cert inspector/cron explainer/resource-unit converter) should ship a Copy button on its own output(s) by default - this is not something that needs asking for per tool again.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-page-ui-polish` fixed two real captain-reported bugs in the merged page: a cramped landing grid, and monospace text ignoring the app's font-size setting.** **Grid**: `ToolsController.rebuildGrid()` used to hardcode `columnsPerRow = 3` and a fixed `card.widthAnchor.constraint(equalToConstant: 268)`, so the grid never responded to how much width it actually had - measured live before the fix (a temporary debug probe) at a 1220pt window: container 1120pt wide, 3 fixed 268pt cards filling only 824pt of it (~300pt wasted, hugging the left edge); at 1600pt, container 1500pt wide, same 3 cards still 824pt (~700pt wasted). The fix computes `columnsPerRow` from the container's real width against a `minCardWidth` floor (300pt) and lays each row out with `.fillEqually` distribution (no fixed card width) so cards actually stretch to fill whatever width is available - verified after the fix at the same two sizes: 1220pt -> 3 columns of 364pt each (fills all 1120pt), 1600pt -> 4 columns of ~364.5pt each (fills all 1500pt). Card padding grew 12->16pt and title/description font sizes grew slightly (12.5->13, 10.5->11) for breathing room. The grid re-flows live on resize via `containerWidthMayHaveChanged()`, registered against `NSWindow.didResizeNotification` rather than this view controller's own `viewDidLayout()` - confirmed live that `viewDidLayout()` is **not** reliably re-invoked here on a window resize (this page is a body child of `AppShellController`, not the window's own `contentViewController`, which is the only view controller AppKit guarantees that hook for), so the window-level notification is the correct hook, not a stopgap. **Font size**: `FontSizeManager.swift` is a new singleton mirroring `ThemeManager`'s observe/notify shape - the single source of truth for the monospace point size, persisted through the existing `AppSettings.fontSize` key. `ConsoleController` (every terminal tab) and `ToolsController`/`ToolInstance` (every Tools tab's `codeEditor`-based YAML/JSON/Base64/JWT/cert/cron output, plus `tsEpochOutput`/`cpuCoresOutput`/`cpuMillicoresOutput`, plus `DiffResultView`'s row-number/text/collapsed-separator labels) all register via `FontSizeManager.shared.observe` instead of reading `AppSettings.fontSize` once - Settings' presets and the toolbar/menu zoom actions now go through `FontSizeManager.shared.setSize`/`.step(by:)`, so every open terminal tab and every open Tools tab update live from one change, and a Tools tab opened *after* a size change starts at the current size (`ToolInstance.init` calls `applyFontSize(FontSizeManager.shared.size)` itself) rather than only the next live change. Each consumer's font is offset from the shared size by the same delta its original hardcoded point size had from the default 13pt terminal size (code editors were 11.5 = size-1.5; diff line numbers/text/separator were 10/11/10.5 = size-3/-2/-2.5) so relative weight between areas is preserved as the base size changes. **Any new tool's monospace output should go through `ToolInstance.codeEditor()` (already covered) or call `applyFontSize` for anything else** - don't hardcode a point size. **Verified live in the real running app** (not just `swift build`): a temporary env-gated probe drove real window resizes and read back real card/row frame widths for the grid numbers above; a second probe opened a real Diff tab, typed real before/after text, clicked Compare, then called `FontSizeManager.shared.setSize` twice (9 -> 24) and read the *same already-rendered* `DiffRowView` labels' `.font.pointSize` each time (8/8 at floor-clamped 9, then 21/22 at 24) confirming the live update path with no tab rebuild; a third probe opened a brand-new YAML tab after already setting size 18 and confirmed its editors initialized at 16.5 (18-1.5); a full `HelmTheme.allThemes` (12) sweep ran with no crash. All probes were reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention below.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-page-partial-row-fix` fixed a partial-last-row bug PR #93's grid rewrite introduced, and removed the Diff tool's prefilled sample content.** **Partial row**: with 9 tools, a column count that doesn't divide 9 evenly leaves a last row with fewer cards than a full row - since that row's `NSStackView` still uses `.fillEqually` distribution, a lone remaining card (or 2, or 3) stretched to fill the *entire* row width instead of matching a full row's card width. `rebuildGrid()` now pads a partial row's `chunk` out to a fixed `columnsPerRow` slots with plain invisible `NSView()` spacers before building the row, so `.fillEqually` always divides by the same column count regardless of how many real cards are present - real cards stay the same width as every other row's, and the leftover slots are simply empty. Verified live (temporary env-gated probe, reverted before commit, same convention as the ui-polish bullet above): at window widths producing 4/6/7 columns (9 tools -> partial rows of 1/3/2 respectively), every row - full or partial - measured the identical per-card width; live resize back down to the original 4-column width re-flowed correctly with all three rows uniform. **Diff prefill removed**: `ToolInstance.buildDiffPanel()` no longer seeds `diffBeforeInput`/`diffAfterInput` with a sample Kubernetes manifest pair (the `diffBeforeExample`/`diffAfterExample` constants are gone) - a new or duplicated Diff tab now starts with genuinely empty Before/After fields, confirmed live by reading the real editor `.string` right after tab creation. `DiffEngineSelfTest.swift`'s manifest-pair test case is unaffected - it already carried its own local literal copies of that manifest text, not a reference to the removed constants. No other tool's prefilled/placeholder content was touched.
- **`fm/cockpit-tools-yaml-quotes-diff-perf` fixed two more captain-reported bugs found in continued live use: Beautify silently stripping scalar quoting, and the Diff tool itself being slow on a large document (as opposed to the resize-triggered slowness `fm/cockpit-tools-yaml-order-perf-fix` already fixed).**
  - **Quote-preservation fix**: Beautify used to decide on its own, per scalar, whether the output needed quotes (`YamlBeautify.quotedIfNeeded`'s heuristic) - so `selfservice: "tenant-setup-solution-modelling-api-self-service"` lost its quotes (harmless for that specific unambiguous string) but the same logic would have silently changed a quoted `"true"`/`"123"`/`"no"` (a *string* in YAML) into a bare `true`/`123`/`no` (a bool/number, or - since this parser's tokenizer only recognizes `true`/`True`/`TRUE`/`false`/`False`/`FALSE` as booleans, not `yes`/`no` - a plain string that a stricter YAML-1.1 consumer downstream would read as a boolean anyway). Root cause: `Yaml.string` carried only the decoded text, with no record of whether the source scalar was quoted at all. Fixed by adding a `quoted: YamlQuoteStyle` (`.plain`/`.single`/`.double`) associated value to `Yaml.string`, set once at parse time in `YAMLParser.swift`'s three scalar branches (`YAMLQuoteStyle.swift`, a new local patch on the vendored library alongside `YAMLOrderedMap.swift` - see `Vendor/YamlSwift/README.md`'s "Local patch" section). `YamlBeautify.scalarText` now re-emits a `.single`/`.double` scalar quoted, in the same style, unconditionally - never re-decided by the heuristic; `quotedIfNeeded` still runs, unchanged, for a genuinely `.plain` (unquoted-in-source) scalar, since there's no original quoting to preserve there and something still has to decide whether it's safe to leave bare after this beautifier re-indents/re-flows the document. Adding a second associated value to `Yaml.string` required a **manual `hash(into:)`** for the `Yaml` enum (previously fully compiler-synthesized) - `Yaml`'s custom `==` deliberately ignores `quoted` (two `.string`s with the same text are equal regardless of source quoting), and an auto-synthesized `hash(into:)` would have hashed the new field too, breaking Hashable's "equal values hash equally" contract for `YamlOrderedMap` dictionary keys. Regression coverage extended `YamlBeautifySelfTest.swift` (not a parallel test file) with two cases: a quoted `"true"`/`"123"`/`"no"`/`'single-quoted'` mapping asserting every value stays quoted post-Beautify, and the existing 10-key deployment manifest with one field (`selfservice`) quoted in the source, asserting it stays quoted while every other field stays unquoted. Verified live beyond the self-test too: a temporary probe (reverted before commit) ran the captain's exact reported line plus the type-changing cases through the real `yamlBeautifyClicked()` button handler (not just the underlying `YamlBeautify.dump` call) and confirmed byte-for-byte output.
  - **Diff performance fix**: the captain reported the Diff tool itself (not window resize) was noticeably slow on a real 300+ line manifest, specifically at the moment Compare is clicked. A temporary env-gated timing probe (reverted before commit) measured the real per-phase breakdown at 320/340 lines before touching any code: `DiffEngine.lineDiff` (parse + line LCS + per-changed-row word LCS) ~14ms, `DiffResultView` construction ~50ms, the old `setRows` NSStackView-tree rebuild ~90ms - all cheap - versus the subsequent `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded` Auto Layout resolve of that tree: **~13.6 seconds**. Scaling the row count (70/120/220/320 rows -> ~202ms/919ms/4483ms/13598ms of layout time) confirmed this wasn't linear or even quadratic - a ~67x time increase for a ~4.6x row-count increase - the known `NSStackView` pathology where every arranged subview participates in one shared Auto Layout solve simultaneously, so hundreds of rows blow up far worse than the row count. Neither the line/word-diff algorithm nor per-row view construction needed fixing - only how the result set was rendered. Fixed by rewriting `DiffResultView` (`DiffResultView.swift`) from a plain `NSStackView` of permanent per-row `NSView`s into a single-column, view-based `NSTableView`, which is demand-driven - `tableView(_:viewFor:row:)` is only called for rows that actually need to be drawn (the visible rect plus a small buffer), not for every row up front, regardless of total row count. `DiffResultView` is no longer an `NSView` itself - it's a plain object owning `tableView: NSTableView`, which callers (`ToolInstance.buildDiffPanel`) embed directly as the enclosing `NSScrollView`'s `documentView` (this is what makes the demand-driven rendering apply - wrapping the table in another container view would defeat it). Row content (`DiffRowView`/`CollapsedRunView`, the two-column tinted/word-highlighted line pair and the clickable collapsed-run separator) is unchanged, now returned as reusable table cell views via `tableView.makeView(withIdentifier:owner:)` instead of permanent stack arranged subviews. Verified after the fix with the same probe: total Compare-click cost (lineDiff + view construction + `reloadData`) at 320/340 lines dropped to **~140-200ms end to end**, confirmed via three independent measurements (the standalone timing probe, a full-window worst-case `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded` forcing all 340 rows to render at once, and driving the real `diffCompareClicked()` button handler on a real `ToolInstance` tab) - no separate catastrophic layout phase in any of them, and scaling stayed roughly linear up to 500+ lines. `DiffEngineSelfTest.swift`'s pure-logic coverage is unaffected (it never touched `DiffResultView`); the rendering half (row reuse, collapse/expand, theme/font-size re-themeing via `tableView.reloadData()`) was verified live the same way as prior Diff-tool work, via temporary probes reverted before commit.

## Shift

- **`.shift` (`fm/cockpit-shift-foundation`, phase 1 of a multi-phase, captain-approved build) is a rail destination pinned in `navStack` right after `.hosts` and before `.review`** (`fm/cockpit-shift-rail-position` moved it there from right after `.overview`, per a captain correction) - deliberately NOT part of the bottom-anchored Tools/Docs/Updates/Bootstrap/Settings group, since this is a daily-use destination, not a utility. `IconRailController.RailDestination`'s case order is `overview, console, hosts, shift, review, ...`, which is what puts `.shift` there in `navStack`'s loop for free (see the enum's doc comment). Shift is a personal task/follow-up/project tracker backed by local YAML files instead of a database - the plan is multi-phase; this phase is foundation only.
- **The core distinction the captain was explicit about, and that no future phase should blur: a task is something to do, a follow-up is something to check on later.** They are modeled as two separate types (`ShiftTask` vs `ShiftFollowUp` in `ShiftModels.swift`) with different fields, never a shared "item" type with a kind flag. A follow-up is not "a task with a reminder."
- **Data layer (`ShiftStore.swift` + `ShiftYaml.swift` + `ShiftModels.swift`)** lives under a captain-configurable root, overridable via `FM_SHIFT_DIR` - the same convention `HostStore`/`SSHKeyStore`/`SnippetStore` already established with their own `FM_*_FILE` env vars. **As of phase 4 (`fm/cockpit-shift-git-sync`), the default root (when `FM_SHIFT_DIR` is unset) is `personal-tasks/` inside a local git clone of the captain's real `manjesh-config` GitHub repo, not the bare non-git folder phases 1-3 used** - see the "Git sync (phase 4)" bullet below for the clone/commit/push/pull mechanics. Directory layout under that root (batched files, not one file per task or follow-up, per the captain's explicit decision) is unchanged from phase 1:
  ```
  personal-tasks/                    (was a bare `shift/` folder in phases 1-3, see above)
  ├── tasks/
  │   ├── active.yaml
  │   └── completed/<YYYY-MM>.yaml   (one file per month a task was completed in)
  ├── follow-ups/follow-ups.yaml
  ├── projects/projects.yaml
  ├── notes/notes.yaml
  ├── activity/<YYYY-MM>.yaml        (history log, split by month like completed/)
  └── settings.yaml
  ```
  `ShiftStore.setTaskCompleted(id:completed:)` is the one place that moves a task: marking it done removes it from `active.yaml` entirely and appends it to that month's `completed/<YYYY-MM>.yaml` (never just a status flag flipped in place); un-marking it does the reverse lookup across every completed month file and moves it back. `ShiftStore.reloadAll()` re-reads every file from disk - it's what `ShiftController.viewWillAppear()` calls, so the page always reflects real on-disk state, not a stale in-memory copy.
- **YAML I/O reuses the Tools page's vendored library end to end, not a second YAML layer.** `ShiftYaml.swift` converts between the typed models above and `Yaml` trees, then serializes via `YamlBeautify.dump` (the exact function the Tools page's Beautify button uses) - so Shift's files automatically inherit `Vendor/YamlSwift`'s local order-preservation (`YAMLOrderedMap.swift`) and quote-preservation (`YAMLQuoteStyle.swift`) patches for free. One deliberate difference from the Tools page's passthrough Beautify: Shift always decodes a file into its typed Swift struct and re-encodes from a fixed field order (the order listed in the brief: `id, title, description, status, priority, due_date, ...`), rather than preserving whatever arbitrary key order a hand-edited file might have - since Shift owns its own schema end to end, this is a deliberate, simpler contract, not a lost capability. Every string field is written double-quoted (`.string(_, quoted: .double)`) so a value that looks like a YAML reserved word (a task titled `"true"`, a tag `"123"`) round-trips as a real string, not a coerced bool/int - checked directly in `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift`, not assumed to transfer from the Tools page's own equivalent check.
- **List rendering (`ShiftListViews.swift`) is `NSTableView`-based for both the task list and the follow-up list**, following `DiffResultView.swift`'s pattern verbatim rather than a plain `NSStackView` of permanent rows - see this file's Diff-tool history above for the ~13.6-second layout blowup that pattern hit once a list grew into the hundreds. Measured live at 350 rows here: `setTasks` + `reloadData` ~0.4ms, a bounded-height enclosing `NSScrollView`'s `layoutSubtreeIfNeeded` ~70ms - no repeat of that pathology, because the table only builds row views for what's actually visible.
- **`ShiftController.swift` ("My Tasks") mirrors `FleetController.swift`'s page shape** (greeting header, stat tiles, `FlippedView`-backed scroll area) rather than three separate Today/My Tasks/Follow-ups pages - the captain explicitly rejected that split as redundant. Stat tiles: tasks due today, pending follow-ups, overdue tasks. The task list's checkboxes call `ShiftStore.setTaskCompleted` directly (the same move-not-flip behavior above) and the view re-renders from a fresh `store.activeTasks` read afterward.
- **Subtasks (`ShiftSubtask`, nested on `ShiftTask`) are only ever rendered in a project-scoped context, never as flat rows in the main task list** - this is a hard rule, verified directly (not just assumed) across all three phases so far - see the phase-specific Verification bullets below.
- **Verification (phase 1)**: `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SHIFT_STORE_TESTS=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit`, same permanent-self-test convention as `YamlBeautifySelfTest.swift`/`DiffEngineSelfTest.swift`) covers the seed/reload round trip, completion moving a task into the right month file and back on reopen, two different months splitting into two different files, a subtask toggle surviving a reload, and the reserved-word-scalar fidelity check above - all against a real scratch directory, not just in-memory state. Beyond that self-test, this phase was also verified against the real launched app (not just `swift build`) via a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_SHIFT_UI`, reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above): launched with scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`, navigated to `.shift` (firing the real `viewWillAppear` → `reloadAll` + `render` path), confirmed the real table views rendered the right row counts against real store data, ran the exact closure a real checkbox click invokes and confirmed both the in-memory list and the on-disk `active.yaml`/`completed/<YYYY-MM>.yaml` files changed correctly, and confirmed a subtask toggle persisted to disk too.
- **Phase 2 (`fm/cockpit-shift-create-edit`) added task/follow-up creation, editing, and follow-up Done/Snooze actions.** New/Edit Task (⌘N, or the My Tasks header's "+") and New/Edit Follow-up (⌘⇧F, or the Follow-ups header's "+") are `presentAsSheet` forms (`ShiftTaskEditorController.swift` / `ShiftFollowUpEditorController.swift`, following `SnippetEditorController.swift`'s plain-`NSStackView`-form shape rather than `HostEditorController`'s scroll-and-cap treatment, since this field list is short). Both menu items target `AppShellController` directly (`newShiftTaskFromMenu`/`newShiftFollowUpFromMenu`, mirroring the Hosts menu's "New Host…" convention) so they work regardless of which destination is currently showing - the shell switches to `.shift` itself first. Double-clicking a task or follow-up row opens the same editor pre-filled (`ShiftTaskListView.onOpen`/`ShiftFollowUpListView.onEdit`, wired in `ShiftController`); a follow-up row's right-click context menu (`ShiftFollowUpListView.rowMenu()`, same `NSMenuDelegate`-driven pattern as `HostsController`'s row menu) adds Mark Done/Reopen, a Snooze submenu (30 Minutes/1 Hour/Tomorrow/Next Week/Custom…), and Edit….
- **Natural-language date detection (`ShiftDateParser.swift`) is a small hand-rolled scanner, not a dependency** - recognizes `today`/`tomorrow`/`next week`/`next <weekday>`/a bare weekday/a time-of-day (`3pm`, `3:30pm`, `15:00`, `noon`, `midnight`), including simple combinations (`tomorrow 3pm`). A bare time requires an am/pm suffix or a colon so an ordinary number in a title is never mistaken for a time (verified: `"Version 2 release notes"` does not parse). The New Task sheet's Title field wires this live (`ShiftTaskEditorController.controlTextDidChange`): a match auto-checks "Has due date," fills the Due date/time picker, and shows a small dismissible inline confirmation row; once the captain edits the Due controls directly (or dismisses the confirmation), further title edits stop overwriting that choice (`dueManuallyEdited`) - only a still-untouched Due field ever gets clobbered by a new phrase. Permanent coverage: `ShiftDateParserSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SHIFT_DATE_PARSER_TESTS=1`, same convention as `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift`) checks a relative day + time, a specific weekday, a bare time both before and after it's already passed today, `today`/`next week` alone, and the two false-positive guards (a plain sentence, a bare number).
- **`ShiftFollowUp` gained a `followUpTime` field (`"HH:MM"`, mirroring `ShiftTask.dueTime`) and `ShiftYaml`/`ShiftStore` gained real CRUD**: `addTask`/`updateTask`/`addFollowUp`/`updateFollowUp`/`setFollowUpStatus(id:done:)`/`snoozeFollowUp(id:to:)`. A follow-up's "Done" stays in `follow-ups.yaml` (no month-split completed file the way tasks have) but flips `status` to `.done` and logs an activity entry - `ShiftController`'s existing `status == .pending` filter is what makes it disappear from the "active follow-ups" view, matching the brief's "moves it out of the active list, into activity." Snooze's relative math (`ShiftController.snoozeFollowUp`, using `ShiftDateFormatting.dateTime(from:time:)`/`.components(from:)` to convert between a real `Date` and the two persisted scalar fields) lives in the controller; `ShiftStore.snoozeFollowUp` only recomputes+persists+logs once a target `Date` is already decided. Custom snooze opens `ShiftSnoozeCustomController.swift`, a tiny one-`NSDatePicker` sheet.
- **Verification (phase 2)**: `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift` was extended (not a parallel file) with real-disk checks for create/reload, edit-in-place (including confirming the fixed `id` < `title` field order and double-quoting survive an edit, not just a fresh write), follow-up create/edit, Done flipping status, and Snooze actually recomputing `follow_up_at`/`follow_up_time` for two different presets (30-minute and Next Week) - all against a scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`, reloading via a fresh `ShiftStore()` between every step. Beyond the self-tests, this phase was verified against the real launched app (not `swift build` alone): a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above) drove the real ⌘N/⌘⇧F menu actions to present real sheets, set real `NSTextField`/`NSDatePicker` values and called the real `controlTextDidChange`/`save()` handlers, confirmed the New Task sheet's live date detection (typing "tomorrow 3pm" showed the confirmation row and filled the picker to 15:00), confirmed a created task/follow-up appeared in a *separately reloaded* `ShiftStore()` instance (a real disk round trip, not just in-memory state), drove an edit through the exact `onOpen`/`onEdit` closures a real double-click fires (so `save()`'s `dismiss(self)` had a real sheet presentation to dismiss) and confirmed the edited title was double-quoted with `id` still preceding `title` in the raw YAML, and drove two real Snooze presets (30 Minutes, then Tomorrow) through the exact closure the row context menu calls, confirming `follow_up_at`/`follow_up_time` actually changed on disk each time. All temporary access-loosening (`private` -> internal on specific fields, needed only so the probe could reach them) and the probe code itself were reverted before commit.
- **Phase 3 (`fm/cockpit-shift-projects`) replaced the phase-1 placeholder Projects section with a real Projects page.** `ShiftProjectStatus` grew from the placeholder `active`/`archived` pair to 5 states (`notStarted`, `inProgress`, `onHold`, `completed`, `archived`, each with a `displayName`); `ShiftProject` gained `description`/`startDate`/`dueDate`. `ShiftProjectViews.swift`'s `ShiftProjectCardView` is the project card: name, "X of Y tasks completed", a progress bar, and a status pill that is a real control - clicking it pops an `NSMenu` (`ThemeMenu.swift`'s convention) and a selection calls back through `ShiftController` into `ShiftStore.updateProject(_:)`, which writes the whole project back to `projects/projects.yaml`. The pill deliberately lives in its own row, a sibling of the name/description/progress region rather than nested inside it - see `ShiftProjectViews.swift`'s header for why an `NSClickGestureRecognizer` on an ancestor view and a real button living inside that same subtree is a hit-testing hazard worth avoiding structurally rather than relying on precedence between the two. Clicking anywhere else on the card opens that project's own detail view (`ShiftController`'s `projectsView` state, `.grid` vs `.detail(id)`) - an edit form (name/description/status/start date/due date, plain fields, a Save button) built once and mutated in place (so a `render()` triggered by an unrelated subtask toggle never clobbers an in-progress edit - see `lastDetailProjectID`'s guard in `rebuildProjectDetail`), plus a task list scoped to that project (`ShiftStore.allTasks(forProject:)` - both active and completed tasks) where each task expands to show its own subtasks as checkboxes.
- **`ShiftStore.setSubtaskDone` was generalized in phase 3 to toggle a subtask wherever its task currently lives - `active.yaml` or the right completed month file** - phase 1 only supported the active case (subtasks on a completed task were explicitly read-only); project detail's task list includes completed tasks, and the brief's acceptance bar requires a completed task's subtask toggle to persist to its month file too. `ShiftStore.updateProject(_:)` and `taskCounts(forProject:)`/`allTasks(forProject:)` are the other phase-3 additions to the store.
- **Verification (phase 3)**: `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift` gained coverage for a project status update persisting through `updateProject` and surviving a reload, a completed task's subtask toggle persisting to its month file and surviving a reload (not just an active task's), and `taskCounts`/`allTasks` agreeing with each other - all against a real scratch directory. Beyond the self-test, this phase was verified against the real launched app via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above) that drove the real (private but `@objc`-reachable) status-menu-selection handler on a live `ShiftProjectCardView` mounted in the grid and confirmed the new status landed in `projects.yaml` and survived a fresh `ShiftStore` reload, opened a project's real detail view, expanded a real task via its real header-click handler, drove the real subtask checkbox with `NSButton.performClick(nil)` and confirmed the toggle persisted and survived a reload, and swept every `NSTextField` on the page confirming a subtask's title never appears as a flat label outside its own checkbox.
- **Git sync (phase 4, `fm/cockpit-shift-git-sync`) replaced phase 1-3's bare local folder with a real git-backed root, reusing `DotfilesData.swift`'s local-clone-plus-shell-`git` shape and `DocsSyncSource.ghAuthToken()` for auth - not a second git-sync mechanism.** `ShiftGitSync.swift` is the whole implementation: `ShiftGitSync.shared` (the one production instance) clones/pulls/commits/pushes a local working tree at `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/shift-repo/` (override `FM_SHIFT_GIT_CLONE_PATH`) against `DotfilesSource.cloneURL` (override `FM_SHIFT_REMOTE_URL` - how this phase's own verification pointed a real launched instance of the app at a disposable local bare repo instead of the captain's real `manjesh-config`, see below); `ShiftStore.resolveRoot()` returns `<that clone>/personal-tasks` unless `FM_SHIFT_DIR` is set, in which case it bypasses git sync entirely (used by every self-test, and available to any captain who wants a plain local-only folder with no git backing - `ShiftStore.gitSync` is `nil` in that case and `notify()` never calls `markDirty()`). **The speed contract**: every task/follow-up/project mutation already wrote its YAML file synchronously before this phase (see phases 1-3 above) - `ShiftGitSync` only owns what happens *after* that write. `ShiftStore.notify()` calls `gitSync?.markDirty()`, which flips the sync-status pill to "Local changes" immediately (no I/O) and (re)schedules a debounced commit+push on a private serial background queue (3s default) - several rapid edits collapse into one commit, never one per edit, and the UI never waits on git or network. A commit that succeeds locally but fails to push (offline, bad auth, diverged remote) leaves the pill at "Failed" with the local commit - and therefore the edit - fully intact; nothing here ever force-pushes, rebases, or discards local state. `ShiftGitSync.pullNow()` (`git fetch` + `merge --ff-only`, called on `start()` for a pre-existing checkout and on a 300s timer) fast-forwards silently when safe and reports `.diverged` without touching anything when local and remote history have genuinely diverged - real conflict-resolution UI is `cockpit-shift-conflict-handling`, the next queued phase, not this one. The sync-status pill lives in `ShiftController`'s header (`applySyncPill`, reusing `ToolRowLayout.pill`), driven by `ShiftGitSync.observeStatus` - real state from the queue, never a timer or fake cycle, hidden entirely when `store.gitSync` is `nil`. **Legacy-data migration**: the first time `.shared` successfully reaches a working tree whose remote is genuinely `DotfilesSource.cloneURL` (`migratesLegacyData`, gated on that exact match - see below for why), it moves any real content still sitting at the old phase 1-3 path into the new `personal-tasks/` root and renames the old folder aside (`shift.migrated-<timestamp>`, never deleted) rather than silently orphaning it.
  - **A real, live-verification-caught bug, not hypothetical: migration must never fire just because code is running through `.shared`.** `FM_SHIFT_REMOTE_URL` lets a real launched instance of this app point at a disposable test remote while still going through the `.shared` singleton (exactly what this phase's own verification does, and what any future manual testing should keep doing) - gating migration on "is this `.shared`" migrated the captain's real local phase 1-3 Shift data into a throwaway test clone the first time a test run's clone happened to succeed, live, during this phase's own work. Fixed by gating `migratesLegacyData` on the resolved remote genuinely equaling `DotfilesSource.cloneURL`, not on singleton identity.
  - **A second real bug, also live-verification-caught: a narrow but genuine race between this class's own initial clone and `ShiftStore.init()`'s synchronous first-run `settings.yaml` write.** `ShiftStore.init()` calls `ShiftGitSync.start()` (which clones on a background queue) but then runs `reloadAll()` synchronously on the caller's own thread right afterward - on a brand-new root with no `settings.yaml` yet, that write can land at `workingTree/personal-tasks/settings.yaml` in the narrow window before the background clone finishes, and a naive "remove the destination, then clone into it" sequence would silently discard that write. Fixed by cloning into a fresh temp sibling directory and swapping it into place (`ensureWorkingTreeNow`), with `salvageRacedLocalWrites` folding any such raced-in file into the fresh clone before the swap rather than losing it. A related string-prefix bug surfaced while fixing this: naively stripping a raw path prefix off `FileManager.enumerator`'s reported paths corrupted the relative path when an ancestor directory was itself a symlink (macOS's own `/tmp` -> `/private/tmp`, confirmed live: `settings.yaml` became `privatesettings.yaml`) - fixed by resolving both sides through `resolvingSymlinksInPath` before computing the relative path, not by special-casing `/tmp`.
  - **Verification (phase 4)**: `ShiftGitSyncSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SHIFT_GIT_SYNC_TESTS=1`, same permanent-self-test convention as `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift`) creates real disposable local bare git repositories under a scratch temp directory (never the captain's real `manjesh-config`) and covers: a local YAML write staying instant against an unreachable remote; a real commit+push reaching a real second "remote" repo; 5 rapid edits inside one debounce window producing exactly 1 commit, not 5; a real pull (via a second clone that pushed first) bringing new content back; a push failure (remote deleted out from under an already-cloned tree) landing on "Failed" with the local commit still intact; and a genuinely diverged pull (two clones that each committed locally without pulling from each other first) stopping with `.diverged`/"Failed" and no commit gained, lost, or rewritten on either side. Beyond that self-test, this phase was verified against the real *launched* app (not `swift build` alone, per this project's track record): a real built binary, pointed via `FM_SHIFT_GIT_CLONE_PATH`/`FM_SHIFT_REMOTE_URL` at a disposable local bare repo, was run to completion multiple times confirming a clean clone/commit/push cycle every time with no leftover dirty files. **A final, deliberate real pass was then run against the actual `manjesh-config` repo** (per the brief's explicit instruction, with the captain's real local phase 1-3 Shift data temporarily moved aside first so this pass's migration check - `migratesLegacyData` genuinely matches the real remote here - couldn't sweep old dev/test seed content into it): the real app cloned `manjesh-config` fresh, created and pushed the real `personal-tasks/settings.yaml` scaffold (confirmed live via `gh api` reading it back from GitHub), then a task titled `"TEST - cockpit-shift-git-sync verification (safe to delete)"` was added, confirmed live on GitHub, and then deleted and confirmed removed on GitHub (`personal-tasks/tasks/active.yaml` is `"tasks": []`) in the same pass - the `personal-tasks/` scaffold itself (`settings.yaml`, empty `tasks/active.yaml`) is real production infrastructure and was intentionally left in place, only the test task content was cleaned up. The captain's real local phase 1-3 data was restored to its original location immediately afterward.
- **Explicitly out of scope as of phase 4 (later phases own these)**: real conflict-resolution UI (`cockpit-shift-conflict-handling`, still queued), search, the menu bar item, global quick-capture, weekly review, and notifications were delivered in phase 5 below; project deletion is still out of scope (there is no delete action on a project yet, only edit). `ShiftStore` deliberately has no auto-seed call in the production app path (`seedIfEmpty()` exists but is only invoked from tests/verification) - a fresh captain profile shows a genuinely empty state rather than fabricated starter content.
- **`fm/cockpit-shift-ui-polish` was a rendering-only pass, not a phase**: the captain compared real screenshots against the captain-approved mockup at `data/cockpit-shift-ui-polish/reviewed-mockup-reference.html` and found the shipped Shift screens looked like plain stock system UI next to the mockup's considered dark theme, serif/mono typography, and colored tiles/pills. **Root-cause finding: Shift was already correctly wired into `ThemeManager`/`HelmTheme`/`HelmTint`/`HoverHighlightView` before this pass** - every color (priority pills, status pills, the sync pill, project status tint) already resolved against the active theme, not a hardcoded palette; the app's own dark/light state just happened to be light on the machine the captain screenshotted (a leftover `ThemeManager` preference, not a Shift bug, and out of scope to force from one page). The one genuine gap was typography: every Shift label used the app's default `.systemFont` sans face with no serif/mono distinction anywhere, and there was no shared bordered-panel container matching the mockup's `.panel`/`.panel-head`. Fixed by adding exactly one new file, `ShiftTypography.swift`: `ShiftFont.serif`/`ShiftFont.mono` (Georgia / `.monospacedSystemFont`, Shift-scoped - not a second app-wide font system, per the brief's "extend the tokens where Shift needs something the rest of the app doesn't have yet"), `ShiftPanelView` (a reusable bordered/rounded header+divider+body container, now wrapping the Tasks and Follow-ups sections), and `ShiftEmptyStateView` (a centered icon+copy placeholder, replacing bare one-line empty-state text in the task list, follow-up list, and projects grid). Also: task/follow-up rows now wrap their content in `HoverHighlightView` (previously only the project-detail task rows did), the overdue stat tile's number renders in the theme's danger/critical tint via a new `tint` parameter on `statTile`, and each section header gained a live monospace count badge (e.g. "My Tasks" + a separate "12" badge, mirroring the mockup's `.cnt`) instead of baking the count into the header string. A true multi-item Shift-specific sidebar (Notes/Archive/Weekly Review as real nav destinations, matching the mockup's inner `.shift-nav`) was deliberately NOT added - that would be new navigation functionality, which the brief explicitly excluded, and conflicts with the captain's own earlier, recorded decision (see the phase-1 bullet above) to keep Tasks/Follow-ups/Projects as one page rather than split into a multi-page nav. **Verified live, not just via `swift build`**: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above - this sandbox has no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission) launched the real app against scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`, dumped real font names/point sizes/colors for the greeting/headers/badges/stat tiles/panel background in both the empty state and after seeding real tasks/follow-ups/a project, and swept `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark` via the `-fm.themeID <id>` `NSUserDefaults` argument-domain override with no AutoLayout warnings or crashes in any of them - confirming Georgia serif and true monospace fonts render as intended and every color still comes from the active `HelmTheme` (e.g. `helm-dark`'s panel background/border and the overdue tile's danger-red measured directly off the real `CALayer`).
- **Phase 5 (`fm/cockpit-shift-power-features`) added the five "power features" the captain explicitly asked for now rather than deferred: global search, Weekly Review, a menu bar item, global quick-capture, and due-item notifications** - matching the interaction shape of `data/cockpit-shift-ui-polish/reviewed-mockup-reference.html`'s own `.palette-overlay`/`.mb-popover`/`.capture-overlay`, translated into real AppKit rather than copied wholesale. A Notes screen was explored separately and dropped by the captain before this phase - search only covers tasks/follow-ups/projects, there is nothing else to index yet.
  - **Weekly Review** is a second top-level view *inside* the Shift destination (a two-tab switcher - "My Tasks"/"Weekly Review" - in `ShiftController`'s header, `HoverHighlightView`-based pills mirroring the mockup's `.shift-nav .item` look), not a new rail icon: the captain's phase-1 decision to keep Shift one consolidated destination (see above) still stands, and a second rail icon literally named "Review" would also collide with the unrelated, pre-existing PR-review `.review` destination. `ShiftController.showWeeklyReview()`/`showDashboard()` toggle `dashboardContainer`/`weeklyReviewContainer`'s `isHidden`, both permanent siblings inside the page's `contentStack` (never rebuilt), matching every other "hide, don't tear down" destination in this app.
  - **`ShiftStore.weeklySummary(reference:)`** computes all three headline numbers from data phases 1-4 already persist, per the brief's explicit "don't invent new fields" instruction - it does not add tracked fields to `ShiftTask`/`ShiftFollowUp` themselves. The one real gap found: phases 1-4 never logged *why* a due date changed, so `ShiftActivityEntry` gained an optional `targetID: String?` (nil-safe for every pre-phase-5 entry) and `ShiftStore.updateTask` now logs a `task_due_date_changed` activity entry (via the *existing* activity-log mechanism, not a new counter) whenever an already-set due date is edited to a different value - a task getting its first due date is not a "push back." "Pushed back 2+ times" groups `task_due_date_changed`/`follow_up_snoozed` activity entries (already logged pre-phase-5 for snoozes) by `targetID` over a 2-month lookback (`recentActivityEntries`), keeping only ids with 2+ occurrences whose task/follow-up still exists. "Completed this week" counts a task's real `completedAt` plus a follow-up's `follow_up_completed` activity entries (a follow-up has no completion timestamp field of its own). "Coming up next week" counts active tasks/pending follow-ups due in the 7 days immediately after the current `Calendar.current` week. Permanent coverage: `ShiftWeeklySummarySelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SHIFT_WEEKLY_SUMMARY_TESTS=1`) builds all three numbers from real seeded store data using `Date()`-relative offsets (not hardcoded calendar dates, since the method keys off "this week" relative to whatever `reference` it's given) and specifically confirms a single push does NOT count as "2+ times."
  - **Global search (`ShiftSearch.swift`) is `⌘⇧P`, not the mockup's own `⌘K`** - this app already bound `⌘K` to "Find in Terminal" (`AppShellController.activateConsoleFind`) well before this phase existed, so reusing it would have silently broken an already-shipped shortcut. `ShiftSearchIndex.search` is pure data-layer (active tasks, all follow-ups, all projects, simple case-insensitive substring match); `ShiftSearchController` is a small `NSPanel`-based palette (arrow keys/Return/Escape via `NSTextFieldDelegate.control(_:textView:doCommandBy:)`, not a `keyDown` override - a plain `NSTextField`'s command keys route through the shared field editor to the delegate, not to the field's own `keyDown`). Selecting a result calls back through `AppShellController.openShiftTask`/`openShiftFollowUp`/`openShiftProject`, which switch to `.shift` and open the same editor sheet / project detail a real row click already uses - one "open this item" behavior regardless of entry point.
  - **The menu bar item (`ShiftMenuBar.swift`)** is a plain `NSStatusItem` + `NSPopover` reading/writing the same shared `ShiftStore` instance the main window uses (see below) - not a second store. Its quick-add field reuses `ShiftDateParser.parse` (phase 2's natural-language date detection), so typing "tomorrow 3pm renew the cert" into the popover behaves identically to typing it into the full New Task sheet.
  - **Global quick-capture (`ShiftQuickCapture.swift`)** is `⌥Space` (matching the mockup's own stated shortcut), backed by `ShiftGlobalHotkey`: a **local** `NSEvent` monitor (fires while this app is frontmost but some other window has focus - needs no permission) plus a **global** monitor (fires while a *different* app is frontmost - the actual "from anywhere" case) that, per Apple's own documentation, only delivers keyDown events once the process is a trusted Accessibility client (`AXIsProcessTrusted`/`AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions`) - exactly the "one-time macOS permission" the reviewed mockup's own capture-overlay copy already calls out, not a bug to fix. A Shift-menu "Quick Capture" item (⌥Space) is a permission-free in-app fallback for the same action.
  - **Notifications (`ShiftNotifications.swift`)** follow `FleetNotifier.swift`'s established shape exactly (background poll + "already notified" set + an immediate trigger-less `UNNotificationRequest`), tracking each due item's *last-notified due `Date`* (not just a notified/not-notified flag) so an edited or re-pushed due date can notify again while an unchanged one never double-fires. **A real crash this phase's own live verification caught and fixed**: `UNUserNotificationCenter.current()` throws an uncaught `NSInternalInconsistencyException` ("bundleProxyForCurrentProcess is nil") when the running process has no real Info.plist/bundle identifier - true for the bare `swift run`/`.build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` dev workflow this project's own README documents as normal, and the exact crash class `UpdatesController.notify`'s header already documents - `ShiftNotificationScheduler.start()`/`.notify(...)` now guard on `Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier != nil` the same way; the packaged app (a real bundle) is unaffected either way.
  - **`ShiftStore` is now built once by the app delegate (`AppDelegate.shiftStore`) and shared** by `AppShellController`'s `ShiftController`, the menu bar item, the search palette, and quick capture - `AppShellController.init` takes a `shiftStore:` parameter instead of constructing its own, so all four surfaces read/write the identical in-memory + on-disk state rather than four independent stores that could drift out of sync with each other.
  - **Verified against the real *packaged* app, not the bare dev binary** (`native/build_native_app.sh`'s output) - the bare `.build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` binary has no bundle identity, which is exactly what the notifications crash above needed a real bundle to even reach the code path that exposed it. A temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above, including two call sites' access levels temporarily loosened from `private` and reverted after) launched the packaged app against scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`, created a real task/follow-up during the run and confirmed `ShiftSearchIndex.search` found each one, drove `AppShellController.openShiftTask`/`openShiftFollowUp`/`openShiftProject` and confirmed the rail switched to `.shift` and the real editor sheet's `presentedViewControllers` was non-empty, toggled Weekly Review with no crash, drove the menu bar's real quick-add path and confirmed the created task landed in the shared store, drove quick capture's real capture path and confirmed the same, fired a **synthetic `⌥Space` `NSEvent` through `NSApp.sendEvent`** (the same dispatch path local monitors hook into) and confirmed the capture overlay's window actually became visible, and added a task due 60 seconds out then called the notification scheduler's poll directly - `UNUserNotificationCenter.getNotificationSettings`/`getDeliveredNotifications` confirmed `authorizationStatus == .authorized` on this machine and a real delivered notification for that exact task. **Honestly not verifiable in this sandbox**: the global monitor's true system-wide (other-app-frontmost) case, since `AXIsProcessTrusted()` reported `false` and this sandbox has no way to grant Accessibility permission non-interactively (same constraint the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention already documents for Screen Recording/Accessibility) - the mechanism is implemented and the in-app/app-frontmost path is confirmed working, but the fully-detached case needs the captain's own real machine to grant that one-time permission and confirm.
- **Phase 6 (`fm/cockpit-shift-conflict-handling`) closes out the originally-scoped Shift build** by giving phase 4's deliberately-safe-but-unhelpful non-fast-forward `pullNow()` `.diverged` outcome a real resolution path, instead of leaving the sync pill stuck on a generic "Failed".
