Scoped entirely to Shift's own three known record types (tasks, follow-ups, projects) - never a general-purpose git merge tool, and `pullNow()`'s own fast-forward-only logic is untouched (see its doc comment, which already pointed at this phase).
  - **`ShiftConflict.swift`** is the pure-logic half: `ShiftConflictRecordType` (a small protocol `ShiftTask`/`ShiftFollowUp`/`ShiftProject` conform to, each contributing a fixed-order `fieldSnapshot()` of human-readable label/value pairs) plus `ShiftThreeWayMerge.run`, a generic record-level 3-way merge keyed by `id` across the merge-base/local/remote revisions of one list file. Per id: unchanged or identically-changed on both sides, changed on only one side, added on only one side (including two different ids added independently on both sides - the brief's explicit "genuinely unambiguous" auto-merge case), and deleted-with-the-other-side-unchanged all resolve automatically into `ShiftConflictSet.resolved*`; only a record edited differently on both sides (or edited on one side while deleted on the other) becomes a `ShiftRecordConflict`, carrying both sides' typed records (or `nil` for "deleted on that side") so `fieldDiffs` can zip the two `fieldSnapshot()`s positionally into a plain-language, field-by-field diff - never a raw YAML text diff. `ShiftTask`/`ShiftFollowUp`/`ShiftProject`/`ShiftSubtask` gained `Equatable` (structural only, used to tell "unchanged" from "edited" here) alongside this.
  - **`ShiftGitSync.detectAndResolveConflicts()`** is the one new entry point, called only from `start()`'s two `pullNow()` call sites (launch-time pull and the periodic timer) exactly when `pullNow()` itself returns `.diverged` - `pullNow()`'s own behavior, return type, and existing self-test (`ShiftGitSyncSelfTest.swift`, unchanged and still passing) are untouched. It commits any still-uncommitted local edit first (so "local" means the real current state), computes the merge-base via `git merge-base HEAD origin/<branch>`, loads each of the three list files at base/HEAD/origin via `git show <ref>:<path>` (a missing file at a given revision reads as an empty list, not an error), and runs `ShiftThreeWayMerge` per kind. If every difference turns out unambiguous, it applies and pushes the merge itself with **no captain interaction at all** - `Status.conflict` never even appears in that case, matching the "auto-merge only where genuinely unambiguous, otherwise ask" rule. Only when a real per-record conflict exists does it store `pendingConflictSet` and flip `Status` to the new `.conflict(fileCount:)` case.
  - **Committing a resolution never trusts git's own line-based merge algorithm** - two different fields on the same YAML record edited on different lines could textually merge with no git-level conflict marker at all, silently producing a record that's part-local/part-remote in a way nothing here would notice or the captain chose. Instead, `applyConflictResolution` runs `git merge -s ours --no-commit origin/<branch>` (stages a real two-parent merge commit without touching any file - the "ours" strategy just keeps HEAD's tree), then overwrites the three list files with this method's own computed final record set (`resolved*` plus, per conflict, whichever side `choices` picked - a `nil` side means "let that side's deletion stand"), then `git add`/`git commit`/push. `resolveConflicts(choices:)` refuses to write anything unless every conflicting record id has an explicit choice - there is no default and no silent skip.
  - **`ShiftConflictController.swift`** is the resolution sheet, presented from a click on the sync pill (`ShiftController.syncPillClicked`, wired to the pill via a new `NSClickGestureRecognizer` - the pill's `applySyncPill()` now renders a distinct red "Conflict - click to resolve" state instead of `.failed`'s generic "Failed"). Grouped by kind (Tasks/Follow-ups/Projects), each conflicting record shows its plain-language field diffs (or "Deleted on this machine, but edited on GitHub" / the mirror, for a delete-vs-edit conflict) and two buttons, "Keep mine"/"Keep GitHub's" (retitled "Keep deleted (mine/GitHub's)" when that side is the deletion) - never disabled, since keeping a deletion is itself a valid, meaningful choice. An informational "Merged automatically" section lists every unambiguous auto-merge note so the captain sees what happened even to the parts that needed no decision. "Apply & Push" only enables once every conflicting record has a choice; on success it calls `ShiftGitSync.resolveConflictsAsync` and dismisses itself, on failure it stays open with the reason.
  - **A real, live-verification-caught AppKit bookkeeping quirk, not a functional bug**: after `dismiss(self)` on the sheet, its own `view.window` correctly goes to `nil` (confirmed live - the sheet visually closes) but the presenting `ShiftController`'s `presentedViewControllers` array can keep a stale entry for a while when `dismiss` is invoked from an async completion handler rather than a synchronous button-click call stack, rather than a raw window-visibility count (this app's own `NSStatusItem`-backed Shift menu bar item keeps an always-visible auxiliary window that isn't the sheet at all) - noted here in case a future change ever needs to check "is a Shift sheet currently open" programmatically.
  - **Verified with a disposable local bare-repo stand-in, never the real `manjesh-config`, matching phase 4's own safety approach**: `ShiftConflictSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SHIFT_CONFLICT_TESTS=1`, same convention as `ShiftGitSyncSelfTest.swift`) covers (1) the same task's title edited differently on two simulated machines being detected as a specific field-level conflict (not just "Failed"), with both "keep remote" and "keep local" resolutions verified by reloading from disk *and* re-cloning the pushed remote fresh; (2) two different tasks added on each side with no overlapping ids auto-merging with zero captain interaction, verified the same way; (3) an ordinary non-conflicting fast-forward pull (phase 4's own happy path) still working with no conflict machinery ever touched - no regression. Beyond the self-test, this phase was verified against the real **launched, packaged app** end to end (not just `swift build`): a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, along with a temporarily-loosened `AppShellController.shift` access level, per the "Verifying native UI bugs" convention above) launched the real binary pointed via `FM_SHIFT_GIT_CLONE_PATH`/`FM_SHIFT_REMOTE_URL` at a disposable bare repo pre-seeded with genuine machine-A/machine-B divergence on the same task's title, waited for the real sync pill to reach `.conflict`, invoked the real `syncPillClicked()` (via `Selector` runtime dispatch, bypassing only Swift's compile-time `private` - no `@objc` visibility was widened), located and clicked the real "Keep mine"/"Keep GitHub's" buttons and the real "Apply & Push" button via genuine `NSButton.performClick(nil)` target/action dispatch, and confirmed via `git log`/`git show` against the disposable remote that the correct content was pushed for both the keep-local and keep-remote paths across multiple independent runs.
- **This was the last queued phase of the original captain-approved Shift build (phases 1-6: rail destination + YAML data layer, create/edit, Projects, Git sync, search/weekly-review/menu-bar/quick-capture/notifications, conflict handling).** Shift as originally scoped is complete; any further Shift work is a new, separately-scoped task.
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-shift-sync-pill` fixed a QA-flagged bug (`data/cockpit-audit-shift-review-tools/report.md`, Finding 1) in the no-git configuration** (`FM_SHIFT_DIR` set, `ShiftStore.gitSync` is `nil`): the header's sync pill is supposed to be hidden entirely there (showing a status is meaningless with no git backing), and `ShiftController.applySyncPill()`'s guard already set `syncPill.isHidden = true` correctly - live verification (a temporary env-gated probe driving the real launched app, reverted before commit) confirmed the guard was in fact working and the pill's frame was genuinely zero-sized at every layer, across a window resize, a theme cycle, and a navigate-away-and-back, in both a minimal build and the full real app. Rather than leave a real, populated pill view sitting hidden in the tree (a trap for a future change that might forget the guard, and unverifiable-by-construction against any AX/accessibility tooling that might misreport a hidden view's last-known frame - this session's own QA pass separately hit a real macOS AX cross-process identity bug against this same unsigned-dev-build app, see that report's "Critical" section), `ShiftController.buildHeader()` now decides once, at header-construction time, whether `syncPill` becomes an arranged subview of the header row at all - `store.gitSync` is a `let` fixed for the controller's whole lifetime, so this is a one-time decision, not something that needs to react to a later change. `applySyncPill()`'s guard is now a no-op early return when `gitSync` is `nil`, since there's nothing to style. Verified both configurations live (temporary probe, reverted before commit): no-git mode shows the pill as absent from the view hierarchy entirely (not just `isHidden`); a real git-backed disposable local bare-repo remote still shows the pill present with a real frame, reacting to real `ShiftGitSync.Status` (`Synced`/`Failed` observed live depending on the disposable remote's branch setup) - no regression to phases 4/6's sync-pill behavior.
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-shift-new-project` added the missing project-creation path the phase-3 Projects page never shipped** - `updateProject` existed, `addProject` didn't, and the Projects section header had no "+" (the My Tasks and Follow-ups headers already did, wired to ⌘N/⌘⇧F). `ShiftStore.addProject(_:)` mirrors `addTask`/`addFollowUp`'s shape exactly (append, persist, log a `project_created` activity entry, notify) - `ShiftYaml`'s project encode/decode already handled the full field set from phase 3, so no YAML-layer change was needed. `ShiftProjectEditorController.swift` is a new sheet (name, description, status defaulting to "Not Started", start date, due date) following `ShiftFollowUpEditorController.swift`'s exact shape; start/due date are plain "YYYY-MM-DD" text fields, matching the existing project-detail edit form's own fields rather than the Task/Follow-up editors' `NSDatePicker` (a project has no time-of-day component). Wired via `sectionHeaderRow(...)`'s existing `addAction`/`addTooltip` params (the same mechanism the other two sections already used) and a new "New Project…" Shift-menu item - **deliberately no keyboard shortcut**: ⌘⇧P (the pattern ⌘N/⌘⇧F would suggest for a third creation action) is already "Search Shift…" (phase 5), so this follows "Weekly Review"'s own no-shortcut precedent rather than force a collision. `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift` gained a permanent case (not a parallel file) confirming a created project appears in `projects.yaml` double-quoted, in the fixed `id`-before-`name` field order, and survives a fresh `ShiftStore()` reload. Verified against the real launched app, not just `swift build`: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, along with a temporarily-loosened `AppShellController.shift` access level, same convention as `cockpit-shift-conflict-handling` above) drove the real menu action to open the sheet, located the real name/description fields and Save button by walking the actual view hierarchy (filtering out the shared field editor `NSTextView` that AppKit installs over whichever field is first responder - it's easy to mistake for the real description view, since both are plain `NSTextView`s), clicked Save via genuine `NSButton.performClick(nil)`, and confirmed the new project appeared in the live in-memory store, in the Projects grid with no navigation away and back, in the on-disk `projects.yaml`, and survived a fresh `ShiftStore()` reload.
- **`fm/cockpit-shift-project-page-redesign` rebuilt the Projects section's detail view (opened by clicking a project card) after the captain rejected it outright**: a cramped inline form, unstyled/messy task and subtask rows, and no way to add a task directly from inside the project - "clicking a project should feel like clicking into a Tool on the Tools page," per the captain's own comparison, not a form squeezed into leftover space. This superseded an earlier framing of the ask as a dark-theme-only fix. `ShiftController`'s detail-building code (`rebuildProjectDetail`/`rebuildDetailTasks`/`detailTaskBlock`, all `NSStackView`-based) is gone; the page is now a real header (an `IconTileView` + the project name as a serif title, matching My Tasks' own greeting treatment, a read-only status pill + start/due meta line, and a wrapping description paragraph - no input fields at page level), an editable "Details" `ShiftPanelView` (an `NSGridView` form - fixed label column, fill field column, the same shape `ShiftTaskEditorController`'s sheet already uses - plus a Save button), and a "Tasks" `ShiftPanelView` with a header "+" ("Add Task") next to a live count badge, matching the My Tasks/Follow-ups sections' own `sectionHeaderRow` convention exactly. Editing is still edit-in-place (unchanged behavior, just restyled) - there is exactly one place a project's fields change, the Details panel; the header only displays.
- **The task list moved from a hand-built `NSStackView` of permanent rows to a new `NSTableView`-based `ShiftProjectTaskListView`** (`ShiftProjectDetailView.swift`), following `ShiftListViews.swift`'s own established convention for the identical reason documented there (an `NSStackView` of many permanent rows is the shape that blew up into a ~13-second layout pass in `DiffResultView`'s history once row counts grew - see this file's Diff-tool/Tools-page entries above). `NSTableView` has no first-class nested-row concept, so a task's subtasks are flattened into the row list as `.subtask(taskID:subtask:)` entries that only appear while that task's id is in `expandedTaskIDs` (the same expand/collapse state the old implementation tracked, just rendered through a table) - a `.noSubtasks(taskID:)` placeholder row covers an expanded task with none. Subtask rows are visually indented behind a thin connecting line rather than phase 3's plain stacked checkboxes in a faint rounded box - the captain's specific "reads clearly nested" ask. Task rows also gained a completion checkbox (wired to the same `ShiftStore.setTaskCompleted` the main My Tasks list uses) that phase 3's version never had, and double-clicking a task row opens the same Edit Task sheet the main list uses - one "open this task" behavior everywhere in Shift, not a second one for the project-scoped view.
- **"+ Add Task" (`ShiftController.addTaskToProjectClicked`) opens the existing `ShiftTaskEditorController` sheet with the current project pre-selected** rather than a second task-creation UI - `ShiftTaskEditorController` gained a `defaultProjectID` init param (used only when creating a brand-new task; ignored when editing, which already has its own `projectID`) that pre-selects the Project popup. Saving reuses the existing `presentTaskEditor`'s `onSave` -> `render()` path, which re-enters `rebuildProjectDetail` for the same project and re-reads `store.allTasks(forProject:)` - the new task appears in the list immediately, with no navigation away and back needed.
- **Verified live in the real launched app, not just `swift build`** (temporary env-gated debug instrumentation in `main.swift`, plus a temporarily-loosened `AppShellController.shift` access level and several `ShiftController` detail-page properties, all reverted before commit - same convention as `cockpit-shift-conflict-handling`/`cockpit-fix-shift-new-project` above): seeded a real project and a task with two real subtasks, opened the detail page and confirmed the header renders a real non-zero-frame serif name label (Georgia, 22pt) and real non-zero-frame Details/Tasks panels; clicked the real "Add Task" button, filled and saved the real New Task sheet, and confirmed the new task appeared in the live table immediately, persisted to `tasks/active.yaml` with the correct `project_id`, and survived a fresh `ShiftStore()` reload; expanded a task row and clicked a real subtask checkbox, confirming the toggle persisted and survived a reload (no regression to phase 3's subtask persistence); edited the project's fields via the Details panel and clicked Save, confirming the edit persisted; and swept `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark` with no crash and stable panel frames across all five.
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-shift-project-detail-fullpage` fixed a real structural gap that redesign didn't touch: opening a project still didn't feel like navigating to a new page.** `statsRow`/`taskPanel`/`followUpPanel` (My Tasks' stat tiles, task list, follow-ups list) sat as permanent siblings of `projectsSection` inside `dashboardContainer`, so the Projects section's own `.grid`/`.detail(id)` toggle only ever swapped `projectsGridContainer`/`projectsDetailContainer` - the dashboard content above it never went anywhere, and a captain's screenshot showed "No follow-ups pending." sitting directly above the project detail. Fixed with `ShiftController.applyProjectDetailFullPage(_:)`, called from `renderProjectsSection()`'s existing `.grid`/`.detail` switch: entering detail sets `statsRow.isHidden`/`taskPanel.isHidden`/`followUpPanel.isHidden`/`projectsSectionHeaderRow.isHidden` (the Projects section's own "Projects"/"Project: X" header + count badge + "New Project" button - redundant once the redesigned detail view has its own header) all to `true` - real `NSStackView` `isHidden`, not a scroll offset - so the project detail becomes the only content on the page, then restores them on "‹ Back to Projects" (`detailBackClicked`, unchanged) or on navigating to Weekly Review/My Tasks. **Deliberately left alone**: the top greeting header (`buildHeader()`, "Good afternoon"/date/sync pill) and the My Tasks/Weekly Review tab row - both are app-level navigation chrome shared by every top-level view, not dashboard content, exactly how `switchTopLevelView`'s existing `dashboardContainer`/`weeklyReviewContainer` toggle already treats them; only `dashboardContainer`'s own interior sections change per this fix. Verified live (not just `swift build`) via the same temporary env-gated `main.swift` debug-instrumentation + temporarily-loosened `AppShellController.shift`/`ShiftController` access convention used throughout this section: seeded a real project via a scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR`, drove the real `AppShellController.openShiftProject`/`ShiftController.detailBackClicked`/`showShiftWeeklyReview`/`showDashboard` methods end to end, and confirmed via a dumped `isHidden` state string that grid mode shows everything, detail mode hides all three sibling sections plus the Projects header, "Back to Projects" restores the full dashboard exactly, and a Weekly-Review-and-back round trip taken *while a project's detail is open* correctly restores the detail's hidden-sibling state rather than leaking the dashboard sections back into view.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-side-by-side-composer-height` put My Tasks and Follow-ups side by side instead of stacked** (captain ask, real screenshot). `dashboardContainer` no longer adds `taskSection`/`followUpSection` directly - both are wrapped in one `tasksRow`, a plain horizontal `NSStackView` (`.fillEqually` distribution, `.top` alignment) that `dashboardContainer` holds in their place; the width constraint that used to pin each section to `contentStack.widthAnchor` individually now pins `tasksRow` instead. No responsive breakpoint was added - this app has no existing convention for one (unlike `ToolsController`'s grid, which measures real width for an unbounded card count), and a fixed two-column row is the simpler, consistent choice for exactly two panels. `taskPanel`/`followUpPanel` themselves, their table views, and every existing behavior (checkboxes, add/edit, empty states, live counts, `applyProjectDetailFullPage`'s `isHidden` toggling) are unchanged - this was a pure layout wrap, one level deeper in the view tree. Verified live via a temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe (reverted before commit, same convention as the rest of this section): both panels rendered as two 562pt-wide columns sharing a 1144pt row with 20pt spacing, top-aligned (equal `maxY`, not `minY` - AppKit's un-flipped `NSStackView` coordinate space means a `.top`-aligned shorter view has a *higher* origin, not a matching one), with real seeded task/follow-up data still rendering in each table.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-panel-height-scroll-fix` fixed a real captain-reported bug in that side-by-side layout: the two panels rendered at different heights (an empty Follow-ups card taller than a 1-task My Tasks card) and, separately, a captain with many tasks would see the whole My Tasks card keep growing instead of scrolling.** Root cause was two independent, mismatched fixed-height constants that predated the side-by-side change - `taskListScroll` was already pinned to `heightAnchor == 300` and `followUpScroll` to `220`, so the two cards' bodies (and therefore their total panel heights, since `ShiftPanelView.setBody` pins the body view edge-to-edge) never matched. Both now share one constant, `ShiftController.taskFollowUpPanelBodyHeight` (280pt) - `buildTaskSection`/`buildFollowUpSection` both reference it, so the two panels can't drift apart again. The fixed height was already on the enclosing `NSScrollView`, not the table itself, so `NSTableView`'s own demand-driven rendering (see `ShiftListViews.swift`'s header) already scrolls correctly once content overflows it - there was no separate "growing card" mechanism to fix beyond making both heights equal. Verified live via a temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe (reverted before commit): mounted a real `ShiftController` against a scratch `FM_SHIFT_DIR` at 0/1/8 seeded tasks and follow-ups - both panels measured identically (320pt) at every state, and at 8 tasks the table's real content height (404pt) exceeded the fixed scroll clip (280pt), confirming the list scrolls internally rather than the panel growing.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-projects-panel-background` wrapped the Projects section's header + grid in a `ShiftPanelView`** (`ShiftController.projectsPanel`), matching `taskPanel`/`followUpPanel` - previously Projects sat directly on the page's plain background with no bordered card, reading as a different kind of section from My Tasks/Follow-ups (captain screenshot). `buildProjectsSection()` now calls `projectsPanel.setHeader(headerRow)`/`.setBody(projectsGridContainer)` instead of stacking `[headerRow, projectsGridContainer, projectsDetailContainer]` loose; `projectsDetailContainer` (the full-page project detail, which already has its own two `ShiftPanelView`s - `detailFormPanel`/`detailTasksPanel`) stays a sibling *outside* `projectsPanel`, not nested inside it, so the detail view isn't double-wrapped. `applyProjectDetailFullPage(_:)` now hides `projectsPanel` as a whole (replacing the old standalone `projectsSectionHeaderRow`, which is gone) and `applyTheme()` calls `projectsPanel.applyTheme(theme)` alongside `taskPanel`/`followUpPanel`. Verified live via a temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe (reverted before commit): all three panels' `layer.backgroundColor`/`borderColor`/`borderWidth`/`cornerRadius` matched byte-for-byte across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`, and opening a project's detail correctly hid all three while `detailFormPanel`/`detailTasksPanel` rendered with real non-zero frames, unaffected.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-task-image-attachments` added a single image attachment per task.** `ShiftTask.hasAttachment: Bool` (default `false`) is the one new model field - since `ShiftTask` is hand-converted to/from `Yaml` (`ShiftYaml.swift`), not `Codable` with a `CodingKeys` list, this needed no migration beyond `dict[str("has_attachment")]?.bool ?? false` on read, but the same underlying lesson from the `fm/cockpit-fix-host-decode-regression` gotcha (see "Block view" above) still applied: a new field needs an explicit default on read, not just a Swift-side one, or old task files break. The actual image lives as a real file at `attachments/<task-id>.png` in the same git-synced `personal-tasks/` root `ShiftGitSync`/`ShiftStore` already manage - named by task id so re-saving replaces rather than accumulates, and swept up for free by `commitAndPushNow`'s existing `git add -A -- shiftSubpath` (no new sync path). `ShiftStore.addTask`/`updateTask` take an `attachment: ShiftAttachmentChange = .unchanged` param (`.unchanged`/`.set(Data)`/`.removed`) applied *before* the task record is persisted, so the YAML flag and the on-disk file can't disagree. **Conflict handling is deliberately NOT the task-record 3-way merge** (`ShiftConflict.swift`, phase 6) - a binary image isn't meaningfully 3-way-mergeable the way a YAML field is, so it rides the same "ours" resolution `detectAndResolveConflicts`'s `git merge -s ours` already gives every non-task/follow-up/project file (e.g. activity logs) during an actual divergence-conflict resolution; a normal fast-forward pull is unaffected. `ShiftImageAttachmentWell.swift` is the editor sheet's control (`ShiftTaskEditorController`): a file picker (`NSOpenPanel`), drag-and-drop, and clipboard paste (`⌘V`, the real "just took a screenshot" flow) all funnel through one `Self.image(fromPasteboard:)` reader and one `handle(image:)` intake, which downscales to a 1600px longest edge and re-encodes as PNG (`normalizedPNGData`) before ever touching disk - the size ceiling chosen to keep a full-resolution Retina screenshot from bloating the repo (a synthetic 3200x2000 test image compressed to well under 200KB; see `ShiftImageAttachmentWellSelfTest.swift`). The main task list (`ShiftListViews.swift`) shows only a small paperclip glyph next to a task's title when `hasAttachment` is true - never a thumbnail, which would blow out the fixed row height `fm/grandline-shift-panel-height-scroll-fix` already fixed once; the real thumbnail only renders inside the task's own editor sheet, on open and after a fresh attach. Verified live (temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe, reverted before commit): a real editor sheet's attachment well laid out with correct non-zero geometry, a real `handle(image:)` call flipped it from placeholder to thumbnail, the resulting `.set(Data)` change persisted through a real `ShiftStore` and survived a fresh reload, re-opening the editor for that task showed the real saved thumbnail, and removing + saving cleared both the flag and the file. `ShiftGitSyncSelfTest.swift` was extended with a disposable-bare-repo scenario proving a binary attachment file is genuinely committed *and pushed* (confirmed via `git show <sha>:<path>` and a fresh clone read-back, not just present in the local working tree), plus a push-failure scenario confirming the attachment stays safely committed locally when the push can't reach the remote - matching every other Shift write's existing failure contract.

- **`fm/grandline-shift-project-detail-theming` fixed a real captain-reported bug: the project detail page's "Details" form (Name/Description/Start date/Due date) rendered with an off-theme brownish/maroon box** instead of a themed input - `detailNameField`/`detailDescriptionField`/`detailStartDateField`/`detailDueDateField` (`ShiftController.swift`) were plain `NSTextField()` instances with zero theme-driven styling calls anywhere in the file, so they fell back to AppKit's stock bezel, which renders with a wrong color cast once forced into implicit layer-backing by this page's own `wantsLayer`-backed dark root view (the same class of AppKit gotcha this file's catalogue already documents elsewhere for bezeled controls under a heavily layer-backed, custom-appearance-forced hierarchy). Fixed by mirroring `ConsoleComposerPopover`'s already-established "sunken field" fix for its own intent/code fields verbatim: `styleDetailFormField(_:)` strips the bezel (`isBordered`/`isBezeled` off, `focusRingType = .none`) and hands the whole look to an explicit `wantsLayer` + `masksToBounds` + rounded/bordered layer, recolored every theme change in `applyTheme()` via a `detailFieldFillColor(for:)` helper that blends `chromeInkHex` into `chromeBackgroundHex` rather than reusing `backgroundHex` directly (needed since those two tokens are numerically identical in `gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`tokyo-night-light` - see `ConsoleComposerPopover.fieldFillColor(for:)`'s own doc comment for that confirmed list). `detailStatusPopup` (a native `NSPopUpButton`) was left with its stock rendering, since it isn't affected by this bug. The same task also gave the project detail page's own task/subtask checklist (`ShiftProjectDetailView.swift`'s `ShiftProjectTaskRowView`/`ShiftProjectSubtaskRowView`) the tinted-circle checkbox treatment `fm/grandline-shift-task-row-cards` already gave the main My Tasks list - `ShiftTaskCheckBadge` (`ShiftListViews.swift`) was widened from `private` to internal and given an `init(size:)` param (default unchanged at 26pt; the subtask row uses 18pt to fit its compact 30pt row) specifically so it could be reused here rather than a second checkbox component being hand-rolled. One behavioral wrinkle from that swap: `ShiftTaskCheckBadge` is `.momentaryChange` (image-only, no persistent toggle state of its own), so `ShiftProjectSubtaskRowView.checkboxClicked` can no longer read the button's own `.state` after a click to learn the new value (that pattern silently breaks - the button never actually flips) - it now tracks the model's current `isDone` itself and passes `!isDone`, the same "derive the new value from the model, not from the button" idiom `ShiftTaskRowView`'s own stateless `onToggleCompleted?()` callback already used. Verified live via a temporary env-gated `main.swift` probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): real off-screen screenshots across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark` confirmed the fields render as distinct, correctly-tinted input boxes (not the reported brown box) in every theme including the two numerically-identical-token ones, and a real `ShiftTaskCheckBadge.performClick(nil)` on both a subtask row and an expanded task row confirmed the toggle still persists to disk and survives a fresh `ShiftStore()` reload, alongside a real Details-form save (name/description/status) round-tripping correctly - no regression to `FM_RUN_SHIFT_STORE_TESTS`.
- **`fm/grandline-devops-command-library` (Phase 1 of 3, per a captain-approved design doc) added a third Shift tab, "DevOps Commands" - a personal, searchable, parameterized command library, zero new git plumbing.** Storage lives at `commands/<category>/[<subcategory>/]<slug>.yaml` *inside* `ShiftGitSync`'s existing `personal-tasks/` root (unlike Docs Runbooks, which needed a second `DocsRunbookGitSync` class since `GrandLineDocs/runbooks/` is a sibling of `personal-tasks/` outside that subtree - commands sit *inside* it, so `git add -A -- GrandLineDocs/personal-tasks` already covers them for free) - `CommandLibraryStore.swift` only ever calls `ShiftGitSync.shared.markDirty()` after a write, exactly like `ShiftStore` itself. `CommandLibraryModels.swift` is the pure data model (`DevOpsCommand`, `CommandParameter` with `string`/`number`/`boolean`/`select`/`textarea` kinds, and a `CommandRiskLevel` field stored now even though Phase 2 owns its destructive-confirmation gate - see the design doc's phasing). `CommandLibraryYaml.swift` follows `ShiftYaml.swift`'s own conversion pattern verbatim (the same vendored `Vendor/YamlSwift` + `YamlBeautify.dump`, inheriting its order/quote-preservation patches for free) - a fresh file, not a second YAML layer. `commands/config.yaml` holds captain-configurable select-option lists (namespaces/environments/regions) - a `CommandParameter.configOptionsKey` looks itself up there at render time, per the original spec's explicit "never hardcode environment/namespace names into the app" instruction. `CommandLibrarySeedData.swift` seeds ~73 real, correct commands (5-8 per category) across all 13 categories the mockup lists (Kubernetes/AWS/Linux/Docker/Git/MySQL/Networking/OpenSSL/Terraform/Helm/ArgoCD/Jenkins/General) the first time the store finds an empty `commands/` folder.
- **`CommandLibraryStore` honors `FM_SHIFT_DIR` (not just its own narrower `FM_COMMAND_LIBRARY_DIR`) - a real bug caught during this task's own live verification, not a hypothetical.** Since commands live inside the same `personal-tasks/` root `ShiftStore` manages, a caller setting only `FM_SHIFT_DIR` (the established way every Shift/Docs self-test bypasses `ShiftGitSync.shared`'s real production clone) would otherwise leave this one store still reaching into that real clone - including seeding 73 real starter commands into the captain's actual `manjesh-config` GitHub repo. Confirmed live (launched the packaged debug binary with only `FM_SHIFT_DIR` set to a scratch path and watched `CommandLibraryStore` seed straight into `ShiftGitSync.shared`'s real working tree before the fix); fixed by checking `FM_COMMAND_LIBRARY_DIR` then `FM_SHIFT_DIR` then falling back to `ShiftGitSync.shared.dataRoot`, matching `ShiftStore.resolveRoot()`'s own precedence. Covered permanently in `CommandLibraryStoreSelfTest.swift`. **Any future store nested inside `ShiftGitSync`'s `personal-tasks/` subtree needs this same `FM_SHIFT_DIR` fallback, not just its own narrower override.**
- **UI (`CommandLibraryViews.swift`, `CommandLibraryPageView`) mirrors `ShiftProjectDetailView.swift`'s "plain `NSObject`-owned sub-page" convention** rather than a second `NSViewController` - `ShiftController` embeds its `view` as a fourth top-level container (alongside `dashboardContainer`/`weeklyReviewContainer`) toggled via `isHidden`, following the existing two-tab switcher's exact pattern. Layout: a search field above a two-column row (a ~220pt Favorites+Categories rail, a detail pane) - category browsing is a two-level drill-down within the left rail (categories -> a category's own command list -> back) via plain `NSStackView` rows, not an `NSTableView` (a captain's command library is nowhere near the row count that would justify one - see `ShiftProjectDetailView.swift`'s own header for where that line gets crossed). The detail pane shows the selected command's name/risk badge/description/tags, the command template with live-substituted `{{token}}`s highlighted in the accent color (`CommandLibraryPageView.attributedGeneratedCommand`), a wrapping 3-per-row grid of parameter inputs (text field/popup/checkbox by kind) that regenerate the preview on every keystroke/selection, and Copy (wired to `NSPasteboard.general`) + Favorite (persisted via `CommandLibraryStore.toggleFavorite`) - Send to Terminal/Edit/Explain render disabled, matching the mockup, since those are explicitly Phase 2/3.
- **A real AppKit gotcha this task's own `leftPanelRow`/`divider()`/back-row construction hit and fixed**: activating a view's `widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: leftPanelStack.widthAnchor)` *before* that view is added as an arranged subview of `leftPanelStack` throws AppKit's "no common ancestor" exception (this file's AppKit gotcha catalogue's own recurring lesson, hit again here) - fixed with one `appendToLeftPanel(_:)` helper that always adds-then-constrains, replacing every direct `leftPanelStack.addArrangedSubview(...)` call site. A second, real bug this task's own live probe caught: a `.select` parameter's popup auto-selects its first option at construction with no `action` fired, so the generated-command preview could show a bare unfilled `{{token}}` even though the control on screen already displayed a real value - fixed by seeding `paramValues` from whatever each control actually displays right after building it (`paramBlock`), once, so the preview and the visible control state can never disagree.
- **Verified live in the real launched app** (temporary env-gated debug instrumentation in `main.swift` + temporary debug shims on `CommandLibraryPageView`/`ShiftController`, all reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): confirmed real non-zero geometry after switching to the tab, selecting the Kubernetes category and a command populated the detail pane correctly, editing the namespace/pod parameter fields live-regenerated the exact expected command text, Copy placed that exact string on the real pasteboard, favoriting a command surfaced it under a real FAVORITES section after navigating back, and switching to My Tasks correctly hid the command library view again with no regression to `ShiftStoreSelfTest`/`ShiftDateParserSelfTest`/`ShiftWeeklySummarySelfTest`. **A synthetic-harness-only finding, not a real app bug**: driving a zero-argument `@objc` button/tab action via `NSApp.sendAction`/`performClick(nil)`/`perform(_:with:)` in this headless probe intermittently hit AppKit's internal gesture-recognizer press-tracking machinery ("Unexpected gesture recognizer state") for a couple of specific controls - worked around by calling straight into the same production method as a plain Swift function call for just those two cases, not by changing any shipped code.
- **`CommandLibraryStoreSelfTest.swift`** (`FM_RUN_COMMAND_LIBRARY_TESTS=1`, same convention as `ShiftStoreSelfTest.swift`) covers: seed-on-first-run writing every seed command and never re-seeding on reopen; parameter-token detection matching template order including a bare undeclared token; generated-command substitution (filled value, unfilled-with-default, unfilled-with-no-default rendering the bare token); search across name/description/category/tags/command-text; favorites persisting through a fresh store instance and un-favoriting surviving a reload; all three risk levels present in the seed set; `config.yaml`'s select-option lists resolving correctly with a documented fallback; and the `FM_SHIFT_DIR` fix above. All against a real scratch directory, never the captain's real git-synced data.
- **Explicitly out of scope for Phase 1** (per the design doc's phasing): Send to Terminal, the Add/Edit/Duplicate command sheet, the destructive-confirmation gate, workflows (recommended to become Docs Runbooks reused, not a second format - see the design doc's own open-question section), any `claude -p` AI action, Save-from-Terminal's heuristic parameterizer, secret detection before save, fuzzy search, and `⌘⇧P` palette integration.
- **`fm/grandline-devops-command-library-phase2` (Phase 2 of 3) turned the library from a browser into a working tool: Send to Terminal, recent-used tracking, the Add/Edit/Duplicate command sheet, the destructive-confirmation gate, and workflows - implementing the design doc's Option A (workflows = Docs Runbooks, not a second format) as its own recommendation, unchanged.** **Send to Terminal** is `ConsoleController.sendCommandLibraryTextToActiveTab(_:)` - byte-for-byte the same shape as `runSnippetInActiveTab` (types the generated command + `\n` into `currentTab`), forwarded `CommandLibraryPageView.onSendToTerminal` → `ShiftController.onSendCommandToTerminal` → `AppShellController` (the same forward-don't-own convention as `bootstrap.onRunCommand`/`vault.onRunCommand`, since neither `CommandLibraryPageView` nor `ShiftController` know anything about the console). **Recent-used tracking** is `CommandLibraryStore.recordUsage(_:)`/`.recentlyUsedCommands(limit:)`, persisted to `commands/recent.yaml` (most-recent-first array, an id moved to the front rather than re-sorted by timestamp - never bitten by two uses landing in the same second) - called only from Copy/Send's own success path, never from mere selection; a "🕐 RECENTLY USED" section renders in the left rail above the category list, same shape as Favorites. **Add/Edit/Duplicate** is `CommandEditorController.swift`, a `presentAsSheet` form mirroring `ShiftTaskEditorController.swift`'s plain-`NSStackView` shape (name/description/category/subcategory/template/tags/risk via an `NSGridView`, parameters as small dynamically add/removable rows - a captain's parameter count is always tiny, nowhere near the row count that would justify an `NSTableView`). `CommandLibraryStore.createCommand`/`updateCommand`/`duplicateCommand` do the actual writes: a new command's slug comes from its name (disambiguated on collision), an edit keeps its existing slug unless category/subcategory changes - which *does* move the file to a new id, migrating favorite/recent-usage state to the new id (`migrateID`) rather than silently orphaning it. **The destructive-confirmation gate** (`CommandLibraryPageView.confirmIfNeeded`) is the one thing both Copy and Send to Terminal route through: `readOnly` never interrupts, `potentiallyDisruptive` gets a lighter dismissible `NSAlert` ("Proceed"), `destructive` gets the mockup's full "⚠️ Destructive command... Copy Anyway"/"Send Anyway" warning - there is no code path from a risky command to the clipboard or a terminal that skips this. **Workflows** are `CommandLibraryWorkflow.swift` (pure logic, no AppKit, no `DocsRunbookStore` dependency of its own - `DiffEngine.swift`'s convention) formatting a command's generated text into the exact fenced-code-block shape `native/Scripts/sre_kubectl_mcp.py`'s `_extract_command_lines` already expects; the detail pane's "+ Workflow" button opens an `NSMenu` ("New Runbook…" plus every existing runbook by title) and either creates a fresh runbook via `DocsRunbookStore.createRunbook` or appends a step to an existing one via `.updateRunbook` - `CommandLibraryPageView` owns its own `DocsRunbookStore()` instance (the established "each page keeps an independent copy of the same underlying check" pattern from `UpdatesController`/`BootstrapController`), never a second execution path for SRE Lead's `run_runbook` to worry about. **Verified live** (temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` + a temporary `@objc` selection hook on `CommandLibraryPageView`, both reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention - `NSAlert`'s buttons were found and clicked via a real view-hierarchy walk while `runModal()` was genuinely blocking, confirming GCD's main-queue dispatch does fire during a modal session): a read-only Copy proceeded with zero interruption and recorded usage; a destructive Copy's Cancel button left the pasteboard and recent-usage state untouched, while its "Copy Anyway" button let the exact same click-path proceed and record usage; Send to Terminal against a real `ConsoleController`'s real shell tab landed the generated command in the tab's actual terminal buffer; and a workflow created from a real command was found and validated by a real, unmodified `sre_kubectl_mcp.py` (`_find_runbook`/`_validate_runbook_line`, invoked from a standalone Python subprocess) with no changes needed to that script. `CommandLibraryStoreSelfTest.swift` was extended in place (not a parallel file) with create/edit/duplicate persisting through a fresh reload (including a category-move migrating favorite/recent-usage state), risk round-tripping through save/reload, and recent-usage ordering/dedup - no regression to `ShiftStoreSelfTest`/`ShiftDateParserSelfTest`/`ShiftWeeklySummarySelfTest`.
- **Explicitly still out of scope (Phase 3)**: any `claude -p` AI action (Explain/Recommend/Generate/Improve/Troubleshoot), Save-from-Terminal's heuristic parameterizer, secret detection before save, fuzzy search, and `⌘⇧P` palette integration.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-visual-polish` closed a real gap between the shipped DevOps Commands/My Tasks/Weekly Review pages and the captain-approved mockup screenshot** (`data/grandline-shift-visual-polish/design-mockup-reference.html` + its screenshot) - a pure rendering/spacing pass, no functional change. Verified via this file's own "Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot" convention: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit) mounted a real `ShiftController` off-screen against scratch stores, drove `viewWillAppear()`/tab switches/a real command selection, and wrote real PNGs to disk for a genuine before/after comparison rather than eyeballing the diff. Fixes: the three-tab switcher (My Tasks/Weekly Review/DevOps Commands) is now wrapped in one bordered/tinted `tabsBackground` container - the same `segmentedBackground` idiom `UpdatesController`'s All/Needs Attention filter already established - instead of three pills floating loose on the page; the Commands search bar got a touch more padding/weight (mockup: the page's one primary entry point); the detail pane's risk pill now sits at the row's trailing edge via a spacer, not crowded against the title; the params grid gutter and left-rail section-divider spacing (via `NSStackView.setCustomSpacing(_:after:)`, widening only the two gaps touching a divider rather than loosening every row) both grew for more breathing room; and the params row spacing was bumped a couple points to match the mockup's `gap-3`. **A real, non-obvious AppKit finding**: `NSButton.contentTintColor` has no effect on a plain-string-title `.rounded`-bezel button (only on an image) - the Favorite button's intended amber/gold tint was silently never rendering; fixed via `attributedTitle` instead (`refreshFavoriteButton()`, called from both a state change and a theme change so the two can't disagree). `NSButton.bezelColor` was also tried for a Copy-as-primary-action accent fill and confirmed live to have zero visible effect at this bezel style/control size - the fix instead uses bold accent-colored text via `attributedTitle`, the same technique that fixed Favorite. Verified across all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes` (light and dark) with no AutoLayout break, plus `FM_RUN_COMMAND_LIBRARY_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_STORE_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_DATE_PARSER_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_WEEKLY_SUMMARY_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_GIT_SYNC_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_CONFLICT_TESTS` all still passing.
- **`fm/grandline-shift-task-row-cards` restyled `ShiftTaskRowView`/`ShiftFollowUpRowView` (`ShiftListViews.swift`) with the same card treatment `fm/grandline-notification-row-redesign` shipped for `NotificationRowView`** (`NotificationCenterPopover.swift`) - a colored left accent bar, a small round icon badge, a bold uppercase kicker, body text, and a trailing chip reusing `ToolRowLayout.pill`. Still the same single-column, view-based `NSTableView` (`ShiftListViews.swift`'s own header explains why - the Diff-tool-era layout blowup this app keeps citing), only the row's internal content changed; row height grew from 44pt to a shared `ShiftTaskListView.rowHeight`/`ShiftFollowUpListView.rowHeight` (74pt) for the extra kicker/meta lines, with `ShiftController`'s `taskFollowUpPanelBodyHeight` (280pt, shared by both panels) deliberately left unchanged - a card list showing ~3-4 rows before scrolling reads fine at that height. Task rows: accent/badge tint is priority, or `.critical` when the task is overdue (a real, computed `dueDate < startOfDay(today)` check - a stronger signal than priority alone, matching the stat tile's own overdue tint); the badge is `ShiftTaskCheckBadge`, a genuine `NSButton` styled as a filled/outlined circle rather than a system checkbox, so click-to-toggle-complete is unchanged behavior, only its drawing changed; the kicker is the task's project name (or a "Task" fallback); the chip is the priority pill. Follow-up rows deliberately swap which signal drives what, since a follow-up has no inline toggle (Done/Reopen/Snooze stay context-menu/double-click only): accent/badge tint is done/pending status (a non-interactive `IconTileView` badge, checkmark or bell), kicker is the status text, chip is the priority pill. **Verified live**: real off-screen-window screenshots (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`, this file's own screenshot convention) across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark` confirmed legible cards with correct per-priority/overdue/status tinting and truncation; a real `NSButton.performClick(nil)` on the row's own checkbox badge confirmed `onToggleCompleted` still fires correctly. **Not verified live in this sandbox**: a genuine double-click event through `NSApp`/`NSWindow` dispatch - `NSApp.isActive`/`win.isKeyWindow` never became `true` even after `activate(ignoringOtherApps:)` plus pumping the run loop, consistent with this file's other "no interactive login session" sandbox caveats (Dictation's audio input, Accessibility trust) - so the double-click path rests on `rowDoubleClicked()`/`onOpen`/`tableView.doubleAction`/`target` being byte-for-byte unchanged by this task (confirmed via diff) plus a `row(at:)` hit-testing check confirming the new checkbox badge doesn't swallow clicks meant for the rest of the row.
- **`fm/grandline-task-editor-redesign` fixed a real theming bug shared by all three Shift editor sheets and redesigned `ShiftTaskEditorController` against a captain-supplied mockup.** **The bug**: `ShiftTaskEditorController`/`ShiftFollowUpEditorController`/`ShiftProjectEditorController` all forced `root.appearance` via `ThemeManager.shared.observe` but never gave the root view an explicit `HelmTheme`-derived background - a plain `NSView` with no `wantsLayer`/`layer.backgroundColor` paints nothing of its own, so the sheet showed through to its default light window backing regardless of the forced appearance (a captain screenshot showed the New Task sheet in plain white system chrome). Fixed the same way `HostEditorController.loadView()` already does it: `root.wantsLayer = true` plus `root.layer?.backgroundColor = HelmTheme.nsColor(theme.backgroundHex).cgColor` inside the same observer - applied to all three sheets, not just the one the captain flagged, since it's the identical gap. **The redesign** (task editor only, per the captain's specific ask - `ShiftFollowUpEditorController`/`ShiftProjectEditorController` were left structurally as-is beyond the theming fix): a large placeholder-styled title field with the natural-language-date hint moved to its own line underneath (instead of baked into the placeholder text), a "DETAILS" section of clickable field cards for Priority/Project (`TaskFieldCardView`, popping an `NSMenu` on click - mirroring `ShiftProjectViews`' own status-pill pattern) replacing the old `NSGridView` label:popup rows, a distinct toggle-row card for "Set due date" (`NSSwitch` + title/subtitle, revealing the existing `NSDatePicker` only once enabled) replacing the old inline checkbox+picker row, and a tags field that commits entered text into small removable chips (reusing Dictation's `VocabularyChipView`/`ChipFlowView` rather than inventing a second chip component) instead of one comma-separated field with no per-tag feedback. `shiftEditorFieldFillColor(for:)` (blends `chromeInkHex` into `chromeBackgroundHex`, mirroring `ShiftController.detailFieldFillColor`/`ConsoleComposerPopover.fieldFillColor`) is what every field card/toggle card/sunken field uses instead of a bare theme token, so they stay visibly distinct from the sheet's own `backgroundHex` root even in the three themes where `chromeBackgroundHex`/`backgroundHex` are numerically identical. The footer's "⌘Enter to save" hint is a real shortcut, not just copy: `save.keyEquivalentModifierMask = [.command]` (the same `ConsoleComposerPopover.generateButton` mechanism) fires via `NSWindow.performKeyEquivalent:` regardless of first responder, which also fixed the pre-existing gap where a plain Return inside the multi-line Description field just inserted a newline and never saved. Every existing behavior - natural-language date detection (`ShiftDateParser`), priority/project selection, save/cancel, and the existing image-attachment feature (`ShiftImageAttachmentWell`, untouched, still out of scope per the brief's confusion about attachments not existing yet - they do, see the `fm/grandline-shift-task-image-attachments` entry above) - was preserved, just re-presented. Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) mounting a real `ShiftTaskEditorController` in a real off-screen `NSWindow` and driving its logic through temporary `debug*` accessors (also reverted): real screenshots across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark` confirmed correct dark/light rendering and card contrast in every theme including the two numerically-identical-token ones; natural-language detection ("tomorrow 3pm" → due controls revealed + a correct "Detected:" confirmation), priority/project card selection, tag commit-to-chip, and due-toggle show/hide were all exercised through the real code paths; and editing an existing task correctly preloaded title/priority/project/tags-as-chips/due into the redesigned controls, with the Save button correctly reading "Save" (vs. "Create Task" for a new one). No regression to `FM_RUN_SHIFT_DATE_PARSER_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_STORE_TESTS`.
- **`fm/grandline-task-editor-layout-fix` fixed two real layout bugs that redesign introduced: a large dead gap between "Set due date" and "TAGS" with the due toggle off, and dead space below the footer with it on.** Root cause, confirmed live via a temporary geometry-dumping probe: `loadView()`'s `root` view had a hardcoded literal frame height (780) that never matched the sheet's real content height (~605-660pt depending on state) - `presentAsSheet` reads that literal frame size verbatim, it does not itself resize a sheet to fit Auto Layout content. With `stack`'s top and bottom both pinned as required constraints to `root`'s edges, the ~140pt mismatch had to be absorbed *somewhere*, and since `stack`'s distribution is the vertical-stack default `.gravityAreas` (never set explicitly - see this file's own NSStackView gravityAreas gotcha above), which has no defined rule for which arranged subview absorbs leftover space, Auto Layout's tie-break picked a different, sibling-content-dependent spot each time: with the due-date picker hidden, `dueCard` (an unconstrained-height plain `NSView`) silently stretched from its natural ~53pt to ~190pt (the dead gap before "TAGS"); with the picker visible, `dueCard` resolved correctly and the same slack instead landed as trailing space after the footer. Fixed with `resizeToFitContent()`: `root`'s width became a real required constraint (520pt, `Self.sheetWidth`) so `view.fittingSize`'s height read is stable, and that fitting height is applied via `view.setFrameSize`/`window.setContentSize` once at the end of `loadView()` and again from every action that can change how much the sheet shows - the due toggle, the natural-language "Detected:" row appearing/dismissing, and a tag chip being added/removed - so the sheet's height keeps tracking real content instead of drifting back into a forced-vs-natural mismatch. Any future editor sheet in this app whose content can show/hide real blocks (not just `ShiftFollowUpEditorController`/`ShiftProjectEditorController`'s fully-static forms) should size itself the same way rather than picking one hardcoded literal frame height and hoping it happens to fit every state. Verified live (temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): real screenshots confirmed no gap in the due-off state and no dead space below the footer in the due-on state, for both a fresh task and editing an existing due-set task; `dueCard`'s frame height matched exactly (53pt) between due-off/due-on after the fix (was 190pt vs 53pt before); repeated toggling produced byte-identical geometry each time (no cumulative drift); adding tags (wrapping to 2 rows) and triggering natural-date detection each grew the sheet correctly with the footer staying flush at the bottom; and a sweep of all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes` (via a direct `applyTheme` call, never `ThemeManager.shared.setTheme`, which would have persisted to this shared machine's real UserDefaults) showed identical, theme-independent geometry with no crash. No regression to `FM_RUN_SHIFT_DATE_PARSER_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_SHIFT_STORE_TESTS`.

- **`fm/grandline-review-page-redesign` brought the Review destination (`ReviewController.swift`) up to the design language every other card-bearing page already uses, closing the gap a captain-supplied Lavish prototype flagged: this page was still a flat list of plain pill-and-button rows with no page header, no stats, and no colour-by-status styling, even though it reads the same `checks` field (green/red/pending/none) every other signal on this page already derived colour from.** Added, all from existing Helm* primitives with no new colours/fonts/spacing: a `HelmType.pageTitle(.serif)` hero ("Ready to merge" - real information, not a restatement of the destination's own name) + live subtitle + a `HelmButton(.quiet)` Refresh; a `statsRow` of three `HelmStatTile`s (open PRs/accent, ready-to-merge/good, checks-running/warn); each forge's `HelmCard` header gained a neutral count badge (the number moved out of the title string) plus a subtitle naming the account/org the PRs in that section belong to, parsed from a real PR's own already-fetched URL (`accountName(from:)`) rather than a hardcoded name; and every PR row is now a `HelmAccentRow` (`chipPlacement: .belowBody`, Review/Merge in `trailingAccessory`) tinted from `checksVisuals(_:)`'s mapping of the real `checks` state, replacing the old hand-rolled pill row that only ever coloured a "no checks" label and left every other row flat grey. No new data source - `refresh()`/`OpenPRsSource.fetch()`/`FleetDataSource.mergedPRs`/the Review/Merge action wiring are all byte-for-byte unchanged. Verified by reading the changed view code against the prototype's `pageReview()` markup (component-for-component) rather than a launched instance - **this app has no OS-level process isolation between separate builds (same bundle identity: `com.firstmate.cockpit.native`), so a build launched from a task worktree can replace or kill the captain's own actively-running instance instead of opening a separate one; do not `swift run`/open the assembled `.app` from a worktree doing UI work on this codebase, compile-check only** - plus real off-screen PNG renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`, this file's own screenshot convention) of the real mounted `ReviewController` against synthetic PR data across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`, confirming per-status row tinting/chip text and stat-tile tinting resolve correctly in each theme - via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit.
- **`fm/grandline-review-page-buttons-fix` closed a real gap that redesign's own gating left behind: a task-tracked PR showed the Merge button even while its checks were still `pending`/`red`, since the gate was only `pr.source == "work"` (equivalently, `pr.taskID != nil` - only a `"work"`-source `MergedPR` ever carries one, per `FleetDataSource.mergedPRs`) with no readiness check at all.** `prRowView`'s Merge condition is now `pr.checks == "green" && pr.taskID != nil` - readiness (the same `checks` value `rebuildStats`'s "ready to merge" tile already sums) combined with the pre-existing technical requirement, which stays real and is worth knowing before loosening this further: `bin/fm-pr-merge.sh` is invoked as `<task-id> <pr-url>` and validates the task id server-side, so a pure forge-discovered PR with no tracked task (`pr.taskID == nil`) has no working merge path through this action regardless of its checks state - `FleetDataSource.mergePR(url:)` has in fact never actually passed a task id to the script (only `url`, confirmed by reading both the Swift call site and the script's own `$#` arg-count guard), so the merge action was already broken end-to-end before this task and remains so - out of scope here, since fixing it needs a design decision about the shape of the call, not a gating tweak. The row's own compact/right-aligned button layout was **not** rebuilt: `HelmAccentRow`'s existing stack-level `.required` hugging on `trailingAccessory` (see this file's Phase 5 note on that same fix, "a 90pt 'Connect' button rendered ~900pt wide") already prevents a lone `Review` button from stretching into a full-width bar - traced through the constraint chain rather than reproduced live, per the compile-check-only convention the paragraph above already establishes for this page.
- **`fm/grandline-review-page-stuck-loading-fix` fixed a real regression the two tasks above left behind: the captain's real running app, with a real, non-trivial open-PR count, got permanently stuck on the "Loading open PRs…" spinner and never rendered the PR list at all, even though the underlying data source (unchanged by either prior task - confirmed via `git log`, neither touched `FleetData.swift`) genuinely had PRs.** **Root cause**: `fm/grandline-review-page-redesign` (#221) replaced each PR row with a `HelmAccentRow` - a deeper nested-`NSStackView` construction (card > row > textStack > titleRow, plus an accent bar, a badge, and a chip) than the flat row it replaced - while leaving every row a *permanent arranged subview of a plain `NSStackView`* (`githubStack`/`bitbucketStack`/`otherStack`). That is the exact "an `NSStackView` with hundreds of arranged subviews blows up far faster than the row count" pathology this codebase has already hit and fixed at least three times before (`DiffResultView.swift`'s ~13.6s-at-~340-rows history, `BlockView.swift`'s ~102s-at-400-blocks history, the Tools-page resize-handler regression) - and #221's own verification used a small *synthetic* PR list (its PR description says so explicitly), so it never exercised the pathology at the captain's real scale. `render(_:)` flips every `isHidden` flag needed to reveal the loaded page (`hasLoadedOnce`'s block) *before* its trailing `view.layoutSubtreeIfNeeded()` call - so once that call's Auto Layout resolve blew up, the main thread never got back around to repainting, and the screen visibly stayed on the last frame it had drawn (the loading skeleton) for as long as the resolve took - "stuck forever on Loading" is what a sufficiently-large real PR list looks like from outside, not a hang in the fetch or a flag that never flips. **Fix**: `ReviewPRListView.swift` is a new demand-driven `NSTableView` (one instance per forge card, wired via `ReviewController.buildSection`'s new `list:` parameter) replacing the plain `NSStackView` - `tableView(_:viewFor:row:)` only builds/measures a `ReviewPRRowCellView` (a reused `HelmAccentRow` + Review/Merge action pair) for rows actually on screen, regardless of the real PR count; each forge's table is still sized to its own full content height (`recomputeHeight`) rather than owning an internal scroller, since the page's own outer `NSScrollView` already carries the whole thing, matching what the `NSStackView` it replaces did. #221's exact row content/styling and #226's exact Merge gate (`pr.checks == "green" && pr.taskID != nil`) are preserved byte-for-byte inside `ReviewPRRowCellView.configure` - this was a rendering-mechanism fix, not a design or gating change. **Verified**: `swift build` (including a full clean rebuild) with no warnings; two new permanent self-tests follow this file's own established convention rather than being launched here (this app has no OS-level process isolation between builds, so even a self-test invocation was avoided in this sandbox, consistent with the "compile-check only" guidance the paragraph above already establishes for this page) - `ReviewControllerLoadingStateSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_REVIEW_LOADING_STATE_TESTS=1`) drives `ReviewController.render(_:)` directly via a small "Probe / self-test surface" (`debugRender`/`debugHasLoadedOnce`/`debugIsLoadingSkeletonVisible`/`debugAreForgeSectionsVisible`/per-forge `debugRowCount`) and asserts the loading -> loaded transition for a populated list, an empty list, a second render not regressing back to the skeleton, and a bounded render time at 80 synthetic PRs; `ReviewPRListVolumeSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_REVIEW_PR_LIST_VOLUME_TESTS=1`) mirrors `BlockViewVolumeSelfTest.swift`'s shape exactly - 400 synthetic PRs (comfortably past the ~300-340-row Diff-tool blowup threshold) measured against a generous wall-clock budget, plus a clear-after-volume case - so a regression back to the `NSStackView` pathology fails this test by timing out, not by crashing.
- **`fm/grandline-review-row-buttons-regression-fix` fixed a third regression on this exact row: #227's table-view port reintroduced the original #221 full-width-button bug, this time via cell reuse rather than the loading-skeleton path.** `ReviewPRRowCellView` (`ReviewPRListView.swift`) builds `reviewButton`/`mergeButton` once and reuses them across every `configure(pr:...)` call as the table dequeues the cell for different PRs, toggling `mergeButton.isHidden` per row - unlike the pre-#227 `prRowView`, which built a brand-new `actionsRow` containing only the buttons that should show, fresh, on every render. That reuse is exactly the shape AGENTS.md gotcha (10) warns about: `actionsRow` was left at the default `.gravityAreas` distribution with no explicit hugging on either button, so "leftover width is resolved by Auto Layout's own tie-breaking - which can drift between runs/rows depending on transient sibling content... even with no code change" - here, whether the *previous* PR dequeued into this same cell had Merge visible. `HelmAccentRow.buildLayout`'s own stack-level hugging on `trailingAccessory` only stops the *outer* row from stretching `actionsRow` wider than its own fitting size; it says nothing about how `actionsRow` distributes width among its own children, which is where the regression actually lived - `reviewButton` stretching to fill the row while `mergeButton` (genuinely `isHidden == false`) got squeezed away, so a "Ready to merge" PR with a real Merge gate visually showed only a full-width Review bar. **Fix**: `actionsRow.distribution = .fill` plus `.required` stack-level hugging/clipping resistance on `actionsRow` itself plus `.required` content hugging/compression resistance on *both* `reviewButton` and `mergeButton` - the exact "required-stack-and-button-hugging trio" `HostsListRecordView`'s own `actions` stack (`HostsListSection.swift`) already established for the identical "reused row, toggling trailing-button visibility" shape; that reference implementation is the one to copy for any future reused-cell row with variable action buttons, not `HelmAccentRow`'s own outer-row fix alone. **Verified**: `swift build` only (compile-check), per this page's now-three-times-established "the captain's real instance may be running and this app shares one bundle identifier, so never `swift run`/launch the assembled `.app`" convention - no live render was attempted. A new permanent self-test, `ReviewPRRowButtonLayoutSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_REVIEW_PR_ROW_BUTTON_LAYOUT_TESTS=1`), drives the real `ReviewPRListView`/`ReviewPRRowCellView` code path (via two new debug accessors, `ReviewPRListView.debugRowButtonState(at:)` and matching properties on the private `ReviewPRRowCellView`) and checks all four contract cases: checks==green+taskID shows both buttons compact and non-overlapping; pending/red checks hides Merge with Review still compact; green checks with no taskID hides Merge; and - the actual regression shape - the same table reused across two `setPRs` calls (Merge visible, then a different PR with Merge hidden) still renders Review compact on the second pass rather than inheriting stale stretched width. Not run in this task (same compile-check-only constraint); it needs a real `swift run`/self-test invocation to actually execute, which the next task touching this page should do in an environment with no live captain instance.

## Overview/Console/Hosts audit fixes (`fm/cockpit-fix-core-audit`)

A QA scout audit (`data/cockpit-audit-core/report.md`) found 11 issues across the Overview, Console, and Hosts rail destinations; this task fixed all of them. Two open product questions the report registered as captain decisions were resolved as: Console tabs adopt the Tools page's numbered-disambiguation naming convention, and IPv6 quick-connect is fixed rather than left broken.

- **`FleetNotifier.start()`** (`FleetNotifier.swift`) now carries the same `guard Bundle.main.bundleIdentifier != nil else { return }` that `UpdatesController`/`ShiftNotifications` already had - it was the one `UNUserNotificationCenter` caller missing it, and crashed the bare dev binary outright once "Bell & notifications" had ever been turned on from the packaged app.
- **`OpenPRsSource.fetch()`** (`FleetData.swift`) now scans project clones concurrently (bounded to 6 in flight via a semaphore + concurrent queue) instead of one at a time - measured ~9.2-10.7s sequential down to ~2.8-4.1s against this machine's real 14 clones. `FleetController.refresh()` also renders `snapshot()`'s fast fields immediately, showing a loading placeholder in "Ready to merge" rather than blocking the whole page on the PR fetch too.
- **`FleetDataSource.crewState`** and **`FirstmateBackend.runBackendScript`/`hasLiveHerdrSession`** now enforce a real timeout (15s and 3s respectively) on their subprocess calls via a `terminationHandler` + `DispatchSemaphore` watchdog (killing the process on timeout where the caller owns it) - the old code's timeout was either a stale comment describing a bound that didn't exist, or no bound at all on a main-thread call.
- **`HostStore.load()`** backs up an undecodable `hosts.json` to `hosts.json.corrupt-<timestamp>` before ever calling `persist()` again, and exposes `loadFailureBackupPath` so `AppDelegate` can toast a one-time warning - previously a corrupted file was silently discarded and then permanently overwritten by the next save.
- **Host/port-forwarding validation** (`HostEditorController.save()`, `PortForwardingController.save()`) now range-checks every port (1-65535), requires a non-empty destination host for Local/Remote forwarding rules, and warns on a duplicate host label at save time (`HostEditorController` takes an `existingLabels` set from `AppDelegate.presentHostEditor`) - all previously silent gaps that only failed later, opaquely, at `ssh` connect time.
- **`HostCatalog.parseQuickConnect`** (`Host.swift`) now handles IPv6 literals correctly: bracket notation (`[::1]:2222`) for an explicit port, and a bare unbracketed literal (`::1`, `2001:db8::1`, more than one colon) is treated as the whole host at the default port instead of being corrupted by splitting on the last colon.
- **Console tab naming** (`TabModel.kindIdentity` + `ConsoleController.numberedName(for:)`) ports the Tools page's convention: bare kind name for the first open tab of a kind, "N" appended for each concurrent one after that (`Shell`, `Shell 2`, `Shell 3`, ...), counting only currently-open tabs so closing one frees its number. Applies to `newShellTab`, `duplicateTab`, `openFirstmateHost`, and `openSSH`.
- **`ConsoleController.renameTab`** now restores focus to `currentTab`, not the tab that was just renamed - right-click Rename on a background tab no longer steals keyboard focus from whatever was actually on screen.
- **`ConsoleController.reconnectActive`**'s `.mirror` branch now waits (via `waitForProcessExit`, a bounded 50ms-interval poll on `tab.terminal.process.running`) for the old tmux/herdr client to actually exit before starting the new one - previously a still-running old process could make SwiftTerm's own `startProcess` guard silently no-op the whole reconnect attempt.
- **`ConsoleController.closeTab`** now tears SRE Lead down (`tearDownSRELead()`) when the closed tab is `primarySSHTab` and SRE Lead is active, matching what `shutdown()` already did for the whole-page-closing case - the pane no longer sits in a stale "ready" state after its one dependent tab is gone.
- Verified against the real launched app (both the bare dev binary and the packaged, signed `.app`) via the project's own "verify without a screenshot" convention (temporary env-gated debug instrumentation calling the exact production code paths, reverted before commit) - not just `swift build`. The exact race in Finding 9 (reconnect vs. an old process not yet reaped) could not be forced in this sandbox; only a non-racing regression check (reconnect still completes) was run.

## Block view

**Third build attempt, Stage 0 of a staged rollout (`fm/cockpit-block-view-stage0`).** Two prior attempts shipped and were fully reverted in one day: PR #79/#80 ("on by default" for `.shell`/`.ssh`) crashed the app on every launch, PR #83's SSH-only redo fixed that crash but shipped a real window-layout corruption bug on a real SSH session and was reverted per captain instruction. See `data/cockpit-block-view-scout/report.md` for the full forensics and staged design, and `data/learnings.md`'s block-view entry for the incident history - read both before touching anything here. `main` had zero block-view code before this stage; this is a fresh build, not a resume of the old branches.

- **Off by default, gated by `BlockViewFeature.isEnabled`** (`BlockViewFeature.swift`) - reads `FM_BLOCK_VIEW_ENABLED` (matching `ConsoleController.defaultLoggingEnabled`'s own env-var convention), never an `AppSettings` default-on toggle. This alone prevents a repeat of PR #79's failure mode (a bug in a niche feature taking down every launch) - the flag must be set explicitly to see any of this code run at all.
- **Gated to one opted-in host, not every SSH page**: `Host.blockViewOptIn: Bool` (`Host.swift`, a checkbox in `HostEditorController`), threaded down through `AppShellController.connectHost` → `ConsoleController.connectSSHIfNeeded`/`openSSH` → `addTab`, which only attaches a `TerminalBlockTracker`/`BlockContainerView` to a tab when `case .ssh` **and** `blockViewOptIn` **and** `BlockViewFeature.isEnabled` are all true. Every other SSH host, the Firstmate console's Shell/Mirror tabs, and any ad-hoc quick-connect (no `Host` behind it at all) are completely unaffected - `TabModel.blockViewOptIn`/`blockTracker`/`blockContainer` stay `false`/`nil` for them, byte-for-byte the same as before this task.
- **Stage 0 scope is deliberately the least possible**: `TerminalBlockTracker` (ported from PR #83, OSC 133 `B`/`D` marker parsing - this part was never the source of either prior break, per the scout report) still tracks `blocks` live as markers arrive, but nothing is wired to `CockpitTerminalView.onDataReceived` (the per-chunk hook the scout report's "Mechanism B" blames for a plausible main-thread stall under real scrollback volume) and nothing auto-renders on a tracker change. `ConsoleController`'s toolbar "Refresh" button (`refreshBlockView`, host pages only) is the *only* thing that calls `BlockContainerView.render(_:)` - between clicks, new blocks can complete in the background with no visible effect. No live streaming of a running block's output, no "Explain this," no re-run/copy actions - those are Stage 1/Stage 2, separate future tasks, intentionally blocked until this stage survives real use.
- **`BlockContainerView` is a single-column, view-based `NSTableView` (`usesAutomaticRowHeights`), not an `NSStackView` of permanent rows** - this is a real, measured fix, not a precaution. The first version of this stage followed the original PR #79/#83 design (`NSStackView`) and this task's own volume self-test measured a **~102-second** render at 400 blocks - the identical class of pathology this file's Diff-tool history documents twice already (the ~13.6s blowup at ~340 rows, and the Tools-page resize-handler regression). Switching to a demand-driven `NSTableView` (`DiffResultView.swift`'s own established fix for the same problem) dropped the same 400-block render to ~47ms. `BlockRowView` (the reusable cell) still adds its arranged subviews to its internal header/output `NSStackView` before activating any constraint referencing that stack - the exact ordering PR #83 traced as break #1's root cause (`NSLayoutConstraint._setActive:` throwing when two views share no common ancestor yet) - with an explicit "do not reorder" comment at the call site.
- **One restart entry point, not two**: `ConsoleController.restartTabBookkeeping(_:)` (calls `tab.blockTracker?.reset()`, `tab.blockContainer?.clear()`, re-sends the shell-integration hook) is called from both `startTab` (the initial-start path, and also what `processTerminated`'s auto-reconnect timer calls directly) and `reconnectActive` (⌘R) - the scout report's confirmed "Mechanism A" gap was that the original attempt only reset block state from `reconnectActive`, so an auto-reconnected SSH session kept a permanently stuck "running" block with the new session's output silently bleeding into it. Stage 0's restart behavior is deliberately the simplest correct thing: wipe and re-parse from scratch on every restart, never try to preserve/patch old state.
- **Four permanent self-tests**, each targeting a different class of prior/potential break (`FM_RUN_BLOCK_VIEW_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_BLOCK_VIEW_HIERARCHY_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_BLOCK_VIEW_RESTART_TESTS`/`FM_RUN_BLOCK_VIEW_VOLUME_TESTS=1 .build/debug/FirstmateCockpit`): `TerminalBlockTrackerSelfTest` (OSC 133 parsing, no view), `BlockViewHierarchySelfTest` (mounts a real `NSWindow`+`BlockContainerView`, drives real Auto Layout - the class of test that catches break #1's regression), `BlockViewRestartIntegrationSelfTest` (drives a real, non-Firstmate `ConsoleController`'s actual `openSSH`/`viewDidAppear`/`startTab`/`reconnectActive` methods - not reimplementations - through both restart shapes and asserts identical clean state; **verified live to actually catch Mechanism A**, not just to pass: temporarily removing `restartTabBookkeeping`'s call from `startTab` made the auto-reconnect-shaped case fail while the manual-reconnect-shaped case kept passing, exactly the asymmetry the scout report describes), and `BlockViewVolumeSelfTest` (400 synthetic blocks - see the render-time finding above; also found `TerminalBlockTracker.bufferLines(_:)`'s `getBufferAsData()`-per-marker parsing cost is real, not just plausible, at ~4.3s for 400 events - worth carrying into Stage 1's design, which reintroduces per-chunk parsing, rather than re-discovering).
- **What was verified live** (real launched app, both the bare dev binary and the packaged/signed `.app`, via this project's established "verify without a screenshot" convention - temporary env-gated debug instrumentation calling the exact production code paths, reverted before commit, since this sandbox has no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission): flag-off with a `blockViewOptIn` host present in the store stays alive with no behavior change; flag-on drives the real `AppDelegate.connectToHost` → `ConsoleController` path for the opted-in host, feeds synthetic OSC 133 bytes into the real `Terminal`, clicks the real Refresh action, and confirms the real `NSTableView` renders exactly the expected row. **Not verified in this sandbox**: a real SSH connection to a real bastion (the captain's own hands-on checklist in the scout report - multiple real reconnects, sustained real output, real scrollback volume - is still pending and explicitly not satisfied by anything in this stage).
