- **Remaining stages, still blocked on Stage 0 merging cleanly and surviving real use first**: Stage 1 (live streaming of a running block's output, reintroducing a debounced `onDataReceived` hook with the output-length cap and diffed-render mitigations the scout report specifies up front) and Stage 2 (interactive actions - re-run, copy, "Explain this"). Neither is scoped or built yet.
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-host-decode-regression` fixed a real production incident this stage's own `blockViewOptIn` field caused**: Swift's compiler-synthesized `Decodable` requires every key listed in a type's `CodingKeys` to be present in the JSON being decoded, regardless of whether the Swift property has its own default value (`= false`) - a default only applies to Swift-side construction (`Host(label:...)`), never to key lookup for a key `CodingKeys` lists. Adding `blockViewOptIn` to `Host`'s `CodingKeys` therefore broke decoding of every `hosts.json` saved before that field existed - the captain's real file, two real hosts, otherwise perfectly valid JSON - and `HostStore.load()` (correctly, by its own existing design) treated the decode failure as file corruption, backed the file up to `hosts.json.corrupt-<timestamp>`, and reset the in-memory host list to empty. The real file on disk was never touched, but the Hosts page showed nothing. **Fix**: `Host` now has a custom `init(from decoder:)` that uses `decodeIfPresent(_:forKey:) ?? <default>` for every field that has a Swift-side default (not just `blockViewOptIn`), so this class of bug can't recur the next time a field is added - `label`/`address` (no default) stay `decode`, required. Declaring a custom `init(from:)` suppresses Swift's synthesized memberwise initializer, so `Host` also grew an explicit memberwise `init(...)` with the same parameter defaults every call site (`Host(label:...)`, self-tests, the host editor) already relied on. **Lesson for any future field added to `Host` (or any `Codable` type with an explicit `CodingKeys` list, e.g. `ShiftTask`/`ShiftFollowUp`/`ShiftProject`): a new field needs an optional type, a custom decode fallback, or a real data migration - a Swift-side default value alone does not protect old on-disk JSON.** Regression coverage: `HostStoreSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_HOST_STORE_TESTS=1`, same convention as `BackupSelfTest.swift`) decodes an old-format JSON blob with no `blockViewOptIn` key at all, drives it through the real `HostStore.load()` path, confirms no corrupt-backup is created, and round-trips a re-save/reload confirming every other field (port, username, agentForward, tags, jumpVia) survives untouched - verified live to actually catch the regression by temporarily reverting the `Host.swift` fix (keeping the test) and confirming the test's exact failure mode reproduces the captain's real bug (decode fails, host list goes empty). Also verified against a read-only copy of the captain's actual real `hosts.json` (never the live file) and against the real launched app's real `HostsController` row content, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention.
- **`fm/cockpit-fix-block-view-stage0-bugs` fixed two real bugs from the captain's first hands-on test of Stage 0 against a real bastion.**
  - **Bug 1 - duplicate/empty blocks**: a real, reproduced root cause - pressing Enter on a blank prompt line (a common habit, and also how the shell hook's *own* installation cycle behaves) still fires `PROMPT_COMMAND`/`precmd`, closing whatever block is currently open with the *previous* real command's text (`history 1`/`fc -ln -1` doesn't change on an empty line) and empty output, indistinguishable in the UI from a genuine repeat. Confirmed via a real pty harness before touching any code (not assumed): typing the same command, then a blank Enter, then the same command again produced 4 `D` markers all carrying that command's base64 text. **Fix**: the hook now also compares the current history number/counter (`history 1`'s leading number for bash, `$HISTCMD` for zsh) against what it saw at its last close, tagging the OSC 133 `D` marker with a 4th field (`exit;base64cmd;is-real`) - `D` still always fires (so `B`/`D` stay strictly 1:1 and nothing gets stuck `.running`), but `TerminalBlockTracker.closeBlock` discards a `0`-flagged close instead of finalizing it. A genuine back-to-back repeat of the same command still gets its own real, populated block (verified: `TerminalBlockTrackerSelfTest`'s `repeatedIdenticalCommandStillProducesTwoRealBlocks`/`blankEnterBetweenTwoRealRepeatsIsDroppedNotTheReals`). **Known, deliberate residual gap** (documented in `ShellIntegration.swift`'s header, not silently accepted): a remote host with `HISTCONTROL=ignoredups`/`ignoreboth` set also suppresses the history entry for a genuine identical repeat, so that case reports `is-real=0` too and its block is silently dropped rather than shown wrong - judged a smaller, more defensible failure mode (show nothing extra) than the bug it replaces (show something misleading). A full fix would need a bash-preexec-style DEBUG-trap marker, which this file's own header already rejected for misfiring on a compound `PROMPT_COMMAND`.
  - **Bug 2 - the install script visibly echoing**: `ConsoleController.installShellIntegrationIfSupported` used to `send(txt:)` the whole multi-hundred-character one-liner straight into the pty, which a real remote tty echoes back before the shell can run it. Fixed with `ShellIntegration.installSequence` (see its own extensive header comment for the full reasoning): a short `stty -echo`/`unsetopt zle` line (visible, small, unavoidable - echo isn't off yet when it's typed), then the real hook base64-encoded and chunked (600 chars/line, comfortably under a real terminal's canonical-mode line cap - measured 1024 bytes via `fpathconf` on this machine, confirmed live that one long combined line hangs the shell once ZLE is off) accumulating into a shell variable, then a final line that evals it and restores echo/ZLE. `ConsoleController.sendShellIntegrationLines` paces each line 0.12s apart - verified live that zsh's parser loses line-boundary tracking entirely (drops into a `dquote>` continuation prompt) if every line is sent with zero gap. Verified clean (no visible script text, only the one short leading line) via the real `ConsoleController` production path for both bash and zsh across roughly a dozen live end-to-end passes. **Known residual risk, zsh-specific, left honestly documented rather than force-fixed**: exactly once, under this dev machine's own real starship-powered zsh, the full payload leaked visibly instead of just the one expected line - not reproduced again across ~9 further identical passes or a dozen isolated pty probes trying to isolate which line caused it. This pattern points at a race with an async prompt segment (starship forks a background job per prompt) rather than a deterministic bug - rare, not systemic, and not fully eliminable without either accepting the always-visible original behavior or reaching into a specific third-party prompt framework's own job scheduling, which is out of scope. A captain on zsh with an async/background-job prompt (starship, powerlevel10k's async mode, similar) should expect this to be reliable but not airtight.
  - Both fixes verified against the real `ConsoleController` production path (real `addTab`/`startTab`/`installShellIntegrationIfSupported`, real 1.5s + 0.12s timers, a real PTY-backed child process), not just `HeadlessTerminal` - via a temporary env-gated probe (`ConsoleController.debugOpenLocalShellAsSSHTab`/`debugSendToCurrentTab`/`debugCurrentTracker`, reverted before commit per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) that substitutes a real local `/bin/bash`/`/bin/zsh` for the ssh executable, since this sandbox has no reachable bastion - the same "real PTY child, not a real network hop" substitution `BlockViewRestartIntegrationSelfTest` already uses. All four existing block-view self-tests still pass unmodified.
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-per-tab` moved SRE Lead's whole state off `ConsoleController` and onto each `.ssh` tab (`TabModel.sreLead: SRELeadTabState?`, `SRELeadTabState.swift`) - one independent investigation per tab, not one page-level session pinned to `primarySSHTab` (the first ssh tab a host page ever opened, per that field's own now-removed doc comment).** Duplicating a tab (⌘D) or reaching a second server on a dedicated host page previously meant every kubectl injection and every question still targeted tab 1, silently - the design doc that motivated this (`data/grandline-sre-lead-per-tab/design-reference.html`, captain-approved) is Option A there: state on `TabModel`, mirroring `blockTracker`/`blockContainer`'s existing per-tab ownership exactly, rejected Option B (a `[UUID: SRELeadTabState]` dictionary on the controller) since every other per-tab feature in this app already lives on `TabModel` and gets free cleanup on tab removal that way. `ConsoleController` now owns only the shared chrome - the pill, the one fixed slide-open `sreLeadPane`, the header, "Generate Postmortem," and a new shared empty state ("Start SRE Lead for This Tab," `sreLeadEmptyStateView`) - and every started tab's own `SRELeadChatView` is added as a hidden sibling inside the pane, shown/hidden to match whichever tab is `currentTab` (`updateSRELeadPaneContent`, called from `select()`/`applyTheme()`/every phase transition via `updateSRELeadControls()`) - this app's usual "hide, don't rebuild" convention. **Off by default for every tab, including a freshly duplicated one** - a new `TabModel` always starts with `sreLead == nil`; SRE Lead only starts when the captain toggles it on for that specific tab, via the toolbar pill (`toggleSRELead`, now operating on `currentTab`) or the pane's own empty-state Start button (`startSRELeadForCurrentTabClicked`) - both call the same `startSRELead(for:)`, which targets that tab's own terminal as the `SRELeadBridge`'s injection point, never any other tab's. **A captain-specified cap: at most 5 tabs on one page may run SRE Lead (`.starting`/`.ready`) at once** - `activeSRELeadTabCount()` backs it, a 6th attempt shows a clear `NSAlert` ("SRE Lead limit reached... stop SRE Lead on another tab first") rather than silently queuing or refusing, and the pill's own tooltip explains the cap state up front via `updateSRELeadButtonTooltip`. `closeTab()`'s old `if tab === primarySSHTab` special case is gone - **every** closed tab with `sreLead != nil` tears down unconditionally (`tearDownSRELead(for:)`, stopping that tab's own bridge timer, cancelling its own in-flight `claude` process, and removing its own scratch dir - never a sibling's); the shared pane only collapses closed once no tab on the page has any SRE Lead state left (`anySRELeadTabHasState()` - **removed by `fm/grandline-sre-lead-polish`, see the follow-up bullet below**), so tearing down or closing one tab never disturbs a sibling tab's still-running investigation or yanks the pane out from under it. "Generate Postmortem" (`fm/grandline-sre-lead-postmortem`) is unchanged in spirit, just rebased onto `currentTab.sreLead?.chatView` and `currentTab.name` (was `primarySSHTab?.name`) instead of the removed page-level fields. **Verification**: `SRELeadBridgeSelfTest.swift` gained `twoConcurrentBridgesNoCrossTalk` - two independent bridges/dirs/fake terminals, interleaved ticks, confirming neither's request or response (or terminal content) ever crosses into the other, the exact risk the design doc flagged as "should be safe by construction, but not yet actually proven." A new permanent integration self-test, `SRELeadPerTabSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_SRE_LEAD_PER_TAB_TESTS=1`, same convention as `BlockViewRestartIntegrationSelfTest.swift` - drives the real `ConsoleController`'s real `startSRELead(for:)`/`handleSRELeadSubmit(_:in:)`/`tearDownSRELead(for:)`/`closeTab`/`select` methods against a real multi-tab host page, not reimplementations), covers: two tabs reaching `.ready` independently and a fake `claude` script (`SRELead.claudePathOverrideForTests`, same seam as `DictationCleanup`/`SRELeadPostmortem`/`ConsoleCommandComposer`) answering two concurrent distinct questions with zero cross-talk in either chat; switching to a not-yet-started tab showing the empty state (never the other tab's transcript or a blank pane) and switching back showing the original transcript intact; the 5-tab cap refusing a real 6th attempt with no crash and no silent start; and closing one tab leaving a sibling's `.ready` session, transcript, and open pane completely undisturbed. **Verified this last case actually catches a regression, not just passes**: temporarily disabling the cap guard reproduced a 6th tab starting anyway and the corresponding self-test case failing with the exact wrong active-count, confirmed, then reverted.
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-polish` fixed two captain-reported UI/UX bugs in the per-tab feature above.** (1) **Pane open/closed state now tracks the *currently selected* tab's own `sreLead`, not "does any tab on the page have state"**: `updateSRELeadPaneContent()` (the one place `select()`/`applyTheme()`/every phase transition already funnels through via `updateSRELeadControls()`) now also calls `setSRELeadPaneOpen(current.sreLead != nil)` on every invocation - so creating/switching to a fresh or duplicated tab with no `sreLead` state shows the pane fully CLOSED (not the open empty state), and switching back to a tab that has one (running, ready, or failed-with-a-visible-error) reopens it showing that tab's own preserved chat. The old `anySRELeadTabHasState()` helper and its call in `tearDownSRELead` (which only closed the pane once *no* tab anywhere had state) are gone, along with the redundant `setSRELeadPaneOpen(true)` in `startSRELead` - one mechanism decides pane-open state now, not two that could disagree. (2) **The pane is now a visually distinct surface, not the same fill as the terminal**: `sreLeadPane`/`sreLeadEmptyStateView` are filled with `theme.chromeBackgroundHex` (this app's "surface" token) instead of `theme.backgroundHex` (the terminal's own token) - **but this pass missed `SRELeadChatView`, which is what actually fills the pane once a tab has a chat, so the fix only ever applied to the not-yet-started empty state; fixed in `fm/grandline-visual-polish-round2`**, the boundary separator (`sreLeadPaneSeparator`) widened from 1pt to 3pt and re-tinted with `theme.accentHex` instead of the subtle `chromeLineHex`, and a new 1pt `sreLeadHeaderDivider` was added between the header and body since both now share the same `chromeBackgroundHex` fill. **A real finding worth knowing before touching this again**: `chromeBackgroundHex` is NOT guaranteed distinct from `backgroundHex` in every theme - checked live across all 12 `HelmTheme.allThemes`, they're numerically identical in `gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`tokyo-night-light` (3 of 12). The fill-color fix alone would have silently failed to fix the melding complaint in exactly those three themes; the bold `accentHex`-tinted separator bar is what actually guarantees an unmistakable boundary in every theme regardless of whether the two fills happen to match, since every theme's accent is a deliberately bold, saturated color far from either background shade. Any future change to this pane's coloring should keep relying on that separator (or an equivalently theme-proof signal), not just on the two fills differing. **Verified**: `SRELeadPerTabSelfTest.swift`'s `tabSwitchRebindsPaneToCurrentTab` case was extended with explicit `debugSRELeadPaneOpen()` assertions (open for a started tab, closed for a tab with no state, reopened on switching back) - confirmed live to actually catch the regression by temporarily reverting the fix and seeing exactly this case fail (a 6th-tab-cap-style regression check, not just a passing test); the theme-token-parity finding above was confirmed via a temporary env-gated probe dumping every theme's `chromeBackgroundHex`/`backgroundHex`/`accentHex` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention).
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-separator-fix` fixed a real follow-up bug in the same separator: it was only visible next to the header text, not along the pane's full height.** Root cause: `sreLeadPaneSeparator`'s own constraints spanned the full pane height correctly, but `sreLeadEmptyStateView` and every tab's `chatView` (`makeSRELeadChat`) had their `leadingAnchor` pinned to `sreLeadPane.leadingAnchor` - the same edge the separator itself starts from - rather than `sreLeadPaneSeparator.trailingAnchor` like `sreLeadHeader` already did. Since both views are added to `sreLeadPane` after the separator, AppKit's default z-order let their own opaque `chromeBackgroundHex` fill paint directly over the separator for the whole height below the header row. Fixed by anchoring both to `sreLeadPaneSeparator.trailingAnchor` instead - one-line-each, matching the header's own existing anchor. Verified live (temporary env-gated probe, reverted before commit): dumped real frames for the separator/header/empty-state/chat views in both the empty-state and started-chat cases, confirmed every other view's `minX` sits at or past the separator's `maxX` in both, and confirmed the probe actually catches the regression by temporarily reverting the fix and seeing the empty-state/chat views' `minX` drop to 0 (covering the separator) before re-applying it.
- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-app-feel` gave the SRE-Lead-active layout a real "app" shape: with SRE Lead up on the current tab, the terminal renders as a rounded, bordered, elevated card beside the SRE Lead panel, which is now a `HelmCard` surface of its own (captain reference: `data/grandline-sre-lead-app-feel/reference-mockup.html` plus two screenshots in that folder's `captain-screenshots/`; read the mockup, not just the screenshot - its real CSS is the precise source for the spacing/radius shape).** Rendering only; every terminal, tab and SRE Lead behaviour is untouched.
  - **The whole design is shaped by one hard constraint: the terminal's frame must never change when SRE Lead toggles.** That is the bug `fm/cockpit-sre-lead-ux-fixes` fixed once already (a frame change on a SwiftTerm view triggers `resize(cols:rows:)`, which reflows the buffer and can truncate a captain's scrollback), and it is why the pane overlays `content` rather than pushing it. So the tempting implementation - wrap the terminal in a card view and inset it when the pane opens - is exactly what must not be built. Measured, not assumed: building it reproduced 98 cols -> 57 cols and a real buffer change (19208 -> 19509 chars) on open.
  - **How the card exists anyway.** Every terminal on a **host page** is inset from `content` by a *permanent* `ConsoleController.terminalInset` (`cardMargin` 12 + `cardInnerPadding` 8 = 20pt, fixed for the controller's lifetime), and `ConsoleCardChrome` - a hit-test-transparent overlay that is always `content`'s topmost subview - draws the workspace floor, both cards' drop shadows and the terminal card's border on top of it. Toggling SRE Lead only flips that overlay's `isHidden`/`needsDisplay`, so it is a pure appearance change. With the pane closed the inset band is `content`'s own `backgroundHex` - the terminal's *own* background colour - so it reads as ordinary terminal padding, not a card. `terminalInset` is **0 on the shared Firstmate console**, which can never show SRE Lead, keeping the Shell tab and the tmux/herdr Mirror tab byte-for-byte flush at full column count.
  - **`ConsoleCardChrome.hitTest` returning `nil` is load-bearing, not tidiness** - proven by removing it: the overlay then swallowed every click in the terminal area, so the terminal would go dead whenever the pane was up.
  - **The card's drawn trailing edge stops `gap` short of the pane**, so the terminal's rightmost ~1.5 columns are covered while SRE Lead is open - alongside the ~45 the pane itself has always covered. Nothing is covered on the other three edges, because the terminal is genuinely inset there. That is the deliberate price of never resizing.
  - **The 3pt `accentHex` separator bar is gone.** It existed because `chromeBackgroundHex == backgroundHex` in three of the twelve palettes, so a fill difference alone could not prove the pane was a separate surface (`fm/grandline-sre-lead-polish`). The replacement is structural rather than tonal - a real 12pt gap of workspace floor with a 1pt outline down each side of it - which no palette can collapse, confirmed in real renders of `tokyo-night-dark` and `gruvbox-light` (two of those three). `sreLeadPane` is now just a transparent clipping backdrop; the visible panel is `sreLeadCard`, which clips its own children so the header and chat inherit its rounded corners with no per-child corner masking. Its header gained the mockup's identity block (a `sparkles` `IconTileView` + title + a live phase pill through the shared `ToolRowLayout.pill`).
  - **`HelmCard.elevation(for:)` is the app's one floating-card shadow**, deliberately *not* applied by `applyCardSurface` (every other card in this app is flat on purpose, and `applyCardSurface` is usually paired with clipping, which kills a layer shadow). It is drawn by `ConsoleCardChrome` for both cards using the CG "clip to outside the path, then fill the path" technique, which is the only way to cast an outward shadow onto already-painted pixels without an inner-shadow artefact inside the card.
  - **Decided and worth not re-litigating: no traffic-light header strip inside the terminal card.** The mockup shows one, but the app already has a real tab strip immediately above `content` whose chip carries exactly what that header's title shows, macOS draws the real window controls at the window level (a second set inside a card would be non-functional decoration), and a permanent 42pt strip would either steal rows from every tab forever or force a frame change on toggle - the one thing forbidden here.
  - **Verified**: `SRELeadPerTabSelfTest.scrollbackSurvivesSRELeadToggle` is the permanent guard - it seeds 300 real lines into a real tab, drives the real start/teardown, and diffs the whole buffer *and* `cols`/`rows`; **both halves were confirmed to catch the regression independently**, not just to pass. `HelmContrastSelfTest.checkConsoleCardChromeGeometry` pins the card geometry (card edges on the terminal's inset, exactly one `s3` gap, equal window margins, empty rects at degenerate sizes) and was likewise confirmed to fail on a real regression. Plus real off-screen renders of the real mounted host page across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`/`solarized-light` in four states each (SRE Lead off, empty state, active with a real Finding/Recommendation exchange, off again) and of the Firstmate console for the zero-inset case, with the terminal's frame measured identical across every toggle and hit-testing confirmed to reach the real `CockpitTerminalView`. All 28 self-test suites pass.
  - **Two probe lessons that cost real time here, both generalisable.** (1) **A SwiftTerm `TerminalView` never calls `draw(_:)` at all in a window that was never `orderFront`ed**, so an off-screen probe captures an empty terminal that looks exactly like a rendering bug - `orderFront(nil)` the probe window first. (2) Even then, **an ancestor's `cacheDisplay` does not capture the terminal's glyphs** (the same view cached on its own is full of them), so a full-page render has to cache the terminal separately and composite it into the card rect - a probe artefact, not an app defect, and worth knowing before chasing a "blank terminal" that is really a capture limitation.
- **`fm/grandline-input-composer-redesign` gave SRE Lead's question box and the Console Composer's intent box a real "composer card" treatment, replacing a bare flat field/textarea in both (captain screenshots + `data/grandline-input-composer-redesign/reference-mockup.html`).** `HelmComposerCard` (`HelmUIComponents.swift`) is the one new shared component - a rounded, `HelmField.fill`-sunken card whose border/glow brightens (`accentHex`, via a `layer.shadowColor`/`shadowPath` on an un-clipped outer wrapper around a clipped, bordered `contentContainer`) while a text control inside it has focus. Both callers build their own toolbar/footer inside `contentContainer`, per the captain's own "related, but not identical" framing - the component owns only the card, not a text field or a submit button. `SRELeadChatView`'s composer is now: a small uppercase kicker ("ASK A QUESTION"), the card holding a genuinely multi-line, auto-resizing `NSTextView` (grows between `minTextHeight`/`maxTextHeight` by hand-adjusting a height constraint on `textDidChange`, since `NSTextView` has no such behaviour built in) and a toolbar row with a `HelmButton(.primary)` send button - `.primary`'s own `isEnabled` dimming is exactly the "muted until there's real text" behaviour, no extra styling needed. Plain Return still submits (unchanged behaviour); Shift+Return inserts a newline, a purely additive capability a single-line field never had room to offer. No left-side toolbar icons and no separate "connection status" chip were added - neither has a real backing action/signal that isn't already shown by the pane's own header pill (`ConsoleController.sreLeadStatusPill`), and adding either would have been inventing functionality or duplicating a signal already visible a few dozen points above, against this task's explicit "layout redesign, not a functional change" scope. `ConsoleComposerViewController`'s intent field moved onto the same card (kicker "COMMAND INTENT", the textarea plus a footer row of the `⌘⏎` hint + primary Generate button, both now inside `contentContainer` instead of the old separately-bordered `intentScroll`), and gained one cheap, real addition: a `ChipFlowView` (reused verbatim from `DictationHistoryAndVocabularyViews.swift` rather than a second wrap-layout implementation) of three static, generic example intents that fill the field on click - deliberately generic/non-fabricated, matching the reference mockup's own hardcoded examples.
  - **A real, non-obvious AppKit bug found and fixed along the way, worth knowing before adding another floating placeholder label on a scroll view**: a placeholder `NSTextField(labelWithString: "")` whose real text is assigned later via `.stringValue =` (rather than at construction) can permanently resolve to a near-zero width (`intrinsicContentSize` reports the correct value when queried directly, but the auto-generated `NSContentSizeLayoutConstraint` backing the view's actual layout never re-resolves to it, with no Auto Layout ambiguity/conflict warning printed) when that label is a bare subview of an `NSScrollView` rather than an arranged subview of an `NSStackView` (which recomputes intrinsic size fresh on every layout pass instead of relying on that cached constraint). Confirmed by direct comparison against `ConsoleComposerViewController.intentPlaceholderLabel`, which had never hit this because its real placeholder text was always passed straight into `NSTextField(labelWithString:)` at construction, never reassigned. Fix: construct the label with its final text up front (`SRELeadChatView.textPlaceholderLabel` no longer needs a runtime `.stringValue =` at all, since the placeholder text is static) - any future placeholder label added directly to a scroll view (not through a stack) should do the same, or verify the bug isn't reproducing if `.stringValue` genuinely must be set after construction. A second, smaller ordering gotcha found in the same investigation: activate a floating overlay label's constraints once its parent is embedded in the real view tree (in the outer method's own final `NSLayoutConstraint.activate([...])` block), not eagerly inside the smaller builder method that constructs it while it's still an orphaned view - moving the activation didn't fix the width bug above on its own, but is the pattern `ConsoleComposerViewController` already used and is safer regardless.
  - **Verified** with real off-screen PNG renders (`bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`) of both composers - empty, focused (a real accent-glow border, confirmed rendering correctly per theme), and with real typed/generated text - across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`, via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) that also caught and confirmed the fix for the placeholder bug above. **One accepted probe limitation, not re-litigated**: `HelmButton` self-observes the real global `ThemeManager.shared.theme` (Phase 2's own design), so a probe that calls `composer.applyTheme(theme)` directly for a theme other than whatever is genuinely active on the machine gets a correctly-recoloured card/text but `HelmButton`-based pieces (send/Generate/Copy/Run/example chips) rendered in the real active theme regardless - confirmed by comparing two "different-theme" renders and finding their buttons identical. This is a known, already-documented trade-off (see the Notification Center section's own probe note) for not calling `ThemeManager.shared.setTheme` on a shared dev machine, not a defect in either composer's own theming. No regression to `SRELeadPerTabSelfTest`/`SRELeadMarkdownSelfTest`/`SRELeadBridgeSelfTest`/`ConsoleCommandComposerSelfTest`/`HelmContrastSelfTest`/`NotificationCenterSRELeadSelfTest`.

## Vault

**`.vault` (`fm/grandline-vault-tab`) is a rail destination pinned directly above `.docs` and below `.tools`** (so the bottom-anchored utility group reads Tools, Vault, Docs, Updates, Bootstrap, Settings), embedding Automic Vault (https://github.com/automic-vault/automic-vault) rather than reimplementing its security model - the same "thin native window onto another system" pattern this whole app already follows for firstmate/herdr/Homebrew.

- **`VaultData.swift` is the only code that shells out to the real `av` CLI**, mirroring `UpdatesData.swift`/`NotSyncedData.swift`'s `Process`+`Pipe` plumbing exactly. `av list`/`av doctor --json` only ever return secret *names* and tool metadata (confirmed live - never a value), so those run as an ordinary background check. Install/update reuses `UpdatesSource.check`/`.update` on the *existing* `DependencyCatalog` "automic-vault" entry (`UpdatesData.swift`, Security category) - not a second brew-cask implementation.
- **`av save NAME` is never run as a background `Process` - confirmed live it fails outright ("failed to open /dev/tty") when piped a value via stdin**, since it reads the secret value from the real terminal's own tty. `VaultController` only ever builds the command *string* (`VaultSource.saveSecretCommand(name:)`, name-only, value never touches this app) and runs it through `AppShellController.runInConsole` - the same real Console-tab mechanism every sudo/interactive Bootstrap/Settings action already uses. `av inject +NAME -- cmd` is confirmed live to work fine as a background process (Automic Vault's own approval prompt, when one fires, is handled by its separate menu-bar app, not `/dev/tty`) but is *still* routed through the same Console tab rather than captured by this app, since the injected command is free text and may print anything - Grand Line must never be the thing that logs a command's real output.
- **`VaultSource.isSafeToken` is a conservative allowlist (letters/digits/underscore/dash)** gating what's allowed to be spliced unquoted into the `-lc` shell string `openCommandTab` runs - stricter than whatever `av save` itself accepts, since the only job here is "safe with no quoting," not "matches every name av would allow."
- **No secret value is ever stored, cached, or logged by this app** - every listed secret gets a static "Hardened" label (true by construction: `av list` only returns Keychain-backed entries), and `av doctor --json`'s per-tool `issues` count drives a status pill plus the page's one quiet-until-it-matters attention banner (hidden whenever no tool has real issues - no polling, no fake liveness, per `PRODUCT.md`).
- **Verified live against the real `av` CLI on this machine** (real secrets, real "claude" launcher, real brew-cask "Update Available" status) via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`/`AppShellController`/`VaultController` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) - `av save`/`av inject`'s Console-tab path was verified by code review + the isolated `/dev/tty` and background-process probes above, not by triggering a real interactive save/inject from the running app in this sandbox.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-copy-name` added a "copy secret name" icon button to each secret row**, pinned to the row's true trailing edge (captain-specified placement, matching the "+ Add Secret" header button) rather than clustered next to the pill/Run injected button - achieved by wrapping `ToolRowLayout.build`'s returned row in an outer `.fill`-distribution `NSStackView` with the row set to `.defaultLow` horizontal hugging and the copy button to `.required`, the same pin-to-edge pattern this file's AppKit gotcha catalogue already documents. Copies only the name string already shown in the row (`NSPasteboard.general`) - never a secret value, which this app has no access to regardless (see the bullets above).
- **`fm/grandline-vault-ui-polish` reverted the two decisions above after live captain feedback with a screenshot.** The page-level `titleLabel` ("Vault", 22pt) duplicated the topbar's own destination title (`AppShellController.show(.vault)` already calls `topBar.setTitle("Vault")`) - removed, matching Tools/Updates/Bootstrap/Settings/Overview, none of which repeat their destination name in-page (only Docs/Hosts/Review still do, and this page didn't need to match those). The copy control moved from the row's pinned trailing edge into `ToolRowLayout.build`'s own `trailingViews` array (right after "Run injected...", both grouped together as the row's action buttons) and changed from an icon-only borderless button to a real bordered `NSButton(title: "Copy Name", ...)` at `.controlSize = .small`, matching "Run injected..."'s own style - same clipboard behavior (name only, never a value), purely a presentation change.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-row-polish` gave `ToolRowLayout` two new opt-in parameters - `cardStyle`/`attentionHex` - rather than forking a Vault-specific row style**, after live captain feedback that the Secrets/Verified Launchers rows looked cramped with no visual separation. `cardStyle: Bool = false` on `build()`/`applyTheme()` (default preserves every existing caller - Updates, Bootstrap - byte-for-byte) switches a row from "flat, hover-only highlight" to a persistent `chromeBackgroundHex` fill + `chromeLineHex` border and roomier internal padding (4pt -> 14pt/12pt) - the same card tokens `ShiftPanelView.applyTheme` already uses, not a new color scheme. `attentionHex`, meaningful only with `cardStyle`, tints a specific row's border for a real "needs attention" state. Vault's secret and tool rows now pass `cardStyle: true`; the tools list's `needsAttention` state also moved from red/`.critical` to amber/`.warn` (`attentionHex: theme.ansiHex[3]`) - red stays reserved for a genuine check/update failure elsewhere on this same page, and a tool with real `av doctor` issues isn't broken the same way. **A per-secret "Read Only"/"Approval Required" access-level pill (as shown in a captain-reviewed mockup) was deliberately NOT added** - `fm/grandline-vault-recipe-backup` (below) already confirmed live that `av` has no CLI surface exposing a secret's or tool's configured Access Level, so rendering those pills would mean inventing data Automic Vault never reports; secrets stay "Hardened"/green (the one real state `av list` implies) and launchers stay Hardened/green vs needs-attention/amber. Verified live via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift` (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) that seeded two real secrets and two real tools (one needing attention) and dumped every row's real `HoverHighlightView` layer background/border color and `IconTileView` frame across `helm-dark`/`helm-light` - confirmed card rows render a real non-clear background + 1pt border (vs. the install-status row above them, which correctly stays borderless/transparent), consistent 10pt inter-row spacing, uniform 34x34 icon tiles, and the amber attention border firing only on the needs-attention tool row in both themes.
- **`fm/grandline-setup-attention-row-style` added a third opt-in `ToolRowLayout` parameter, `accentBar` (`build()`'s geometry always wired up hidden; `applyTheme(accentBar:)` shows/colors it only when `cardStyle && accentBar && attentionHex != nil`)** - the colored left accent strip `NotificationRowView`/`ShiftTaskRowView` use, generalized via two new standalone helpers, `ToolRowLayout.attachAccentBar`/`.setAccentBar`, so a page with its own bespoke small container that doesn't use `ToolRowLayout.Views` at all (`AutomationController`'s software-checklist chips) can reuse the identical idiom. Default `false` everywhere, so Vault's own `cardStyle: true` rows (every row, not just flagged ones) are unaffected. Wired into the four dense-checklist pages (Updates, Bootstrap's drift card, Automation's stepper + chip grid, GitHub Sync) so only the row(s) actually flagged pick up the card fill/border/accent-bar treatment - a healthy row stays exactly as flat/compact as before this task. Each page reuses whatever "needs attention" predicate it already had (`DependencyStatus.showsUpdateButton` for Updates, `GitHubSyncStatus.showsSyncButton || failed` for GitHub Sync, `AutomationStepStatus.failed`/`.waitingForCaptain` and a tool chip's `checkFailed`/`updateFailed` for Automation) - no new detection logic anywhere. **A real constraint this task had to design around**: `UpdatesController`/`GitHubSyncController` build each row's `Views` exactly once at page load and only ever call `applyTheme` again on a status change (calling `build()` a second time on the same `Views` instance would leave a stale, dangling `column`/`topRow` subview behind, the exact class of bug this file's AppKit-gotcha catalogue already documents for a rebuilt container) - so `cardStyle`'s bigger padding/corner-radius (baked into `build()`) never applies to those two pages' attention rows, only the fill/border/accent-bar (all purely `applyTheme`-driven, safe to flip repeatedly). Bootstrap's drift card and Automation's stepper/chip grid fully tear down and rebuild on every render, so their attention rows get the full `cardStyle: true` treatment (bigger padding included) at `build()` time too. Automation's stepper additionally tracks `stepAccentBars: [(bar: NSView, hex: (HelmTheme) -> String)]` so a live theme switch (`ThemeManager.shared.observe`, which calls `applyTheme()` alone, never `rebuildStepper()`) can re-derive each flagged step's bar color instead of it going stale - the chip grid doesn't need the equivalent, since it's always fully rebuilt by the time any theme-driven re-render would need it (a pre-existing, unrelated staleness quirk in the chip's own fill/dot/border colors on a pure theme switch predates this task and was left alone, out of scope). Verified via this file's "Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot" convention (real off-screen `bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay` PNGs, reverted before commit) across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`/`tokyo-night-dark`, confirming the accent bar/card fill/border render only on seeded attention rows (an update-available/check-failed Updates row, a behind/diverged GitHub Sync row, every row in Bootstrap's drift card, a failed/needs-you Automation step, and a checkFailed/updateFailed software chip) with zero visual change to every healthy row alongside them.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-header-and-avatar-divider` removed Vault's own install-status row entirely** (icon/name/version/pill/Check/Install-Update buttons at the top of the page) after captain feedback that it duplicated the real one on the Updates and Bootstrap pages - the Vault page now starts directly at "Secrets." The underlying `VaultSource.checkInstall()` check is still called (headlessly, no UI) purely to decide whether the Secrets/Verified Launchers sections have anything to show when `av` genuinely isn't installed; `VaultSource.updateInstall()` is untouched and still used by Updates/Bootstrap. Also added the one rail-divider boundary that was missing: a hairline `NSBox(.separator)` between Settings (the last utility row) and the avatar pinned at the bottom of `IconRailController`, matching every other boundary in that bottom-up anchor chain (see the `fm/grandline-rail-utility-separators` bullet above). Verified live in both `helm-dark`/`helm-light` via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) that walked the real view hierarchies confirming no install-row identifier/labels/buttons remain on Vault and exactly one non-zero-frame, visible separator sits between Settings and the avatar.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-recipe-backup` added "backup the recipe, not the values"**: a new "Recipe Backup" panel (`VaultController.buildRecipeSection`) with "Export Recipe" and "Check Against Backup" buttons. `VaultRecipe.swift` (pure logic, no AppKit/`Process`) builds a `VaultRecipe` from a `VaultSnapshot` - secret *names* (`av list`) and per-tool hardened status + launcher command names (`av doctor --json`, i.e. the existing `VaultTool.commands`) - and `VaultRecipeChecklist.build` diffs a stored recipe against a live snapshot into matches/missing/new-since-backup per item, the "missing" case being the one the task cared about (what to redo after a wipe). **A real CLI-surface finding, not an assumption**: `av --help`/`av doctor`/`av hardeners --json`/the `automic-vault` README's "Secret Gates"/"Access Levels" section were all checked directly, and Automic Vault has no command that reports a secret's or tool's configured Access Level or a captain's own per-Launcher override - that state lives only in the menu-bar app's own local store (confirmed by reading its source), never surfaced to the CLI. `av hardeners --json`'s `secret_gate.routes[].caller_identifiers` looked promising but is a static built-in catalog value, identical on every install, not a captain-configured list - so the recipe honestly records only what `av` actually exposes, rather than fabricating an access-level field. `VaultRecipeGit.swift` writes the JSON into `automatic-vault-details-backup/automic-vault-recipe.json` inside the captain's real `manjesh-config` clone - located via `DotfilesSource.resolvedDotfilesPath()` (the same `~/.dotfiles` symlink Bootstrap's dotfiles card already resolves; failing with a message pointing at that card when it doesn't exist yet, rather than a second hardcoded path) - then commits and pushes with a plain `git add`/`commit`/`push` against that already-configured working tree, reusing `ShiftGitSync.swift`'s exact Basic-auth-via-`GIT_CONFIG_*`-env-vars shape (`DocsSyncSource.ghAuthToken()`) for the push step only; no separate managed clone or merge logic is needed since this operates directly on the captain's own checkout. Verified live end to end against a disposable local bare-repo remote (never `manjesh-config` itself): export wrote the file, committed, and pushed (confirmed via `git show <sha>:<path>` on the bare repo), a second export with no changes correctly reported "nothing to push" instead of an empty commit, and a decoded-and-diffed round trip matched byte-for-byte. `VaultDataSelfTest.swift` covers `VaultRecipe.build`/`VaultRecipeChecklist.build`'s pure logic permanently (JSON round trip, missing/matches/new-since-backup against a simulated post-wipe snapshot).
- **`fm/grandline-vault-export-push-fix` fixed a real captain-reported push rejection on "Export Recipe"** (`git push` failing with `[rejected] HEAD -> main (fetch first)` whenever the captain's `manjesh-config` clone was behind `origin` for reasons unrelated to the export - another machine had pushed). `VaultRecipeGit.export` now runs `git fetch origin <branch>` + `git merge --ff-only origin/<branch>` **before the recipe commit is created**, not between commit and push - fast-forwarding *after* the recipe commit already exists can't work, because by then the recipe commit and origin's tip are two different children of the same old base (a narrow but real divergence, even in the plain "purely behind" case) - confirmed live against disposable local bare-repo pairs before writing the fix, since the task's own originally-specified ordering (fetch/merge between commit and push, matching `DotfilesRunCommand.rebuildCommand`'s superficial shape) does not actually fix the bug. The corrected order is: write file -> `git add` -> the existing "nothing to push" `git status --porcelain` short-circuit (unchanged, still runs before any network call, so a no-op export never fetches) -> fetch + `git merge --ff-only` (with the recipe file already staged but not yet committed - verified live that a fast-forward leaves a staged-but-uncommitted new file untouched) -> commit -> push. A genuine divergence (real local-only commits predating this export, not just this export's own commit) still aborts cleanly with no force-push and nothing discarded, pointing at Bootstrap's "Dotfiles & machine config" card to resolve by hand - same as every other git-writing path in this app. Verified live against three disposable local bare-repo scenarios (never the captain's real `manjesh-config`, per this codebase's own convention): (1) purely behind + an unrelated dirty working-tree file - fetch+merge succeeds, push succeeds, dirty file untouched; (2) a genuine divergence (a real unpushed local-only commit plus a real remote-only commit) - merge refused, nothing lost or force-pushed, origin unchanged; (3) the existing nothing-to-push no-op - unaffected. No permanent self-test exists for `VaultRecipeGit`'s git/filesystem half (`VaultDataSelfTest.swift`'s own header explains why - a real `git commit`/`push` against a throwaway remote isn't something a pure in-process self-test can safely cover) - this fix followed that same live-disposable-repo-pair verification convention rather than adding one.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-global-hotkey-and-theme-fixes` fixed a real theme-staleness bug in the Recipe Backup card: `recipeDetailLabel.textColor` was set ad hoc at each call site (construction, busy/status updates, the two error paths) using whatever `theme` was current AT THAT MOMENT, with nothing re-deriving it when the theme later changed afterward** - `VaultController.applyTheme()` re-themes every other label on the page but never touched this one. A captain who switched themes without touching this card again (the common case) was left with a color computed against the *previous* theme rendered on the *current* one - a captain screenshot showed this as the card's description text barely legible against a light theme. Fixed by tracking which color *category* the label is currently showing (`recipeLabelKind: .info`/`.warn`/`.error`, not the literal color) via a new `setRecipeLabelColor(_:)` helper used at every call site, so `applyTheme()` can re-derive the right color from the *current* theme on every change via `recipeLabelColor(for:)` - the same pattern every other theme-aware label on this page already follows. Verified live across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`catppuccin-latte`/`gruvbox-light` via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit) computing the real WCAG contrast ratio between the label's resolved `textColor` and the page's resolved background after a live theme switch - all five themes measured well above the 4.5 legibility floor (6.6-18.1), where the un-fixed code would have kept showing whatever color was set before the switch.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-page-redesign` brought the page's remaining hand-rolled chrome up to a captain-reviewed Lavish prototype's shape, reusing existing Helm* components rather than inventing new ones.** Three real gaps, reconciled: (1) all three section headers (Secrets/Verified Launchers/Recipe Backup) moved off a hand-built title-only row onto `HelmCard`'s structured `setHeader(symbol:tint:title:subtitle:actions:)` - each now gets its own icon tile (green lock / violet shield / accent box), a fixed subtitle line, and its count badge + action button(s) pinned as trailing `actions`, matching how every other icon-tile card on this page and elsewhere in the app is built; (2) the Refresh button became a labeled `HelmButton(title: "Refresh", variant: .quiet, symbol: "arrow.clockwise")` instead of a bare icon, and the header subtitle gained the prototype's "names and metadata only, never a value" clause; (3) secret and tool rows moved off `ToolRowLayout`'s dense-checklist shape onto `HelmAccentRow` - the component this app already has for exactly "a colored left accent bar, a tinted badge, an uppercase kicker, a body line, a meta line, and a trailing accessory for the row's own action buttons" (see `HelmAccentRow`'s own doc comment on why it's deliberately not merged with `ToolRowLayout`). Every secret row is green/"Hardened" (mirroring the prototype's `tint-good`); every tool row is green/"Hardened, 0 issues" or amber/"Needs Attention" with the real `av doctor` issue count in the meta line - never a fabricated severity. `HelmAccentRow.Content` gained a small, additive `titleIsCode: Bool = false` (mirroring the existing `metaIsCode`) so a secret's name renders in `HelmType.code()` monospace, matching the prototype's `font-family: var(--mono)` treatment for exactly this kind of env-var-shaped identifier - the one new field added to a shared component, and it changes nothing for any existing caller (`FleetController`/`ShiftListViews`/`HostsListSection`/etc., which all leave it at its `false` default). **Two things deliberately NOT done, and why**: no per-secret human-readable description (the prototype's "App lock"/"GitHub CLI" are illustrative examples - `av list` only ever returns bare names, confirmed in `VaultData.swift`'s own header, so the meta line stays the existing generic "Stored in Automic Vault's Keychain" rather than a fabricated purpose); no "Harden" action button on a needs-attention tool row (the prototype shows one, but no such action exists anywhere in this app's real code for an arbitrary `av doctor`-reported tool name - `NotSyncedController`'s `av harden gh`/`av harden aws`/etc. are a fixed, unrelated set of pre-verified commands for a different card - so a generic "Harden" button here would mean inventing an unverified `av harden <tool>` invocation against a security tool, which is out of scope for a presentation-layer pass and was left out rather than guessed at). Also added a small, real (non-fabricated) improvement: the Recipe Backup card's body now shows "Last exported to `<repo>` N days ago" - or "No recipe backup found yet" - read straight off whatever `automic-vault-recipe.json` already exists on disk (`VaultRecipeGit.loadExistingRecipe`, a plain local file read, no network) on every `refresh()`, replacing a static description that never changed; the static description itself moved into the header's fixed subtitle instead of being lost. **Verified via a temporary env-gated probe** (`FM_DEBUG_VAULT_REDESIGN` in `main.swift` + a temporary `VaultController.debugSeedAndRender` hook, both reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot" convention) that mounted a real `VaultController` with fake secret/tool names (never real captain data) in a real off-screen window and rendered it to PNG across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light` - confirming the header/card/row shapes matched the prototype and catching one real regression along the way: the row's own `HelmButton` action pair (a plain, un-configured `NSStackView`) rendered with one button stretched to ~1000pt wide, the exact AGENTS.md gotcha (12) shape (`.gravityAreas` distribution + no stack-level hugging) - fixed with the same `.fill`-distribution + required-stack-and-button-hugging trio `HostsListSection`'s own `actions` stack already uses as its reference. **A follow-up correction mid-task**: this app has no OS-level process isolation between a `.build/debug` binary and the captain's real packaged `dist/*.app` (same bundle identifier), so launching *any* build of this app for visual verification risks colliding with a real, already-running captain session - the probe above was run before that constraint was made explicit, confirmed harmless in this instance (a separate `.build/debug` process that exited cleanly on its own via `exit(0)`, distinct from and never touching the real running `dist/Manjesh Grand Line.app` process), and both the probe code and its output were fully reverted before commit. Any future verification pass on this app should default to `swift build` (compile-check only) plus reading the view code against a design reference, never launching a built copy - and if a real rendered screenshot is genuinely needed, ask for one from an already-running captain session rather than starting a new process.

## Rail navigation

- **`fm/grandline-rail-setup-group` merged the standalone "Updates" and "Bootstrap" rail icons into one "Setup" entry** (pinned where "Updates" used to be, directly above `.docs`), after a captain-approved discussion that they're both environment/dependency-setup concerns distinct from Settings' app preferences (Settings stayed untouched, its own separate icon). `.updates`/`.bootstrap` remain real `RailDestination` cases with unchanged pages - only their rail position/visibility changed. Two captain corrections landed before the final shape: an in-rail expanding drawer (pushing rows below it up/down) was rejected in favor of a small flyout `NSPopover` anchored to the button's trailing edge, so the rail's own divider rhythm never moves; and click-to-open was rejected in favor of hover-to-open (Dock/menu-bar-submenu style), so there's no disclosure chevron either. `IconRailController.buildSetupButton()`/`showSetupFlyout()`/`SetupFlyoutViewController` are the whole implementation - `HoverTrackingButton`/`HoverTrackingView` report mouse-enter/exit via closures (`NSButton`/`NSView` have no built-in hover callback), and `scheduleShowSetupFlyout()`/`scheduleCloseSetupFlyout()` add a short (`setupCloseDelay`, 0.2s) close-on-hover-out delay so crossing the visual gap between the button and the flyout doesn't dismiss it - `scheduleShowSetupFlyout()` cancels the pending close the moment either view reports the cursor back over it. The flyout's own rows reuse `IconTileView`/`HoverHighlightView` from `HelmUIComponents.swift` (icon-in-tinted-square + label, horizontal instead of the rail's own icon-above-label) so it reads as this app's chrome, not a generic system menu; each row is an `NSClickGestureRecognizer` on a `HoverHighlightView` with no nested real control (same hit-testing-hazard avoidance as the Vault section's status pill). **A real, non-obvious `NSPopover` fact found while verifying this live**: `isShown` does not flip to `false` synchronously the instant `performClose`/`close()` is called - it lags by roughly the close animation's duration - so any test/verification code checking `isShown` immediately after a close call will see a stale `true`; check well after (verified live with an env-gated probe reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention, using real `HoverTrackingButton`/`HoverTrackingView` hover-simulation and a real synthetic `NSEvent` click through the flyout's own gesture recognizer, swept across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`).

- **`fm/grandline-lock-and-rail-fixes` reworked the rail's spacing rhythm and added a "more hosts" overflow entry**, after live captain feedback that the rail reads crowded/uneven at real-world density (all 11 destinations + a HOSTS section + badges) - density it was never actually tuned against before (every prior spacing pass happened against a shorter rail). Three named constants on `IconRailController` (`rowHeight` 46 (was a hardcoded 52), `rowSpacing` 3 (the gap *within* a group), `sectionGap` 14 (the gap *between* groups - mark/nav/hosts/utility/avatar)) replace what used to be a scatter of inconsistent literals (52/4/10/12/14 doing similar jobs) - any future rail spacing change should go through these constants, not a new one-off literal. Separately, with 2+ pinned hosts, only the first got its own row - the rest collapsed into one "+N" entry (`IconRailController.buildHostsOverflowButton`/`setHosts`) that reveals the remaining hosts on hover, reusing the exact `HoverTrackingButton`/`NSPopover`-on-hover mechanism `buildSetupButton()`/`showSetupFlyout()` already established (see the bullet above) rather than a second interaction pattern - `HostsOverflowFlyoutViewController` is the content-side counterpart to `SetupFlyoutViewController`, kept as its own type since a host's icon tints with its own per-host `accentHex` rather than one of the fixed `HelmTint` cases every `RailDestination` row uses.
- **`fm/grandline-rail-overflow-and-spacing` raised the host-overflow threshold to 3+ pinned hosts** (`hosts.count > 2` in `setHosts`) after captain feedback that collapsing at exactly 2 (1 shown + a "+1" entry) was premature - exactly 1 or 2 hosts always show every row normally. This part of that task held up. **Its accompanying "dead gap" fix did not** - it capped the HOSTS-to-utility-group boundary with a `[sectionGap, sectionGapMax]` range plus a soft (priority 800) bottom pin on `avatar`, and its own live verification only tested one window height, so it missed that the range/soft-pin combination let `hostsStack` itself - not the boundary it was watching - become the actual flexible spacer: `hostsStack` has no height constraint of its own, so with its top pinned via a required chain from the window's top edge (moves up in absolute terms as the window grows) and its bottom pinned near `toolsButton` (held near-fixed by the soft avatar pin), the stack view's frame stretched to fill the gap, growing 1:1 with window height (live-measured: 133pt/433pt/833pt/1433pt at 900/1200/1600/2200pt windows, with only ~103pt of real host-icon content). A captain screenshot on a maximized window showed this as a large dead gap below the pinned host icons. `fm/grandline-rail-spacing-fullheight` replaced the whole range/soft-pin mechanism with a single required, fixed `toolsButton.topAnchor == dividerBelowHosts.bottomAnchor + sectionGap` equality, making the *entire* rail (mark -> nav -> HOSTS -> utility group -> avatar) one fixed top-anchored chain with no free segment anywhere - `vaultButton`/`docsButton`/`setupGroup`/`avatar` no longer need their own anchor to `dividerBelowHosts` at all, since the existing bottom-up equalities already chain each of them to `toolsButton` (Settings had its own standalone row here too, at the time; `fm/grandline-avatar-menu-and-setup-guide` later removed it - see the bullet below). With nothing left to stretch, `avatar`'s position is fully determined top-down, and any leftover space at a tall window collects below it instead - verified live (fresh `IconRailController` + container per test, since reusing one instance across sizes leaves stale layout state behind) at 720/900/1200/1600/2200pt, at 0/1/2/3 pinned hosts (confirming the overflow threshold above is unaffected), and across both `helm-dark`/`helm-light`. **The already-documented, separate, still-open issue remains true and is unaffected by this fix**: the rail's total required content height (~836pt) exceeds this app's own default 720pt-tall launch window, so a too-short window still overflows/clips the avatar off the bottom - confirmed identical before and after this change (both designs are a required, mostly-fixed chain; only where the "flex" lived differed) - not something this task's scope covered.

- **`fm/grandline-avatar-menu-and-setup-guide` removed Settings' standalone rail row entirely** - it is still a real `RailDestination` for switching purposes (`AppShellController.show(.settings)` unchanged, the Settings page itself unchanged), but its only entry point is now a "Settings" row inside the avatar popover, above a divider, above "Logout" (`AvatarLogoutPopoverController`, `IconRailController.swift`) - Settings routine, Logout consequential, per captain-approved ordering. The popover is deliberately kept to just these two rows - an identity/account-style menu, not a general dumping ground for other rail items. The bottom-anchored utility chain (see the fullheight-chain bullet above) collapsed by one row: Tools -> Vault -> Docs -> Setup -> avatar, with `dividerSetupAvatar` now the one boundary directly above the avatar. The same task also collapsed `IconRailController.logoutClicked()` from two sequential `NSAlert`s down to one (captain reversing the original app-lock design's double-confirmation after live use showed it was too much friction) - the single remaining alert keeps the second alert's "you'll need your Grand Line password to get back in" copy, since it states the real stakes most clearly.

- **`fm/grandline-rail-unified-rework` consolidated three near-duplicate row builders (`railButton(for:)`, `buildSetupButton()`, `hostRailButton(for:)`) into one `buildRailRowButton(title:symbol:pointSize:tooltip:existingButton:)`**, after the accumulated per-task patches above left Setup's row subtly out of sync with the rest. The row-content geometry constants (`iconSize`, `contentSpacing`, `titleFontSize`/`titleFont`/`titleHeight`, and a derived `iconCenterYOffsetFromRowCenter`) moved from `CenteredImageAboveButtonCell`'s own private stored properties to `fileprivate static` members on `IconRailController` itself, so the cell that draws the icon and the badge anchor that has to line up with it read exactly one set of numbers - `CenteredImageAboveButtonCell.iconSize`/`.contentSpacing` are now computed properties reading straight through to `IconRailController`'s constants rather than independently-defaulted copies. **A real, verified badge-anchoring lesson**: `attachBadge`'s badge used to overlap a fixed amount into the icon's own top-trailing corner (tuned through `-6`/`6`, then `-12`/`12`, then `-20`/`20` - each verified live) but every overlap amount still visibly collided with `.review`'s `arrow.triangle.branch` glyph, whose ink (confirmed by rendering the SF Symbol standalone to a bitmap and inspecting it) fills *both* top corners of its 20x20 box almost edge-to-edge - a two-pronged upward fork, not a compact centered icon like `.overview`'s `server.rack`. No overlap-based anchor can clear every glyph's ink; the fix was to stop overlapping the icon's box at all - `container.leadingAnchor == iconAnchor.trailingAnchor + 3` places the badge entirely to the icon's *right* (an SF Symbol never draws outside the rect it's given, so this can't collide with any icon regardless of shape), with `centerYAnchor == iconAnchor.topAnchor + 2` keeping it looking like a corner badge with no headroom-above-the-row risk (the earlier `-20` overlap attempt exceeded the ~6-7pt of space above the icon within its own row and got visually clipped against the divider above - any future badge-position tuning needs to stay aware of that same headroom ceiling if it ever reaches back toward overlapping the icon). **Setup switched from hover-to-open back to click-to-toggle** (`setupClicked()`, mirroring `avatarClicked()`/`avatarPopover`'s `.transient` open-if-closed/close-if-open pattern) per live captain feedback during this task - superseding `fm/grandline-rail-setup-group`'s original hover-driven design (the `scheduleShowSetupFlyout`/`scheduleCloseSetupFlyout`/`setupCloseWorkItem` hover-delay machinery is gone for Setup specifically; `hostsOverflowButton`'s own hover flyout is untouched and still uses `setupCloseDelay`). A new `dividerAboveTools` marks the utility group's own top edge (mirroring `dividerAboveNav`'s role below the logo mark) - needed once the two-anchor-groups layout below could leave a large, boundary-less gap directly above Tools. **Layout: two independently-anchored groups, not one centered block** - this task's own first attempt (per its original brief) vertically centered the *entire* rail as one block within the window; the captain watched that running live and overrode it mid-task: the top group (mark through the HOSTS section) stays pinned to the window's top edge exactly as before this task, the bottom group (Tools through the avatar) is newly pinned to the window's *bottom* edge (`avatar.bottomAnchor == root.bottomAnchor - railEdgeMargin`, a real anchor that didn't exist before - previously the whole rail was one single top-anchored chain with no bottom anchor at all, which is *why* leftover space in a tall window used to collect below the avatar), and the only flex point is a required `>=` inequality (`dividerAboveTools.topAnchor >= dividerBelowHosts.bottomAnchor + sectionGap`) between the two groups' own already-fully-determined edges - never a range or soft-priority pin. Structurally this can't regress into PR #131/#132's bug (`hostsStack` itself stretching) since each group is still its own single rigid, fully-required chain; the *only* degree of freedom in the whole layout is that one inequality, verified live to grow correctly (leftover space collects only between HOSTS and Tools) across multiple window heights. **Avatar got a real visual pass**: `avatar` is now a `HoverTrackingButton` (not a plain `NSButton`) with a `CAGradientLayer` (flat avatar color -> the active theme's accent) replacing the old flat fill, and a `layer.borderColor`/`borderWidth` ring that brightens on hover (`restyleAvatar(_:)`, split out of `restyle(_:)` since it also has to re-run on `avatarIsHovering` changes, not just theme changes) - click behavior (`avatarClicked`/`avatarPopover`) is unchanged.

- **`fm/grandline-automation-pipeline` added a third row, "Automation," to the Setup flyout** (`showSetupFlyout()`'s `destinations` array, now `[.updates, .bootstrap, .automation]`) - `.automation` is a real `RailDestination` (own symbol `bolt.fill`, `isDailyUse: false`) with no rail row of its own, exactly like `.updates`/`.bootstrap`. See "Automation" below for the destination itself.

- **A now-historical redesign replaced the always-visible per-host icon block with a single "Hosts" `navStack` row that opens a flyout on click**, reusing the exact `SetupFlyoutViewController`/click-to-toggle mechanism above rather than a second interaction pattern - `dividerAboveHosts`/`hostsSectionLabel`/`hostsStack`/`dividerBelowHosts` (the old per-host icon block, plus its own "more hosts" overflow flyout at 3+ hosts from `fm/grandline-lock-and-rail-fixes`/`fm/grandline-rail-overflow-and-spacing` above) are all deleted outright, not hidden. `IconRailController.buildHostsButton()`/`hostsClicked()`/`showHostsFlyout()` is the rail-row builder, positioned inside the same daily-use `navStack` loop right where `.hosts` (still a real `RailDestination`, case order unchanged) already sat - "Hosts" highlights whenever `.hosts` or a saved host's own dedicated page (`activeHostID != nil`) is showing (there's no more per-host icon to highlight individually). `HostsFlyoutViewController` (replacing `HostsOverflowFlyoutViewController`) lists every saved host - not just the overflow - with a "Manage Hosts & Keys…" trailing row that calls `setActive(.hosts)`/`onSelect?(.hosts)`, opening the real, unchanged `.hosts` destination (`HostsController`) exactly like Setup's own flyout rows dispatch to their destinations; connecting a host still goes through `onConnectHost` unchanged. This same redesign originally added a sibling "VPN" flyout row alongside "Hosts" - see the "VPN (built, then removed)" section for that history; the row and its supporting code no longer exist.
- **Verified live**: a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) mounted a real `IconRailController` in an off-screen window and confirmed exactly 7 root-level separator boxes with no doubling between the daily-use rows and the utility group, the rail row order Overview/Console/Hosts/Tasks/Review, and the real Hosts flyout rendering un-truncated "DEV Bastion"/"Prod Bastion" plus a real "Manage Hosts & Keys…" row - all via real `NSPopover`s actually shown and read back from `NSApp.windows`, not just constraint math.

- **`fm/grandline-rail-unify-and-mark-polish` supersedes the two-anchor-groups layout from `fm/grandline-rail-unified-rework` above with one continuous, evenly-spaced list.** Real captain screenshots showed the old top-pinned-group/bottom-pinned-group split reading as two visually different gaps (more space above "Tools" than below it; more space after "Setup" than between any other row pair) plus a missing divider after Review. Fix: Tools/Vault/Dictation/Docs/"Setup" - previously individually-positioned `root` subviews, kept out of `navStack` only so the old variable-height per-host icon block above them could grow freely - now simply continue `navStack`'s own arranged-subview loop, separated by the exact same `navStackDivider()` rhythm the daily-use rows already used. That old reason to keep them out of `navStack` no longer applied once the hosts-flyout redesign replaced the variable-height per-host block with the fixed-height "Hosts" flyout row (a sibling "VPN" flyout row existed at this point too - now removed, see the "VPN (built, then removed)" section). **Unified order (current, after VPN's removal)**: Overview, Console, Hosts, Tasks, Review, Tools, Vault, Dictation, Docs, Setup - Overview/Console stay first (existing convention), then daily operational rows (Hosts, Tasks, Review), then frequently-reached utilities (Tools, Vault, Dictation, Docs), then Setup last as the least-frequent maintenance/bootstrap entry, directly above the profile - a captain-specified frequency ordering that matches `RailDestination`'s case order. Only the avatar keeps its own independent bottom anchor with its own divider (`dividerSetupAvatar`) above it - the one deliberate visual exception. The sole remaining flex point is a required `>=` inequality between `navStack.bottomAnchor` (top-anchored from `root.topAnchor`) and `dividerSetupAvatar.topAnchor` (bottom-anchored from `root.bottomAnchor` via `avatar`) - mirroring the old design's connector reasoning, so leftover height in a tall window collects in exactly the gap that already read as "more space after Setup" before this change, rather than a new mechanism. **A real bug caught by this task's own verification probe**: `restyleMark`/`restyleAvatar`-style theming registered via `ThemeManager.shared.observe` in `loadView` fires *synchronously at registration*, which happens before `mark`'s `wantsLayer`/`markGradientLayer` setup runs later in the same method - the first call's `mark.layer?.borderWidth = 1` silently no-oped on a nil layer. `restyleAvatar` already avoided this by virtue of `restyle(_:)` being called a second time at the end of `loadView`, after `avatar` is fully built; `restyleMark` needed the identical second call added there. Confirmed via a temporary probe dumping `mark.layer?.borderWidth` (0.0 before the fix, 1.0 after) across `helm-dark`/`helm-light` at 720/900/1200/2200pt window heights, alongside the divider-gap dump (every inter-row gap in the unified list a uniform 48pt regardless of height; the one flex gap growing 59→227→527→1527pt; the avatar's own gap a constant 62pt) and the bob-animation/lock-state check (`hasBobAnim` true after `setUnlocked(true)`, false after `setUnlocked(false)`) - all reverted before commit.
- **Same task gave the sailboat mark a richer look** (captain: "very bland") via the exact "flat color -> theme accent `CAGradientLayer` + soft accent ring" treatment `restyleAvatar`/`avatarGradientLayer` already established, reused rather than inventing a new visual language or a custom raster asset (`CaptainIcon.swift`'s convention was considered and judged heavier-weight than this needed) - `markGradientLayer` sized once (`mark`'s bounds are fixed 34x34) and re-tinted by a new `restyleMark(_:)`, mirroring `restyleAvatar(_:)`. The existing bob animation and `setUnlocked(_:)`'s lock/unlock weight change are untouched - only the mark's background/tint changed.
- **Same task also added a real active/pressed state to the Hosts/Setup flyout-trigger rows** (a VPN row existed here too at the time - since removed) - previously neither showed any highlight while their own flyout was open, unlike a real destination such as Console. `IconRailController` now conforms to `NSPopoverDelegate`; each `show*Flyout()` sets `popover.delegate = self` and a new `isHostsFlyoutOpen`/`isSetupFlyoutOpen` flag before showing, and `popoverDidClose(_:)` clears the matching flag by comparing `notification.object` against the stored `hostsPopover`/`setupPopover` - firing for *every* dismissal path (explicit re-click via `*Clicked()`, a row selection, or a `.transient` popover's own outside-click auto-dismiss, which never goes through `*Clicked()` at all). `restyle(_:)` folds each flag into that row's existing active-state coloring. Incidentally fixed a related pre-existing gap in the same check: Setup's active-state color never included `.automation`, even though its flyout lists Automation as a third destination - added alongside the flyout-open fix since both are the same class of "does this row's own state look active" bug. Verified live via a temporary probe calling each row's private `*Clicked()` selector by name (`perform(Selector(...))`), confirming a real non-clear `layer.backgroundColor` while open, then calling `performClose(nil)` directly on the popover object (bypassing the controller's own toggle method entirely) and confirming the tint clears - proving `popoverDidClose` itself, not just the explicit re-click path, does the clearing.
- **A follow-up fix addressed a real, live-reproduced bug in the "Setup→avatar" divider `fm/grandline-rail-unify-and-mark-polish` added: every hairline divider in this whole file was silently rendering via AppKit's own system `.separator` color, not this app's Helm theme, because `NSBox(boxType: .separator)` unconditionally ignores any `.fillColor` set on it** - confirmed empirically (not assumed) with a standalone probe: setting `.fillColor = .red` on a `.separator`-typed `NSBox` produced zero visible change. Every divider in this file (`dividerAboveNav`, every `navStackDivider()`-built row separator, and `dividerSetupAvatar`) was `.separator`-typed with no `.fillColor` ever set - so all of them, not just the one the captain flagged, were rendering at only a ~0.09 RGB delta from this rail's own dark chrome background (measured live via a bitmap-sampling probe: `bitmapImageRepForCachingDisplay`/`cacheDisplay`, sampling pixel colors along each divider's own y-center). The Setup→avatar divider happened to be the one the captain actually noticed missing because it sits alone in an otherwise-blank gap with nothing else nearby to visually frame it - `dividerAboveNav` sits right below the mark, and the inter-row dividers sit between visibly distinct icon rows, so their own equally-faint lines are less conspicuously absent. **Fix**: every divider in this file is now a plain `wantsLayer`-backed `NSView` (matching `edgeLine`'s own already-working pattern, and the same pattern used by every OTHER divider throughout this app outside `IconRailController.swift` - Settings/Bootstrap/Shift/Docs/Review/Fleet/`ToolRowLayout`, all a plain layer-backed `NSView` with an explicit `theme.chromeLineHex`-derived `layer.backgroundColor`, never `NSBox`) via a new `makeThemedDivider()` helper + `railDividers: [NSView]` registry + `restyleDividers(_:)`, called a second time at the end of `loadView()` for the same "the `ThemeManager.shared.observe` closure fires synchronously at registration, before later-built divider views exist yet" reason `restyleMark` already needed a second call for (see the bullet above) - `railDividers` only had `dividerAboveNav` in it on that first synchronous run. Verified live (temporary env-gated probe, reverted before commit): all 12 dividers (1 `dividerAboveNav` + 10 `navStackDivider()` rows + 1 `dividerSetupAvatar`) now share the identical, real, working `theme.chromeLineHex`-at-0.5-alpha fill - confirmed via `Mirror`-reflection dumping `railDividers`' real `CGColor`s - across window heights 500-1400pt with no constraint warnings, and the pixel-sampling probe confirmed the color genuinely changed (was the raw system separator color; now the theme-derived blend).

- **`fm/grandline-sre-lead-and-setup-icons` gave each Setup-flyout row its own hue, and re-verified two adjacent surfaces the captain suspected rather than assuming them.**
  **The one real defect:** `SetupFlyoutViewController.loadView()` passed a hardcoded `tint: .accent` for every row, so Updates / Bootstrap / Automation / GitHub Sync all rendered the identical tile - measured in a real off-screen render, all four glyphs sampled the same `rgb(165,218,229)` in `helm-dark`.
  Fixed with `RailDestination.flyoutTint` (declared beside `symbol`/`title`), consulted only where a destination is drawn as an `IconTileView` rather than a bare rail glyph.
  The per-row mapping was not guessed from the captain's colour words - it was recovered by sampling the proposed mockup's own pixels, which resolve to `HelmTint` cases this app already has: Updates `.info`, Bootstrap `.warn`, Automation `.accent`, GitHub Sync `.violet`.
  **A cross-theme trade-off worth knowing before touching it:** `.accent` for Automation collides with a neighbouring row in exactly 3 of the 12 palettes, because those palettes genuinely define their accent as that same hue (`tokyo-night-dark`/`-light` accent == their blue; `rose-pine-main` accent == its magenta).
  `.good` was measured to collide in zero themes but is green in `helm-dark`, where the reference mockup clearly shows the accent cyan - so mockup fidelity in the reference palette won, and the full measurement is recorded on `flyoutTint`'s own doc comment rather than left for someone to rediscover.
  `HelmContrastSelfTest.checkSetupFlyoutTintsAreDistinct` guards the *mapping* (four distinct `HelmTint` cases), deliberately not four distinct resolved hexes - confirmed to catch a real regression, not just to pass, by reverting all four rows to `.accent` and seeing it fail by name.
  The tint hues themselves need no new check: `checkIconTiles` already sweeps every `HelmTint` against the 3:1 icon floor in all 12 themes.
  **Two things verified as ALREADY correct, and left untouched.** The Hosts flyout was already per-host tinted - a real render against the captain's own two saved hosts is a pixel-level match for the proposed screenshot (DEV Bastion amber, Prod Bastion teal, "Manage Hosts & Keys…" neutral), because `HostsFlyoutViewController`'s theme observer tints each row from that host's own `accentHex` and the manage row from `.neutral`.
  The SRE Lead pane's Finding / Recommended-next-action rendering was already the `HelmAccentRow` treatment the mockup proposes - driving a real conversation through the fake-`claude` seam produced a populated pane matching the reference (accent bar, round badge, uppercase `mutedInk` kicker, tinted card border), so the captain's reported gap was the empty-vs-populated state, not a rendering bug.
