  **A probe lesson that cost real time here:** an off-screen probe window must call `window.followHelmTheme()` the way `main.swift` does for the real window, or an `NSTextField`'s `.textBackgroundColor` resolves against `.aqua` and the SRE Lead input renders as a glaring white box that looks exactly like a real theming defect.
  The same probe run also confirmed behaviour was preserved by firing every flyout row's real `NSClickGestureRecognizer` target/action: all four Setup destinations and both host connects dispatched correctly.

## Automation

**`.automation` (`fm/grandline-automation-pipeline`) is a new rail destination, reachable only via the Setup flyout's third row** (`AutomationController.swift`) - a single-page pipeline view: a "Run Automation" button, a progress summary line, and a vertical stepper covering all five setup steps Bootstrap's own stepper knows about (Firstmate home, Dotfiles & machine config, Agent instructions, Software checklist, and - unlike Bootstrap's own "Run full setup," which deliberately excludes it - Restore Grand Line config too). `BootstrapController.swift`'s own page, cards, and "Run full setup" button are untouched by this task.

- **Zero duplicate check/install logic - everything routes through the same shared layer Bootstrap already used.** `SetupStepChecks.swift` is a new file holding `SetupStepKind` (moved out of `BootstrapController`, which previously declared it as a private nested enum) plus `SetupStepChecks`, a set of pure `isDone` predicates (`firstmateHomeDone`/`dotfilesDone`/`agentInstructionsDone`/`softwareDone`/`restoreConfigDone`) extracted from `BootstrapController.stepIsDone(_:)`'s old body. `BootstrapController.stepIsDone(_:)` is now a thin wrapper delegating to these same functions (and widened from `private` to internal for `AutomationController` to call the identical decision logic) - the two pages can never disagree about what "done" means for a step. `DotfilesRunCommand.runOrCloneCommand`/`.rebuildCommand` (same file) is the one place the dotfiles step's clone-vs-rebuild command is built; `BootstrapController`'s own `rebuildCommand`/`runSetupStepDotfiles` now call through to it too. `SetupStepKind.isPartOfFullSetupSequence` (Bootstrap's own "exclude restore config from the automated chain" flag) stays defined as a file-scope extension in `BootstrapController.swift`, unchanged - it has no meaning for Automation's own sequencer, which always runs all five steps.
- **`AutomationController` keeps its own independently-cached copies of the underlying checks** (`dotfilesRepoPath`/`repoState`/`agentItems`/`toolRows`, populated by calling `DotfilesSource.resolvedDotfilesPath()`/`.repoState(at:)`/`.agentInstructionItems(repoPath:)` and `UpdatesSource.check`/`.update` per `DependencyCatalog.items` directly) rather than reaching into `BootstrapController`'s own view-bound private state - the same "each page caches the same underlying check independently" pattern `UpdatesController` and `BootstrapController` already established for the same `DependencyCatalog`.
- **The Software checklist step renders as ~13 individually status-tinted chip rows** (one per `DependencyCatalog.items`, wrapped 3-per-row via `Array.chunked(into:)`), never a collapsed "Software (N tools)" summary line - a soft green tint for an installed tool, an accent-bordered highlight for the one `installAllMissing` is currently working on, muted/plain for a tool not yet reached.
- **Sequencing**: `runAutomationClicked()` walks all five `SetupStepKind` cases in order, re-running whichever background check a step's `stepIsDone` depends on right before deciding skip-vs-run (mirroring `BootstrapController.runSetupStepDotfiles`'s own "never trust a stale in-memory result" comment), marking a satisfied step "Skipped - already there" and stopping the whole run on the first real failure. The dotfiles step reuses `onRunCommandTracked` (wired by `AppShellController` to the same `runInConsole` Bootstrap's own dotfiles step uses) since cloning/rebuilding needs a real interactive `sudo` TTY; the software step reuses a local `installAllMissing` that calls `UpdatesSource.update`/`.check` one row at a time (never concurrently, matching `BootstrapController.installAllMissing`'s own "don't race two package-manager invocations" reasoning). **Restore config is the one step that pauses for a real captain interaction**: it sets that step's status to `.waitingForCaptain` and renders "Choose file…"/"Skip" buttons inline on the step row - "Choose file…" calls the same `BackupUI.importFlow` Bootstrap's own restore card uses, "Skip" marks it skipped - either way the sequence resumes from there. Firstmate home has no captain-facing action on this page (matching Bootstrap's own sequencer for this step): if it isn't already set up, the run stops and points at Bootstrap's Firstmate home card.
- **Verified live** (this project's own "Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot" convention, no Screen Recording/Accessibility permission in this sandbox): a temporary env-gated probe (`FM_DEBUG_AUTOMATION`, reverted before commit) mounted a real `AutomationController` in a real off-screen window against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`, confirmed a real non-zero `runButton` frame and exactly 5 real stepper rows and 13 real chip rows, forced a spread of step statuses across all five kinds and one tool row to "current" and confirmed the stepper rebuilt with the right pill/dot state and the chip grid rendered three distinct visual states (green/done, accent-bordered/current, muted/pending, matching the required layer background/border values), then swept `helm-dark`/`helm-light` confirming stable geometry with no crash.

## GitHub Sync

**`.githubSync` (`fm/grandline-setup-github-sync`) is a fourth Setup-flyout-only rail destination, alongside Updates/Bootstrap/Automation** (`GitHubSyncController.swift` + `GitHubSyncData.swift`) - pulls the latest upstream changes into each of the captain's personal GitHub forks from inside the app, rather than by hand per repo. Page shape and per-repo row are deliberately copied from the existing sub-modules, not invented: card chrome + `FlippedView`/`NSScrollView` from `AutomationController.swift`'s `loadView`/`card(icon:title:content:)`, and per-repo rows from `ToolRowLayout` (`HelmUIComponents.swift`) - the exact same shared assembly `UpdatesController`'s per-tool rows use, including the check/action/busy-state/re-check-after-action contract (`UpdatesController.check(_:)`/`.update(_:)`).

- **`GitHubSyncCatalog.repos` lists 8 forks** (the captain's original 7 plus `quota-axi`, added mid-task at the captain's request once firstmate had freshly forked it) - **confirmed live via `gh api repos/manjesh-raj/<repo>` before writing the catalog, not assumed from the repo name**. All 8 are real forks of `kunchenguid/<repo>` **except `automic-vault`, which is a genuine non-fork** (`.fork == false`, `.parent == nil` - it's a private mirror the captain pushed by hand, not a GitHub-forked copy).
- **`fm/grandline-github-sync-manual-upstream` gave a non-fork repo a second, explicit path instead of a permanent "Not a Fork."** `GitHubSyncRepoConfig.manualUpstreamOwner`/`manualUpstreamName` (set for `automic-vault` -> `automic-vault/automic-vault`, the real upstream per the captain) is a small, per-repo, hand-declared config living right in the catalog - not automatic guessing, and not a second config file. **Why this needs a genuinely different mechanism, not a tweak to the fork path**: `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/{a}:{branch}...{b}:{branch}` only works within a real GitHub-recorded fork network - confirmed live, a cross-repo compare between `manjesh-raj/automic-vault` and `automic-vault/automic-vault` 404s even though the two repos share real git history. So a repo with a manual upstream configured goes through `GitHubSyncSource.checkManual(_:upstreamFullName:)`/`syncManual(_:upstreamFullName:)` instead of `checkFork`/`syncFork` (both now private, dispatched from the still-public `check(_:)`/`sync(_:)` based on whether `manualUpstreamFullName` is set) - a small local scratch git clone (`localCloneDir(for:)`, under `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/github-sync-repos/`, override `FM_GITHUB_SYNC_CLONE_ROOT` - never the captain's own working copy of the repo) used to run the real git operations by hand: add/point an `upstream` remote, fetch both remotes, `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/<branch>...upstream/<branch>` for real ahead/behind counts (the exact same semantics as `compare`'s `ahead_by`/`behind_by`, just computed locally), and - only when the local-only count is genuinely `0` - `git checkout -B <branch> origin/<branch>` + `git merge --ff-only upstream/<branch>` + `git push origin <branch>:<branch>`. `--force`/`--rebase`/`-X ours`/`-X theirs` are never used here either, matching `syncFork`'s own "never force" guarantee - a repo with real local-only commits is refused (`refusedDiverged`), never overwritten. Auth reuses `DocsSyncSource.ghAuthToken()` via the identical `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0`/`http.extraheader` Basic-auth injection `ShiftGitSync.runGit` already established (`ShiftGitSync.swift:748-759`) - no second credential path. `GitHubSyncStatus.notAFork` is now reached only by a repo that's both genuinely not a fork *and* has no manual upstream configured.
- **Verified live end to end against the real `manjesh-raj/automic-vault` repo, not simulated**: at the time this shipped, `automic-vault` was 332 commits behind `automic-vault/automic-vault` with 0 local-only commits (a clean, safe fast-forward - confirmed via `git rev-list --left-right --count` before writing any code). The real sync path was run - real clone, real fetch of both remotes, real `merge --ff-only`, real `git push` to `origin` - and confirmed afterward via `gh api repos/manjesh-raj/automic-vault/commits/main` that the pushed SHA (`72972be4...`) exactly matches `automic-vault/automic-vault`'s own `main` tip. A second pass through the real, shipped `GitHubSyncSource.check(_:)` code path (via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit) then confirmed the row now reports `.inSync` with `upstreamFullName == "automic-vault/automic-vault"`, not `.notAFork` - and the same pass confirmed no regression across all 7 real-fork repos (`chrome-devtools-axi`, `treehouse`, `tasks-axi`, `gh-axi`, `no-mistakes`, `lavish-axi`, `quota-axi`), all still resolving through `checkFork` to their real `kunchenguid/*` upstream exactly as before. The diverged-refusal path for a manual upstream was not live-tested against a genuinely diverged repo (none existed to test against, and fabricating one would risk real data) - it rests on the same `git merge --ff-only`/`rev-list --left-right` semantics already exercised live above, which refuse by construction whenever the local-only count is nonzero.
- **Status is always live, never cached**: `GitHubSyncSource.check(_:)` calls `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` for fork/parent/default-branch, then `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/{owner}:{branch}...{parentOwner}:{branch}` - `compare`'s `ahead_by` is how many commits upstream has that the fork doesn't (how far behind), `behind_by` is how many commits the fork has that upstream doesn't (real diverged local commits, the exact number `gh repo sync` itself needs to be `0` to fast-forward safely). `behind_by > 0` maps to `GitHubSyncStatus.diverged`, never silently treated as just "behind."
- **Sync itself is `gh repo sync {owner}/{repo}` (source defaults to the repo's real parent) - no hand-rolled fetch/merge/push, and `--force` is never passed anywhere in this file, under any condition.** A refusal (real diverged local commits) is detected by matching `gh`'s own stderr text - `"can't sync because there are diverging changes; use --force to overwrite the destination branch"` (confirmed by inspecting the strings embedded in the real `gh` binary on this machine, `gh version 2.97.0` - `github.com/cli/cli/v2/pkg/cmd/repo/sync`) - and reported back as `GitHubSyncSyncOutcome.refusedDiverged`, distinct from a genuine failure (network/auth/etc). A diverged repo is left untouched by both the per-row "Sync now" action and "Sync all" (which only targets rows currently `showsSyncButton == true` - see the next bullet).
- **`GitHubSyncStatus.showsSyncButton` mirrors `DependencyStatus.showsUpdateButton`'s exact rule, per captain feedback mid-task: the Sync button (and "Sync all") never shows for a repo already `.inSync`** - only `.behind`/`.syncFailed`. A row always shows its live status pill (In Sync / N behind / Diverged / Not a Fork / Check Failed / Sync Failed) at a glance; the Sync button appears only when there's genuinely something to pull, exactly the "tell me the status, and if it's not up to date, give me a button to sync" shape the captain asked for - not an always-present button that's a no-op when already current.
- **`GitHubSyncSource` is this app's sixth near-duplicate copy of the `resolveExecutable`/`RunResult`/`run` Process-plumbing trio** (`UpdatesData.swift`, `VaultData.swift`, `NotSyncedData.swift`, `DocsData.swift`'s `ghAuthToken`-adjacent shelling, `BackupGitHub.swift`, and now this) - consolidating them remains explicitly out of scope per this codebase's own established convention (see `VaultData.swift`'s header).
- **Verified live, real network operations, not simulated**: every one of the 8 catalog repos' fork/parent/ahead/behind status was confirmed directly via `gh api` before this catalog was written; `GitHubSyncSource.check`/`.sync` were exercised end to end (not just the raw `gh` CLI) via a standalone compiled harness importing `GitHubSyncData.swift` directly, confirming two real forks (`chrome-devtools-axi`, `treehouse`) genuinely advanced from N-commits-behind to `inSync` through this file's own code path. The divergence-refusal path was **not** live-tested against a real diverged repo (none of the 8 catalog repos were diverged, and deliberately fabricating one to test this would risk real data, per this task's own explicit instruction) - it rests on the confirmed real refusal string above plus `gh repo sync`'s own documented fast-forward-only contract. The app was also relaunched standalone (scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_DICTATION_DIR`) confirming no regression to Updates/Bootstrap/Automation or app startup.

## App lock

**`fm/grandline-app-lock` adds an app-level password gate independent of the Mac's own login/Touch ID** - a captain-approved night-sailing lock screen covering the whole window (rail included) until the correct password is typed. Reuses Automic Vault for the password itself rather than inventing a second secret store.

- **The password is a fixed secret name, `GRANDLINE_APP_PASSWORD` (`VaultSource.appPasswordSecretName`), set by the captain themselves via `av save`/the Vault tab - this app never writes it and has no first-run setup screen.** `VaultSource.checkAppPasswordConfigured()` (`av list`) decides which of the lock screen's two states to show - a real password form, or (if the secret doesn't exist) a static "run `av save GRANDLINE_APP_PASSWORD`, then relaunch" message with no form at all. Absence of the secret always means "can't get in," never "let anyone in."
- **`VaultSource.verifyAppPassword(_:)` mirrors the Vault tab's own `av inject +NAME -- command` mechanism** (see the Vault section above) but runs it as a background `Process` rather than through a visible Console tab - a password check has nothing worth showing the captain. The typed guess travels to the comparison shell via one environment variable (`GRANDLINE_LOCK_CANDIDATE`), never spliced into argv/the shell command text, so it never appears in a process listing. **A real, load-bearing finding**: on an unsigned dev binary rebuilt each time, Automic Vault's own approval flow can require a real interactive click on its menu-bar app's popup (a "new" caller identity each rebuild) - invisible to this app, since the check runs headlessly with no Console tab. A shell-invoked `av inject` (a long-trusted caller) auto-approves near-instantly by comparison; this asymmetry, not a bug in this app's code, is what made early live verification of this path flaky in this sandbox until tests were restructured to drive `AppLockController`'s pure timer state directly instead of a real password submission for anything that isn't specifically testing the password check itself.
- **`AppLockController` (`AppLock.swift`) is the whole idle/hard-logout state machine, with no Vault/UI dependency of its own** - `onLock: (AppLockReason) -> Void` is the only way it talks to the outside world, wired to `AppShellController.showLock` in `main.swift`. Every threshold (`idleThreshold` 1h, `sessionThreshold` 12h, `pollInterval` 30s) is overridable via `FM_APP_LOCK_IDLE_SECONDS`/`FM_APP_LOCK_SESSION_SECONDS`/`FM_APP_LOCK_POLL_SECONDS` purely for live verification, never read from `AppSettings` (no way to configure a longer window from inside a locked app). `tick()` checks the 12h-since-last-unlock condition *before* the 1h-idle condition, so a captain who happens to cross both at once gets the `.sessionExpired` message, not the ordinary idle one - covered permanently, deterministically, by `AppLockControllerSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_APP_LOCK_TESTS=1`) via an injected fake clock/idle-time provider (no real Timer or real `CGEventSource` involved), including the exact "unlock at hour 11 resets the 12h clock from there, not from launch" case the brief called out.
- **Idle detection is `CGEventSource.secondsSinceLastEventType(.hidSystemState, eventType: .null)`** - the standard, no-extra-permission "system-wide seconds since last real HID event" recipe (unlike a global `NSEvent` monitor, this needs no Accessibility permission). **`fm/grandline-lock-and-rail-fixes` fixed a severe live regression here: the captain reported the app re-locking every `pollInterval` (10-30s) even during continuous active use**, right after this feature (PR #129) shipped. The threshold/poll constants were already correct (3600s/30s, confirmed by the self-test, which passes unchanged before and after this fix) - trusting the system-wide counter as the *only* idle signal was the bug. Two changes: the state ID moved from `.combinedSessionState` (which additionally folds in "events posted by the current process via a private event source" - the wrong signal for a system-wide check, and the more plausible one to misbehave on this unsigned/ad-hoc-signed dev build) to `.hidSystemState`; and `AppLockController` now also tracks `lastLocalActivityAt` via a **local** `NSEvent` monitor (`localActivityMonitor`, added in `start()` - needs no Accessibility permission since it only observes events already targeted at this app's own windows) and uses `min(systemIdleSeconds(), secondsSinceLocalActivity())` as the effective idle reading (`effectiveIdleSeconds()`) - so a captain demonstrably, continuously interacting with this app's own UI can never be misread as idle no matter what a single system-wide counter reports on a given machine. Genuine inactivity still locks correctly since both signals go stale together. **Known sandbox-only caveat, not a defect**: the system-wide counter alone returned a huge, clearly-bogus value (~935,000s) when the *test binary* was launched headlessly (`nohup ... &`, no real interactive login/WindowServer session) in this repo's own agent sandbox - the same sandbox quirk plausibly explains why software-injected/synthetic events (which don't register as real HID input) can't move that counter either, which is exactly why the local-activity fallback above was verified live in this sandbox by injecting synthetic `NSEvent`s via `NSApp.sendEvent` and confirming they still suppressed a lock (through the local monitor, not the system counter) while a genuinely idle period with no such events still locked on schedule.
- **`AppLockController.start()` opts the poll timer out of macOS App Nap** (`ProcessInfo.processInfo.beginActivity(options: [.userInitiated, .idleSystemSleepDisabled], reason:)`, held for the controller's whole lifetime) and schedules the `Timer` in `.common` run-loop modes, not just `.default`. This is a real fix, not a precaution: App Nap throttling a backgrounded app's timers is exactly the failure mode this feature most needs to avoid, since its entire point is catching an app left unattended/backgrounded - confirmed live in this sandbox that a plain `scheduledTimer` fired once near launch and then stopped firing entirely for many seconds of otherwise-idle time until this fix was applied.
- **`LockScreenController.swift`** is the night-sailing scene - `CAGradientLayer` sky, fixed-position `CALayer` stars (deterministic, not randomized, so it renders identically on every launch/probe), a `CAShapeLayer` wave drifting via a looping `CABasicAnimation`, and a bobbing sailboat. **The sailboat is a real `NSImageView` laid out inside the same `NSStackView` as the title/subtitle/form** (Auto Layout-positioned, still layer-animatable via `.layer?.add`), not a freeform `CALayer` placed at a fixed fraction of the window's height - the first draft used the latter and a captain screenshot on a real (much taller than the 1220x720 default) window caught the boat landing squarely on top of the password field, since a height-fraction and an Auto-Layout-centered stack are two independent layout systems with no reason to agree at every window size. The password field is a custom-styled translucent pill (`isBordered = false`, `focusRingType = .none`, a `lock.fill` icon, a manually-built `placeholderAttributedString` since the plain `placeholderString` setter always renders in the system's default gray) rather than the stock system text field, after live captain feedback that the stock look clashed with the scene; its text is vertically centered by pinning the field's own natural-height frame to its taller pill container's `centerYAnchor` rather than top/bottom-pinning it to the container's full height - a plain `NSTextFieldCell` does not vertically center text within a frame taller than its own line height (confirmed live: it stayed visibly top-aligned until this fix). **Distinct animations for success vs. failure, per captain ask**: a wrong password shakes the field's pill container *and* rocks the boat (`playUnlockFailureAnimation`); a correct one sails the boat off to the right while fading it out (`playUnlockSuccessAnimation`), and **the overlay only actually hides once that animation's `CATransaction` completion block fires** (`onUnlockAnimationFinished`, not `onAttempt`'s own completion) - hiding immediately on a successful verify would cut the flourish off before it's ever visible, since the overlay would already be gone. **`fm/grandline-lockscreen-theme` reversed the original "fixed palette regardless of theme" decision above** per a later captain ask: the scene now follows the app's active Helm theme's light/dark `mode`, live, the same way every other view in this app does (`ThemeManager.shared.observe` registered in `loadView`, per `ThemeManager.swift`'s own checklist) - it is a permanent, app-lifetime singleton (owned by `AppShellController`), so the returned observation token is discarded like every other such observer in this app. Night mode is unchanged (the original palette above); day mode is a genuine second "daytime sailing" palette, not a dimmed copy - a glowing sun layer swapped in for the star layers (both built once at `loadView` time and toggled via `isHidden`, never added/removed, so there's no frame with neither or both), a brighter blue-to-pale-horizon sky gradient, a lighter turquoise wave, and dark ink/boat/field-pill colors instead of white (a translucent white pill and white text lose contrast against a pale sky). All of this lives in one `applyTheme(_ theme:)` method that the observer calls on every change, including while the screen is already on-screen and locked - it does not force `view.appearance`, since every color here is drawn explicitly (layer fills, `NSAttributedString` foregrounds) rather than via a system-semantic color. Verified live (not just `swift build`) via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) driving the real controller end to end in both themes: initial dark-mode colors, a live theme switch to light while already locked (confirmed via the same instance, not a fresh one), a wrong-password shake+distress animation firing correctly in both themes, and a successful-unlock sail-away+fade animation completing (`onUnlockAnimationFinished` firing) in both themes.
- **The avatar's click action (`IconRailController.avatarClicked`) is now a themed `NSPopover` "Logout" row (`AvatarLogoutPopoverController`), replacing the old `NSApp.orderFrontStandardAboutPanel` call.** A bare `NSMenu` (this task's first draft) read as unstyled system chrome next to the rest of this app's deliberately-themed surfaces, per live captain feedback - follows `ShiftMenuBarController`'s own small-`NSPopover` convention instead. Logout is double-confirmed via two sequential `NSAlert`s (captain's explicit ask) before calling `onLogoutRequested`, which the app delegate wires to `appLock.lock(reason: .manualLogout)`.
- **While locked, every main-menu item except Edit (Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All/Find, all `nil`-target/responder-chain-routed) and the App menu's Hide/Quit is disabled** (`AppDelegate.setContentMenusEnabled`, driven by `AppShellController.onLockStateChanged`). The lock overlay being opaque and topmost already blocks mouse clicks on hidden content via ordinary AppKit hit-testing, but most of this app's menu items have a concrete `target` (not first-responder-routed), so a keyboard shortcut like ⌘N would otherwise still reach a hidden destination's action. Edit is deliberately left alone since the lock screen's own password field needs standard Cut/Copy/Paste (e.g. pasting a password from a manager via ⌘V) - and since the overlay always grabs first responder for its own field while locked, those actions can't reach anything else regardless.
- **Verified live** (real launched dev binary, real `GRANDLINE_APP_PASSWORD` secret in this machine's actual Automic Vault, real captain live-review across several iterations - see this task's PR for the screenshot-driven history): launch always shows the lock screen before any content; a wrong password is rejected with the shake/rock animation and the field stays enabled for another try; a correct password plays the sail-away/fade animation and then unlocks (content visible, menu re-enabled); the idle re-lock path was confirmed via `AppLockControllerSelfTest`'s deterministic proof plus a real Timer-driven live run showing `isLocked` correctly flip back to `true` after a shortened threshold. The 12-hour hard-logout path's *live* Timer-driven confirmation was inconclusive in this specific sandbox for the reasons above (App Nap before the fix; CGEventSource's headless-launch quirk potentially still masking the session check behind a spuriously-early idle firing even after the fix) - its correctness rests on the deterministic self-test (which explicitly proves session-expired takes priority over idle at the same tick) rather than a clean live 12-hour-equivalent run; a future task touching this area should re-attempt a live confirmation from a real, interactively-launched instance rather than assuming the sandbox's headless-launch behavior generalizes.
- **`fm/grandline-lock-and-rail-fixes` added `IconRailController.setUnlocked(_:)`** - the rail's own sailboat mark (`IconRailController`'s `mark`, now a stored property rather than a `loadView`-local `let`) switches to a heavier SF Symbol weight and gains a subtle continuous bob (the same `CAKeyframeAnimation`-on-`transform` recipe `LockScreenController.startAnimationsIfNeeded`'s boat bob uses, smaller-amplitude since this is a fixed 34x34pt rail icon, not a 72pt centerpiece) once the captain is past the lock screen; static/inert while locked. `AppShellController.showLock`/`hideLock` call `rail.setUnlocked(false)`/`setUnlocked(true)` respectively - a captain locking mid-bob always sees the mark go static immediately, not finish its cycle.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-bootstrap-fix` closed two gaps in the lock screen's `av`-availability messaging and fixed the impossible bootstrap instruction in `setup-guide.md`.** `setup-guide.md` no longer offers the Vault tab's "+ Add Secret" as a first-setup option for `GRANDLINE_APP_PASSWORD` - that tab lives inside the main UI, behind this same lock screen, so it's only reachable to rotate an already-set password, never to set the first one; the terminal `av save` command (or Automic Vault's own native app) is the only real first-bootstrap path. `VaultData.swift`'s `AppPasswordAvailability.avUnavailable` case (av not on PATH at all) is now wired up in `LockScreenController` with its own message plus a real "Install Automic Vault" button (`onInstallAutomicVault`, wired in `AppShellController` to `VaultSource.updateInstall()` - the same Homebrew-cask mechanism Updates/Vault already use for this catalog entry) - verified live via a temporary `FM_DEBUG_FORCE_APP_PASSWORD_STATE` override (reverted before commit) plus a real end-to-end install click against this machine's already-installed cask. A second, captain-flagged gap surfaced mid-task: `av list` can fail with "Automic Vault approval service is not running" when its LaunchAgent-backed menu-bar helper isn't up (confirmed live: `launchctl bootout gui/<uid>/com.automicvault.menubar-helper.plist` reproduces the exact error) - `checkAppPasswordConfigured()` previously folded this into `.avUnavailable`, wrongly implying no password is configured. It's now a distinct `.serviceNotRunning` case (matched via a `serviceNotRunningMarker` substring check on the combined stdout/stderr), with its own "starting it now" message and no false "no password configured" claim. `showLock` calls the new `VaultSource.ensureServiceRunning()` (`open -a "Automic Vault"`, fire-and-forget) before the first check, and `AppShellController.applyPasswordAvailability` retries every 1.5s indefinitely while `.serviceNotRunning` persists and the lock screen is still showing. **Live-verification finding**: launchd's on-demand socket activation means a plain "menu-bar app quit" (`osascript -e 'quit app "Automic Vault"'`, LaunchAgent still registered) is invisible to this whole mechanism - `av list` transparently respawns the agent itself, no error at all - so `.serviceNotRunning` in practice only fires for a genuinely deregistered/not-yet-installed LaunchAgent. Also confirmed live: after a real `bootout`, `open -a` on the plain `.app` bundle can spawn a non-launchd-supervised duplicate that leaves subsequent `av` calls hanging indefinitely rather than failing fast - recovered by killing that duplicate and re-registering via `launchctl kickstart -k gui/<uid>/com.automicvault.menubar-helper`. Not a code fix (this is `av`'s own launchd-activation behavior, out of this app's control), but worth knowing before trying to force this state again: use `bootout`, not a menu-bar quit, and prefer `kickstart -k` over `open -a` to restore it afterward if a test run goes sideways.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-wake-recheck-fix` closed the remaining gap: a captain-reported false "not installed"/"not configured" lock-screen message after waking a Mac that had been asleep for hours, despite `av` being genuinely installed and the password secret genuinely present.** Root cause, live-reproduced (not just inferred): `checkAppPasswordConfigured()`'s failure branch only special-cased the exact `serviceNotRunningMarker` text - *any other* non-zero-exit `av list` failure fell straight through to `.avUnavailable` ("Automic Vault isn't installed"), and a plain `av list` had no timeout at all, so a transiently unresponsive approval helper (confirmed live: `kill -STOP` on the `AutomicVaultMenubar` process makes `av list` hang indefinitely - still blocked after 90+s with zero output, no internal timeout of its own) blocked the check forever with no error text to even classify. Fixed with a new `AppPasswordAvailability.transientFailure` case covering both (1) the check timing out (`VaultSource.runWithTimeout`, a `DispatchSemaphore`+`terminationHandler` watchdog mirroring `FleetData.swift`'s `crewState` pattern, bounded to 5s and scoped only to this app-lock check - `loadSnapshot()`'s own `av list`/`doctor --json` calls for the full Vault page are unbounded exactly as before) and (2) any non-zero exit whose text doesn't match the known marker. `AppShellController` retries `.transientFailure` on the identical 1.5s cadence already used for `.serviceNotRunning` (factored into a shared `scheduleAppPasswordAvailabilityRetry` helper) rather than ever showing it as a hard, final state. Live-verified all four reachable classifications on this machine without touching the real secret: `.configured` (clean success), `.notConfigured` (a safe temporary probe checking a bogus, definitely-unsaved secret name against a real successful `av list`, never the real `GRANDLINE_APP_PASSWORD`), `.serviceNotRunning` (real `bootout`, unchanged), and `.transientFailure` (real `kill -STOP`/`kill -CONT` on the helper - confirmed the check now returns in exactly the 5s timeout bound instead of hanging forever, then correctly resolves back to `.configured` once resumed). `.avUnavailable`'s own guard (`resolveExecutable("av") == nil`) is untouched by this fix and wasn't re-verified live, since forcing it would mean moving/hiding the real root-owned `/usr/local/bin/av` on the shared dev machine - out of scope for a safe, reversible check.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-background-launch` fixed a captain-reported focus-stealing bug in the same `ensureServiceRunning()`**: plain `open -a "Automic Vault"` activates (brings to the front) whatever it targets regardless of that app's own `LSUIElement` setting - `LSUIElement` only suppresses a Dock icon/menu bar presence, it does not stop `open` from activating the process - so Automic Vault visibly came to the foreground on every Grand Line launch/relock, not just the "service wasn't running yet" case. Fixed by adding `-g` (do not bring the app to the foreground) to the same `open -a` call - no other change to `ensureServiceRunning()`'s call site, timing, or the `.serviceNotRunning`/`.avUnavailable` retry logic above. Verified live: quit Automic Vault entirely, ran `open -g -a "Automic Vault"` directly, confirmed its `AutomicVaultMenubar` process started and `av list` succeeded, while the frontmost app never changed away from the app already in focus.
- **`fm/grandline-vault-no-unnecessary-relaunch` found the `-g` fix above was incomplete: Automic Vault's real main window ("Detectors/Hardened Tools/Secrets") was still visibly popping up on nearly every Grand Line launch/relock.** Root cause, confirmed live: `/Applications/Automic Vault.app`'s executable (`AutomicVaultMenubar`) is kept alive independently by its own `RunAtLoad: true` LaunchAgent (`~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.automicvault.menubar-helper.plist`), so it is almost always already running by the time `ensureServiceRunning()` fires - and `open -a`/`open -g -a` on an app that's already running triggers macOS's standard reactivation/"reopen" handling regardless of `-g`, since `-g` only suppresses *foreground activation*, not the app's own window-restore behavior on reopen. The fix is `VaultSource.isServiceRunning()` (`pgrep -x AutomicVaultMenubar`) - `ensureServiceRunning()` now calls `open -g -a "Automic Vault"` only when the helper genuinely isn't already running; when it is, it does nothing. Verified live end to end: with the helper running, calling `ensureServiceRunning()` is a confirmed no-op (no `open` call at all); with the helper genuinely killed (`launchctl bootout` + killing any lingering process), `ensureServiceRunning()` still starts it and `checkAppPasswordConfigured()` recovers from `.serviceNotRunning` to `.configured` - the `fm/grandline-vault-bootstrap-fix` retry flow is unaffected. `isServiceRunning()` is `internal`, not `private`, so `VaultDataSelfTest` can assert it against this machine's real state.

## Dictation

**`.dictation` (`fm/grandline-dictation-mvp`, phase 1 of a captain-approved Lavish plan) is a rail destination pinned directly above `.docs` and below `.vault`** (so the bottom-anchored utility group reads Tools, Vault, Dictation, Docs, Setup, avatar) - a first-party, in-process dictation pipeline. A third-party "OpenSuperWhisper" integration was explicitly rejected as impractical during plan discussion (different build system, a Rust-based engine incompatible with this app's plain-`swift build`-only, no-Xcode convention) - see `native/README.md`. Phase 1 is deliberately minimal: real permission status, the fixed hold-to-record shortcut, and the actual capture/transcribe/paste pipeline - no history, no personal vocabulary, no shortcut recorder, no cleanup pass (all explicitly phase 2/3, not built at the time).

- **Engine**: 100% Apple frameworks, no vendored engine (`DictationEngine.swift`). `AVAudioEngine` captures microphone audio into an `SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest` while the hotkey is held; `SFSpeechRecognizer` transcribes it, requesting `requiresOnDeviceRecognition = true` whenever `recognizer.supportsOnDeviceRecognition` is true for the current locale/OS (falling back to Apple's server-backed recognition otherwise - not every locale/OS combination supports on-device). `DictationPermissions` is the one source of truth for all three permission reads (`AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus(for: .audio)`, `SFSpeechRecognizer.authorizationStatus()`, `AXIsProcessTrusted()`) and the three real system-prompt request calls - both `DictationController`'s manual "Request access" buttons and `DictationEngine.startRecording()`'s own inline auto-request (see below) go through it, never a second permission-reading path.
- **Hotkey mechanism reuses Shift's own global-hotkey pattern, not a second one.** `DictationHotkey.swift` is built on the identical local+global `NSEvent` monitor / `AXIsProcessTrusted` shape `ShiftGlobalHotkey` (`ShiftQuickCapture.swift`, phase 5) already established for ⌥Space - see that file's header for the full local-vs-global-monitor/Accessibility-trust reasoning, which applies here unchanged. The one real difference: Right ⌥ Option is a modifier key, so it fires `.flagsChanged` events (keycode 61, `kVK_RightOption` - Left Option, keycode 58, is deliberately never matched), not `.keyDown`/`.keyUp`; `DictationHotkey.handle(_:)` tracks held/released transitions itself rather than relying on distinct down/up event types. Both hotkeys' monitors coexist independently - confirmed live (a real launched app with both features active; also structurally true since each installs its own independent `NSEvent` monitor pair). There is one shared Accessibility trust grant for the whole app (one "Grand Line" entry in System Settings) - Shift's launch-time `requestPermissionIfNeeded()` and Dictation's page-driven one both call the same `AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions` API and don't conflict.
- **Permission requests fire "the first time genuinely needed," per the task brief - not eagerly at launch (unlike Shift's own eager Accessibility request).** Microphone/Speech Recognition: `DictationEngine.startRecording()` (called from the hotkey's onDown) checks `DictationPermissions` itself and, if a permission is `.notDetermined`, calls the real `AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess`/`SFSpeechRecognizer.requestAuthorization` inline before actually opening the microphone - this is the real "hold Right ⌥ Option" usage flow, not just the Dictation page's manual buttons. A `wantsToRecord` flag guards against the key being released before the async system dialog resolves (a very likely shape for a captain's first-ever tap) - if the key comes up before permission resolves, recording never starts even if the grant lands moments later. Accessibility: requested only from the Dictation page's own "Request Accessibility Access" button (`DictationPermissions.requestAccessibility()`), since there's no natural "just tried to use it" moment to hook for a modifier-key hold that literally cannot fire without that trust already granted.
- **Paste mechanism: `NSPasteboard` + a synthetic Cmd+V (`CGEvent`), not the Accessibility `AXUIElement` text-insertion API.** `AXUIElement` insertion (e.g. `kAXSelectedTextAttribute`) only works against apps exposing a conforming Accessibility text role for their focused element - which excludes most terminal emulators, including this app's own SwiftTerm-based Console tabs, and many Electron/web-based editors. A synthetic Cmd+V lands anywhere a real paste keystroke would, a strictly larger and more predictable target set, for the same Accessibility-trust cost. `DictationEngine.pasteAtCursor` always writes the pasteboard (useful even without trust - a captain can paste manually) and only posts the synthetic keystroke once `AXIsProcessTrusted()` is true.
- **The Dictation page (`DictationController.swift`) is deliberately sparse**: a Status card (icon/title/detail driven by `DictationStatus`, plus a "Request access" button for whichever permission is missing) and a Shortcut card (states the fixed Right ⌥ Option combo and that a shortcut recorder is coming later), following `VaultController`/`ShiftPanelView`'s existing card layout rather than inventing new chrome. Status refreshes on every `viewWillAppear` (no polling, per PRODUCT.md's "quiet until it matters") and live via `DictationEngine.onStatusChanged` → `AppShellController.setDictationEngineStatus` → `DictationController.setEngineStatus` while the page happens to be visible, so a captain watching this page while dictating sees "Recording…"/"Transcribing…" for real.
- **`Info.plist`** (`native/build_native_app.sh`, the only place this repo assembles a bundle `Info.plist` - see that file's own header) gained `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`/`NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription`.
- **Explicitly out of scope for phase 1, still to come**: transcription history, a personal-vocabulary editor (`contextualStrings`), a configurable shortcut recorder (phase 1 ships the fixed Right ⌥ Option combo only), and a "clean up my sentences" polish pass via `claude -p` (needs network + Claude auth) - none of this is scaffolded yet. Vendoring `whisper.cpp` is only a possible later upgrade path if Apple's Speech framework proves insufficient in real use; not started.
- **Verified**: `DictationHotkeySelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_DICTATION_HOTKEY_TESTS=1`, same permanent-self-test convention as `AppLockSelfTest.swift`) drives real synthetic `.flagsChanged` `NSEvent`s through the real `DictationHotkey.handle(_:)` - hold/release fires onDown/onUp exactly once each, repeated same-state events don't double-fire, Left Option never triggers, an unrelated modifier keycode never triggers. The rail placement (button frame between Vault and Docs, non-hidden, correct title/tooltip, real dividers) was verified live via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`. The full app was also launched standalone against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR` and confirmed to start and stay running with no crash. **Not verified in this sandbox**: the actual end-to-end hold-Right-Option → capture real audio → on-device transcribe → paste-into-a-real-target-app path, and the real system permission-prompt dialogs themselves - this sandbox has no real audio input device, no interactive login session, and no way to grant Microphone/Speech Recognition/Accessibility permissions non-interactively (the same class of constraint this file's "Verifying native UI bugs without a real screenshot" entry already documents for Screen Recording/Accessibility). That last-mile live check - a real hold, a real transcription, a real paste into a real other app - is the captain's own to confirm on real hardware before relying on this in daily use.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-transcribe-hang-fix` fixed a real captain-reported bug in the core pipeline above: a hold-and-speak often showed "Recording…" then "Transcribing…" for a long time, then silently reverted to "Ready" with the transcript never pasted - no error, no feedback.** This machine turned out to have a real audio input device and a real interactive login session (contrary to the "not verified in this sandbox" note directly above, which was written from a different, non-interactive sandbox), so this was reproduced live rather than patched blind - a standalone harness mirroring `beginCapture` exactly, feeding it real mic audio via a real `AVAudioEngine` tap while `say` produced real speech through the speakers. **Root cause, confirmed live - a delayed empty result, not a true hang**: with `shouldReportPartialResults = false` (the original setting) and on-device recognition, holding the hotkey through a few seconds of trailing silence after speech ends (an entirely ordinary usage pattern - not releasing the instant the last word finishes) reliably (3/3 live runs) produces a **non-final** result carrying the correct, complete transcript, followed several seconds later by a **final** result (`result.isFinal == true`) whose `bestTranscription.formattedString` is an **empty string** - a real `SFSpeechRecognizer` quirk. Since `shouldReportPartialResults = false` meant the non-final result's real text was never looked at, and `finish(text:)`'s "only paste on final, only if non-empty" guard discarded the empty final text, the outcome matched the report exactly: no paste, no error, `finish` calling `onStatusChanged?(DictationPermissions.currentStatus())`, which resolves straight back to `.ready`. A quick-release-right-after-speaking case (no trailing silence) was unaffected by this quirk and always worked, which is presumably why phase 1's own original live verification pass didn't catch it. **Fix (`DictationEngine.swift`)**: `shouldReportPartialResults` is now `true`, and every result (partial or final) with non-empty text updates a new `bestTranscriptSeen`; `finish(text:)` falls back to it whenever the result it was actually called with is empty or nil (covering the empty-final-result quirk above, and a genuine recognition error trailing a good partial). `stopRecording()` also schedules an 8s bounded timeout (`finishTimeoutWorkItem`) after `endAudio()` that calls `finish(text: nil)` if no final result has landed by then, so a final result that genuinely never arrives can no longer wait forever - it finalizes with whatever was heard, or nothing. A new `DictationStatus.didNotCatchThat` case ("Didn't catch that - try again") is what `finish` shows on genuine silence/no usable transcript, replacing what used to be an indistinguishable silent revert to `.ready`. **Verified live end to end** (same real hardware/session as the repro): a real hold → real `say`-produced speech → real trailing silence → real release round trip pasted the correct transcript into a real, frontmost TextEdit document (confirmed by reading the document's real text afterward, not just the pasteboard) in every run; a silence-only hold → release showed the new "Didn't catch that" status with no paste and no crash; the quick-release-right-after-speaking case (already working) was unaffected. `DictationHotkeySelfTest` was re-run post-fix with no regression - the hotkey mechanism, permission-request flow, and `pasteAtCursor` itself were untouched, per this fix's own scope.
- **Phase 2 (`fm/grandline-dictation-phase2`) added the three things phase 1 deferred: history, personal vocabulary, and a configurable shortcut.** The "clean up my sentences" polish pass was still out of scope at the time (phase 3, below).
  - **History + vocabulary (`DictationData.swift`)**: `DictationStore` mirrors `HostStore.swift`'s shape exactly - two sibling JSON files (`history.json`, `vocabulary.json`) under `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/dictation/`, overridable via `FM_DICTATION_DIR`. `DictationEngine.onTranscript` now carries `(text, duration)` and only fires on a real successful paste (never on `.didNotCatchThat`) - `AppDelegate` (`main.swift`) wires it straight to `DictationStore.recordHistory`. `DictationEngine.vocabularyProvider` is read fresh on every `beginCapture` and, when non-empty, set on `SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest.contextualStrings` - a real, documented Speech-framework API for exactly this bias, not a cosmetic list. Live-verified (temporary env-gated probe, reverted before commit): the real request object's `contextualStrings` reflected the store's vocabulary at record time.
  - **Rendering (`DictationHistoryAndVocabularyViews.swift`)**: the history list is `NSTableView`-based (`DictationHistoryListView`), not a plain `NSStackView` of permanent rows - same reasoning as `ShiftListViews.swift`/`DiffResultView.swift` (see those entries above for the measured multi-second-layout blowup that pattern hits at a few hundred rows), since history is unbounded and only grows. The vocabulary chip row is small/bounded by contrast, so it's a plain frame-based flow layout (`ChipFlowView`, manual wrap on `layout()`, `isFlipped` for top-to-bottom row order) rather than a table.
  - **Configurable shortcut**: `DictationShortcut` (`DictationHotkey.swift`) replaces the phase-1 fixed Right ⌥ Option combo, persisted via `AppSettings.dictationShortcut` (JSON-encoded `Data`, same convention as every other `AppSettings` value). It's either `isModifierOnly` (a single held modifier key, phase 1's original shape - `.flagsChanged` events) or a regular key + modifiers combo (e.g. ⌘⇧D - `.keyDown`/`.keyUp` events); `DictationHotkey.start()`/`updateShortcut(_:)` install only the monitor pair the current shape needs and tear down/reinstall on change, so switching shapes never leaves a stale monitor listening. Only the four standard modifiers (⌘⌥⌃⇧) are tracked (`DictationShortcut.relevantModifierMask`) - Caps Lock/Fn are deliberately excluded since they reflect toggle/ambient state, not a deliberate press. `DictationShortcutRecorderView.swift` is the small self-built `NSView` recorder control (no external dependency, per this app's zero-remote-SPM-dependency convention) - click to record, first modifier-down transition + release with no regular key in between finalizes a modifier-only combo, any `keyDown` while recording finalizes a regular-key combo immediately (overriding a pending modifier-only state, since a modifier held right before a regular key was always part of a combo). `DictationController.onShortcutChanged` → `AppShellController.onDictationShortcutChanged` → `AppDelegate` calling `dictationHotkey.updateShortcut(_:)` is the one path a new recording takes effect on the *live* hotkey instance, mirroring `SettingsController.onFontSizeStep`'s forward-don't-own convention. `DictationStatus.detail` became `detail(shortcutDisplay:)` since the shortcut text can no longer be a static per-case literal.
  - **Verified**: `DictationHotkeySelfTest.swift` gained `handleKeyEvent(_:)` coverage (regular-key combo hold/release, auto-repeat absorption, exact-not-`.contains` modifier matching, `updateShortcut` switching mechanisms) alongside phase 1's existing `handle(_:)` coverage. `DictationDataSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_DICTATION_DATA_TESTS=1`) covers `DictationStore`'s history/vocabulary persistence (real scratch disk, reloaded via a fresh instance) and `DictationShortcut`'s encode/decode + display-string logic. Live-verified beyond the self-tests (temporary probes, reverted before commit): a real `NSApp.sendEvent`-driven synthetic Right ⌥ Option press+release fired the real installed `DictationHotkey` monitor, then `updateShortcut` to ⌘D confirmed the OLD combo no longer fired and the NEW one did; `contextualStrings` on the real request object matched the store's vocabulary (see above); and a real recording attempt (via `say` + a real mic tap) reached `.recording` → a real `SFSpeechRecognizer` error → `.didNotCatchThat`, confirming no regression to the hang-fix's failure-state handling. **Not verified in this sandbox**: a real successful end-to-end transcription (this machine's `SFSpeechRecognizer` calls fail with "Siri and Dictation are disabled" - a system-level Setting, unrelated to this app's own permission grants or code) - the history-persistence-on-a-real-completed-dictation path is covered by `DictationDataSelfTest`'s real-disk round trip instead, not a live spoken utterance.
- **Phase 3 (`fm/grandline-dictation-phase3`, the final planned phase) added the "clean up my sentences" polish pass - all three originally-planned phases are now complete.** A new "Cleanup" card on the Dictation page (`DictationController.buildCleanupSection`) has one `NSSwitch` toggle, persisted via `AppSettings.dictationCleanupEnabled` (off by default, since this is the one step in an otherwise fully on-device/offline pipeline that needs network access and the captain's own `claude` authentication - the toggle's own description text says so). `DictationCleanup.swift` runs the rewrite as a single non-interactive `claude -p "<prompt>" --output-format json` call, deliberately mirroring `SRELeadRunner.swift`'s own `Process`/argument/parsing shape (see that file's header) rather than reusing it directly or inventing a third way of invoking `claude` - this is a stateless one-shot rewrite with no `--resume`/conversation state, no MCP config, and no persona, so a second small type was clearer than stretching `SRELeadRunner` to cover both call shapes. `SRELead.resolveClaude()` (already `internal`, built for exactly this kind of second caller) is reused as-is. **Verified live** (real `claude` CLI on this machine, not assumed): a bare `-p` prompt with the rewrite instruction returns `result` as clean, unwrapped text with no preamble or quotes - `DictationCleanup`'s parsing matches `SRELeadRunner`'s exactly (last non-empty stdout line, `is_error`, `result`) and additionally strips a wrapping quote pair defensively, since the model can occasionally add one depending on phrasing (not load-bearing for the common case, confirmed live). `DictationEngine.finish(text:)` is the one call site: when `cleanupEnabledProvider` (wired in `main.swift` to `AppSettings.dictationCleanupEnabled`, read fresh per-dictation, not cached) is on, a new `DictationStatus.cleaningUp` status shows while the rewrite runs, and the result (cleaned on success, the original raw transcript on *any* failure - no network, not authenticated, `claude` missing, a 20s timeout, a garbled response) is what gets pasted **and** recorded into history - `deliver(_:duration:)` is the one place both paths converge, so paste and history always agree on which text was actually used. A cleanup failure never blocks or loses the dictation. **Verified live end to end, not just by reading the code**: two real rough/rambling transcripts run through the real `claude -p` path came back visibly cleaned up (filler words and false starts removed, meaning preserved) and were confirmed on the real pasteboard; with the toggle off, the raw transcript passed through byte-for-byte with no `claude` call at all (proven by pointing the resolved-claude path at a nonexistent binary and confirming it was still never consulted); and with the toggle on but a forced `claude` failure (bad binary path), the dictation still delivered the raw transcript with no crash and no lost paste - all three exercised via a temporary env-gated hook on `DictationEngine` (a `debugFinishForTests` passthrough to the private `finish(text:)`, since the alternative was a real microphone/speech-recognition round trip) and a temporary probe in `main.swift`, both reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention. `DictationCleanupSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_DICTATION_CLEANUP_TESTS=1`) is the permanent regression coverage for `DictationCleanup.rewrite` itself - a real disposable fake-`claude` shell script (`DictationCleanup.claudePathOverrideForTests`, a test-only seam) drives the actual `Process`/parsing code end to end for a well-formed success, a quote-wrapped success, `is_error:true`, garbled/non-JSON output, and a nonexistent binary path, plus a check that an empty/whitespace-only transcript never spawns a process at all. No regression to phase 1/2's pipeline, permissions, hotkey, vocabulary, or history - `DictationHotkeySelfTest`/`DictationDataSelfTest` were re-run post-change with no change in outcome, and the app was relaunched standalone against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_DICTATION_DIR` with no crash. Vendoring `whisper.cpp` remains the only item ever mentioned for a possible future task - not scoped or started, and there is no signal yet that Apple's Speech framework is insufficient.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-vocab-backup` folded the vocabulary list and configured shortcut into the existing portable-config bundle (`BackupData.swift`/`BackupUI.swift`, see the local-state-portable bullet below) instead of building a second export/import feature.** `GrandLineBackup.dictation: BackupDictation?` carries `vocabulary`/`shortcut`, built via `BackupDictation.fromCurrent(store:)` and applied via `.apply(to:)` (words added through `DictationStore.addVocabularyWord`'s existing case-insensitive dedup, never a raw overwrite). Deliberately excludes `DictationStore.history` - per-machine usage data, the same "not portable configuration" bar that already keeps `fmHome` out of `BackupSettings`. The field is optional so this needed no `formatVersion` bump: an old bundle decodes with `dictation == nil` via Swift's synthesized `Decodable` (`Optional` properties decode as `decodeIfPresent`), and a new bundle read by pre-this-task code just ignores the extra key. `BackupImport.diff`/`.apply` extend the same new/changed/unchanged diff-and-preview flow - vocabulary words diff individually (`.new`/`.unchanged` only, matched case-insensitively, never `.changed`), the shortcut diffs as one `.changed`/`.unchanged` value. `SettingsController`/`BootstrapController`/`AutomationController` all gained a `dictationStore` init param threaded through from `main.swift`/`AppShellController` (which already held one) - no new UI entry points, per the task's scope. `BackupSelfTest.swift` gained this coverage in place (not a parallel file): a real vocabulary word importing as new, an already-present one (different casing) as unchanged, a shortcut change applying and persisting, and a grep of the actual exported bytes confirming no history text and no `"history"` key at all. **The one thing this task's own self-test had to guard against**: `AppSettings` is backed by real `UserDefaults.standard` with no scratch-file override the way `HostStore`/`DictationStore` have `FM_*_FILE`/`FM_DICTATION_DIR` - the test saves the real machine's configured shortcut before mutating it and restores it via `defer`, so running the self-test never leaves the captain's actual dictation shortcut altered (confirmed live by reading the real preferences plist before/after a run).
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-global-hotkey-and-theme-fixes` investigated a captain report that the global hotkey "only works while Grand Line itself has focus" - extensive live reproduction against the real signed app (HID-level synthetic `CGEventPost` events, indistinguishable from real hardware, with a genuinely different frontmost app rigorously re-confirmed immediately before every keypress) found NO active defect**: the default modifier-only shortcut fired correctly with TextEdit frontmost, and a full modifier-only → regular-key-combo → modifier-only `updateShortcut` round trip (the exact "switching monitor mechanisms" scenario suspected as the culprit) fired correctly at every stage. One early, sloppier test pass produced a false negative from focus having silently reverted to Grand Line between the frontmost-check and the keypress (a real, observed phenomenon on this shared dev machine, most plausibly a human using the machine concurrently - not a code defect). **The one real, confirmed gap**: `DictationHotkeySelfTest.swift` never installed a real monitor at all before this task - every test called `handle(_:)`/`handleKeyEvent(_:)` directly, proving the hold/release decision logic but never proving `start()`/`updateShortcut(_:)` actually register a live `NSEvent.addGlobalMonitorForEvents` monitor. `DictationHotkey`'s four monitor-token properties are now internal (not `private`) so three new structural self-test cases can confirm the correct local+global pair is installed/torn down for each mechanism at every stage of a switch round trip - this is what actually protects against the class of regression the brief worried about, since a future edit that silently drops the global registration for one mechanism would now fail a test immediately rather than only failing silently in the field. If this symptom recurs, suspect environment/focus interference (a third-party app's own event tap, a stale second running instance, revoked-then-silently-stale Accessibility trust) over a `DictationHotkey` code defect - re-run the same rigorous CGEventPost-based reproduction (a genuinely different app frontmost, re-confirmed immediately before every keypress) before assuming otherwise.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-global-hotkey-and-theme-fixes` also fixed a real, confirmed theme bug: the Dictation page's root view had `wantsLayer = true` (`DictationController.loadView`) but `applyTheme()` never gave that layer a background color** - unlike every other full-size destination (`VaultController`/`FleetController`/etc., see this file's AppKit gotcha #8), so the layer stayed transparent and whatever sat behind it in the window showed through as a visible seam between the panels' own explicitly-themed fills - a captain screenshot showed this as a mismatched brown/maroon color between cards. Fixed with one line in `applyTheme()` (`view.layer?.backgroundColor = HelmTheme.nsColor(theme.backgroundHex).cgColor`), matching every sibling page. Verified live across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`catppuccin-latte`/`gruvbox-light` via a temporary env-gated probe (reverted before commit) confirming the root layer's background is non-transparent and matches each theme's `backgroundHex` after a live theme switch.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-stuck-transcribing-fix` fixed a real, live-reproduced bug: the status page could get stuck on "Transcribing…" forever, with no error and no revert - captain-reported with the shortcut set to Right ⌘ Command, but the root cause turned out to be keycode-independent, not a Command-specific interaction at all.** Reproduced live with real HID-level `CGEventPost` events on this machine (whose `SFSpeechRecognizer` fails fast with `kLSRErrorDomain` "Siri and Dictation are disabled" - a system-level setting, same constraint the phase-2 verification bullet above already documents) and confirmed to reproduce identically with the default Right ⌥ Option, proving the race has nothing to do with which key is held. **Root cause**: recognition can complete (a real final result, or - as reproduced here - a fast error) *before* the hotkey is ever released. `finish(text:)` used to never reset `isRecording`, so the *later* `stopRecording()` call (on release) still passed its own `guard isRecording`, overwrote `finish(text:)`'s already-correct final status back to `.transcribing`, and scheduled a second `finishTimeoutWorkItem` - whose eventual `finish(text:)` call then hit the existing `guard !isFinishing` and silently returned, never updating the status again. Stuck on "Transcribing…" forever. **Fix (`DictationEngine.swift`)**: `finish(text:)` now tears down audio capture (`isRecording = false`, remove tap, stop the engine) itself the moment recognition actually ends, whenever `isRecording` is still `true` at that point - so a `stopRecording()` call arriving later on release correctly no-ops via its own existing `guard isRecording` instead of stomping the already-finalized status. The normal case (hotkey released before recognition completes) is unchanged, since `isRecording` is already `false` by the time `finish(text:)` runs in that order. **Defense-in-depth, per the task brief's explicit ask**: every `onStatusChanged` call now goes through a new `report(_:)` helper that also arms a `hardCeilingWorkItem` (`finishTimeout + 5` = 13s) whenever `.transcribing` is reported - an absolute wall-clock watchdog that forces the displayed status to `.didNotCatchThat` if still showing `.transcribing` by then, deliberately bypassing `isFinishing`/`isRecording` entirely so "stuck forever" stays impossible even against a future bug this fix didn't anticipate, not just the specific race found here. Verified live (temporary env-gated logging in `DictationEngine.swift`/`DictationHotkey.swift`, plus a temporary `main.swift` hook to force the hotkey's shortcut in-process without ever touching the real, shared `AppSettings.dictationShortcut` UserDefaults key - both reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): the exact stuck state reproduced pre-fix with Right ⌘ Command, resolved correctly post-fix for both Right ⌘ Command and Right ⌥ Option (no regression), and all three existing Dictation self-tests (`DictationHotkeySelfTest`/`DictationDataSelfTest`/`DictationCleanupSelfTest`) still pass.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-visual-feedback-hud` added a floating on-screen HUD (`DictationHUD.swift`) since Dictation previously gave zero visual feedback anywhere except its own rail page - useless for a feature whose whole point is dictating into whatever app currently has focus, never Grand Line's own window.** `DictationHUDController` is a small, additive second subscriber to the exact same `DictationEngine.onStatusChanged` callback the Dictation page's status card already used (`main.swift` now fans that one callback out to both `AppShellController.setDictationEngineStatus` and `dictationHUD.handle(status)`) - no second status-broadcast mechanism was invented. The HUD itself is a borderless `NSPanel` (`[.borderless, .nonactivatingPanel]`, `level = .floating`, `ignoresMouseEvents = true`, shown via `orderFrontRegardless()` - never `makeKeyAndOrderFront`) so it can appear above whatever app the captain is dictating into without ever taking key-window status or activating Grand Line - confirmed live (see below). Position is fixed (bottom-center of whichever screen has the mouse cursor), not cursor-tracked - Apple's own dictation HUD tracks the real text caret via Accessibility, but that's an explicitly out-of-scope nice-to-have for this pass. **The one real design problem this task had to solve**: the engine has no distinct "success" status of its own - a successful paste and a Dictation-page permission-button click can both resolve to the same `.ready`/`.needsMicrophone`/etc. value. `DictationHUDController` tracks its own `wasActive` flag, set only on a real `.recording` status and cleared on the next terminal status (any non-in-progress status, or `.didNotCatchThat`) - so the HUD only ever reacts to a value it has already seen a live `.recording` precede, and a bare permission-button `.ready`/`.needsMicrophone` with no recording in between never shows it. States: `.recording` → pulsing waveform icon, "Listening…" (stays up, no timer); `.transcribing`/`.cleaningUp` → matching icon/text (also no timer - these are true in-progress states, ended by the next real transition, not a clock); a terminal `.ready`/permission-state → a checkmark "Pasted" flash (auto-hides after 1.1s); `.didNotCatchThat` → the existing failure text (auto-hides after 1.8s) - never left on screen indefinitely, never needs a click to dismiss. Deliberately not theme-aware (unlike almost everything else in this app) - the HUD floats over arbitrary other apps' windows, not Grand Line's own chrome, so a fixed dark/translucent "system HUD" look (mirroring Control Center's own on-screen indicators) was chosen over trying to blend with a Helm theme that has no relationship to whatever's behind it. **Verified live** (temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): drove the real `dictationEngine.onStatusChanged` callback through recording → transcribing → success and, separately, recording → transcribing → `.didNotCatchThat`, with a genuinely different real app (TextEdit, activated via `NSWorkspace`) frontmost throughout - the real panel's `isKeyWindow` stayed `false` at every step and frontmost stayed TextEdit through the always-attributable steps (a couple of readings later in the run showed `FirstmateCockpit` frontmost, consistent with this shared dev machine's own documented focus-interference caveat elsewhere in this file, not with the panel itself - it never became key at any point, which is the actual mechanism that prevents focus-stealing); confirmed the success/failure states auto-hide on their own after their fixed delays with no click; and confirmed a bare `.ready` with no prior `.recording` correctly never shows the HUD at all (the `wasActive` gate). Also confirmed no regression: `DictationHotkeySelfTest`/`DictationDataSelfTest`/`DictationCleanupSelfTest` all still pass, and the app launches and stays running standalone against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_DICTATION_DIR` with no crash. **Not verified in this sandbox**: a real end-to-end hold-the-shortcut-and-speak session (this sandbox's `SFSpeechRecognizer` fails with "Siri and Dictation are disabled," the same pre-existing constraint documented earlier in this section) - the HUD's own state machine was instead driven directly through the real `onStatusChanged` callback, which is the one path both the real hotkey flow and this verification share.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-system-disabled-message` gave the "Siri and Dictation are disabled" case its own distinct, actionable status instead of the generic "Didn't catch that."** Every prior entry above that hit this exact condition (the phase-2 verification bullet, `fm/grandline-dictation-stuck-transcribing-fix`) treated it only as a sandbox constraint blocking further testing - this task is what actually handles it as a real captain-facing usability gap. **Live-confirmed on a real Mac** (toggling System Settings > Keyboard > Dictation off and running the real `recognitionTask(with:)` completion closure): that case reports `domain == "kLSRErrorDomain"`, `code == 201`, `"Siri and Dictation are disabled"` - a genuine no-speech miss with the system setting still on instead reports a different, unrelated shape, `domain == "kAFAssistantErrorDomain"`, `code == 1110`, `"No speech detected"`. `DictationEngine.swift`'s completion closure now checks for the first shape specifically (`systemDictationDisabledErrorDomain`/`Code` constants) and routes it to a new `DictationStatus.systemDictationDisabled` case via `finish(text:systemDictationDisabled:)`, bypassing the generic empty-text-falls-through-to-`.didNotCatchThat` path entirely - the second shape is untouched and still resolves to `.didNotCatchThat` exactly as before. Both `DictationController`'s Status card and `DictationHUD.swift`'s floating HUD consume the new case automatically through `DictationStatus`'s own `title`/`detail`/`symbol`/`tint`, with one addition needed in each file's own exhaustive switch (the page's action-button switch, the HUD's `handle(_:)` mapping to a distinct "System Dictation is off" pill). **Verified live end to end**, not just by reading the code: with system Dictation off, a real recording cycle reported `.systemDictationDisabled`; with it back on and genuine silence, the same cycle still reported `.didNotCatchThat` unchanged; with it on and real ambient speech recognized, the cycle still reached `.ready` (a successful paste) unchanged - all three driven through the real `DictationEngine.startRecording()`/`stopRecording()` on the real signed app via a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-whisper-engine` added an optional local Whisper engine (vendored `whisper.cpp`) as an alternative to the Apple Speech framework path above - the "vendoring `whisper.cpp` is only a possible later upgrade path" note in the phase-3 bullet is now acted on.** `Vendor/whisper.cpp/` vendors upstream `ggml-org/whisper.cpp` (MIT, pinned to tag `v1.9.2`) as plain C/C++ source compiled by a hand-written SPM target (`CWhisper` in `Package.swift`) - CPU-only with the baseline ARM NEON kernels (`ggml-cpu/arch/arm/*`), no Metal/CUDA/Accelerate/AMX/llamafile/dynamic-backend-loading - see `Vendor/whisper.cpp/README.md` for the exact file list trimmed from upstream and why (mirrors `Vendor/SwiftTerm/README.md`/`Vendor/YamlSwift/README.md`'s own convention). The official `whisper.spm` remote package was explicitly rejected per the captain-approved plan, matching this app's zero-remote-SPM-dependency rule.
  - **`WhisperEngine.swift`** is the whole Swift wrapper: `WhisperCppEngine` (`init?(modelPath:)` fails, never crashes, on a missing/corrupt/truncated model; `transcribe(samples:initialPrompt:language:)` runs `whisper_full` with greedy decoding) and `DictationAudioResampler` (`AVAudioConverter`-based, resamples the live tap's native-rate audio to the 16kHz mono Float32 `whisper_full` requires - `SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest` does this resampling internally, but whisper.cpp needs the caller to hand it 16kHz samples directly).
  - **`WhisperModel.swift`** (`WhisperModelManager`, app-lifetime singleton) downloads the quantized `large-v3-turbo` ggml model (`ggml-large-v3-turbo-q5_0.bin`, ~547MB) on demand into `~/Library/Application Support/FirstmateCockpit/whisper/` (override `FM_WHISPER_MODEL_DIR`, same convention as `DictationStore`'s own `FM_DICTATION_DIR`) from `https://huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp/resolve/main/ggml-large-v3-turbo-q5_0.bin` - live-verified to resolve to a real ~547MB file (HTTP 302 → 200, matching content-length) before wiring this up, and the same host/path convention upstream's own `models/download-ggml-model.sh` uses. `WhisperModelState` (`.notDownloaded`/`.downloading(progress:)`/`.ready`/`.failed(String)`) drives a real `NSProgressIndicator` on the Dictation page. **Validation, not a checksum** (upstream publishes no checksum manifest for these files - checked directly): `WhisperModelManager.validate(fileAt:)` requires a size floor (catches a near-empty/aborted transfer) and the file starting with `GGML_FILE_MAGIC` (`0x67676d6c`, whisper.cpp's own model-loader magic check) before `didFinishDownloadingTo` ever moves the downloaded temp file into place - a file that fails either check is never moved anywhere real, so a failed/interrupted download can't be mistaken for a valid model later. A file that passes both but is still truncated mid-tensor-data is caught one layer up, by `WhisperCppEngine.init?` failing to load it.
  - **`DictationEngine` runs both engines in parallel when the toggle is on, not one-then-fallback-to-the-other sequentially**: `beginCapture` decides once (`usingLocalWhisperThisRecording`, via a new `localWhisperEnabledProvider`) whether this recording also resamples audio into `DictationAudioResampler` - the existing `SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest` pipeline keeps running completely unchanged either way, so the toggle-off default path is byte-for-byte identical to before this task. In `finish(text:)`, when the toggle was on, the resampled buffer is handed to a background-queue `whisper_full` call (vocabulary joined into one string as the `initial_prompt` - whisper.cpp's real, if less precise, equivalent to `SFSpeechRecognitionRequest.contextualStrings`, since it has no per-word vocabulary-biasing API) and whichever text comes back (local Whisper's, or the already-computed Apple transcript if Whisper's load/transcribe failed or came back empty) is what `finishWithFinalText` then runs through the existing cleanup-toggle/paste/history pipeline, unchanged. A loaded `WhisperCppEngine` is cached per model path (`loadWhisperEngine(modelPath:)`) so a ~547MB model isn't re-parsed on every single recording.
  - **`AppSettings.dictationLocalWhisperEnabled`** (off by default) is the toggle; deliberately **not** added to `BackupSettings`/`BackupDictation` (the local-state-portable bundle, see below) - the ~547MB model file itself obviously isn't portable across machines, so enabling this on a fresh machine with no model downloaded would just silently fall back to Apple Speech anyway, the same "per-machine, not portable configuration" reasoning that already keeps `fmHome` out of that bundle.
  - **Verified live, not just via `swift build`**: `WhisperEngineSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_WHISPER_ENGINE_TESTS=1`, same convention as every other Dictation self-test) covers `WhisperModelManager.validate` (too-small file, bad-magic file, a realistic-looking fixture) and `DictationAudioResampler` (a synthetic 44.1kHz buffer resamples to ~16kHz) unconditionally, plus a real `whisper_full_params.initial_prompt` C-struct round trip (writes a vocabulary-derived string the same way `WhisperCppEngine.transcribe` does, reads it back via `String(cString:)`, confirms every vocabulary word survived) - this is the "verified by inspecting the real prompt passed to whisper.cpp" check, and needs no model file. When `FM_WHISPER_TEST_MODEL_PATH`/`FM_WHISPER_TEST_AUDIO_PATH` point at a real downloaded model and a real WAV file, it also runs the full real pipeline - confirmed against a real `large-v3-turbo-q5_0` download (content-length matched a live HEAD request, `GGML_FILE_MAGIC` bytes correct) and whisper.cpp's own famous `samples/jfk.wav` fixture: the real model produced a byte-perfect transcript ("And so, my fellow Americans, ask not what your country can do for you, ask what you can do for your country."). **A real, worth-knowing performance finding at the time**: this baseline CPU-only NEON build took several minutes of wall-clock time to transcribe that ~11-second clip on real Apple Silicon hardware - real speech was recognized perfectly, but this build was not fast enough for comfortable interactive use as shipped. **This was fixed by `fm/grandline-dictation-whisper-metal-accel`, which enabled the vendored whisper.cpp's Metal backend** - the same clip on the same model now transcribes in ~0.48s with Metal enabled (live-measured, ~180-490x faster than the CPU-only baseline, ~23x faster than real-time) - see `Vendor/whisper.cpp/README.md`'s "Metal acceleration" section for the full mechanism (how the shader is built/embedded with no CMake/Xcode step, and a real crash found and fixed one layer inside whisper.cpp itself when Metal fails to load with `use_gpu` left `true`). The model-load-failure fallback contract was also verified live directly (a corrupt/truncated file handed to `WhisperCppEngine.init?` returns `nil`, logging whisper.cpp's own real "invalid model data (bad magic)"/"failed to load model" - exactly the signal `DictationEngine.loadWhisperEngine` treats as "fall back to the already-computed Apple transcript"), and a real mid-download cancel (a temporary env-gated probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention) confirmed `WhisperModelManager` lands back on `.notDownloaded` with zero bytes written to the model directory - no partial file left anywhere a later check could mistake for a valid model.
