The app was also relaunched standalone against scratch `FM_HOSTS_FILE`/`FM_KEYS_FILE`/`FM_SNIPPETS_FILE`/`FM_SHIFT_DIR`/`FM_DICTATION_DIR`/`FM_WHISPER_MODEL_DIR` with no crash, confirming no regression to the toggle-off default path or any other Dictation surface (hotkey, HUD, history, shortcut recorder, cleanup toggle, backup/export).
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-whisper-metal-accel` enabled the vendored whisper.cpp's Metal backend, fixing the CPU-only baseline's "several minutes for an 11-second clip" performance gap above** - see `Vendor/whisper.cpp/README.md`'s "Metal acceleration" section for the full writeup; the short version worth knowing before touching this again: (1) the vendored `ggml-metal.metal` shader is pre-merged (its `#include "ggml-common.h"`/`#include "ggml-metal-impl.h"` inlined, mirroring upstream's own `GGML_METAL_EMBED_LIBRARY` CMake mode) by `native/Scripts/build-whisper-metal-shader.py` into a generated `WhisperMetalShaderSource.swift` (base64-embedded, same convention as `CaptainIcon.swift` - re-run that script after any whisper.cpp re-sync, it does not regenerate itself); (2) `WhisperMetalRuntime.swift` writes that text to a real file under the same directory `WhisperModelManager` already uses and points whisper.cpp's own `GGML_METAL_PATH_RESOURCES` env var at it, since this app's `build_native_app.sh` never copies any SwiftPM resource bundle into the assembled `.app`; (3) a real crash was found (not hypothetical) one layer inside whisper.cpp itself: `whisper_init`'s `make_buft_list` adds the GPU device's buffer type to the model's buffer list whenever `params.use_gpu` is `true`, regardless of whether the Metal library actually loaded, so a failed Metal init used to abort the whole process instead of falling back - fixed by never setting `cparams.use_gpu = true` until `WhisperMetalRuntime.metalCanCompileShader()` has already confirmed, via a real pre-flight compile of the identical shader source, that Metal actually works; whisper.cpp's own source (`whisper-src/whisper.cpp`) is otherwise unmodified. `WhisperEngineSelfTest.testMetalFallbackDoesNotCrash()` automates a live regression check for this by spawning a genuinely separate child process with `FM_WHISPER_METAL_RESOURCES_OVERRIDE` pointed at an empty directory (a real crash can only be observed as a child's exit status, never caught in-process) and asserting a clean exit with the correct transcript. Live-measured on real Apple Silicon (Apple M5 Pro): the same `samples/jfk.wav` clip and `large-v3-turbo-q5_0` model that took ~236s CPU-only now takes ~0.48s with Metal - same transcript either way, byte-for-byte.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-long-utterance-status-race` fixed a real captain-reported bug: a genuinely long (~30+ second, multi-sentence) dictation with the local Whisper engine enabled would paste and record the correct transcript to history, but still show "Didn't catch that" on screen** - history and the visible status disagreeing, only for long utterances (a short utterance already worked correctly). **Root cause, live-reproduced before touching any code** (real mic, real `say`-produced ~49s utterance, local Whisper enabled): `fm/grandline-dictation-transcribe-hang-fix`'s hard-ceiling watchdog (`DictationEngine`'s `hardTranscribingCeiling`, then a single fixed 13s) is armed the moment `.transcribing` is reported and forces the display to `.didNotCatchThat` if nothing else has superseded it by then - but it doesn't know how much audio was actually captured, so a long recording whose real pipeline (Apple's own finalization wait plus, once local Whisper is enabled, `whisper_full`'s own processing - both scale with audio length) genuinely needs more than 13s hits the ceiling *before* `finish(text:)` actually completes. Confirmed live by disabling Metal (`FM_WHISPER_METAL_RESOURCES_OVERRIDE` pointed at an empty directory, forcing slower-than-this-machine's-own-Metal transcription): the ceiling fired at exactly 13s while the real transcription was still running in the background, which then completed moments later with the correct transcript - pasted and recorded to history exactly as the captain reported, with the visible status never catching up. **Fix, both halves of what the task brief called for**: (1) `DictationEngine.hardCeilingDuration(forCapturedAudioSeconds:)` replaces the fixed 13s constant with `13s + the recording's own captured duration`, so a longer recording gets a proportionally longer allowance before it's considered stuck (a short utterance's behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged, since the floor is still 13s) - live-verified this alone is enough to cover realistic variance on real Metal-accelerated hardware (a ~49s utterance's whole pipeline completes in ~5-6s, comfortably inside the new ~62s ceiling). (2) Since no duration-scaled ceiling can rule out every legitimately-slow case (a cold model load, thermal throttling, genuinely slower hardware) without also risking "wait minutes for a hung case," `DictationEngine.report(_:isCeilingTimeout:)`/`onStatusChanged` gained a second parameter, `isCeilingTimeout`, `true` only for the ceiling's own forced call - the real pipeline keeps running regardless of the ceiling firing and always calls `report(_:)` again with the true outcome once it completes (this already happened before the fix; the missing piece was purely on the consuming side). `DictationController`'s status card has no gating and already showed that eventual correction; `DictationHUDController` (the floating on-screen indicator, the one most likely to be watched during a real dictation into another app) was the actual gap - its `wasActive` flag used to be cleared by *any* `.didNotCatchThat`, silently discarding a later real success. `handle(_:isCeilingTimeout:)` now only clears `wasActive` when `isCeilingTimeout` is `false`, so a later real completion (success or a genuine failure) still correctly supersedes a ceiling-forced display. Accepted trade-off, documented at the call site: if a dictation attempt truly never completes (hung, not just slow), `wasActive` stays `true` until the next `.recording`, so one unrelated permission-button click in that narrow window could show a spurious "Pasted" flash - judged far smaller than the bug this fixes. **Verified live end to end** (real mic, real `say`, real local Whisper engine, no mocks): a realistic long-processing case (an artificial delay within the new ceiling) completed with no ceiling firing and a correct success display; a deliberately slower case (an artificial delay exceeding even the new ceiling) showed the ceiling fire first (`isCeilingTimeout=true`, "Didn't catch that", `wasActive` left `true`) and then the real completion correctly superseded it moments later (`HUD.present(success)`, `wasActive=false`) - the exact fix confirmed working; a short utterance and the Apple-only path (local Whisper toggle off) were both re-verified unchanged. All temporary debug logging/artificial-delay hooks used for this investigation were reverted before commit - none of it should reappear in `DictationEngine.swift`/`DictationHUD.swift`/`main.swift`.
- **`fm/grandline-whisper-carry-prompt-fix` closed a real, code-confirmed gap in the vocabulary-biasing mechanism described above: `WhisperEngine.swift`'s `transcribe(...)` never set `params.carry_initial_prompt = true`, so it stayed at whisper.cpp's own default of `false`.** Traced directly in the vendored source (`whisper-src/whisper.cpp`, the `prompt_past0`/`prompt_past1` handling around the "prepare prompt" block and the per-window prompt-building loop): with the flag `false`, the captain's vocabulary-derived `initial_prompt` seeds `prompt_past1` (the *dynamic* rolling context) at the very start, but that gets fully overwritten by whisper's own just-decoded tokens the moment the first internal decode window (~30s) completes - so a recording longer than the first window loses the vocabulary bias partway through, silently. With the flag `true`, the prompt instead seeds `prompt_past0` (a *static* slot only ever populated once, `if (prompt_past0.empty())`), which every subsequent window's prompt-building step re-consults (`can_take0 = params.carry_initial_prompt && !prompt_past0.empty()`) - so the bias now persists for the whole recording, not just the first window. Live-verified with the real `large-v3-turbo-q5_0` model on real Apple Silicon: a short (~2.5s) `say`-generated utterance and a long (~36s, past the window boundary) `say`-generated utterance, each containing a real vocabulary word ("herdr"), transcribed identically before and after the fix in both cases - no regression to short-utterance behavior. **Worth knowing if this is ever re-investigated**: whisper.cpp's `initial_prompt`/`carry_initial_prompt` is a soft prior-context nudge on decoder token probabilities, not a hard per-word constraint (unlike Apple's `contextualStrings`) - it does not force an exact, possibly out-of-vocabulary spelling like "herdr" to appear even when carried forward, so a live before/after transcript comparison can legitimately show no visible difference for a given word/audio pair without indicating the fix is ineffective. The structural code trace (which C-struct field the prompt lands in, and whether that field survives the per-window reset) is the definitive proof here, not the transcript diff alone. The "Words I use often" explainer text (`DictationController.swift`'s `buildVocabularySection`) was also reworded to stop implying one uniform strong mechanism, since Apple's path treats each phrase as a real per-phrase hint while Whisper's path treats the whole list as a much softer style nudge.
- **`fm/grandline-dictation-page-redesign` fixed a real captain-reported layout bug on this page: every card's title+description text was confined to roughly the left half of the card, leaving a large blank gap before the trailing toggle/button/chip** - a proposed Lavish/Claude-artifact design-system-pass prototype (not new colors/components, per its own annotations - everything maps to an existing `HelmTheme`/`HelmCard`/`HelmButton`/`IconTileView` primitive) made the gap obvious by comparison against its `.card-head` shape (tile + `.grow` text + a right-anchored chip). **Root cause was the same class of bug `ToolRowLayout`'s own history documents twice (see this file's "status column jitters... because textStack keeps its natural width" entry above), stacked two-deep**: every one of this page's `[icon tile, vertical title/description text stack, trailing switch/button]` rows (`buildStatusSection`/`buildCleanupSection`/`buildLocalWhisperSection`'s `toggleRow`/`modelRow`, plus `buildShortcutSection`'s two-element row) was left at the default `.gravityAreas` distribution (AGENTS.md gotcha #10 - no view's hugging priority matters until `distribution = .fill` is set), **and** each row's text stack called the content-level `setContentHuggingPriority`, which is a no-op on an `NSStackView` (gotcha #12) - the stack-level `setHuggingPriority`/`setClippingResistancePriority` (`ToolRowLayout.columnHugging`'s own API) is what actually lets it absorb a row's leftover width. Fixing only those two (row `.fill` + stack-level hugging) is still not enough for a *wrapping* label, unlike `ToolRowLayout`'s truncating one: a wrapping `NSTextField`'s intrinsic width comes from its own `preferredMaxLayoutWidth`, which those two fixes don't touch, so the text stack's frame correctly grows to fill the row while the visible text keeps wrapping at whatever hardcoded pixel constant (460/520/620) it started with, leaving the same blank gap just relocated *inside* the stack's own bounds. The third piece is `HelmEmptyState.layout()`'s already-established pattern (`HelmDesignSystem.swift`) - read a container's real, already-resolved width back on every layout pass and feed it to the wrapping label directly - reused here via `DictationController.viewDidLayout()`, which syncs `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` from each row's own text stack (or, for the vocabulary card's single-column body, straight from its externally-pinned `setBody` container) to the label on every pass; the hardcoded constants survive only as the *initial* seed for the very first pass, before any real width is known, so a first-pass Auto Layout conflict from an unbounded (0-width-cap) wrapping label can't happen. **This task's own body copy was also tightened to the prototype's terser phrasing** (Cleanup/Local Whisper/Shortcut/vocabulary explainer descriptions dropped to one line each, with the nuance that got cut - network access, the Apple-vs-Whisper vocabulary-hint difference - still available via the page's existing footnote or, for the vocabulary explainer, a hover tooltip) **and gained the two trailing status chips the prototype shows and the live page was missing**: a "On device" pill on the Status card (`statusChip`, mutually exclusive with the existing permission-request button, shown only for `.ready` - never during `.cleaningUp`, which genuinely does reach the network via the claude CLI rewrite and would make the claim false) and a "Model ready" pill replacing the Local Whisper card's plain text for that one state (every other state - downloading/failed/not-downloaded - keeps its existing longer, dynamic plain-text message, since the prototype only actually depicts the terse ready state as a chip). Both pills reuse `ToolRowLayout.pill(text:colorHex:into:label:theme:)` verbatim - the app's one shared, contrast-corrected chip component (see `HelmContrast`'s doc comment) - rather than a new one-off. **Verified by `swift build` plus constraint/frame-code reasoning only, deliberately not by launching the app**: this task's own brief (and repeated real incidents on sibling redesign tasks) established that this app has no OS-level process isolation between a worktree's own build and the captain's actively-running signed instance, so launching any built copy - including a quick self-test-and-exit invocation of `.build/debug/FirstmateCockpit` - was avoided entirely; the width-flow chain (row width, fully pinned top-down via each card's `widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: contentStack.widthAnchor)` and `HelmCard.setBody`'s leading+trailing insets, down through `.fill` distribution's fixed-vs-flexible sibling split) was traced by hand instead of measured live. The next real visual check of this page is the captain's own, against their already-running instance.

## Claude usage panel (Herdr-backed tabs)

`fm/grandline-herdr-utilization-panel` added a "Claude usage" toolbar icon and popover, visible only on a Herdr-backed `.mirror` tab (`ConsoleController.updateUtilizationControls`, gated the exact opposite of `updateComposeControls` - see the Compose entries above) - built from a scout report (`data/grandline-herdr-utilization-panel-research/report.md`) and a captain-approved design plan.

- **Data (`QuotaData.swift`)** shells out to `quota-axi --json --provider claude --allow-keychain-prompt`, following `VaultData.swift`'s own "thin window onto a CLI tool" shape (its own purpose-built `run`/`resolveExecutable`/`RunResult` trio, per that file's own stated convention - consolidating this app's five near-duplicate copies of that helper is still out of scope). Parses `windows[]` entries keyed `id: "five_hour"`/`"seven_day"` into session/weekly `percentUsed`/`resetsAt`/`pace.status`. **Real gotcha hit and fixed**: `resetsAt` comes back as e.g. `"2026-08-17T18:50:00.081363+00:00"` - a plain `ISO8601DateFormatter()` (default format options) returns `nil` for this because of the fractional seconds; `.formatOptions = [.withInternetDateTime, .withFractionalSeconds]` is required (confirmed live) - `QuotaSource.parseResetsAt` tries fractional-seconds first, then falls back to the plain formatter.
- **`fm/grandline-quota-percent-fix` fixed a real captain-reported bug: the popover always showed "Couldn't parse quota-axi's output."** - the original parser read a `percentUsed` key that never existed in the real output; `quota-axi`'s real `windows[]` entries carry `percentRemaining` instead (confirmed live: `{"id": "five_hour", "percentRemaining": 91, ...}`), so every `guard` failed and both `session`/`weekly` stayed `nil`. Fixed by reading `percentRemaining` and converting once, at the parse boundary (`percentUsed = 100 - percentRemaining`), rather than threading a "remaining" semantic through `QuotaWindow`/`QuotaUsagePopover.swift`'s bar-fill/80%-90% threshold code, which is all written in terms of "used." The real output also carries two other window kinds (`"id": "model:fable"`, kind `model`; `"id": "extra_usage"`, kind `credits`) that the existing `switch id { ... default: continue }` already ignores correctly - `QuotaDataSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_QUOTA_DATA_TESTS=1`) pins this with a real-shaped payload (copied from a live `quota-axi` run) asserting those two never get parsed as session/weekly, plus one-window-present, and genuinely-unparseable-input cases. Verified live against the real, installed `quota-axi` CLI on this machine (a real session/weekly fetch converted 91%/79% remaining into 9%/21% used correctly) via a temporary debug probe in `main.swift`, reverted before commit.
- **UI (`QuotaUsagePopover.swift`)** structurally mirrors `ConsoleComposerPopover.swift` (transient `NSPopover`, `IconTileView` header tinted `.violet`, live `ThemeManager` observation, `wantsLayer` root with an explicit theme background per gotcha #8). Icon: `gauge.with.dots.needle.33percent` (SF Symbols 4, this project's macOS 13 minimum) - a gauge-family glyph chosen specifically to not be confused with the adjacent `themeButton` (a half-filled circle). Bar/pace-chip colors come from `HelmTint.good`/`.warn`/`.critical` at the captain-specified 80%/90% thresholds; crossing 90% shows an explicit warning line, not just a red bar. Auto-refreshes every 10 minutes while open (`Timer`, invalidated in `NSPopoverDelegate.popoverDidClose` - mirrors `FleetNotifier`'s `timer?.invalidate(); timer = nil` teardown) plus once immediately on open.
- **Real layout gotcha, live-caught via pixel measurement (not just eyeballing a screenshot)**: a vertical `NSStackView`'s `alignment = .leading` does **not** stretch arranged subviews to the stack's own width the way `.fill`/an explicit width constraint does - it only left-aligns them at their own intrinsic width. `rowsStack` (holding the session/weekly rows) had this exact gap: each row sized to its own narrower intrinsic content, leaving a large blank strip between the progress bar/pace-chip row and the popover's actual right inset - confirmed by sampling real pixel colors in a captured screenshot (the bar's fill/track color stopped well short of where the header row's text ended). Fixed with explicit `sessionRow.widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: rowsStack.widthAnchor)` / `weeklyRow...` constraints, matching this app's established "explicit width-equal-to-stack constraint" convention (`ConsoleComposerPopover.swift`'s own `titleRow.widthAnchor.constraint(equalTo: stack.widthAnchor)` etc.) rather than relying on stack alignment to stretch anything.
- **A separate, pre-existing (not introduced by this task) `.transient` `NSPopover` interaction worth knowing about**: reassigning `popover.appearance` on an already-shown popover can occasionally cause AppKit to auto-dismiss it (observed intermittently on both this popover and, once, on Compose's own identical `popover.appearance =` line) - `QuotaUsageController`'s `NSPopoverDelegate.popoverDidClose` correctly detects this and stops the refresh timer either way (no leaked timer), so the only user-facing symptom, if it recurs, is the popover occasionally closing on its own during a theme switch - not a data or timer bug. Not chased further since Compose has the identical code path and this predates this task.

## VPN (built, then removed)

A rail section and a Tools panel for controlling Barracuda VPN and OpenVPN Connect were built across several tasks (`fm/grandline-vpn-toggle-integration`, `fm/grandline-hosts-vpn-flyout-redesign`, `fm/grandline-rail-unify-and-mark-polish`, `fm/grandline-vpn-divider-and-connect-fixes`) and then removed entirely (`fm/grandline-remove-vpn-feature`) once live testing showed both clients have restrictions that make reliable control from this app impractical: Barracuda's own Network Extension crashes internally (a nil-argument exception in its own `PacketTunnel` code) on any connect triggered outside its own app UI - confirmed via its own crash logs across 5 real attempts, and not an "app needs to be running first" issue - and OpenVPN Connect is Electron/Chromium-based, which does not expose its real UI content to macOS's Accessibility API even after trying the standard techniques to force it. Neither is fixable from this app's side. If this is ever revisited, re-confirm both clients' restrictions haven't changed before rebuilding any of it.

## Notification Center

`fm/grandline-notification-center` added a bell + badge + dropdown panel between `TopBarController`'s `searchPill` and `themeButton` (design: `data/grandline-notification-center/design-reference.html`), aggregating nine already-computed "the captain should know about this" signals into one place instead of six separate pages.

- **`GrandLineNotificationCenter.swift`** is the store: an app-lifetime singleton (`ThemeManager`-style `observe`/notify), `AppNotification` (id/title/subtext/kind/tint/navigate), and one entry point, `set(_:id:)` - present means "this condition is true right now," `nil` means "resolved." One id per source, so dedup is structural, not a diffing step. `.actionNeeded` ("waiting for you": fleet decisions, PRs ready, SRE Lead replies, fleet tasks finished) auto-clears on resolution only, no manual dismiss. `.informational` ("FYI": tool updates, fork drift, Vault attention, setup drift, Shift due items) also supports `dismiss(id:)`/`markAllRead()`, remembered by the dismissed entry's exact subtext (`dismissedDetail`) - the same condition with unchanged text stays hidden, a materially changed detail resurfaces it. See the file's own header for the full reasoning.
- **`NotificationSources.swift`** is the only place that formats each signal's title/subtext/tint - no detection logic lives here, every function takes a count/list a page or poller already computed.
- **`NotificationCenterPopover.swift`** is the bell (`NotificationBellButton`, fixed white-on-systemRed badge, same non-theme-tinted convention as `IconRailController.attachBadge`) and the panel (`NSPopover`, mirrors `ConsoleComposerPopover.swift`/`QuotaUsagePopover.swift`'s idiom exactly). `TopBarController.notificationCenter` owns both.
- **`fm/grandline-notification-bell-badge-fix-2` fixed the bell badge for good, after a first pass (`fm/grandline-notification-bell-badge-fix`, shaving the overlap from 5pt to 2pt) still wasn't enough per a second captain screenshot.** Root cause: at a 34x34 box with a 9pt corner radius, the rounded curve starts well before the flat edges, so *any* small overlap positioned at that diagonal corner cuts across the curve itself - shaving the overlap number further was never going to fix it. This is the exact same lesson `IconRailController.attachBadge` already learned the hard way (two overlap-tuning attempts before it stopped overlapping the icon's box entirely - see that method's own doc comment). The fix applies that same shape here: `NotificationBellButton` now separates the *visible bordered icon square* (`iconBackground`, a fixed 34x34 subview matching `TopBarController.themeButton` exactly) from the button's own overall frame, which is widened (`NotificationBellButton.controlWidth` = icon + a 3pt gap + a 32pt reserved badge zone) so the badge sits entirely to the icon's right, never overlapping its frame - verified live via a temporary geometry probe confirming zero intersection between the badge's frame and the icon square's frame at every count (including "99+"). `TopBarController`'s bell width constraint reads `NotificationBellButton.controlWidth` instead of a literal `34`; its leading/trailing anchor formulas versus `searchPill`/`themeButton` were deliberately left untouched, so both gaps stay the same 10pt they always were - the reserved badge zone just occupies what used to be dead space in the existing gap to `themeButton`, rather than shrinking it. The popover itself anchors on `bell.visibleIconFrame` (the icon square), not `bell.bounds` (now wider), so it still opens flush under the icon. Any future badge placed near a rounded corner in this app should go straight to the "fully outside the box" shape rather than re-attempting an overlap-amount tweak.
- **Wiring, per signal** (see `AppShellController.loadView()`/`connectHost`, `main.swift`, `FleetNotifier.swift`, `ShiftNotifications.swift`, `ConsoleController.swift`):
  1. Fleet decisions/blocked and 2. PR-ready reuse `FleetController.onNeedsDecisionCountChanged`/`ReviewController.onOpenPRCountChanged` verbatim (already computed for the rail badges) - zero new polling.
  3/4/5/6 (tool updates, GitHub Sync drift, Vault attention, Bootstrap setup drift) are polled by **`BackgroundSignalsPoller.swift`**, a new slow (15-minute) background poll - deliberately not `FleetNotifier`'s 30s cadence, since these four each shell out to `brew`/`npm`/`gh api`/`av` per catalog item/repo/tool; see that file's header for the cadence tradeoff (a deliberate decision, not a default).
  7. SRE Lead replying on a backgrounded tab is detected in `ConsoleController.handleSRELeadSubmit` (fires `onSRELeadReplyWhileBackground` when the replying tab isn't `currentTab` or the page is hidden) and cleared in `select(tabID:)`/`markCurrentTabAsRead()`/`closeTab`/`shutdown()` - the one signal with more than one live id at once (`sre-lead.<tabID>`).
  8. Shift due/overdue is fed from `ShiftNotificationScheduler.poll()`'s own existing due-detection (`onDueCountsChanged`) - the same computation that already drives the OS banner, not a second implementation.
  9. Fleet tasks finishing (done/failed) has no other computed home - `FleetNotifier` was extended to detect it. This also required decoupling `FleetNotifier`'s poll from the "Bell & notifications" Settings toggle: the poll (`start()`) now always runs from launch regardless, and `setEnabled(_:)` only gates whether an OS banner is *also* posted - the in-app center must stay current whether or not OS banners are opted into. A finished task is "acknowledged" (stops resurfacing) only once the captain opens the aggregated notification (`FleetNotifier.acknowledgeFinishedTasks(ids:)`), separate from the OS banner's own one-shot dedup set.
- **Self-tests**: `GrandLineNotificationCenterSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_NOTIFICATION_CENTER_TESTS=1`) covers the store's pure logic (dedup, both clear semantics, dismiss/resurface-on-change, badge count, observer fan-out). `NotificationCenterSRELeadSelfTest.swift` (`FM_RUN_NOTIFICATION_CENTER_SRE_LEAD_TESTS=1`) drives the real `ConsoleController` (via its existing `debugStartSRELead`/`debugAskSRELead`/`debugSelectTab`/`debugCloseTab` hooks, same fake-`claude` harness as `SRELeadPerTabSelfTest.swift`) for signal #7 specifically - the trickiest one, since nothing outside a tab's own pane previously observed its phase changing.
- **`fm/grandline-notification-row-redesign` restyled each panel row (`NotificationRowView`, `NotificationCenterPopover.swift`) from a flat, borderless dot+title+subtext line into its own bordered card** - a colored left accent bar, a small round `IconTileView`-style icon badge, a bold uppercase kicker label, the body message, and a trailing chip carrying the entry's source/clear-rule text (captain reference: a Slack-RCA claims panel). Rendering-only: the store, the 9 signal adapters, dedup/clear semantics, and the badge count are untouched. `NotificationRowPresentation` maps each entry's already-stable `id` (from `NotificationSources.swift`) to a per-source icon + kicker - a rendering-layer lookup, not a new field on `AppNotification`; a future signal added without a matching case here still renders via a generic fallback. The card border/fill reuses `ToolRowLayout`'s `cardStyle` idiom and the chip reuses `ToolRowLayout.pill` directly (`HelmUIComponents.swift`), but the row itself stays a bespoke view rather than going through `ToolRowLayout.build` - that assembly's icon-tile/trailing-stack/chevron/log shape has no concept of a left accent bar and carries controls (expandable log, button stack) this row doesn't need. The panel widened from 320pt to 340pt to fit the richer card without feeling cramped. Each row's own leading/trailing anchors to `rowsStack` (not a width-equal-to-stack constraint) are what create the card's margin from the panel edges - the old full-bleed row relied on width-equal-to-stack. Verified live (temporary env-gated probe, reverted before commit, per this file's "Verifying native UI bugs" convention): all 8 real sourced entries rendered as distinct, non-overlapping cards with no element overflowing its card's bounds, across `helm-dark`/`helm-light`/`catppuccin-mocha`/`gruvbox-light`, plus confirmed click-to-navigate and "Mark all read" visibility are unaffected. The probe deliberately never called `ThemeManager.shared.setTheme` (which persists to the real `UserDefaults.standard` "fm.themeID" key) - it re-themed the panel instance directly via `applyTheme(_:)` to avoid clobbering the real captain's saved theme preference on a shared dev machine; any future multi-theme sweep probe in this codebase should do the same rather than reach for `setTheme`.

## Maintaining this file

Keep this file for knowledge useful to almost every future agent session in this project.
Do not repeat what the codebase already shows; point to the authoritative file or command instead.
Prefer rewriting or pruning existing entries over appending new ones.
When updating this file, preserve this bar for all agents and keep entries concise.
